### POAM-2026-082: Hugging Face Hub Token Exposure in vLLM Image Config, Cloud Build, and Terraform State

**Control:** NIST IA-5, SC-7, SC-28
**Risk Level:** High
**Status:** Open
**Date Opened:** 2026-09-28
**Target Closure:** 2026-10-05

**Description:**
Five coupled defects exposed `HUGGING_FACE_HUB_TOKEN` and prevented vLLM from loading model weights on GKE:
1. [`deployment/docker/Dockerfile.vllm`](../deployment/docker/Dockerfile.vllm) declared `ARG HF_TOKEN=""` and `ENV HUGGING_FACE_HUB_TOKEN=${HF_TOKEN}`, persisting the token in the container image configuration despite a comment claiming it was not baked in.
2. [`deployment/docker/cloudbuild.vllm.yaml`](../deployment/docker/cloudbuild.vllm.yaml) and [`scripts/build_images.sh`](../scripts/build_images.sh) passed `_HF_TOKEN` as a plaintext Cloud Build substitution visible to anyone with build-viewer permissions, even though the image build only runs `pip install`.
3. [`infra/modules/app_secrets/main.tf`](../infra/modules/app_secrets/main.tf) stored `hf_token` in Terraform state and created `hf-token-secret`, which [`infra/modules/governed_advisor/main.tf`](../infra/modules/governed_advisor/main.tf) mounted into the advisor pod even though no code in `src/` reads it.
4. `default-deny-external-egress` in `infra/targets/gcp-gke/network_policy.tf` (49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/network_policy.tf) blocked all external egress for `vllm-inference` and `vllm-reasoning` pods (including GKE Workload Identity metadata server `169.254.169.254:80` / `169.254.169.252:988` and GCS `storage.googleapis.com:443`), and `local.fqdn_network_policies` was only hashed into `terraform_data.network_policy_workload_rollout` without a `kubernetes_manifest` resource to materialize the `FQDNNetworkPolicy` objects in the cluster.
5. `infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf` (49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf) set `vllm_load_format = "gcs_filesystem"` (an invalid vLLM loader name instead of `"runai_streamer"`), omitted `--load-format $VLLM_LOAD_FORMAT` and `--served-model-name $SERVED_MODEL_NAME` from `vllm_command`, and defaulted `model_fast` / `model_reasoning` to bare Hugging Face Hub IDs instead of `gs://` paths in the model bucket.

**Remediation Plan:**
1. **(Completed in code/IaC)** Removed `ARG HF_TOKEN` / `ENV HUGGING_FACE_HUB_TOKEN` from [`deployment/docker/Dockerfile.vllm`](../deployment/docker/Dockerfile.vllm), `_HF_TOKEN` from [`deployment/docker/cloudbuild.vllm.yaml`](../deployment/docker/cloudbuild.vllm.yaml) and [`scripts/build_images.sh`](../scripts/build_images.sh), and `hf_token` / `hf-token-secret` from [`infra/modules/app_secrets/`](../infra/modules/app_secrets/main.tf), [`infra/modules/governed_advisor/main.tf`](../infra/modules/governed_advisor/main.tf), [`infra/targets/gcp-gke/`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/variables.tf), `2bacc478:infra/targets/agnostic/variables.tf`, [`deploy_all.sh`](../deploy_all.sh), and [`infra/load_env.sh`](../infra/load_env.sh).
2. **(Completed in code/IaC)** Configured all GKE postures to load `model_fast` and `model_reasoning` from `gs://` paths in the model bucket via `vllm_load_format = "runai_streamer"`, passing `--load-format $VLLM_LOAD_FORMAT` and `--served-model-name $SERVED_MODEL_NAME` with `HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1` and `TRANSFORMERS_OFFLINE=1` in [`infra/modules/vllm_inference/`](../infra/modules/vllm_inference/main.tf) and `infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf` (49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf).
3. **(Completed in code/IaC)** Added `kubernetes_network_policy_v1.vllm_egress_l3_l4` (DNS + GKE metadata server `169.254.169.254:80` and `169.254.169.252:988`), added `vllm_egress_fqdn` (`storage.googleapis.com` and `oauth2.googleapis.com` on TCP 443) to `local.fqdn_network_policies` and `de5534ac:deployment/k8s/cilium/egress-lockdown.yaml`, and materialized `local.fqdn_network_policies` via `kubernetes_manifest.fqdn_network_policy` in `infra/targets/gcp-gke/network_policy.tf` (49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/network_policy.tf).
4. **(Pending operator action required before closure)** Rotate the exposed Hugging Face token on `huggingface.co` and delete all historical `gcr.io/$PROJECT_ID/vllm-streamer` image digests that contain `ENV HUGGING_FACE_HUB_TOKEN` in their image configuration.
### POAM-2026-083: GKE Image Digest Pinning and Binary Authorization Attestation Chain (Follow-on to POAM-2026-013)

**Control:** NIST SI-2, SI-3, SI-7, CM-7
**Risk Level:** Low (code and IaC remediated; open only pending live staging cluster verification)
**Status:** Open
**Date Opened:** 2026-09-28
**Target Closure:** 2026-10-15

**Description:**
While `POAM-2026-013` pinned third-party image tags in `deployment/k8s/`, the sole GKE Terraform target (`infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf` (49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf)) and Cloud Build configurations ([`deployment/docker/`](../deployment/docker/)) still defaulted to `:latest` tags, and `google_binary_authorization_attestor.build_attestor` in [`infra/targets/gcp-gke/perimeter.tf`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/perimeter.tf) declared no `public_keys` block or signing step. Consequently, enabling `enable_binary_authorization = true` with `evaluation_mode = "REQUIRE_ATTESTATION"` and `enforcement_mode = "ENFORCED_BLOCK_AND_AUDIT_LOG"` caused GKE Binary Authorization admission to reject every workload pod.

**Root Cause Analysis (2026-10-01 Staging Rollout):**
1. **Cloud Build Identity Mismatch (P1):** Key-level IAM on `google_kms_crypto_key.binauthz_attestor`, `google_binary_authorization_attestor.build_attestor`, and `google_container_analysis_note.build_attestor_note` was bound to the legacy Cloud Build service account (`[PROJECT_NUMBER]@cloudbuild.gserviceaccount.com`), whereas projects created after May 2023 execute builds without an explicit `serviceAccount:` as the Compute Engine default service account (`[PROJECT_NUMBER]-compute@developer.gserviceaccount.com`) — which is also the GKE node identity and must never hold `roles/cloudkms.signer` on the Binary Authorization attestor key.
2. **GA vs. Beta `gcloud` Track & Duplication (P2):** `sign-and-create` exists only under `gcloud beta container binauthz attestations sign-and-create` (and `alpha`), not the GA `gcloud container binauthz attestations` track. Because the inline shell snippet was duplicated across 10 files (`deployment/docker/cloudbuild.*.yaml`, [`scripts/build_images.sh`](../scripts/build_images.sh), and [`scripts/mirror_and_attest_images.sh`](../scripts/mirror_and_attest_images.sh)) without a post-signing verification check, every automated attestation step failed on the CLI track mismatch.

**Break-Glass Record (2026-10-01):**
- commits `ad9ba9c7` (`gateway`) and `8d2ccf03` (`compliance-bridge`) were attested manually by an operator on `2026-10-01` while P1/P2 blocked in-build signing. These digests are superseded once all images are rebuilt and attested by `cage-cloudbuild-staging` under PR-2.

**Remediation Implemented in Code & IaC:**
1. Provisioned an asymmetric Cloud KMS signing key (`google_kms_crypto_key.binauthz_attestor` in [`infra/targets/gcp-gke/kms_signing.tf`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/kms_signing.tf)) with authoritative key-level IAM granting `roles/cloudkms.signer` exclusively to `local.cloudbuild_member`, and wired its public key into `google_binary_authorization_attestor.build_attestor` via `public_keys { pkix_public_key { ... } }` in [`infra/targets/gcp-gke/perimeter.tf`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/perimeter.tf).
2. Replaced all `:latest` image references across `infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf` (49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf), [`infra/targets/gcp-gke/variables.tf`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/variables.tf), and [`deployment/docker/`](../deployment/docker/) with `var.image_digests` (`name -> repo@sha256:<64-hex>`), backed by a fail-closed Terraform `validation` block rejecting tag-only or `:latest` references when `enable_binary_authorization = true`.
3. **(PR-2 — `fix/cloudbuild-identity`)** Provisioned a dedicated, least-privilege Cloud Build service account (`google_service_account.cloudbuild`, `cage-cloudbuild-${var.environment}`) in [`infra/targets/gcp-gke/iam.tf`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/iam.tf) with `roles/logging.logWriter`, `roles/artifactregistry.writer`, and bucket-scoped `roles/storage.objectViewer` on `${var.project_id}_cloudbuild`, plus `roles/containeranalysis.notes.occurrences.viewer` in [`infra/targets/gcp-gke/perimeter.tf`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/perimeter.tf), and repointed `local.cloudbuild_member` in [`infra/targets/gcp-gke/kms_signing.tf`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/kms_signing.tf) so the authoritative KMS policy revokes the legacy `@cloudbuild.gserviceaccount.com` binding automatically.
4. **(PR-2 — `fix/cloudbuild-identity`)** Consolidated all 10 inline attestation snippets into a single fail-closed script ([`scripts/attest_image.sh`](../scripts/attest_image.sh)) that invokes `gcloud beta container binauthz attestations sign-and-create` and verifies the created occurrence via `gcloud beta container binauthz attestations list`, and replaced the per-service duplicate configs with [`deployment/docker/cloudbuild.image.yaml`](../deployment/docker/cloudbuild.image.yaml) and [`scripts/render_image_digests.sh`](../scripts/render_image_digests.sh).
5. Updated [`compliance/lula/lula-validation-si2.yaml`](../compliance/lula/lula-validation-si2.yaml) to validate `@sha256:` digest pinning and `imagePullPolicy in {"Always", "IfNotPresent"}` across all Terraform-managed Deployments (`gateway`, `governed-financial-advisor`, `vllm-inference`, `vllm-reasoning`, `nemo-guardrails`, `agentsight-ui`, and optional `compliance-bridge`), and updated the OSCAL SI-3 narrative in [`src/gateway/governance/oscal_ssp_exporter.py`](../src/gateway/governance/oscal_ssp_exporter.py) and [`compliance/oscal/system-security-plan.yaml`](../compliance/oscal/system-security-plan.yaml).

**Remaining Closure Criteria:**
1. Every image digest referenced by [`infra/targets/gcp-gke/staging.tfvars`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/staging.tfvars) carries a Binary Authorization attestation whose creator is `cage-cloudbuild-staging@<project>.iam.gserviceaccount.com`; zero manual `sign-and-create` executions since PR-2.
2. Confirm all pods in `governance-stack` pass Binary Authorization admission and record live `lula validate -f compliance/lula/lula-validation-si2.yaml` evidence.

### POAM-2026-099: FRIA Declared but Never Enforced (EU_ECB)

> Renumbered on 2026-10-04 from POAM-2026-084, which was also assigned to the evidence pipeline integrity item (AU-9). The content is unchanged.

**Control:** EU AI Act Art. 27 (`CTRL_FRIA_006`), NIST SI-10
**Risk Level:** High
**Status:** Open (remediated in `refactor/fria-jurisdiction`, pending merge)
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-01
**Target Closure:** 2026-10-15

**Description:**
From `v2.0.0-dev.1` (commit `1902c92`, 2026-06-01) until `refactor/fria-jurisdiction`, the Fundamental Rights Impact Assessment existed only as the `enforce_fria_boundary()` primitive in `normative_provider.py`. `run_pipeline()` never called it, and no other in-tree caller did. No EU_ECB decision therefore consulted an impact assessment or the normative provider's `validate_fria()`, even though `CTRL_FRIA_006` was listed as an EU_ECB control. The primitive also let an action through on model confidence ≥ 0.95 without any assessment. The universal confidence band was misnamed "FRIA" (`fria.zone_allow` / `fria.zone_defer`) in every region.

**Remediation Implemented in Code:**
1. The `fria` tier ([`FriaTier`](../src/gateway/governance/jurisdiction/eu_ai_act/fria_tier.py)) is contributed only by the EU_ECB entry of [`JURISDICTIONS`](../src/gateway/governance/jurisdiction/registry.py) and assembled by [`assemble_governor()`](../src/gateway/governance/governor/assembly.py) as a phase-1 tier after `causal`. It denies when the deployer's FRIA artefact is stale or missing (Art. 27(2), 365-day interval), and when the provider is unavailable, times out or refuses. It requires approval on a `needs_human_review` finding. There is no confidence fast path.
2. `assert_production_posture()` ([`posture.py`](../src/gateway/governance/governor/posture.py)) refuses an enforcing EU_ECB posture backed by the stub `NormativeProvider` (`jurisdiction_requirements` check).
3. `enforce_fria_boundary()` and `FRIAEnforcementResult` are deleted. The universal band is renamed `confidence.agent_threshold` / `confidence.defer_floor` ([`BREAKING_CHANGES.md`](BREAKING_CHANGES.md) P6-1).
4. `ControlRegistry.reconfigure()` no longer resets `active_region` to the default after loading a region. Without this fix, a reconfigured EU_ECB registry would have assembled without the `fria` tier.
5. Verified by [`tests/governor/test_jurisdiction_wiring.py`](../tests/governor/test_jurisdiction_wiring.py), which observes each fail-closed path failing, and by the `JURISDICTION_TIERS` sub-proof in [`proof/model.py`](../proof/model.py), with parity in [`tests/test_formal_profile_parity.py`](../tests/test_formal_profile_parity.py). [`compliance/lula/lula-validation-eu-fria.yaml`](../compliance/lula/lula-validation-eu-fria.yaml) now also asserts `CAGE_DEPLOYMENT_REGION=EU_ECB`.
6. (GHSA-f6xq-23f7-9grm) The tier was not re-run after a human approval, so a determination revoked while a request was held was never observed before the seal. Jurisdiction tiers now declare `revalidated_after_approval` and POST_HITL re-runs them; a `FRIA_EXTERNAL_HOLD` is answered only by an approval given against it (`PostHitlApproval.approved_holds`). The provider payload now carries the verified workload identity (`actor_id` / `operator_urn`) and the trace id (`correlation_id`). Proof claims 10 (`post_hitl_rechecks_jurisdiction`) and 14 (`approval_answers_only_its_holds`), with runtime parity and scenario tests in the files above.

**Remaining Closure Criteria:**
1. Merge `refactor/fria-jurisdiction`; record the merge commit SHA and the actual merge date here.
2. Independent external FRIA validation remains tracked by EU-001 (provider credentials).

### POAM-2026-100: SWIFT/BIC Sanitizer Pattern Redacted Governance Verdicts

**Control:** NIST SI-10, AU-10
**Risk Level:** High
**Status:** Open (remediated in `fix/pii-swift-false-positive`, pending merge)
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-04
**Target Closure:** 2026-10-15

**Description:**
[`EvidenceStreamSink`](../src/gateway/governance/evidence/stream.py) sanitizes every event before JCS canonicalization and hashing. The SWIFT/BIC pattern in [`pii_sanitizer.py`](../src/gateway/governance/pii_sanitizer.py) (`[A-Z]{4}[A-Z]{2}[A-Z0-9]{2}(?:[A-Z0-9]{3})?`) matched any eight- or eleven-character upper-case token. Governance verdicts and node statuses (`APPROVED`, `REJECTED`, `ESCALATE`, `COMPLETE`, ...) were permanently replaced by `[REDACTED_SWIFT]` in tamper-evident evidence. Two events differing only in such a word produced identical payloads, which undermines non-repudiation of the recorded decision.

**Remediation Implemented in Code:**
1. A BIC is redacted only when a `BIC`/`SWIFT` label precedes it in text, or when it is the value of a BIC/SWIFT dict key. Its positions 5-6 must also be an ISO 3166-1 alpha-2 country code. A country check alone does not separate English words (`ESCALATE` → `AT`), so the contextual cue is required.
2. [`tests/test_pii_sanitizer_bic.py`](../tests/test_pii_sanitizer_bic.py) covers labelled true positives, key-context positives, and a property test showing that no upper-case string literal in `src/`, and no `GovernanceDecision` / `OpaVerdict` / `FTRAVerdict` value, is redacted. Hypothesis tests show that unlabelled upper-case words are never redacted and that every structurally valid labelled BIC is.

**Remaining Closure Criteria:**
1. Merge `fix/pii-swift-false-positive`; record the merge commit SHA and the actual merge date here.

### POAM-2026-101: Base-Image CVEs Without a Debian Fix

**Control:** NIST RA-5, SI-2
**Risk Level:** Moderate
**Status:** Open (remediated in `fix/ci-test-errors`, pending merge)
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-05
**Target Closure:** 2026-11-15

**Description:**
The SBOM/CVE Trivy scan ([`sbom.yml`](../.github/workflows/sbom.yml)) failed on HIGH findings in the Debian 12 OS layer of `python:3.12-slim-bookworm`. On 2026-10-05 the gateway leg reported 73 findings (44 unique CVEs) and the compliance-bridge leg 34 (5 unique). All had Trivy status `affected` and no Fixed Version, so the build-time `apt-get upgrade` could not remove them. 39 came from `linux-libc-dev` (kernel headers pulled in by the C toolchain). The other five were util-linux CVE-2026-76642, CVE-2026-78408, CVE-2026-78409, CVE-2026-78410 and OpenSSL CVE-2026-84782 (DTLS). Separately, `.trivyignore` held about 40 suppressions whose review dates (2026-09-05, 2026-09-08) had passed, and `sbom.yml` scanned the repo-root advisor Dockerfile under the name `gateway`, so `src/gateway/Dockerfile` was never scanned.

**Remediation Implemented in Code (`fix/ci-test-errors`):**
1. [`Dockerfile`](../Dockerfile), [`src/gateway/Dockerfile`](../src/gateway/Dockerfile) and [`src/compliance_bridge/Dockerfile`](../src/compliance_bridge/Dockerfile) are two-stage builds on `cgr.dev/chainguard/wolfi-base`, pinned by digest (`ARG WOLFI_BASE`). The compiler and uv stay in the builder stage. The runtime stage installs only `python-3.12`, `libstdc++`, `libgcc` and `tzdata`, runs `apk upgrade`, and contains no util-linux, perl or ncurses.
2. The advisor image copies an explicit source allowlist instead of `COPY . .`, which had shipped local `node_modules` (5 HIGH `fast-uri` CVEs) into the image.
3. `sbom.yml` scans all four shipped Python images (advisor, gateway, compliance-bridge, NeMo Guardrails) with `--show-suppressed`. [`Dockerfile.nemo`](../deployment/docker/Dockerfile.nemo) moved to the same Wolfi base. It had been unscanned, ran as root, and installed an unpinned `grpcio-tools` into its runtime venv.
4. [`cloudbuild.verify.yaml`](../deployment/docker/cloudbuild.verify.yaml) builds a Dockerfile on the GKE architecture without pushing. It smoke-imports the entry module as the image user and applies the same Trivy gate.
5. Every `.trivyignore` entry was pruned. No suppression matched any finding in the new images.

**Verification (2026-10-05, Cloud Build):**
| Image | Build | Smoke import | Trivy HIGH/CRITICAL | Suppressed matches |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| governed-financial-advisor | `575e3466` | `src.governed_financial_advisor.server` ok | 0 | 0 |
| gateway | `3e268752` | `src.gateway.server.hybrid_server` ok | 0 | 0 |
| compliance-bridge | `1df4fe5d` | `compliance_bridge.main` ok | 0 | 0 |
| nemo-guardrails | `82604db1` | `src.integrations.nemo.server` ok | 0 | 0 |

**Remaining Closure Criteria:**
1. Merge `fix/ci-test-errors`. Record the merge commit SHA, and the date all three SBOM/CVE legs pass on `main`, here.

### POAM-2026-125: Output Semantic Validation Has Never Operated

**Control:** NIST SI-15 (Information Output Filtering), SI-10 (Information Input Validation)
**Risk Level:** High
**Status:** Closed (2026-10-28)
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-28
**Target Closure:** 2026-11-30

**Description:**
The advisor graph's output rail calls [`validate_output_semantics()`](../src/integrations/nemo/manager.py), which is meant to ask a model whether an answer is safe. It never asks. The call goes through NeMo's rails with the auditor instruction as a system message, but the Colang configuration has only an input flow:
- the only model call, when there is one, is the input judge's YES/NO prompt run on the output text ([`prompts.yml`](../config/rails/prompts.yml), [`main_logic.co`](../config/rails/main_logic.co));
- the reply the check parses is never a model's answer to its question. Today it is empty or NeMo's canned refusal, and both read as safe.

**Measured on 2026-10-28** (main's code, NeMo's real routing, the model client stubbed, nothing sent):

| Model reply | Auditor prompt sent | Read as |
|---|---|---|
| `UNSAFE: fabricated regulation` | no | safe |
| `<think>…</think>` then `UNSAFE: fabricated regulation` | no | safe |
| `SAFE` | no | safe |
| `YES` / `NO` | no | safe |
| endpoint down | no | safe |

With no model configured, the input judge's failure becomes the canned refusal, which also reads as safe (verified by the inference-consumer extraction; see the [jurisdiction inventory](operations/JURISDICTION_INVENTORY.md)). Text containing a finance keyword passes the deterministic stage with no model call at all.

**Since when (read).** The check arrived in `6012f067` (2026-06-03), and the rails config has never had an output flow (`git log -p config/rails/config.yml`). Until `94f920bc` the main flow echoed the user's text back as the reply, so the check parsed the output itself. Since then the reply is empty or the canned refusal. Two different mechanisms across two periods, and in neither did a model see the question.

**Severity.** High, and unconditional. Unlike POAM-2026-124 (paths that reach no verdict), the scope is simply "always": the control returns safe with a working model, a dead endpoint, or no model at all.

**How this entry was corrected.** The first version described a lenient parse defeated by reasoning traces. Its evidence and the strict-parse fix (#568) used a mocked reply channel that the real call never fills. #568 would have refused every output, and was closed unmerged.

**6-cell judge measurement (verified 2026-10-28, direct `rails.llm` invocation with strict `SAFE` parser):**
Evaluated `Qwen/Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct` (the `vllm-fast` model configured for `gcp-gke`) and `Qwen/Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct` (comparator) at `temperature=0.0` across three prompts on (a) 20 multi-paragraph benign financial advisory answers (152–188 words each) and (b) 20 labelled must-refuse outputs (4 each across 5 categories: fabricated regulatory citation, harmful/misleading financial advice, unsupported regulatory claim, leaked PII/credentials, and downstream prompt-injection/safety-bypass):

| Prompt variant | Judge model | Benign false-refusal rate (20) | Unsafe catch rate (20) | Fab. citation (4) | Harmful advice (4) | Unsupp. reg. claim (4) | Leaked PII/creds (4) | Prompt injection (4) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `manager_system_prompt` ([`manager.py`](../src/integrations/nemo/manager.py)) | `Qwen/Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct` | **7/20 (35.0%)** | **12/20 (60.0%)** | 2/4 | 4/4 | 1/4 | 1/4 | 4/4 |
| `manager_system_prompt` ([`manager.py`](../src/integrations/nemo/manager.py)) | `Qwen/Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct` | **0/20 (0.0%)** | **20/20 (100.0%)** | **4/4** | **4/4** | **4/4** | **4/4** | **4/4** |
| `actions_vague_prompt` ([`actions.py`](../config/rails/actions.py)) | `Qwen/Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct` | **11/20 (55.0%)** *(8 format failures: `**SAFETY**`, `SAFETY`, Cyrillic `SAFЕ`)* | **20/20 (100.0%)** *(3 accidental format catches)* | 4/4 | 4/4 | 4/4 | 4/4 | 4/4 |
| `actions_vague_prompt` ([`actions.py`](../config/rails/actions.py)) | `Qwen/Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct` | **0/20 (0.0%)** | **19/20 (95.0%)** | 3/4 | 4/4 | 4/4 | 4/4 | 4/4 |
| `structured_rubric_prompt` | `Qwen/Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct` | **0/20 (0.0%)** | **9/20 (45.0%)** | **0/4** | 3/4 | 2/4 | **0/4** | 4/4 |
| `structured_rubric_prompt` | `Qwen/Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct` | **0/20 (0.0%)** | **19/20 (95.0%)** | 3/4 | 4/4 | 4/4 | 4/4 | 4/4 |

- **Capacity vs. prompt:** `Qwen/Qwen2.5-1.5B-Instruct` cannot serve as the semantic output judge under any prompt. On the shipped `manager_system_prompt` it refuses 35% of benign advisory answers (`UNSAFE: The response contains financial advice...`) and misses 40% of unsafe outputs; adding an explicit rubric eliminates false refusals (`0/20`) by collapsing catch rate to `9/20 (45%)` (`0/4` on fabricated citations and `0/4` on leaked credentials). `Qwen/Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct` on the unchanged `manager_system_prompt` achieves `0/20 (0.0%)` false refusals and `20/20 (100.0%)` catch across all five categories.

**Remediation & Verification (2026-10-28, `refactor/nemo-opt-in`):**
1. **Direct `rails.llm.ainvoke()` invocation & strict verdict parsing:** [`validate_output_semantics()`](../src/integrations/nemo/manager.py) rejects empty or whitespace-only `output_text` before any LLM call, verifies `rails.llm` is configured (`CONTENT_SAFETY_RAILS_UNAVAILABLE` otherwise), and invokes `await llm.ainvoke([SystemMessage(content=_SEMANTIC_SAFETY_SYSTEM_PROMPT), HumanMessage(content=f"Assistant output to evaluate:\n---\n{output_text}\n---")])` directly (never `rails.generate_async()`). It strips closed `<think>...</think>` blocks, rejects unclosed `<think>` / stray `</think>` or empty post-strip output, accepts only exact ASCII `"SAFE"` (`(True, "")`), extracts the reason on `"UNSAFE"` prefix (`(False, reason)`), and fails closed with `CONTENT_SAFETY_ERROR` on all other replies (`"YES"`, `"NO"`, `"I'm sorry, I can't respond to that."`, `"**SAFETY**"`, Cyrillic `"SAF\u0415"`).
2. **Opt-in NeMo semantic rails (`CAGE_NEMO_ENABLED=false` by default) & startup posture check:** [`nemo_node_factory.py`](../src/gateway/governance/langgraph_harness/nemo_node_factory.py) sets the `CAGE_NEMO_ENABLED` default to `"false"`, [`mcp_tool_server.py`](../src/gateway/server/mcp_tool_server.py) and [`hybrid_server.py`](../src/gateway/server/hybrid_server.py) gate `initialize_rails()` behind `if nemo_rails_enabled():`, [`posture.py`](../src/gateway/governance/governor/posture.py) (`nemo_rails_backend`) refuses startup when `CAGE_NEMO_ENABLED=true` and `CAGE_INFERENCE_BACKEND=none`, and [`src/integrations/nemo/README.md`](../src/integrations/nemo/README.md) documents the `>= 7B` instruction-tuned judge model requirement.
3. **Mutation verification (`POAM-2026-120` forward closure requirement, executed 2026-10-28):**

| Mutation ID | File & Reverted Condition | Failing Test(s) in [`tests/test_validate_output_semantics.py`](../tests/test_validate_output_semantics.py) | Observed Exit Code |
|---|---|---|---|
| `OUT-M1` | [`manager.py`](../src/integrations/nemo/manager.py): call `rails.generate_async()` instead of `rails.llm.ainvoke()` in `validate_output_semantics()` | `test_validate_output_semantics_accepts_exact_safe_and_strips_think` & `test_validate_output_semantics_blocks_unsafe_with_reason` (9 cases) | `1` (verified) |
| `OUT-M2` | [`manager.py`](../src/integrations/nemo/manager.py): return `(True, "")` when `verdict` is empty after `<think>` strip | `test_validate_output_semantics_rejects_non_conforming_replies[]`, `[   \n  ]`, `[<think>reasoning</think>   ]` | `1` (verified) |
| `OUT-M3` | [`manager.py`](../src/integrations/nemo/manager.py): return `(True, "")` on non-`UNSAFE` non-conforming reply (`not verdict.startswith("UNSAFE")`) | `test_validate_output_semantics_rejects_non_conforming_replies` (9 cases including canned refusal, `YES`, `NO`, `**SAFETY**`, `SAF\u0415`) | `1` (verified) |
| `OUT-M4` | [`manager.py`](../src/integrations/nemo/manager.py): skip `<think>...</think>` regex strip | `test_validate_output_semantics_accepts_exact_safe_and_strips_think[<think>...]` & `test_validate_output_semantics_blocks_unsafe_with_reason[<think>...]` | `1` (verified) |
| `OUT-M5` | [`manager.py`](../src/integrations/nemo/manager.py): skip empty `output_text` check before LLM call | `test_validate_output_semantics_rejects_empty_output_text_without_llm_call` (3 cases) | `1` (verified) |
| `OUT-M6` | [`nemo_node_factory.py`](../src/gateway/governance/langgraph_harness/nemo_node_factory.py): revert `CAGE_NEMO_ENABLED` default to `"true"` | `test_nemo_rails_enabled_defaults_to_false` | `1` (verified) |

### POAM-2026-140: `CustomSelfCheckOutputAction` Is a Published Security Claim Protecting Nothing

**Control:** NIST SI-15 (Information Output Filtering), SA-11 (Developer Testing and Evaluation), CA-2 (Control Assessments)
**Risk Level:** High
**Status:** Closed (2026-10-28)
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-28
**Target Closure:** 2026-11-30

**Description:**
[`config/rails/actions.py`](../config/rails/actions.py) defined `@action(name="CustomSelfCheckOutputAction")` (registered in [`action_registry.py`](../src/integrations/nemo/action_registry.py) and declared in [`config/rails/definitions.co`](../config/rails/definitions.co)). On 2026-10-03, `CHANGELOG.md` recorded a `[SECURITY]` fix to its Stage 3 LLM judge, and [`tests/test_nemo_self_check_judge.py`](../tests/test_nemo_self_check_judge.py) certified the fix. Three facts verified on 2026-10-28 showed the claim protected nothing:

1. **Uninvoked in Colang.** No flow in [`config/rails/main_logic.co`](../config/rails/main_logic.co) or [`config/rails/config.yml`](../config/rails/config.yml) ever called `CustomSelfCheckOutputAction`. A comment in [`nemo_node_factory.py`](../src/gateway/governance/langgraph_harness/nemo_node_factory.py) noted that authoring a combined Colang output flow calling `CustomSelfCheckOutputAction` was left to a future sprint.
2. **Stage 2 short-circuited on the domain's core vocabulary.** Even if a flow had invoked the action, Stage 2 (`safe_indicators`, [`config/rails/actions.py`](../config/rails/actions.py)) returned `True` without calling the Stage 3 judge whenever the output contained any of 22 common financial words — `"risk"`, `"portfolio"`, `"consider"`, `"consult"`, `"disclaimer"`, `"not financial advice"`, `"diversification"`, `"volatility"`, `"may"`, `"could"`, `"potential"`, `"assessment"`, `"order"`, `"shares"`, `"executed"`, `"cancelled"`, `"filled"`, `"placed"`, `"confirmed"`, `"trade"`, `"position"`, `"market order"`, `"limit order"`, `"stop order"` — provided it avoided nine literal strings in Stage 1 (`"guaranteed returns"`, `"risk-free investment"`, `"can't lose"`, `"insider information"`, `"sure thing"`, `"100% profit"`, `"no risk"`, `"guaranteed profit"`, `"get rich quick"`). Because those 22 words are the vocabulary of nearly every financial advisory answer, Stage 2 would short-circuit to safe on essentially all real finance output and Stage 3 would never run.
3. **Self-certifying unit test.** [`tests/test_nemo_self_check_judge.py`](../tests/test_nemo_self_check_judge.py) called the output action directly with `"hello world"`, bypassing both Colang routing (which never called the action) and Stage 2 (which passed any output containing financial vocabulary).

**Remediation & Verification (2026-10-28, `refactor/nemo-opt-in`):**
1. Removed `CustomSelfCheckOutputAction` and its helper from [`config/rails/actions.py`](../config/rails/actions.py), removed its registration from [`action_registry.py`](../src/integrations/nemo/action_registry.py) and its declaration from [`config/rails/definitions.co`](../config/rails/definitions.co), and regenerated `de5534ac:deployment/k8s/nemo-rails-configmap.yaml` via `make update-nemo-configmap`.
2. Updated [`tests/test_nemo_self_check_judge.py`](../tests/test_nemo_self_check_judge.py) and [`tests/test_nemo_action_registry.py`](../tests/test_nemo_action_registry.py) to assert `CustomSelfCheckOutputAction` is absent and to test only the live input action `custom_self_check_input`; semantic output validation is covered end-to-end by [`tests/test_validate_output_semantics.py`](../tests/test_validate_output_semantics.py) (`POAM-2026-125`).
3. Annotated the `2026-10-03` entry in `CHANGELOG.md` to cross-reference `POAM-2026-140`.

### POAM-2026-141: STPA Compiler Silently Drops Declared Enforcement Targets (`flow stpa_output_guardrail` Is a No-Op `return`) and OSCAL Claims Uncompiled Controls as Implemented

**Control:** NIST SI-10 (Information Input Validation), SC-28 (Protection of Information at Rest / PII Disclosure), SI-4 (System Monitoring), SA-11 (Developer Testing and Evaluation), CA-2 (Control Assessments)
**Risk Level:** High
**Status:** Closed (2026-10-28) — remediated on `fix/stpa-nemo-unbundling` and verified by `STPA-M1`–`STPA-M4` mutations
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-28
**Target Closure:** 2026-11-30

**Description:**
Five STPA Unsafe Control Actions declared enforcement targets (`nemo`, `opa`, or `python`) whose `condition` did not emit an active check in those targets:

| UCA | Source YAML | Hazard | Pre-Fix `enforcement` | Pre-Fix `condition` | Post-Fix `enforcement` | Post-Fix `condition` | Compiled into [`uca_rules.py`](../src/cage_finance/stpa/uca_rules.py) / `c58b0913^:config/opa/generated_stpa_policy.rego`? |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| `UCA-3` | [`trade_hazards.yaml`](../src/cage_finance/config/stpa/trade_hazards.yaml) | `H-3` (PII disclosure; ISO 42001 A.9.2, NIST SC-28) | `[nemo]` | `semantic_pattern: "contains_ssn_or_account_number"` | `[python]` | `{param: output_contains_pii, operator: is_true}` | Yes (`_check_uca_3` in [`uca_rules.py`](../src/cage_finance/stpa/uca_rules.py)) |
| `UCA-4` | [`trade_hazards.yaml`](../src/cage_finance/config/stpa/trade_hazards.yaml) | `H-2` (Atomic failure) | `[langgraph, nemo]` | `semantic_pattern: "partial_transaction"` | `[langgraph, python]` | `{param: partial_transaction_detected, operator: is_true}` | Yes (`_check_uca_4` in [`uca_rules.py`](../src/cage_finance/stpa/uca_rules.py) + [`saga_nodes.py`](../src/cage_finance/stpa/saga_nodes.py)) |
| `UCA-7` | [`core_system.yaml`](../config/stpa/core_system.yaml) & `385e502e:config/stpa_control_structure.yaml` | `H-1` / `H-3` (`override_policy`) | `[opa, nemo]` | `{param: is_valid_admin, operator: is_false}` (no `semantic_pattern`) | `[opa]` | `{param: is_valid_admin, operator: is_false}` | Yes (`opa` in `c58b0913^:config/opa/generated_stpa_policy.rego`) |
| `UCA-10` | [`trade_hazards.yaml`](../src/cage_finance/config/stpa/trade_hazards.yaml) | `H-2` (Stale balance data; ISO 42001 A.9.4, NIST SI-4) | `[nemo]` | `semantic_pattern: "stale_balance_data"` | `[python]` | `{param: stale_balance_data_detected, operator: is_true}` | Yes (`_check_uca_10` in [`uca_rules.py`](../src/cage_finance/stpa/uca_rules.py)) |
| `UCA-12` | [`trade_hazards.yaml`](../src/cage_finance/config/stpa/trade_hazards.yaml) | `H-1`, `H-4` (Bounding contract B1–B10 failure) | `[python]` | `semantic_pattern: "bounding_contract_violation"` (compiled to `return None`) | `[python, opa]` | `{param: bounding_contract_violation_detected, operator: is_true}` | Yes (`_check_uca_12` in [`uca_rules.py`](../src/cage_finance/stpa/uca_rules.py)) |

**Remediation & Verification (2026-10-28):**
1. **Fail-closed STPA compiler validation:** Added `ConditionModel.is_structured()` and updated `UCAModel._validate_enforcement_deps()` in [`stpa_compiler.py`](../src/gateway/governance/stpa_compiler.py) to raise `ValueError` whenever `opa` or `python` is declared without a structured condition (`composite`, or `param` + `operator` with `threshold` / `threshold_ref` where required) or `nemo` is declared without `condition.semantic_pattern`.
2. **Structured conditions & regenerated STPA/OSCAL artifacts:** Updated [`core_system.yaml`](../config/stpa/core_system.yaml), `385e502e:config/stpa_control_structure.yaml`, [`trade_hazards.yaml`](../src/cage_finance/config/stpa/trade_hazards.yaml), and [`NIST_SP800_53.json`](../config/oscal/framework_mappings/NIST_SP800_53.json), and regenerated all 8 STPA artifacts (`check_stpa_freshness.py` passes) and all 3 OSCAL artifacts (`system-security-plan.yaml`, `component-definition.yaml`, `stpa_compiler_ssp_patch.yaml`).
3. **Mutation verification (`STPA-M1`–`STPA-M4`, verified 2026-10-28 on `dev-thin-client`):**

| ID | File & Line Mutated | Mutation | Failing Test (`rc=1`) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `STPA-M1` | [`stpa_compiler.py`](../src/gateway/governance/stpa_compiler.py) (`UCAModel._validate_enforcement_deps`) | Disable `if not self.condition.is_structured():` check for `opa`/`python` | `tests/test_stpa_compiler.py::TestSchemaValidation::test_python_or_opa_enforcement_without_structured_condition_rejected` |

### POAM-2026-142: Deterministic PII Redaction (`UCA-3` / `H-3`) and Keyword Screening Are Coupled Behind `get_nemo_rails()` in `nemo_node_factory.py`

**Control:** NIST SC-28 (Protection of Information at Rest / PII Disclosure), SI-10 (Information Input Validation)
**Risk Level:** High
**Status:** Closed (2026-10-28) — remediated on `fix/stpa-nemo-unbundling` and verified by `M1`–`M7` mutations
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-28
**Target Closure:** 2026-11-30

**Description:**
While [`generated_stpa_rails.co`](../config/rails/generated_stpa_rails.co) emitted no check for `UCA-3` (`H-3`, PII disclosure; POAM-2026-141), CAGE enforced deterministic PII protection on advisor input and output via:
- [`_redact_pii()`](../src/integrations/presidio/redactor.py) (Microsoft Presidio + spaCy NER with regex fallback, fail-closed on exception), called on user input and agent output inside [`src/gateway/governance/langgraph_harness/nemo_node_factory.py`](../src/gateway/governance/langgraph_harness/nemo_node_factory.py); and
- [`scrub_pii()`](../src/gateway/infrastructure/privacy.py) (deterministic regex scrubbing for SSN, credit-card, email, and phone patterns), called via `MaskPIIAction` ([`config/rails/actions.py`](../config/rails/actions.py)).

Previously, inside [`nemo_node_factory.py`](../src/gateway/governance/langgraph_harness/nemo_node_factory.py), both deterministic controls — as well as Stage 1 Aho-Corasick keyword screening (`ac_keyword_scan()`) — were gated behind `rails = get_nemo_rails()`.

**Remediation & Verification (2026-10-28):**
1. **Unbundled deterministic PII redaction and keyword screening from `get_nemo_rails()`:** Added `nemo_rails_enabled()` (`CAGE_NEMO_ENABLED`, raising `ConfigurationError` on invalid values) in [`nemo_node_factory.py`](../src/gateway/governance/langgraph_harness/nemo_node_factory.py). `create_nemo_guardrail_node()` now runs `_redact_pii()` and Stage 1 `ac_keyword_scan()` unconditionally before consulting `get_nemo_rails()`, and `create_nemo_output_rail_node()` runs `_redact_pii()` and `scrub_pii()` unconditionally before consulting `get_nemo_rails()`.
2. **Mutation verification (`M1`–`M7`, verified 2026-10-28 on `dev-thin-client` against [`tests/test_nemo_pii_unbundling.py`](../tests/test_nemo_pii_unbundling.py)):**

| ID | Mutation in [`nemo_node_factory.py`](../src/gateway/governance/langgraph_harness/nemo_node_factory.py) | Failing Test(s) (`rc=1`) |
|---|---|---|
| `M1` | Skip input `_redact_pii()` when `not nemo_rails_enabled()` | `test_input_pii_redacted_when_nemo_disabled`, `test_input_pii_failure_fails_closed_in_enforcing_posture_when_nemo_disabled` |
| `M2` | Skip output `_redact_pii()` when `not nemo_rails_enabled()` | `test_output_presidio_and_regex_scrub_when_nemo_disabled`, `test_output_pii_failure_returns_sentinel_when_nemo_disabled` |
| `M3` | Skip output `scrub_pii()` when `not nemo_rails_enabled()` | `test_output_presidio_and_regex_scrub_when_nemo_disabled` |
| `M4` | Skip input `ac_keyword_scan()` when `not nemo_rails_enabled()` | `test_input_ac_keyword_scan_blocks_without_nemo` |
| `M5` | Skip `_replace_last_message(messages, redacted)` on input when `not nemo_rails_enabled()` | `test_input_pii_redacted_when_nemo_disabled` |
| `M6` | Return raw `output_text` instead of `cfg.output_blocked_sentinel` on output PII exception | `test_output_pii_failure_returns_sentinel_when_nemo_disabled` |
| `M7` | Return `False` instead of raising `ConfigurationError` on invalid `CAGE_NEMO_ENABLED` | `test_nemo_rails_enabled_rejects_invalid_value` |

### POAM-2026-144: `CAGE_NEMO_ENABLED=false` Held Only at Startup — Runtime Paths Rebuilt NeMo Rails

**Control:** NIST CM-6 (Configuration Settings), CM-7 (Least Functionality), SI-10 (Information Input Validation)
**Risk Level:** Moderate
**Status:** Closed (2026-10-29) — remediated on `fix/nemo-flag-binding` and verified by `FB-M1`–`FB-M11` mutations
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-29
**Target Closure:** 2026-10-29

**Description:**
AG-5 (#576) made the NeMo rails opt-in, but `CAGE_NEMO_ENABLED=false` was enforced only where gateway startup chose whether to call `initialize_rails()`. Two runtime paths built rails anyway:
- `POST /inference/v1/chat/completions` ([`inference_proxy.py`](../src/gateway/server/inference_proxy.py)) called `initialize_semantic_rails()` whenever `app.state.nemo_rails` was `None`, which is the state a rails-off startup leaves. It never stored the result, so it built a fresh `LLMRails` on every request.
- The `cage:nemo:reload` Pub/Sub subscriber and `POST /v1/nemo/approve-refinement/{id}` ([`hybrid_server.py`](../src/gateway/server/hybrid_server.py)) both called `_reload_app_nemo_rails()`. The subscriber was started whenever Redis was reachable. The call rebuilt the rails, installed them on every sub-app, and flipped `/health` to `"nemo":"active"`.

Neither path consulted the `nemo_rails_backend` startup check, so an event the system generates itself could route around a startup guard. Both paths failed closed: with no backend, the rebuild refused. That makes this a configuration-integrity and availability defect, not a safety bypass.

The same review found two related defects:
- The three opt-in switches had three parsers. `CAGE_NEMO_ENABLED=yes` raised `ConfigurationError`, but `CAGE_CONSENSUS_CRITICS_ENABLED=yes` and `CAGE_CAUSAL_TIER_ENABLED=yes` silently enabled their components.
- `verify_content_safety` reported `CONTENT_SAFETY_RAILS_UNAVAILABLE` both for "off by configuration" and for "enabled but not initialised".

**Remediation & Verification (2026-10-29):**
1. **One predicate on every path.** Every place that builds rails consults [`semantic_rails_refusal()`](../src/gateway/governance/semantic_rail_factory.py), which reads the same two settings as the startup check.
   - `initialize_semantic_rails()` and `create_semantic_rail_manager()` raise `SemanticRailsDisabled`.
   - [`get_nemo_rails()`](../src/gateway/governance/langgraph_harness/nemo_node_factory.py) checks on every call, even when an instance is cached. `reload_nemo_rails()` refuses and keeps the old instance.
   - `_reload_app_nemo_rails()` refuses before touching app state. The reload subscriber starts only through `_start_nemo_reload_subscriber()`, which returns `None` while the rails are off.
   - Refinement approval returns `409` with the reason code and leaves the proposal `staged`.
   - The inference proxy runs deterministic-only: the Stage 1 keyword scan, then `scrub_pii()` output masking, with ISO 42001 `A.6.1.2` stamped `SKIPPED`. Rails that are on but absent still reach `verify_input(None, …)` and block.
2. **One parser.** [`opt_in_enabled()`](../src/gateway/governance/feature_flags.py) reads all three switches: `nemo_rails_enabled()`, `consensus_critics_enabled()` and `is_causal_tier_enabled()`. `true`/`1` turns a component on, and `false`/`0`/empty/unset leaves it off. Any other value raises.
3. **A distinct reason code.** [`content_safety.py`](../src/gateway/governance/content_safety.py) adds `CONTENT_SAFETY_RAILS_DISABLED`. `CONTENT_SAFETY_RAILS_UNAVAILABLE` now means only "enabled but not initialised".
4. **Mutation verification (`FB-M1`–`FB-M11`, verified 2026-10-29).** The tests are in [`tests/test_nemo_flag_binding.py`](../tests/test_nemo_flag_binding.py) and [`tests/test_inference_proxy_extended.py`](../tests/test_inference_proxy_extended.py). Each mutation was applied alone, the named tests failed (`rc=1`), and the unmutated baseline passed before and after.

| ID | Mutation | Failing Test(s) (`rc=1`) |
|---|---|---|
| `FB-M1` | `initialize_semantic_rails()` skips the refusal | `test_factory_builds_no_rails_when_off` |
| `FB-M2` | `create_semantic_rail_manager()` skips the refusal | `test_factory_builds_no_rails_when_off` |
| `FB-M3` | `get_nemo_rails()` returns a cached instance without the check | `test_cached_rails_are_not_handed_out_when_off` |
| `FB-M4` | `reload_nemo_rails()` skips the check | `test_singleton_reload_refuses_and_keeps_the_old_instance` |
| `FB-M5` | The inference proxy ignores the refusal and builds rails on demand | `test_rails_off_builds_no_rails_and_runs_no_input_rail`, `test_rails_off_masks_output_with_scrub_pii_and_no_model` |
| `FB-M6` | The proxy skips the input rail whenever `rails is None`, not only when off | `test_rails_on_but_absent_still_reach_the_input_rail` |
| `FB-M7` | The reload subscriber starts regardless of the refusal | `test_reload_subscriber_is_not_started_when_off` |
| `FB-M8` | A reload broadcast rebuilds rails (app-level and singleton gates removed) | `test_reload_broadcast_does_not_switch_rails_back_on` |
| `FB-M9` | Approval applies a refinement without the `409` refusal | `test_approving_a_refinement_while_off_is_refused_and_left_staged` |
| `FB-M10` | `verify_content_safety` uses rails left on `app.state` while off | `test_content_safety_reports_disabled_even_with_stale_rails_on_app_state` |
| `FB-M11` | The shared parser accepts `yes`/`no` (the old critics/causal vocabulary) | `test_every_switch_rejects_an_ambiguous_value` |

### POAM-2026-145: The Compliance Artifacts Credited the Optional NeMo Tier With Controls the Deterministic Path Provides

**Control:** NIST CA-2 (Control Assessments), SA-11 (Developer Testing and Evaluation); ISO 42001 A.9.2
**Risk Level:** Moderate
**Status:** Closed (2026-10-29) — remediated on `fix/oscal-nemo-claims` and verified by `OC-M1`–`OC-M13` mutations
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-29
**Target Closure:** 2026-10-29

**Description:**
AG-5 (#576) made the NeMo Colang rails opt-in, and POAM-2026-142 had already taken PII redaction and keyword screening out from behind them. The compliance artifacts still described NeMo as the enforcement, and an assessor would find the discrepancy in either direction:
- **The SSP understated the system.** [`system-security-plan.yaml`](../compliance/oscal/system-security-plan.yaml) listed the NeMo Guardrails Server as `operational` for ISO 42001 A.9.2 and NIST PT-2/PT-3, with "Microsoft Presidio (16 entity types)". The PII control that runs is in-process Presidio (12 identity entity types) and regex `scrub_pii`, with no model or service behind it, and the SSP gave it no credit.
- **The STPA narratives overstated it.** The SSP and [`stpa_compiler_ssp_patch.yaml`](../compliance/oscal/stpa_compiler_ssp_patch.yaml) cited [`generated_stpa_rails.co`](../config/rails/generated_stpa_rails.co) as enforcing nine SP 800-53 controls, eleven mentions in all, but the file defines no rails because no UCA targets `nemo`. The narrative template listed every generator for every control, so it also cited OPA for controls whose UCAs target only Python.
- **The SI-10 statement contradicted POAM-2026-141.** The FTRA SI-10 statements in [`component-definition.yaml`](../compliance/oscal/component-definition.yaml) and [`sp800-53-component-definition.yaml`](../compliance/oscal/sp800-53-component-definition.yaml) credited "NeMo/AGP rails generated from UCA-7", although POAM-2026-141 closed with UCA-7 at `enforcement: [opa]`. The AGP policy takes effect only where an operator uploads it.
- **The same claim recurred elsewhere.** The component definition had a "NeMo Guardrails + Presidio PII Masking" component for A.9.2. The SP 800-53 component definition had a "NeMo Guardrails" component claiming SI-10 and SI-3 through a NeMo manager cited at a kernel path that does not exist. The AARM report card ([`aarm_mapper.py`](../src/compliance_bridge/aarm_mapper.py)) credited NeMo for AARM-V2, V5 and V10, and [`FTRA_SCOPE.md`](governance/FTRA_SCOPE.md) named NeMo rails as the owner of injection screening.

**Remediation & Verification (2026-10-29):**
1. **A component for the control that runs, with its open defects on it.** The SSP adds an operational Deterministic PII Redaction component (`e5000008-0000-4000-8000-000000000008`) that carries A.9.2 and PT-2/PT-3. Moving a control onto a component whose fail-open this change also documents must not trade a visible misstatement for a subtler one, so the component carries POAM-2026-147 wherever its statements claim fail-closed behaviour: in its description, purpose, status remarks and a `poam-gap` prop. A `poam-gap-evidence` prop carries POAM-2026-146, and the component definition's A.9.2 statement carries POAM-2026-147 as well. `test_pii_component_discloses_its_open_poams` requires both caveats for as long as either POAM is open, so closing the POAM is what releases them. The NeMo Guardrails Server keeps its inventory entry, with state `other` as an optional tier. The system description, data flow, authorization boundary, PII information type and gateway component now describe the deterministic screening, with NeMo as optional.
2. **Artifact lists follow the declared targets.** [`oscal_ssp_exporter.py`](../src/gateway/governance/oscal_ssp_exporter.py) builds the narratives' artifact lists, the SSP patch's artifact lines and props, and the compiler component's description from `_enforcement_targets()`, which expands `all` as `stpa_compiler.py` does. [`NIST_SP800_53.json`](../config/oscal/framework_mappings/NIST_SP800_53.json) maps each target to its artifact text, so the kernel holds no domain path (Gate G3). The three exporter-owned artifacts were regenerated with `uv run python -m src.gateway.governance.oscal_ssp_exporter export`.
3. **Statements corrected by hand.** The FTRA SI-10 statement (exporter seed and both component definitions) now says the Stage 1 keyword scan screens every input and UCA-7 compiles only to OPA. The A.9.2 component, AARM-V2 and the SP 800-53 SI-10/SI-3 component describe the deterministic code, and SI-3 is `partial` because nothing deterministic screens model output for harmful content. [`aarm_mapper.py`](../src/compliance_bridge/aarm_mapper.py), [`FTRA_SCOPE.md`](governance/FTRA_SCOPE.md) and [`COMPLIANCE.md`](../COMPLIANCE.md) say the same. POAM-2026-148 records the one NeMo claim left in place, on a component that does not exist.
4. **Mutation verification (`OC-M1`–`OC-M13`, verified 2026-10-29).** The tests are in [`tests/test_oscal_nemo_claims.py`](../tests/test_oscal_nemo_claims.py), which reads the committed artifacts, and [`tests/test_oscal_ssp_exporter.py`](../tests/test_oscal_ssp_exporter.py). Each mutation was applied alone, the named tests failed (`rc=1`), and the unmutated baseline passed before and after.

| ID | Mutation | Failing Test(s) (`rc=1`) |
|---|---|---|
| `OC-M1` | `artifacts_for()` cites every mapped generator, targeted or not | `test_python_target_cites_neither_opa_nor_nemo`, `test_untargeted_nemo_artifact_is_not_cited` |
| `OC-M2` | The SSP patch lists the NeMo artifact line and prop with no UCA targeting `nemo` | `test_untargeted_nemo_artifact_is_not_cited` |
| `OC-M3` | The compiler component names every artifact kind | `test_names_only_targeted_artifact_kinds` |
| `OC-M4` | `_enforcement_targets()` stops expanding `all` | `test_all_target_cites_every_generator` |
| `OC-M5` | The NIST narrative template names the rails file statically | `test_untargeted_nemo_artifact_is_not_cited` |
| `OC-M6` | The FTRA SI-10 seed credits NeMo rails generated from UCA-7 again | `test_ftra_si10_says_uca7_compiles_only_to_opa[exporter-seed]` |
| `OC-M7` | The SSP lists the NeMo Guardrails Server as `operational` again | `test_ssp_credits_pii_controls_to_in_process_redaction_not_nemo` |
| `OC-M8` | `component-definition.yaml` credits A.9.2 to a NeMo component again | `test_deterministic_controls_are_not_credited_to_nemo[component-definition.yaml]` |
| `OC-M9` | `sp800-53-component-definition.yaml` credits SI-10/SI-3 to NeMo again | `test_deterministic_controls_are_not_credited_to_nemo[sp800-53-component-definition.yaml]` |
| `OC-M10` | AARM-V2 cites the optional NeMo manager again | `test_aarm_vectors_cite_default_path_code[AARM-V2]` |
| `OC-M11` | The committed SSP patch names the rails file (a stale regeneration) | `test_rails_file_is_cited_only_while_a_uca_targets_nemo[stpa_compiler_ssp_patch.yaml]` |
| `OC-M12` | The SSP's A.9.2 component drops its POAM-2026-147 `poam-gap` prop | `test_pii_component_discloses_its_open_poams[POAM-2026-147]` |
| `OC-M13` | The component definition's fail-closed A.9.2 statement loses its POAM-2026-147 caveat | `test_pii_component_discloses_its_open_poams[POAM-2026-147]` |

### POAM-2026-136: Declared vLLM URL Variables That Nothing Read

**Control:** NIST CM-6 (Configuration Settings)
**Risk Level:** Low
**Status:** Closed (2026-10-29) — remediated on `refactor/inference-backend-gke` (W6) and verified by `VU-M1`–`VU-M5` mutations
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-28
**Target Closure:** 2026-12-31

**Description:**
`vllm_fast_url`, `vllm_reasoning_url` and `vllm_gateway_url` were declared in the gcp-gke and agnostic `variables.tf`, and `deploy_all.sh` and `infra/load_env.sh` filled them from `.env`. No module read them: every vLLM endpoint in the gcp-gke `main.tf` was a literal service name. An operator who set `VLLM_GATEWAY_URL` to route through the Inference Gateway changed nothing, and [`INFERENCE_GATEWAY_ARCHITECTURE.md`](architecture/INFERENCE_GATEWAY_ARCHITECTURE.md) documented that path as working ([`JURISDICTION_INVENTORY.md`](operations/JURISDICTION_INVENTORY.md) §8).

**Remediation:**
W6 deletes the three declarations in both targets and their exports in both scripts. In the gcp-gke target the vLLM endpoints are now locals in `main.tf` (49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf), empty unless `inference_backend = "vllm"`, and no other line in the file names a vLLM service. `ENV_INTEGRATION.md` drops the three mappings, and the Inference Gateway guide says how to set `vllm_gateway_url` on GKE.

**Mutation verification (`POAM-2026-120` closure requirement, executed 2026-10-29).** Each mutation was applied alone to the W6 tree; the named test in [`test_inference_backend_gke.py`](../tests/infrastructure/test_inference_backend_gke.py) failed (`rc=1`), and the file passed before and after the run.

| ID | Mutation | Failing Test (`rc=1`) |
|---|---|---|
| VU-M1 | Re-declare `variable "vllm_fast_url"` in the gcp-gke `variables.tf` | `test_unread_vllm_url_variables_are_removed` |
| VU-M2 | Re-declare `variable "vllm_gateway_url"` in the agnostic `variables.tf` | `test_unread_vllm_url_variables_are_removed` |
| VU-M3 | Restore a `TF_VAR_vllm_fast_url` export in `deploy_all.sh` | `test_unread_vllm_url_variables_are_removed` |
| VU-M4 | Restore the `VLLM_REASONING_API_BASE` → `TF_VAR_vllm_reasoning_url` mapping in `infra/load_env.sh` | `test_unread_vllm_url_variables_are_removed` |
| VU-M5 | Point the gateway module's `vllm_base_url` at a literal `vllm-service` URL instead of the backend local | `test_no_workload_is_pointed_at_vllm_outside_the_backend_locals` |

### POAM-2026-131: No Profile Was Deployable From the Repository (Placeholder Digests and Projects)

**Control:** NIST CM-7 (Least Functionality), SI-7 (Software, Firmware, and Information Integrity), CM-2 (Baseline Configuration)
**Risk Level:** High (re-rated from Low, 2026-10-30)
**Status:** Closed (2026-10-31) — remediated and merged on `main` in #594 (`7edbf230`, branch head `e28124ba`), #605 (`b66c7344`, branch head `53137452`), and #606 (`d599fc04`, branch head `171acc87`), and verified by live `cage-staging` apply at `3afda5b7` on `2026-10-31`
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-28
**Target Closure:** 2026-11-15
**Date Closed:** 2026-10-31

**Description:**
Opened on 2026-10-28 for the prod-profile instance: every profile except staging inherited the default `image_digests` in [`variables.tf`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/variables.tf), whose first-party entries were `sha256:e3b0c442…`, the SHA-256 of empty input, which no image has. Read again at `34709d55` on 2026-10-30, the defect was wider, and it was categorical:
1. **The maintained GKE profile was not deployable either.** [`staging.tfvars`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/staging.tfvars) pinned all 15 images to `gcr.io/cage-reference/…`, a registry that refuses every pull (`DENIED`, "or it may not exist", verified 2026-10-30 with `gcloud container images describe`). The live staging cluster ran only because its applies predate that rewrite.
2. **The real digests were never consumed.** [`render_image_digests.sh`](../scripts/render_image_digests.sh) printed an HCL block to stdout, [`build_images.sh`](../scripts/build_images.sh) echoed it, and nothing read it. Terraform read only the committed values.
3. **The order could not have worked.** [`deploy_all.sh`](../deploy_all.sh) ran `terraform plan -out=tfplan`, then built the images, then applied the saved plan, so digests rendered by the build could never reach it.
4. **The renderer's fallbacks broke provenance.** For the advisor and vLLM images, it fell back to `gcloud container images list-tags --limit=1`: the image pushed last, not the one built for the SHA being deployed. In a new project that found nothing, and the deploy aborted.
5. **A conflict held it in place.** A working digest pin needs a real registry path, and the project-ID hygiene gate forbids committing the only registry that held the images.
6. **The same root cause in `project_id`.** Six profiles committed a placeholder project (`YOUR_GCP_PROJECT_ID`, `your-production-project`, …). A `-var-file` value outranks `TF_VAR_project_id`, so the placeholder defeated `deploy_all.sh`'s mapping of `GOOGLE_CLOUD_PROJECT` (DEP-22) and pointed Terraform at a project that doesn't exist.

**Why the original Low rating was wrong:** the prod-profile instance was assessed without noticing that the staging instance blocks deployment entirely. A reference architecture whose maintained profile cannot be deployed from the repository makes a false deployability claim; it is not a configuration nit.

**Decision (2026-10-30):** remove the placeholders rather than guard them. A guard that recognises an unsubstituted placeholder needs a sentinel kept in step with the placeholder, and it can be bypassed or get the check wrong. A required variable with no value fails closed on Terraform's own error. With nothing registry-shaped committed, the hygiene conflict stops existing rather than being routed around. The accepted cost: `terraform test` plans with a test-only fixture map.

**Remediation (implemented and merged on `main` in #594, `7edbf230`, branch head `e28124ba`; #605, `b66c7344`, branch head `53137452`; and #606, `d599fc04`, branch head `171acc87`, `2026-10-31`):**
1. `var.image_digests` and `var.project_id` have no default, and no profile commits either. The render command sits in the variable's description, which Terraform shows only in its interactive prompt. It also sits in a trailing comment on the declaration line, the line Terraform quotes in its non-interactive `No value for required variable` error (both verified on Terraform 1.15.8).
2. `var.image_digests`' validation requires exactly the images the profile deploys (`local.required_image_digests` in `main.tf` (49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf), gated like the module counts), in every Binary Authorization mode, and refuses the empty-input digest. Staging under `inference_backend = "none"` needs no vLLM, advisor or NeMo image. Both advisor runtimes consume the advisor image, contrary to an old comment in `build_images.sh`.
3. `render_image_digests.sh` writes a gitignored `image_digests.<short_sha>.tfvars.json`. There is no `list-tags` fallback: an image not built for that SHA is left out and named, never substituted. It fails if no first-party image carries the SHA.
4. `deploy_all.sh` builds the images the profile deploys, renders their digests, then plans. `--image-sha` deploys an earlier build without rebuilding, and the dead `TF_VAR_skip_build` and `TF_VAR_kms_governance_key` exports are gone.
5. The Terraform floor is now 1.9, because the validations reference locals and other variables. The existing `nemo_image` validation already needed 1.9.
6. [`bootstrap_gcp_project.sh`](../scripts/bootstrap_gcp_project.sh) (#605, `b66c7344`) automates clean-project API enablement, state-bucket creation, Cloud Build SA + KMS `binauthz-attestor` key + Binary Authorization attestor bootstrap, and `--import-state` reconciliation; [`staging_lifecycle.sh`](../scripts/staging_lifecycle.sh) (#606, `d599fc04`) passes the rendered `image_digests.<short_sha>.tfvars.json` var file during Phase 6 teardown.

**Verification (2026-10-30 & 2026-10-31):**
- `terraform test` against the staging profile plus the fixture: 44 of 44 runs pass. That includes 9 runs in [`image_digests.tftest.hcl`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/tests/image_digests.tftest.hcl): coverage per posture, the bridge and vLLM overrides, the empty-input digest and a tag reference.
- [`test_image_digests_rendered.py`](../tests/infrastructure/test_image_digests_rendered.py), [`test_gcp_project_bootstrap.py`](../tests/infrastructure/test_gcp_project_bootstrap.py), and [`test_staging_lifecycle.py`](../tests/infrastructure/test_staging_lifecycle.py) pass, including the renderer, bootstrap, and lifecycle runs against stub `gcloud`/`terraform`, and so does [`test_gke_sole_target_6f.py`](../tests/infrastructure/test_gke_sole_target_6f.py).
- **Live `cage-staging` build, render, and apply (`2026-10-31`, `3afda5b7`):** Cloud Build built and attested the three first-party images deployed by `staging.tfvars` (`gateway` → `sha256:3156d3f32e32968aaf2eb987ae04cb377f40440f1bb851d976843fefc6cf80eb`, `agentsight-ui` → `sha256:c140a17b73158bccb4976a3934cab8115f34738a3731e6647a7cf61ea9ce8b73`, `compliance-bridge` → `sha256:54a506138887ba60fb56ce0869ab1d5028e0e488783ea522f685316e9f7a06f4`), `scripts/render_image_digests.sh 3afda5b7` rendered `11` digests into `image_digests.3afda5b7.tfvars.json` (omitting `governed-financial-advisor`, `nemo-guardrails`, and `vllm-streamer`), and `./deploy_all.sh --target gcp-gke --env staging --skip-build --auto-approve` succeeded (`Apply complete! Resources: 7 added, 15 changed, 4 destroyed`) with all `governance-stack` workloads rolling out cleanly and post-apply `terraform plan` showing zero workload diffs.
- Mutations (each made the named check fail, `rc=1`):

| ID | Mutation | Failing check |
|----|----------|---------------|
| DG-M1 | Re-add `default = {}` to `var.image_digests` | `test_variable_has_no_default` |
| DG-M2 | Commit `project_id = "YOUR_GCP_PROJECT_ID"` in `staging.tfvars` | `test_no_profile_commits_digests_or_a_project` |
| DG-M3 | Commit an `image_digests` map in `staging.tfvars` | `test_no_profile_commits_digests_or_a_project` |
| DG-M4 | Remove the render step that precedes the plan in `deploy_all.sh` | `test_deploy_all_builds_then_renders_then_plans` |
| DG-M5 | Re-add a `list-tags` fallback to the renderer | `test_renderer_never_falls_back_to_another_builds_image` |
| DG-M6 | Drop `vllm-streamer` from the renderer's catalogue | `test_renderer_can_render_every_image_terraform_can_require` |
| DG-M7 | Replace the coverage condition with one that always holds (`length(var.image_digests) >= 0`) | `image_digests.tftest.hcl` (`a_missing_deployed_image_is_refused`) |
| DG-M8 | Remove the empty-input digest refusal | `image_digests.tftest.hcl` (`the_empty_input_digest_is_refused`) |
| DG-M9 | Require the vLLM image whatever the backend | `image_digests.tftest.hcl` (`staging_none_needs_only_the_images_it_deploys`) |

**Closure Criteria:**
1. ✅ **Met (`2026-10-31`).** Live `cage-staging` apply at `3afda5b7` consumed `image_digests.3afda5b7.tfvars.json` rendered by `scripts/render_image_digests.sh 3afda5b7` and succeeded (`Apply complete! Resources: 7 added, 15 changed, 4 destroyed`).
2. ✅ **Met (`2026-10-31`).** Merge commit SHAs `7edbf230` (#594, branch head `e28124ba`), `b66c7344` (#605, branch head `53137452`), and `d599fc04` (#606, branch head `171acc87`) recorded here. *(Note: `POAM-2026-131` closed on the rendered-digest apply against the existing `cage-staging` project; the end-to-end clean-project walk from zero and empirical teardown verification of [`bootstrap_gcp_project.sh`](../scripts/bootstrap_gcp_project.sh) and [`staging_lifecycle.sh`](../scripts/staging_lifecycle.sh) are tracked separately in `POAM-2026-164`.)*

### POAM-2026-132: EU GPU Profiles Listed Non-L4 Zone `europe-west1-d` and Per-Zone Counts Multiplied GPU Allocation

**Control:** NIST CM-6 (Configuration Settings)
**Risk Level:** Moderate
**Status:** Closed (2026-10-29) — merged in `#587` as `43c1f3cf` and verified by `GPU-M1`–`GPU-M5` mutations
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-28
**Target Closure:** 2026-11-30

**Description:**
1. **`europe-west1-d` lacks `nvidia-l4` and `g2-standard-8` (`POAM-2026-132`).** Compute Engine `acceleratorTypes` and `machineTypes` in `europe-west1` offer `nvidia-l4` and `g2-standard-8` in `europe-west1-b` and `europe-west1-c`, and neither in `europe-west1-d` (verified via Compute API, 2026-10-28; [`JURISDICTION_INVENTORY.md`](operations/JURISDICTION_INVENTORY.md) §3, §8). Both [`eu-dev.tfvars`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/eu-dev.tfvars) and [`eu-prod.tfvars`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/eu-prod.tfvars) listed `["europe-west1-b", "europe-west1-c", "europe-west1-d"]` with `enable_gpu_node_pool = true`.
2. **Per-zone counts multiplied initial, minimum, and maximum GPU allocation across `gpu_node_locations` (Probe #12).** [`google_container_node_pool.gpu_nodes`](../infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf) used `initial_node_count = var.gpu_node_pool_initial_count` (default `1`) and `autoscaling { min_node_count, max_node_count }`, all of which GKE interprets **per zone** across `node_locations`. On a 3-zone pool, `dev.tfvars` (`initial = 1, min = 0, max = 2`) allocated `3` GPUs on creation and scaled up to `6`, while `prod.tfvars` (`initial = 2, min = 2, max = 5`) held `6` GPUs warm and scaled up to `15`. W6 worked around this on `staging.tfvars` alone by narrowing `gpu_node_locations` to `["us-central1-a"]`, trading away zonal capacity sourcing.

**Remediation:**
1. **Pool-wide total autoscaling and zero initial count.** [`google_container_node_pool.gpu_nodes`](../infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf) replaces `min_node_count` / `max_node_count` with `total_min_node_count = var.gpu_node_pool_min_count`, `total_max_node_count = var.gpu_node_pool_max_count`, and `location_policy = "ANY"`. `gpu_node_pool_initial_count` defaults to `0` in [`infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/variables.tf`](../infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/variables.tf) and [`infra/targets/gcp-gke/variables.tf`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/variables.tf) (`0` per zone = `0` total on creation).
2. **Verified L4 zones and zero warm floor across every profile.** All eight `.tfvars` profiles and `terraform.auto.tfvars.example` set `gpu_node_pool_initial_count = 0` and `gpu_node_pool_min_count = 0` (`max = 2` on dev/staging/example; `max = 5` on `prod`, `eu-prod`, `apac-prod`, none of which any workflow deploys) and list only verified L4 + `g2-standard-8` zones: `["us-central1-a", "us-central1-b", "us-central1-c"]` (restoring 3-zone capacity sourcing on [`staging.tfvars`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/staging.tfvars)), `["europe-west1-b", "europe-west1-c"]` on `eu-dev` and `eu-prod`, and `["asia-southeast1-a", "asia-southeast1-b", "asia-southeast1-c"]` on `apac-dev` and `apac-prod`.
3. **Plan-time preconditions and mock-provider `terraform test` coverage.** `google_container_node_pool.gpu_nodes` enforces two `lifecycle.precondition` blocks: `gpu_node_locations` must be non-empty and within `var.region`, and any L4 / `g2-*` pool in `us-central1`, `europe-west1`, or `asia-southeast1` must draw solely from `local.verified_l4_g2_zones[var.region]`. [`outputs.tf`](../infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/outputs.tf) guards `cluster_ca_certificate` with `try(..., "")` so [`gpu_pool.tftest.hcl`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/tests/gpu_pool.tftest.hcl) plans the real `module.gke` and `gcp_gke_cluster` against `hashicorp/google` v6.50.0 in CI (`terraform-test`).

**Mutation verification (`POAM-2026-120` closure requirement, executed 2026-10-29).** Each mutation was applied alone; both [`test_inference_backend_gke.py`](../tests/infrastructure/test_inference_backend_gke.py) and `terraform test` ([`gpu_pool.tftest.hcl`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/tests/gpu_pool.tftest.hcl)) failed (`rc=1`) where listed, and both passed cleanly before and after the run.

| ID | Mutation | Failing Test(s) (`rc=1`) |
|---|---|---|
| `GPU-M1` | Revert `total_min_node_count` / `total_max_node_count` to per-zone `min_node_count` / `max_node_count` in `infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf` | `test_gpu_node_pool_uses_total_autoscaling_and_zero_initial_default`; `terraform test`: `module_gpu_nodes_uses_total_autoscaling_not_per_zone` |
| `GPU-M2` | Revert `gpu_node_pool_initial_count` default `0 → 1` in `infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/variables.tf` (and separately set `gpu_node_pool_initial_count = 1` in `staging.tfvars`) | `test_gpu_node_pool_uses_total_autoscaling_and_zero_initial_default` (default); `test_staging_keeps_a_three_zone_gpu_pool_that_scales_from_zero` + `terraform test`: `staging_plans_real_gke_module_with_three_verified_zones`, `module_gpu_nodes_uses_total_autoscaling_not_per_zone` (`staging.tfvars`) |
| `GPU-M3` | Re-add `"europe-west1-d"` to `eu-dev.tfvars` `gpu_node_locations` (and separately to `local.verified_l4_g2_zones["europe-west1"]` in `main.tf`) | `test_every_profile_starts_with_zero_gpus_and_lists_only_verified_l4_zones[eu-dev.tfvars]`; `terraform test`: `europe_west1_d_is_refused_for_l4_pool` |
