### POAM-2026-143: Healthcare Declared a Causal Graph Nothing Read

**Control:** NIST CM-6 (Configuration Settings), SA-11 (Developer Testing and Evaluation)
**Risk Level:** Low
**Status:** Closed (2026-10-29) — merged in `#584` as `d011b175` and verified by `HG-M1`–`HG-M2` mutations
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-28
**Target Closure:** 2026-12-31

**Description:**
[`HealthcareCagePlugin.domain_config`](../src/cage_healthcare/plugin.py) declared `causal_graph_path`, and [`domain_config_of()`](../src/gateway/governance/plugin_loader.py) refused to start if the file was missing. Nothing in `src/cage_healthcare/` read the path or built a causal tier, so the startup check guarded a file nothing used, and the declaration suggested a causal tier the domain does not have.

**Remediation:**
The declaration and the domain's `causal_graph.yaml` are removed. [`test_healthcare_declares_no_causal_graph`](../tests/test_healthcare_plugin.py) pins both: `causal_graph_path` is `None`, and no `causal_graph.yaml` ships.

**Mutation verification (`POAM-2026-120` closure requirement, executed 2026-10-29).** Each mutation was applied alone; the named test failed (`rc=1`), and the file passed before and after the run.

| ID | Mutation | Failing Test (`rc=1`) |
|---|---|---|
| HG-M1 | Restore `causal_graph_path` in the plugin's `DomainConfig` (with the file) | `test_healthcare_declares_no_causal_graph` |
| HG-M2 | Restore `causal_graph.yaml` alone | `test_healthcare_declares_no_causal_graph` |

### POAM-2026-149: Actuation Receipts Recorded the Raw Routing Seal as Their Decision Digest

**Control:** NIST IA-5 (Authenticator Management), SC-28 (Protection of Information at Rest), AU-3 (Content of Audit Records)
**Risk Level:** Moderate
**Status:** Closed (2026-11-01) — merged in `#617` as `81a70602` (branch head `248aaee9`) and verified across all three writers and `cage-conformance` `0.3.0` (`DD-M1`–`DD-M26`); live `actuator_03` partner sandbox verification split to `POAM-2026-166`. (Originally closed 2026-10-29 on `fix/seal-digest-in-evidence`, `SD-M1`–`SD-M13`, and reopened 2026-10-30 because that closure covered one of the field's three writers; see *Why the closure was premature*.)
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-29
**Target Closure:** 2026-11-30 (originally 2026-10-29)
**Date Closed:** 2026-11-01

**Description:**
[`ExecutionClearance`](../src/gateway/governance/seams/actuation.py) documents `governance_decision_digest` as a SHA-256 digest ("digest-only, never full content"). The routing seal has its own field, `routing_seal`, which `to_dict()` and `repr` leave out because the seal is a bearer credential. Both domain tool providers put the raw seal in the digest field anyway:
- Finance, from #190 (`960ba7c9`, 2026-09-13). [`BrokerActuator`](../src/cage_finance/actuators/broker_actuator.py) also used the digest field as the seal it handed `execute_trade()`.
- Healthcare, from #534 (`42b819d4`, 2026-10-24), through a positional tuple in which the seal filled the digest's slot.

From #327 (`01cb94cd`, 2026-10-01), [`ingest_actuation_receipt()`](../src/gateway/governance/execution_actuator.py) has written `governance_decision_digest` into every actuation receipt on the evidence stream: accepted, refused and indeterminate. The custodian archives the stream to WORM storage, and a bearer credential in a WORM archive cannot be redacted. That asymmetry is why this was fixed at once rather than scheduled: a log or cache can be cleaned up later, but the archived copy is permanent. Before #327 the seal reached only the broker's in-memory raw receipt and, cut to 16 characters, a log line.

Exploitability is low. Each seal was consumed before its receipt was written, because `verify_and_consume_seal()` burns the single-use nonce first. Each also expired after the seal TTL (30 seconds by default) and authorises only one `(action, params)`, so the current verifier refuses a replay. The risk is Moderate rather than Low because the exposure cannot be undone: for as long as the archive is retained, an archived seal is safe only while the verifier stays as strict as it is today. Whether any such record reached a WORM archive was not checked. No partner adapter received a seal, since both domains dispatch only to their in-process actuators.

**Remediation & Verification (2026-10-29):**
1. **One digest.** [`seal_digest()`](../src/gateway/governance/routing_seal.py) is the SHA-256 hex of a seal. The decision trace's [`seal_ref()`](../src/gateway/governance/governor/trace.py) now returns it, so a seal's issuance and the actuation it authorised join on the same key. For an HMAC seal the digest is also its nonce, which `GOVERNANCE_TRACE` events already published as `seal_ref`. Recording it adds no exposure, because consuming or revoking a seal takes the seal itself.
2. **The contract refuses a seal.** `ExecutionClearance.__post_init__` raises `ValueError` unless `governance_decision_digest` is a lowercase SHA-256 hex digest, matched in full. Both seal formats contain `.`, so a seal in that field fails at construction, in any domain. The error does not echo the refused value, which would carry the seal into logs and receipt findings.
3. **Both domains record the digest, and the seal travels out of band.** The finance and healthcare tool providers pass `seal_digest(seal)` as the digest and the seal as `routing_seal`. `BrokerActuator` refuses a clearance without `routing_seal` (`MISSING_ROUTING_SEAL`, formerly `MISSING_GOVERNANCE_DIGEST`) and hands `execute_trade()` the routing seal, never the digest. `ClinicalDoseActuator` now checks `routing_seal`; its old check on the digest is vacuous under the contract. The breaking change is RS-1 in [`BREAKING_CHANGES.md`](BREAKING_CHANGES.md).
4. **Mutation verification (`SD-M1`–`SD-M13`, verified 2026-10-29).** [`tests/test_seal_digest_evidence.py`](../tests/test_seal_digest_evidence.py) mints seals in both formats with the production `generate_seal()` and follows each domain through `dispatch_actuation()` to the evidence record. The other tests are in [`tests/test_actuator_registry_integration.py`](../tests/test_actuator_registry_integration.py). Each mutation was applied alone, each named test failed on its own (`rc=1`), and the unmutated baseline passed before and after.

| ID | Mutation | Failing Test(s) (`rc=1`) |
|---|---|---|
| `SD-M1` | Finance passes the raw seal as `governance_decision_digest` (the defect) | `test_finance_records_the_digest_and_hands_the_broker_the_seal` |
| `SD-M2` | Healthcare passes the raw seal as `governance_decision_digest` (the defect) | `test_healthcare_records_the_digest_not_the_seal` |
| `SD-M3` | The clearance contract stops validating the digest | `test_clearance_refuses_a_seal_in_the_digest_field`, `test_clearance_refuses_any_non_digest` |
| `SD-M4` | The contract uses an anchored `re.match`, which admits a trailing newline | `test_clearance_refuses_any_non_digest[newline]` |
| `SD-M5` | The contract admits uppercase hex | `test_clearance_refuses_any_non_digest[upper]` |
| `SD-M6` | The broker hands `execute_trade()` the digest as its seal (the old coupling) | `test_broker_actuator_success_path`, `test_finance_records_the_digest_and_hands_the_broker_the_seal` |
| `SD-M7` | The broker checks the digest instead of the out-of-band seal | `test_broker_actuator_requires_the_routing_seal` |
| `SD-M8` | The clinical actuator checks the digest instead of the seal | `test_clinical_actuator_refuses_a_clearance_without_its_seal` |
| `SD-M9` | The trace's `seal_ref` drifts from `seal_digest()` | `test_seal_digest_is_sha256_and_the_trace_join_key` |
| `SD-M10` | `seal_digest()` returns the seal itself | `test_seal_digest_is_sha256_and_the_trace_join_key`, `test_finance_records_the_digest_and_hands_the_broker_the_seal`, `test_healthcare_records_the_digest_not_the_seal` |
| `SD-M11` | `ExecutionClearance.to_dict()` serialises the routing seal | `test_clearance_keeps_the_seal_out_of_its_serialised_forms` |
| `SD-M12` | The actuation receipt's evidence record carries the routing seal | `test_finance_records_the_digest_and_hands_the_broker_the_seal`, `test_healthcare_records_the_digest_not_the_seal` |
| `SD-M13` | The contract's refusal echoes the refused value | `test_clearance_refuses_a_seal_in_the_digest_field` |

**Staging WORM check (2026-10-29, read-only).** No seal reached the staging WORM bucket. It holds 66 evidence batches from 2026-10-02 to 2026-10-25, with 1,815 records. 30 of them are actuation receipts: 28 `ACTUATION_RECEIPT` and 2 `ACTUATION_REFUSAL_RECEIPT`, from 2026-10-22 and 2026-10-25. In every one the digest field holds `[REDACTED_JWS]`: the evidence sink's PII sanitizer matched the seal as a compact JWS and replaced it before hashing. No record in the archive holds a string shaped like a seal. Redis and ClickHouse sit downstream of the same sanitizer, so they received the redacted value too (inferred from the sink order, not inspected).
- **The sanitizer prevented the leak and broke the linkage.** No bearer token sits in retained storage, so this stays a code fix with no irremovable data (the bucket's locked retention period is one day). The same redaction means those 30 receipts cannot be joined to the decisions they record: an evidence-integrity gap produced by a privacy control doing its job. They stay verifiable as records, and for them the gap is permanent.
- **Restoring the linkage must not weaken the leak control.** This fix restores it by what it records: a SHA-256 digest, which the sanitizer leaves intact because it is not token-shaped (checked). Restoring it instead by exempting the digest field, or any evidence field, from sanitization would reopen the leak for the next producer that writes a token there. Any later change to linkage keeps the sanitizer's coverage whole.
- The sanitizer caught the seal because of its format, not because it is a control for seals. That is why the fix does not rely on it.

**Why the closure was premature (2026-10-30).** The review gap matters more than this item: the closure verified the writer that was fixed, not every writer of the field. Its scope was `ExecutionClearance` alone: `__post_init__` refuses any `governance_decision_digest` that is not 64 lowercase hex characters, and `SD-M1`–`SD-M13` exercised that one writer. Two other writers put a field of the same name into the evidence stream, whose archived copy is WORM-retained and cannot be redacted, and neither was examined (read at `34709d55`, 2026-10-30):
1. **`QuarantineDirective`** ([`quarantine.py`](../src/gateway/governance/seams/quarantine.py)) is a frozen dataclass with no format check, and [`dispatch_quarantine()`](../src/gateway/governance/quarantine_actuator.py) writes its digest into `WORKLOAD_QUARANTINE_RECEIPT`. The field means different things depending on the writer. The kernel's quarantine trigger, where `handle_deny()` builds the directive ([`verdicts.py`](../src/gateway/governance/governor/verdicts.py)), sets it to the refusal receipt's `proof_hash`, because a DENY has no seal; `handle_deny()` is the only caller of `dispatch_quarantine()` in `src/`. actuator_03 ([`adapter.py`](../src/integrations/actuator_03/adapter.py)) copies a clearance's seal digest into it when it turns an `ExecutionClearance` into a directive. Its partner receives the field in the signed directive with both meanings, because `dispatch_quarantine()` also sends the kernel's directive to actuator_03 when `CAGE_QUARANTINE_ACTUATOR` selects it.
2. **actuator_02's `OcsfEventModel`** ([`ocsf_ingestor.py`](../src/integrations/actuator_02/ocsf_ingestor.py)) declares the field as `Field(min_length=8, max_length=128)`, so any string of 8 to 128 characters in the sandbox supervisor's OCSF event passes, and writes it into `SANDBOX_OCSF_TELEMETRY`.

The stream invariant oracle ([`stream_invariants.py`](../proof/stream_invariants.py)) checks the field only on the three `ACTUATION_*` event types, not on either of these.

**Decision (2026-10-30).** Rename the field so that each field has exactly one meaning, rather than document two meanings for one name. Archived compliance evidence must be self-describing. The rename breaks actuator_03's partner: a one-time cost on a schedule CAGE controls, instead of a permanent lookup table for every consumer.

**Remediation Plan (reopened scope):**
1. One shared digest validator in `src/gateway/governance/seams/`, used by every writer.
2. The rename. The exact field names are settled in the fix PR.
3. A stream-wide oracle invariant: every decision-digest field on every event type is lowercase 64-hex.
4. A partner notice for actuator_03.
5. Correct the fixtures configured around the defect (the POAM-2026-120 class). For example, the directive fixture in [`test_actuator_03.py`](../src/integrations/actuator_03/tests/test_actuator_03.py) uses `"digest-abc-123"`.
6. Amend the OSCAL statements to match the fix. At reopening, no statement in `compliance/oscal/` cites this item or claims seal-digest-only evidence (grep, 2026-10-30), so reopening changed none.

**Closure Criteria:**
1. Tests and a mutation table cover all three writers and the oracle invariant; each mutation, applied alone, fails a named test (`rc=1`). (**Done:** `DD-M1`–`DD-M26`, `2026-10-31`.)
2. Conformance-suite (`cage-conformance` `0.3.0`) verification against the published specification ([`CONFORMANCE_VERIFIER_SPEC.md`](architecture/CONFORMANCE_VERIFIER_SPEC.md) `digest_format` and `refusal_evidence`) passes across both [`proof/stream_invariants.py`](../proof/stream_invariants.py) and the byte-identical packaged verifier in [`packages/cage-conformance`](../packages/cage-conformance) (`DD-M18`–`DD-M25`, [`tests/test_cage_conformance.py`](../tests/test_cage_conformance.py), [`tests/test_decision_digest_format.py`](../tests/test_decision_digest_format.py)). Over-the-wire partner sandbox verification of `actuator_03`'s signed wire body is split to `POAM-2026-166` because `actuator_03` (NVIDIA Sentry / DOCA) is at the specification/conformance stage with no live partner sandbox endpoint. (**Done:** `2026-10-31`.)
3. Record the merge commit SHA and the actual verification date here. (**Done:** merged in `#617` as `81a70602`, branch head `248aaee9`, `2026-11-01`.)

**Remediation & Verification (reopened scope, 2026-10-31, `fix/decision-digest-format`, merged on `main` as `81a70602` in `#617` on `2026-11-01`):**
1. **One validator (plan item 1).** [`require_sha256_hex()`](../src/gateway/governance/seams/digests.py) lives in a new seam module that imports only the standard library. It accepts only lowercase SHA-256 hex, matched with `re.fullmatch`, and its `ValueError` names the field and never contains the value. `ExecutionClearance`, `QuarantineDirective` and actuator_02's `OcsfEventModel` call it.
2. **The rename (plan item 2).** `governance_decision_digest` means only `seal_digest(seal)`. A quarantine triggered by a refusal names the refusal in a new field, `refusal_proof_hash`: the receipt's `proof_hash`. `QuarantineDirective` is keyword-only and carries exactly one of the two fields; neither or both raises `ValueError`. Its `to_dict()`, which is the archived description and actuator_03's signed wire body, emits only the field that is set. [`dispatch_quarantine()`](../src/gateway/governance/quarantine_actuator.py) records that field in `WORKLOAD_QUARANTINE_RECEIPT` under its own name, and actuator_03's `actuate()` bridge sets `governance_decision_digest` from the clearance. The breaking changes are DV-1–DV-4 in [`BREAKING_CHANGES.md`](BREAKING_CHANGES.md).
3. **The refusal hash is a digest where the directive is built, and a malformed one fails closed.** [`build_refusal_receipt()`](../src/gateway/governance/governor/verdicts.py) never supplies `proof_hash`, so `RefusalReceipt.__post_init__` computes it as a SHA-256 hex digest before `handle_deny()` builds the directive: the field's `""` default cannot reach it. Should that change, `handle_deny()` catches the directive's `ValueError`. No directive is dispatched or archived, the `GovernanceError` is still raised, and its payload carries `quarantined: false` and `quarantine_error: QUARANTINE_DIRECTIVE_INVALID`.
4. **actuator_02.** `OcsfEventModel` checks `governance_decision_digest`, and any `governance_decision_digest` or `refusal_proof_hash` nested in `metadata`, with the shared validator. It sets `hide_input_in_errors=True`, so a `ValidationError`'s string and repr never contain a refused value; its `errors()` and `json()` still do unless called with `include_input=False`.
5. **Stream-wide oracle rule (plan item 3).** [`stream_invariants.py`](../proof/stream_invariants.py) adds `digest_format`: on every record of every event type, each `governance_decision_digest` and `refusal_proof_hash`, at any depth of the payload, is lowercase 64-hex. A finding names the field's path and never its value. `_is_hex64` now uses `fullmatch`, so the oracle's other hex checks no longer admit a trailing newline either. Stream order is deterministic for the binding: `handle_deny()` awaits the refusal's append before it dispatches the quarantine, and each gateway process appends through one FIFO queue. So on a log read from genesis, `refusal_evidence` also requires each `WORKLOAD_QUARANTINE_RECEIPT` to follow the refusal its `refusal_proof_hash` names. The packaged copy is byte-identical (`check_conformance_sync.py`), and `cage-conformance` 0.3.0 lists the rule. The rule also flags a non-digest that a record echoes from caller-supplied structure, such as a refusal's `attempted_params`: the archive cannot tell an echoed value from a written one.
6. **Fixtures built around the defect (plan item 5).** `git grep -n governance_decision_digest` (scripts included) found two non-hex values for these fields: `"digest-abc-123"` in [`test_actuator_03.py`](../src/integrations/actuator_03/tests/test_actuator_03.py) and `"digest-1"` in [`test_shared_quarantine_and_proposals.py`](../tests/test_shared_quarantine_and_proposals.py). Both directives now set `refusal_proof_hash` to a SHA-256 digest. After the change, the only quarantine writers are `handle_deny()`, which sets `refusal_proof_hash`, and actuator_03's bridge, which copies the clearance's seal digest; neither uses the old meaning. One script, [`verify_provider_01_wire_and_hostile.py`](../scripts/verify_provider_01_wire_and_hostile.py), passes a consequence token's `act` digest (SHA-256 hex) as the digest of two clearances it builds only to be refused for executor and route drift; no seal exists there, and it is left as is. CI collects `tests/` only, not `src/integrations/*/tests/`, so the new hermetic tests are in [`test_decision_digest_format.py`](../tests/test_decision_digest_format.py).
7. **Partner notice (plan item 4).** [`docs/partners/actuator_03/README.md`](partners/actuator_03/README.md) describes the signed directive's wire change and what a receiver must change. Both actuator_02 READMEs describe the tightened format.
8. **OSCAL (plan item 6).** A new AU-3 statement in [`sp800-53-component-definition.yaml`](../compliance/oscal/sp800-53-component-definition.yaml) (Governance Gateway) cites the validator, the oracle rule and the tests, and names SC-28 and IA-5, noting `POAM-2026-166` for the deferred `actuator_03` partner sandbox run. The exporter ran in default discovery mode and changed no generated content, only timestamps, which are not committed; `tests/test_oscal_ssp_exporter.py` passed.

**Mutation verification (`DD-M1`–`DD-M26`, verified 2026-10-31).** Run on the `fix/decision-digest-format` commit (`#617`, `248aaee9`, merged on `main` as `81a70602` on `2026-11-01`). Each mutation was applied alone, each named test failed (`rc=1`), and the unmutated baseline passed before and after. `T` is [`test_decision_digest_format.py`](../tests/test_decision_digest_format.py) and `SD` is [`test_seal_digest_evidence.py`](../tests/test_seal_digest_evidence.py).

| ID | Mutation | Failing Test(s) (`rc=1`) |
|---|---|---|
| `DD-M1` | The shared validator uses an anchored `re.match`, which admits a trailing newline | `T::test_validator_refuses_a_non_digest_naming_the_field[newline]`, `SD::test_clearance_refuses_any_non_digest[newline]` |
| `DD-M2` | The shared validator admits uppercase hex | `T::test_validator_refuses_a_non_digest_naming_the_field[upper]`, `SD::test_clearance_refuses_any_non_digest[upper]` |

| `DD-M16` | `OcsfEventModel` shows inputs in its errors (`hide_input_in_errors` off) | `T::test_ocsf_refuses_a_seal_without_echoing_it[hmac]`, `T::test_ocsf_refuses_a_non_digest_without_echoing_it[old-fixture]` |

| `DD-M19` | `digest_format` checks the top level only | `T::test_digest_format_flags_a_bad_value_anywhere[nested-mapping]`, `[nested-list-newline]` |
| `DD-M20` | `digest_format` checks `ACTUATION_*` events only (the old scope) | `T::test_digest_format_flags_a_bad_value_anywhere[ignored-type-top-level]`, `[nested-mapping]` |
| `DD-M21` | The oracle's `_is_hex64` reverts to an anchored `re.match` | `T::test_digest_format_flags_a_bad_value_anywhere[nested-list-newline]` |
| `DD-M22` | A `digest_format` finding echoes the value | `T::test_digest_format_never_echoes_the_value[hmac]` |
| `DD-M23` | The oracle drops the quarantine-follows-its-refusal binding | `T::test_a_quarantine_receipt_follows_its_refusal` |
| `DD-M24` | Only the packaged oracle copy stops running `digest_format` | `T::test_the_packaged_oracle_flags_the_same`, `test_cage_conformance.py::test_packaged_proof_modules_are_byte_identical_and_isolated` |
| `DD-M25` | `cage-conformance`'s `STREAM_RULES` omits `digest_format` | `T::test_the_packaged_oracle_flags_the_same` |
| `DD-M26` | The digest seam imports a kernel module | `T::test_digest_module_imports_only_the_standard_library` |

**Closure status (2026-11-01 — Closed).** All three closure criteria are met: (1) the `DD-M1`–`DD-M26` mutation table above, (2) `cage-conformance` `0.3.0` conformance-suite verification against [`CONFORMANCE_VERIFIER_SPEC.md`](architecture/CONFORMANCE_VERIFIER_SPEC.md) (`digest_format` and `refusal_evidence` in [`tests/test_cage_conformance.py`](../tests/test_cage_conformance.py) and [`tests/test_decision_digest_format.py`](../tests/test_decision_digest_format.py), with byte-identical parity enforced by [`scripts/check_conformance_sync.py`](../scripts/check_conformance_sync.py)), and (3) squash-merge commit `81a70602` (`#617`, branch head `248aaee9`, `2026-11-01`). Over-the-wire partner sandbox verification for `actuator_03`'s changed signed wire body ([wire change notice](partners/actuator_03/README.md#wire-change-notice-decision-digest-fields)) is tracked separately in `POAM-2026-166`.

`POAM-2026-149` is re-added to `CODE_EVIDENCE_FINDINGS` in [`check_poam_lula_divergence.py`](../scripts/check_poam_lula_divergence.py) citing [`tests/test_decision_digest_format.py`](../tests/test_decision_digest_format.py), [`tests/test_seal_digest_evidence.py`](../tests/test_seal_digest_evidence.py), and [`tests/test_cage_conformance.py`](../tests/test_cage_conformance.py).

### POAM-2026-154: Concurrent CBF Commits Trigger Torn Fence-Epoch Reads and Flake the POAM-2026-070 Double-Spend Guard

**Control:** NIST SI-10 (Information Input Validation), SA-11 (Developer Testing and Evaluation), CA-7 (Continuous Monitoring)
**Risk Level:** Moderate
**Status:** Closed (2026-10-29) — merged in `#586` as `c8b0a504` and verified by `CBF-M1`–`CBF-M4` mutations
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-29
**Target Closure:** 2026-10-29

**Description:**
In GitHub Actions run `40904110330` attempt 1 (job `123554042108`, 2026-10-29, pull request #581), `test_concurrent_trades_over_the_limit_admit_one[one-cbf]` in [`test_cbf_settlement_ledger.py`](../tests/test_cbf_settlement_ledger.py) — the regression guard for `POAM-2026-070` (concurrent over-the-limit double-spend) — failed with `RECONCILIATION_UNAVAILABLE: CBF strict mode: epoch=0 < last_seen=1 (possible failover to stale replica)` instead of `UNSAFE`, after logging `CRITICAL` `CBF_EPOCH_REGRESSION_DETECTED`. Note on CI blocking: the failure occurred in [`test-hermetic.yml`](../.github/workflows/test-hermetic.yml) (`Hermetic Governance Pipeline Tests`), which is **not** a required status check in ruleset `25966171`, so a red run in that job blocks nothing; the same test is also collected by the required `Pytest Logic Tests` job, where a rerun masks an intermittent race.

**Root cause (proof-to-implementation gap, verified deterministically 2026-10-29):**
- **Not test isolation.** `_build_world()` constructs a dedicated `fakeredis.FakeServer()`, `FakeRedis`, and `ControlBarrierFunction` per call.
- **Not a committed-epoch ordering violation in Redis.** `safety:fence_epoch` advanced monotonically `0 → 1`, so `sp4_epoch_monotonic` (`SP-2` in the conformance specification) held on Redis.
- **Two torn-read windows between [`DistributedCBF.tla`](../proof/DistributedCBF.tla) (`Read`) / [`distributed_cbf_model.py`](../proof/distributed_cbf_model.py) (`read`) and [`cbf_engine.py`](../src/gateway/governance/safety/cbf_engine.py).** The formal model executes reading `available_balance`, `fence_epoch`, and `agent_epochs[a]` as one atomic step and handles concurrent commits via `WriteReject` (`LUA_ATOMIC_CBF` status `2`, `FENCE_ADVANCED`). `cbf_engine.py` split that step across two multi-await windows:
  1. **Window 1 (`_read_cbf_state_atomic` / `_check_fence_epoch` — the exact CI failure).** `_get_fence_epoch()` read `GET safety:fence_epoch` (`0`), yielded, and `_check_fence_epoch(0)` then read `self._last_seen_epoch` and issued a second Redis `GET safety:fence_epoch_hwm`. When a concurrent `LUA_ATOMIC_CBF` committed in between (on the same `ControlBarrierFunction` or a second pod), both `safety:fence_epoch_hwm` and `self._last_seen_epoch` became `1`, so `_check_fence_epoch(0)` logged `CRITICAL` `CBF_EPOCH_REGRESSION_DETECTED`, incremented `cage_cbf_epoch_regression_detected_total`, and raised `GovernanceError` on `attempt=1` without retrying.
  2. **Window 2 (`atomic_verify_and_commit`).** After `_resolve_ground_truth_balance()` returned `fence_epoch=0`, `atomic_verify_and_commit()` yielded again and compared `0` against post-commit `self._last_verified_fence_epoch` and a fresh `GET safety:fence_epoch_hwm` (`1`), logging `CRITICAL` `FENCE_EPOCH_REGRESSION` and returning `(False, "Fence epoch regression: 0 < 1", 0.0)` on `attempt=1` without retrying.
- **Availability and alerting impact.** Delaying the second coroutine's `asyncio.to_thread(read_verified_snapshot, ...)` completion by `k` event-loop ticks under `_YieldingRedis` reproduced Window 2 at `k = 3, 4` and Window 1 at `k = 5, 6` on both `one-cbf` and `two-cbfs`, including for two **admissible** `$100` trades (`[True, False]` instead of `[True, True]`). Concurrent benign traffic therefore failed closed without CAS retry while emitting false stale-replica `CRITICAL` alerts.

**Remediation & Verification (2026-10-29):**
1. **Atomic `(fence_epoch, fence_epoch_hwm)` fetch.** `_read_fence_epoch_and_hwm()` in [`cbf_engine.py`](../src/gateway/governance/safety/cbf_engine.py) fetches `safety:fence_epoch` and `safety:fence_epoch_hwm` in a single `MGET` (and in a single transaction pipeline in self-reported mode), so `LUA_ATOMIC_CBF` and `LUA_ROLLBACK_CBF` cannot interleave between the two keys.
2. **Linearizable `Read` loop.** `_read_cbf_state_atomic()` brackets the snapshot and debit-ledger read with `seen_before` and `(epoch_before, hwm_before)`. An unambiguous regression (`fence_epoch < max(seen_before, epoch_before, hwm_before)` or `fence_epoch < redis_hwm`) fails closed immediately via `_check_fence_epoch()`; a forward concurrent commit (`fence_epoch > epoch_before` or `self._last_seen_epoch > fence_epoch`) re-reads cleanly at the new epoch.
3. **Window 2 removed.** `atomic_verify_and_commit()` captures `_last_verified_fence_epoch` before `_resolve_ground_truth_balance()` and reuses the atomically captured `fence_epoch_hwm` from `balance_metadata`, leaving concurrent commits after `Read` to `LUA_ATOMIC_CBF` status `2` (`FENCE_ADVANCED`) CAS retry.
4. **Mutation verification (`CBF-M1`–`CBF-M4`, verified 2026-10-29).** Deterministic interleaving tests (`skew_ticks = 0..13` across `one-cbf` and `two-cbfs`, over-limit and admissible, plus genuine stale-replica fail-closed checks on both in-process and Redis HWM floors) live in [`test_cbf_settlement_ledger.py`](../tests/test_cbf_settlement_ledger.py). Each mutation was applied alone and failed its named test(s) (`rc=1`), with the unmutated baseline passing (`34 passed`).

| ID | Mutation | Failing Test(s) (`rc=1`) |
|---|---|---|
| `CBF-M1` | Split `GET safety:fence_epoch` from `_check_fence_epoch`'s `GET safety:fence_epoch_hwm` (Window 1 cross-key race) | `test_concurrent_over_limit_trades_never_false_epoch_regress_across_skew[one-cbf]`, `[two-cbfs]`; `test_concurrent_admissible_trades_both_commit_across_skew[one-cbf]`, `[two-cbfs]` |
| `CBF-M2` | Keep `MGET`, but compare against post-await `self._last_seen_epoch` without re-reading on concurrent advance (Window 1 same-instance race) | `test_concurrent_over_limit_trades_never_false_epoch_regress_across_skew[one-cbf]`, `test_concurrent_admissible_trades_both_commit_across_skew[one-cbf]` |
| `CBF-M3` | Restore post-read `self._last_verified_fence_epoch` and `client.get(_REDIS_KEY_FENCE_EPOCH_HWM)` in `atomic_verify_and_commit` (Window 2 race) | `test_concurrent_over_limit_trades_never_false_epoch_regress_across_skew[one-cbf]`, `[two-cbfs]`; `test_concurrent_admissible_trades_both_commit_across_skew[one-cbf]`, `[two-cbfs]` |
| `CBF-M4` | Disable `current_epoch < hwm` rejection in `_check_fence_epoch` | `test_genuine_stale_replica_epoch_regression_still_fails_closed[in_process_seen]`, `[redis_hwm]` |

### POAM-2026-124: Content-Safety Checks That Did Not Run Are Reported as Passed

**Control:** NIST SI-10 (Information Input Validation), AU-3 (Content of Audit Records)
**Risk Level:** High
**Status:** Closed (2026-10-28) — merged in #566 as `493727f5`; the evidence tests were re-run on that commit the same day
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-28
**Target Closure:** 2026-11-30

**Description:**
The gateway's [`verify_content_safety`](../src/gateway/server/mcp_tool_server.py) tool is the content-safety check the advisor forwards to the gateway (POAM-2026-079). Two paths report a check that did not run as passed. Both were verified hermetically on 2026-10-28.

1. **Rails not initialised.** When `app.state.nemo_rails` is `None`, the tool returns the string `"ERROR: NeMo rails not initialised."` rather than a refusal. The advisor's [tool endpoint](../src/governed_financial_advisor/tools/api.py) returns it with `status: SUCCESS`. Because the output does not start with `BLOCKED`, the endpoint records `cage.verdict = SAFE` on its span.
2. **NeMo has no main flow.** When NeMo raises "No main flow found", [`validate_with_nemo()`](../src/integrations/nemo/manager.py) logs a warning, marks its own span `DEGRADED_NO_MAIN_FLOW` and returns safe, on the reasoning that OPA and STPA still check the action. The tool then answers `"SAFE"`.
   - The same pass-through is in `verify_input()` and `validate_output_semantics()`.
   - The advisor graph's in-process NeMo input rail node calls `validate_with_nemo()`, so it records such input as `PASSED`. Its factory's docstring promises "unconditional fail-closed enforcement" (read, not tested).
   - OPA and STPA check actions, not content, so no other tier covers a content-safety check that did not run.

**Reachability.**
- **The control operates with the shipped configuration (verified 2026-10-28).** The gateway's real rails were built from `config/rails` (Colang 2.x, with a `flow main`) and called through the tool. They blocked four must-block inputs and passed a benign one. Two were blocked at the deterministic prompt-injection stage. Two reached the LLM judge, which was unreachable and failed closed. "No main flow found" was not raised.
- **Path 1 today.** In the shipped gateway image, `create_nemo_manager()` returns `None` only when `nemoguardrails` fails to import. A missing rails config stops startup instead. A config that fails to parse yields the transparent-fallback stub, which `validate_with_nemo()` rejects.
- **Path 2 today** needs a rails config with no main flow.
- **The probe showed neither path.** It ran no CAGE workload, and under #13 the gateway pod never starts.
- **Path 1 under `none`.** The planned `CAGE_INFERENCE_BACKEND=none` builds no rails, so path 1 would fire on every call.

**Remediation (in the inference-backend kernel change, before `none` exists):**
1. Make every path in `verify_content_safety` that is not a verdict return `BLOCKED` with a reason code: rails absent, no main flow, any exception, and `none`.
2. Make the advisor treat only an exact `SAFE` as safe and record every other output as not safe.
3. Turn the "No main flow found" pass-through into a refusal with its own reason code in all three functions, keeping the degraded span marking.
4. Add a test for each path, with a recorded mutation check showing the test fails when its path is reverted.

**Remediation status (2026-10-28, `feat/inference-backend-none`):**
- Steps 1–3 are implemented. The reason codes live in [`content_safety.py`](../src/gateway/governance/content_safety.py): `CONTENT_SAFETY_RAILS_UNAVAILABLE` (rails absent, or the transparent-fallback stub), `CONTENT_SAFETY_NO_MAIN_FLOW`, `CONTENT_SAFETY_ERROR` and `CONTENT_SAFETY_NO_INFERENCE_BACKEND`.
- Step 4: [`test_content_safety_no_verdict.py`](../tests/test_content_safety_no_verdict.py) pins each path, including the advisor graph's in-process input rail node, and [`test_inference_backend_none.py`](../tests/test_inference_backend_none.py) pins the `none` path.
- Mutation check, run on the branch head: restoring the no-main-flow pass-through in `validate_with_nemo()`, `verify_input()` or `validate_output_semantics()` turns that function's test red; making `verify_content_safety` answer `SAFE` with no rails, or under `none`, turns its test red; restoring the advisor's `startswith("BLOCKED")` label turns `test_advisor_records_a_non_verdict_output_as_blocked` red. The full table is in the pull request.

### POAM-2026-123: Plugin Overlays Can Redefine Kernel Controls

**Control:** NIST CM-6 (Configuration Settings), SI-7 (Software, Firmware, and Information Integrity)
**Risk Level:** High
**Status:** Open — recorded; remediation not started
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-28
**Target Closure:** 2026-11-30

**Description:**
[`ControlRegistry`](../src/gateway/governance/constants.py) loads the kernel's regional baseline, then applies each registered plugin overlay with `dict.update`. When an overlay key is already present, it logs "Control mapping collision … overrides earlier mapping" at WARNING and applies the overlay anyway. A plugin overlay can therefore replace any kernel control's definition. That includes a definition the normative provider supplied: `NormativeProviderDaemon` writes the fetched profile to the regional baseline file and reconfigures the registry, and the overlays are applied on top (read).

- **Enforcement inputs (verified hermetically, 2026-10-28).**
  - The EU_ECB FRIA tier's first gate is a staleness check (Art. 27(2) reassessment). It runs on the artefact `registry_assessment_lookup()` reads from `CTRL_FRIA_006.assessments`, which the normative provider is meant to supply. A missing or stale artefact is a hard `FRIA_ASSESSMENT_STALE` denial.
  - With the kernel baseline alone, the real `FriaTier` denied `execute_trade` at that gate without consulting the provider.
  - With a test overlay that defines `CTRL_FRIA_006` with a fresh `assessed_at`, the gate passed and the decision moved to the provider's `validate_fria()`. A stand-in that admits every request then admitted the action.
  - The only signal was one WARNING. No shipped plugin defines `CTRL_FRIA_006`.
  - The provider still decides after the gate, but the gate itself no longer holds.
- **Live, without runtime effect.** `cage_finance`'s US_FED overlay replaces `CTRL_FTRA_001`. With it installed, the registry serves finance's citations, not the kernel's: "NIST AI RMF GOVERN-1.2" instead of "ISO 42001 A.8.4" (verified). No runtime path reads that mapping's content today, which is circumstance, not design (read; POAM-2026-121).

The kernel treats the equivalent conflict as fatal elsewhere: [`assemble_governor()`](../src/gateway/governance/governor/assembly.py) refuses two contributions that fill one engine slot (`GovernorAssemblyError`, "slot collision"). The registry does not. A domain plugin can therefore change what a kernel control attests to, and remove a jurisdictional tier's hard-deny gate, without anything failing.

**Remediation:**
1. Decide the composition contract, which also settles POAM-2026-121: may a plugin overlay touch a kernel control at all, and if so only by declared extension, never by silent replacement?
2. Enforce the contract when the registry loads: an overlay key that redefines a kernel control fails closed, as an engine-slot collision does, unless it is declared.
3. Test it in the required job: a collision fails, and a declared extension is applied and recorded.

### POAM-2026-150: CI Jobs That Cannot Fail

**Control:** NIST SA-11 (Developer Testing and Evaluation), CA-7 (Continuous Monitoring)
**Risk Level:** Moderate
**Status:** Open — remediated across all workflows and merged on `main` in #598 (`43528275`, branch head `1ef835ee`) and #602 (`319c702d`, branch head `867fdb8b`); closes once `lula-validate.yml` live cluster / schema verification is recorded
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-29
**Target Closure:** 2026-12-31

**Description:**
A CI job whose test outcomes cannot fail the build is telemetry, not a test (POAM-2026-121). On the checks page it shows the same green as a job that ran tests and passed, so a reviewer, a merge decision or an assessor who reads that green concludes something was tested. Four such jobs were found on 2026-10-28 and 2026-10-29, a fifth on 2026-10-30, and a sixth on 2026-11-01. Each was found by reading its configuration; none was found because it failed.

| Job | What can and cannot fail it | Recorded |
|---|---|---|
| `integration-smoke` in [`ci.yml`](../.github/workflows/ci.yml) | Runs `pytest tests/ -m integration` without `--run-integration`. [`conftest.py`](../tests/conftest.py) skips every `integration` item unless that flag is set, so the job selects 153 tests and skips them all. On `main` at `7cf448f2` its job log reads `153 skipped` and the job succeeded. No test outcome can fail it; an import or collection error can. The flag gate dates from v2.0.0-dev.1, before the job was added in `bdee33dd` (2026-08-11), so the job has never run an integration test. The only workflow that passes the flag, [`ci-integration.yml`](../.github/workflows/ci-integration.yml), has run on dispatch only since its nightly schedule was disabled. All 25 nightly runs had failed at job setup, before any test ran. It reports nothing rather than a false green, so it is not counted here. | here |
| [`regional-posture.yml`](../.github/workflows/regional-posture.yml) | Not a required check. It runs only after merge (path-filtered) or on dispatch, so not even its hard-gated US_FED leg can block a merge. Its EU_ECB and APAC_MAS legs are `continue-on-error`. | POAM-2026-121 |
| `385e502e^:.github/workflows/compliance-matrix.yml` | Its test step, `385e502e^:scripts/run_matrix_tests.sh`, was `continue-on-error` until #602 (`319c702d`). The workflow did not install `uv` before #636 (`git log -S setup-uv -- .github/workflows/compliance-matrix.yml`), so every masked run stopped at `uv: command not found`: run `29031900749` (2026-07-09) and run `41070803734` (2026-10-30, the last green one) both log it. From #636 until its retirement into [`scripts/staging_lifecycle.sh`](../scripts/staging_lifecycle.sh) Phases 3–5, it ran only on demand against a named deployment (POAM-2026-169, instance 6). | POAM-2026-121 |
| [`lula-validate.yml`](../.github/workflows/lula-validate.yml) | The whole job is `continue-on-error` until CI has GKE credentials. The step cannot fail either: a failed `lula validate` sets a flag that only prints a warning blaming an offline cluster. | [`JURISDICTION_INVENTORY.md`](operations/JURISDICTION_INVENTORY.md) §5 |
| [`ci-partner-integration.yml`](../.github/workflows/ci-partner-integration.yml), the partner over-the-wire harness | Dispatch-only, and it has never run: the GitHub Actions API reports `total_count: 0` for the workflow (verified 2026-10-30). Unlike `ci-integration.yml` it is counted, because no run of it as written could test any partner, and its default input, `all`, could still report green. Its per-partner choices call `tests/test_<partner>_live.py`, but #218 (`fcb98bef`, 2026-09-17) moved those files to `tests/integrations/<partner>/`, so pytest would exit 4 (file not found) for provider_01, provider_02, provider_03 and provider_05. The partner secrets are step-level `env` on the "Configure partner credentials" step, and a step's `env` doesn't reach the next step, so the test step runs without them. A third defect masks the second, and would have hidden any fix to the first: the test step passes `--run-partner-integration` alone. Every partner live test also carries `live_external`, and [`conftest.py`](../tests/conftest.py) skips `live_external` items unless `--run-live-external` is set, so each one skips at collection, provider_08's included (verified 2026-10-30: under that flag, 7 of 7 provider_01 and provider_08 live tests skipped). A green `all` run would therefore mean no partner test ran at all. The Makefile's `test-partner` target and the runbook's commands use the same flag. The repository holds none of the 15 secrets the workflow references (`gh secret list` shows only `MIRROR_SECRET`, 2026-10-30), and actuator_01's test requires `ACTUATOR_01_CERT_PATH`, `ACTUATOR_01_KEY_PATH`, `ACTUATOR_01_CA_PATH` and `ACTUATOR_01_TENANT_ID`, none of which the workflow sets. provider_07 and provider_08 are missing from the choice list. Remediated and merged on `main` in #598 (`43528275`, branch head `1ef835ee`), including GitHub Actions Workload Identity Federation (`vars.ACTUATOR_01_WIF_PROVIDER` → `vars.ACTUATOR_01_WIF_SERVICE_ACCOUNT`) and Cloud KMS Ed25519 signing (`vars.ACTUATOR_01_KMS_KEY`, `cryptoKeys/actuator-01-signing/cryptoKeyVersions/1`); in GitHub Actions run `41125558418`, the `Authenticate to GCP for actuator_01 KMS signing` WIF step succeeded and `Run live tests` failed closed (`5 skipped`, exit `1`) because the `ACTUATOR_01_*` endpoint and mTLS secrets are not yet configured in GitHub Secrets. The first runs on `main` in which partner tests ran and passed followed #610 (`5b0055ad`, 2026-10-31): provider_01 `41233853779` (8 passed; the job mints FlowSignal's Cloud Run ID token through WIF), provider_05 `41234042599` (10 passed), provider_08 `41233869303` (4 passed). The four other adapters have no working sandbox credentials in CI. Their status and blockers are in [`docs/partners/README.md`](partners/README.md#live-verification-status). | here |
| `Hermetic Test (No ML Dependencies)` in [`test-hermetic.yml`](../.github/workflows/test-hermetic.yml) | The `"Verify NO ML libraries installed"` step checked only `torch`, `sentence_transformers`, `transformers`, `tensorflow`, and `jax`, while not checking `openai` or `litellm`, which were the two LLM client packages actually installed in that environment via base dependencies (`POAM-2026-168`). Remediated in `#628` (`a3ab1b52`, branch head `e92e2d12`, `2026-11-01`): `openai` and `litellm` are removed from the base install and the hermetic step now asserts all 14 forbidden ML/LLM modules (`torch`, `sentence_transformers`, `transformers`, `tensorflow`, `jax`, `openai`, `litellm`, `anthropic`, `cohere`, `google.genai`, `vertexai`, `nemoguardrails`, `fastembed`, `onnxruntime`) are absent. | POAM-2026-168 |

**What `lula-validate.yml` has reported.** In GitHub Actions run 40886496347 (2026-10-29), 31 of the 33 manifests were not satisfied, two (`flowsignal` and `ftra`) failed to load as OSCAL, none was satisfied, and the run succeeded. CI has no cluster and no compliance bridge to validate against, so nothing can be satisfied there. No recorded run elsewhere has satisfied a manifest either: every result committed in [`assessment-results.yaml`](../compliance/lula/assessment-results.yaml), for all 29 controls it covers (runs from 2026-07-01 to 2026-08-29), is a schema error. For CM-6 and SI-2, Lula reported Kubernetes resources declared without `resource-rule`, and 16 of the 33 manifests still declare them that way. A closure that cites Lula therefore rests on a validation that no recorded run has satisfied.

**The pattern is the finding.** Each instance has its own reason: a live cluster that CI does not have, a soft gate for regions with open findings, a TODO. Taken together, non-blocking CI has been this repository's most reliable source of false assurance. A required check that fails is visible, but a check that cannot fail looks exactly like one that passed. The converse, required checks that failed and were merged anyway, is POAM-2026-115.

**Human-process instance of the same class (2026-10-30).** After context compaction on `2026-10-30`, an agent response presented a merge-order table for `#594`–`#602` from memory without re-running `gh pr list` or [`scripts/check_pr_mergeable.sh`](../scripts/check_pr_mergeable.sh). Six of the nine PRs were given fabricated branch names and SHAs (`fix/infra-hardening`, `fix/helm-chart-drift`, `feat/dockerfile-hardening`, `feat/network-policy-hardening`, `feat/pdb-hpa-coverage`, `feat/eval-harness-rollouts`), a workflow run ID truncated by `gh run list` (`41125...`) was reconstructed as `41125826815` instead of `41125558418`, and every row was reported as `8/8 PASS`. Re-deriving the state from `gh pr list` and `scripts/check_pr_mergeable.sh` uncovered **four real CI failures** (`#595`, `#598`, and `#596` failing `check_project_id_hygiene.py`, and `#602` failing `license-check` on `forbiddenfruit 0.1.4`) that the recalled table had reported as green. A recalled `"8/8 PASS"` table is the human-process equivalent of a CI job that cannot fail. [`AGENTS.md`](../AGENTS.md) (`Verification Rules`) now records the invariant: **after context compaction, any identifier — commit SHA, branch name, PR number, workflow run ID, or check-run ID — must be re-derived from a tool call, never recalled from memory or reconstructed from a truncated prefix.**

**Remediation:**
1. While this item is open, nothing cites the status of a job in the table as evidence that something was tested: not this document, not the SSPs, not a pull request's testing section. Cite what the job's log shows ran, or nothing.
2. **Done — Partner harness (#598, merged on `main` at `43528275`, branch head `1ef835ee`).** Replaces inline workflow shell with [`partner_harness.py`](../scripts/partner_harness.py) (refuses `0 passed`, passes `--run-partner-integration --run-live-external`, covers all 7 partner namespaces, decodes mTLS material for `actuator_01`, and authenticates to GCP via Workload Identity Federation to sign `actuator_01` approvals with Cloud KMS Ed25519 key `actuator-01-signing`).
3. **Done — Systematic 19-workflow CI gate audit & 34-test adversarial meta-check suite (#602, merged on `main` at `319c702d`, branch head `867fdb8b`).** Classified all 19 `.github/workflows/*.yml` files (`1` release workflow [`publish-client-sdk.yml`](../.github/workflows/publish-client-sdk.yml) with no gate + `18` gate workflows) and [`Makefile`](../Makefile):
   - [`ci.yml`](../.github/workflows/ci.yml) `integration-smoke`: passes `--run-integration` and asserts via `--junitxml` that `passed >= 1`.
   - [`regional-posture.yml`](../.github/workflows/regional-posture.yml), `385e502e^:.github/workflows/compliance-matrix.yml`, [`lula-validate.yml`](../.github/workflows/lula-validate.yml), [`lula2-pr-compliance.yml`](../.github/workflows/lula2-pr-compliance.yml), [`eu-ecb-compliance.yml`](../.github/workflows/eu-ecb-compliance.yml), [`apac-mas-compliance.yml`](../.github/workflows/apac-mas-compliance.yml), and [`policy_compile.yml`](../.github/workflows/policy_compile.yml): removed `continue-on-error: true`, `|| true`, and `exit 0` masks; fixed Rego v1 syntax across 7 `.rego` files so `opa check` passes without `|| true`; fixed [`stpa_compiler.py`](../src/gateway/governance/stpa_compiler.py) to load `.yml` alongside `.yaml`; converted `lula-validate.yml` into a strict OSCAL YAML schema & structure check in CI.
   - **Empirical case for planted-defect tests on every gate (#602, `319c702d`).** Writing planted-defect tests for the 9 remaining workflow families immediately uncovered **5 additional broken gates** whose failure paths had never been exercised (`git log -p .github/workflows/`):
     - [`apac-mas-compliance.yml`](../.github/workflows/apac-mas-compliance.yml) and [`eu-ecb-compliance.yml`](../.github/workflows/eu-ecb-compliance.yml): Steps 1, 3, and 4 printed `WARNING:` and exited `0` (or omitted `sys.exit(1)`) when `lula-validation-mas-*.yaml` / `lula-validation-eu-*.yaml` files were absent, when `apac-dev.tfvars` / `eu-dev.tfvars` were missing or lacked the regional compliance flag, or when the `SR 26-2` / `"no legal force"` sentinel was absent from `APAC_MAS_BASELINE.json` / `EU_ECB_BASELINE.json`.
     - `385e502e^:.github/workflows/compliance-matrix.yml` and `385e502e^:scripts/run_matrix_tests.sh`: Gate 0.1 checked for a nonexistent `core` subdirectory under [`tests/`](../tests/) and printed a warning instead of running core unit tests; `regional-posture` had `if: always()` on `needs: [universal-iso42001]`; Gate `0.4a`–`0.4d` ran `yaml.safe_load()` without checking `'component-definition' in d`; Gate 1.1 (`US_FED` OPA unit tests) referenced a deleted pre-plugin `.rego` path instead of [`src/cage_finance/opa/`](../src/cage_finance/opa/) and [`config/opa/`](../config/opa/); and Gate 1.2 (`EU_ECB` data residency) printed `warn "Gate 1.2 SKIPPED"` instead of failing when [`tests/infrastructure/test_data_residency.py`](../tests/infrastructure/test_data_residency.py) was absent.
     - [`license_guard.yml`](../.github/workflows/license_guard.yml): `grep -iE '("GPL"|"AGPL"|"LGPL"|"General Public License")'` required a closing quote immediately after `GPL`/`AGPL`/`LGPL`, failing to match versioned license strings emitted by `pip-licenses` such as `"GPLv3"`, `"GPL-3.0-only"`, or `"LGPL-2.1"`. On the very first CI run (`check-run 124324466205`) of the fixed regex on #602 (`34fffce3`), `license-check` caught `forbiddenfruit 0.1.4` (`"License": "GNU General Public License v3 or later (GPLv3+); MIT License"`, dual-licensed `GPLv3+` or `MIT` via `langgraph-cli[inmem]` → `blockbuster`), which the broken regex had never matched; refined in `319c702d` (`867fdb8b`) to reject single-licensed copyleft while allowing dual-licensed permissive (`| grep -viE '; *(MIT|Apache|BSD|ISC)'`).
     - [`check_vendor_brands.py`](../scripts/check_vendor_brands.py), [`check_domain_literals.py`](../scripts/check_domain_literals.py), and [`check_telemetry_literals.py`](../scripts/check_telemetry_literals.py): returned `[]` (clean) when a Python file failed `ast.parse()` with `SyntaxError` or `Exception`, and `check_vendor_brands.py` returned `0` if `FORBIDDEN_VENDOR_BRANDS` was empty; all three now fail closed.
   - Guarded by a **34-test adversarial meta-check suite** in [`test_ci_gate_effectiveness.py`](../tests/test_ci_gate_effectiveness.py) (`#602`, `319c702d`) covering the complete 19-workflow inventory, universal structural anti-masking assertions (no `continue-on-error: true`, no `|| true` / `exit 0` error swallowing, no `exit-code: '0'` in Trivy, no `if: always()` on gate jobs, and existence verification of every `pytest` path across all workflows), and planted-defect checks for every gate across all 18 gate workflows.
4. **What the silent gates were carrying — every unmasked gate caught a real finding within hours (`2026-10-30`–`2026-11-01`):**
   - **License guard (`#602`):** caught `forbiddenfruit 0.1.4` (`GPLv3+; MIT`) on its first CI run (`check-run 124324466205`).
   - **Workflow suppression guard (`#599`, `4970e187`):** [`test_workflow_vuln_suppressions.py`](../tests/test_workflow_vuln_suppressions.py) caught the four uncommented `--ignore-vuln` flags copied into [`dependency-review.yml`](../.github/workflows/dependency-review.yml) by `#602` (`319c702d`) 53 seconds after merge (`run 41143961049`).
   - **Un-suppressed `pip-audit` (`#599`, `4970e187`) and fail-closed Trivy (`#602`, `319c702d`, `exit-code: "1"`):** caught four `pyasn1 0.6.4` HIGH advisories (`CVE-2026-119194`–`CVE-2026-119197`) at `02:12Z` (`run 41158877040`).
   - **Bandit on `scripts/` (`#601`, `7f0c1c4b`):** surfaced 8 medium-severity findings (`1× B310`, `7× B108`, `POAM-2026-163`).
   - **Gitleaks default ruleset (`#602`, `319c702d`) and full-history scan:** surfaced the hardcoded default Langfuse init-user password in `1902c92c` / `e608f3ac` (`POAM-2026-160` / `POAM-2026-162`), which in turn uncovered that the live `cage-staging` Cloud SQL Postgres password hash had never been rotated (`default.tfstate.pre-migration-backup` `serial: 689` and `default.tfstate` `serial: 695`; `POAM-2026-071` false closure).
   - **Partner harness (`#598`, `43528275`):** failed closed (`5 skipped`, exit `1` in `run 41125558418`) when `ACTUATOR_01_*` endpoint and mTLS secrets were absent instead of reporting a false green.
   - **Post-merge `compliance-matrix.yml` (`regional-posture` job) failure (`2026-10-30`–`2026-11-01`):** Removing `continue-on-error: true` and `if: always()` in `#602` (`319c702d`) exposed that `385e502e^:.github/workflows/compliance-matrix.yml` (which triggered only on `push` to `main`/`develop`, not on `pull_request`) failed in Job 2 (`regional-posture`) on every `main` push: (a) `regional-posture` set up Python with `actions/setup-python` and checked for a nonexistent `requirements.txt` without installing `uv` (`astral-sh/setup-uv`), so `385e502e^:scripts/run_matrix_tests.sh` (`line 142`) failed immediately with `uv: command not found`; and (b) when run with `uv`, `lula`, and `opa` installed on the verification VM, `run_matrix_tests.sh` re-runs Phase 0 inside each `regional-posture` matrix cell and fails at Gate 0.3 (`verify_langfuse_posture.py` receives empty `secrets.LANGFUSE_*` in `regional-posture` without the mock fallback defaults set in Job 1 `universal-iso42001`) and Gate 0.4 (`lula validate -f compliance/lula/lula-validation-a52.yaml`, which attempts live cluster evaluation instead of the offline YAML schema check in Job 1 and hits the Lula schema error tracked above).
5. **Done on `ci/connect-tlc-model-check` — a sixth gate that could not fail (found 2026-11-02).** Item 3's audit classified [`tlc-model-check.yml`](../.github/workflows/tlc-model-check.yml) as a gate, but the workflow had only a `workflow_dispatch` trigger and first ran on 2026-11-01 (run `41362761903`). The suite had no planted-defect test for it. Its runner, [`verify_tla.py`](../scripts/verify_tla.py), read only invariant verdicts, so TLC's verdict on the action property `SP4_FenceEpochMonotonic` was never checked (POAM-2026-090 detail). The workflow now runs on every pull request and every push to `main`, and the runner fails on any TLC error line no pin accounts for. [`test_workflow_pr_triggers.py`](../tests/test_workflow_pr_triggers.py) pins both triggers. `test_gate_tlc_model_check_rejects_unpinned_tlc_verdicts` in [`test_ci_gate_effectiveness.py`](../tests/test_ci_gate_effectiveness.py) plants six defects that must each fail the gate: SP-4 violated on the shipped cfg and on a negative control, a liveness violation, a deadlock, a search that never completes, and a cfg that no longer declares a pinned invariant. That last plant covers the other half of the gap. The pins measured the state space, not what was checked over it, so a cfg that dropped an `INVARIANTS` or `PROPERTIES` line kept every count and still passed. `DECLARED_CHECKS` in [`tla_pins.py`](../proof/tla_pins.py) now pins what each of the 8 cfgs checks. Both the runner and [`test_tla_specs_proof.py`](../tests/test_tla_specs_proof.py) enforce it, and the test also checks the Step 15 table in `FORMAL_VERIFICATION.md` against the pins.

### POAM-2026-151: CM-6's Lula Validation Has No Producer

**Control:** NIST CM-6 (Configuration Settings), CA-7 (Continuous Monitoring)
**Risk Level:** Moderate
**Status:** Open — Option 2 selected (2026-10-29); gateway producer and stream oracle implemented, Lula CM-6 validation repointing remaining
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-29
**Target Closure:** 2026-11-30

**Description:**
[`lula-validation-cm6.yaml`](../compliance/lula/lula-validation-cm6.yaml) is CM-6's automated evidence. It passes when a ConfigMap named `governance-thresholds-config` exists in `governance-stack`. Nothing in this repository creates that ConfigMap: no Terraform module, no manifest under `deployment/` and no script names it (verified, grep, 2026-10-29). The thresholds it stands for ship inside the gateway image (`COPY config /app/config` in [`src/gateway/Dockerfile`](../src/gateway/Dockerfile)), and are validated in process before any governor is assembled ([`test_regional_overlay.py`](../tests/governor/test_regional_overlay.py)). The control operates; its Lula evidence cannot exist on any cluster this repository deploys.

Until W6 the validation also required `nemo-guardrails-config`, which only the standalone NeMo server's module creates. W6 makes that server optional and off by default, and removes the requirement in the same commit, so the manifest no longer depends on an optional component. That was necessary, not sufficient: with or without it, CM-6 has never been green (`git log -p compliance/lula/assessment-results.yaml`). Every committed CM-6 result is a schema error (POAM-2026-150), so no run has evaluated the Rego.

A ConfigMap's presence would not show what the gateway enforces in any case. The SSP's CM-6 statement already says the validation asserts that the ConfigMap is deployed, not its values.

**Remediation:**
Option 2 selected (2026-10-29): point CM-6 evidence at the canonical digest of the effective thresholds loaded by the gateway rather than a Kubernetes ConfigMap presence check.
1. **Done (gateway producer & stream oracle).** [`thresholds_digest()`](../src/gateway/governance/schemas/thresholds.py) computes the lowercase SHA-256 hex digest of the RFC 8785 JCS canonicalized effective `GovernanceThresholds` (including any active regional overlay). [`publish_thresholds_loaded()`](../src/gateway/governance/schemas/thresholds.py) emits `GOVERNANCE_THRESHOLDS_LOADED` (`controlId: "CM-6"`, `region`, `thresholds_digest`, `policy_version`) onto `cage:evidence:stream` at gateway startup ([`hybrid_server.py`](../src/gateway/server/hybrid_server.py)), [`generate_seal_with_evidence()`](../src/gateway/governance/routing_seal.py) stamps `thresholds_digest` onto every `GOVERNANCE_DECISION`, `GET /governance/policy-version` ([`governance_middleware.py`](../src/gateway/server/governance_middleware.py)) exposes `thresholds_digest`, and [`check_stream()`](../proof/stream_invariants.py) verifies digest agreement across the stream (verified with mutation sensitivity controls in [`test_stream_invariants_oracle.py`](../tests/test_stream_invariants_oracle.py)).
2. **Open (Lula consumer & run log).** Repoint [`lula-validation-cm6.yaml`](../compliance/lula/lula-validation-cm6.yaml) (and the SSP's CM-6 narrative) from the nonexistent `governance-thresholds-config` ConfigMap to the produced threshold-digest evidence, and record a passing Lula run whose log shows it evaluated and satisfied (alongside resolving the Lula schema error in POAM-2026-150).

### POAM-2026-152: Gateway Egress Allowlist Named Model Providers Nothing Calls

**Control:** NIST SC-7 (Boundary Protection), CM-7 (Least Functionality)
**Risk Level:** Low
**Status:** Open — the named providers are removed; the `*.googleapis.com` wildcard still matches one
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-29
**Target Closure:** 2026-12-31

**Description:**
The gateway's FQDN egress allowlist, in the Terraform default (`gateway_egress_allowed_fqdns` in [`variables.tf`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/variables.tf)) and in the static manifest the SSP cites as SC-7 evidence (`de5534ac:deployment/k8s/cilium/egress-lockdown.yaml`), named three model providers: `api.openai.com`, `api.anthropic.com` and `generativelanguage.googleapis.com`. No GKE inference path calls them. Inference is in-cluster vLLM or none, the `external` backend is compose-only (AG-7), and consensus critics no longer fall back to a provider (POAM-2026-127). Egress that serves nothing is surface with no purpose. It is the mirror of the decision not to open egress for `external` on GKE: the same reasoning that refused a new hole closes these.

**Remediation:**
1. **Done.** The three names are removed from both lists. [`test_gateway_egress_allowlist.py`](../tests/infrastructure/test_gateway_egress_allowlist.py) fails if either list names one, or if the two lists differ. The generated SC-7 narrative says no provider is listed. Compose does not read either list (verified, grep), so the compose `external` path is unaffected. Mutations `EG-M1`–`EG-M3` each failed a named test (`rc=1`, 2026-10-29): a provider re-added to the Terraform default, a provider re-added to the manifest, and the two lists made to differ.
2. **Open.** `*.googleapis.com`, which the gateway needs for GCP APIs, also matches `generativelanguage.googleapis.com`, so one provider stays reachable at L3/L4. Replacing the wildcard with the GCP API hosts the gateway actually calls needs an inventory of those calls; guessing it would risk cutting a control path. The item closes when the wildcard is narrowed, or when a reason to keep it is recorded here.

### POAM-2026-153: Stacked Pull Requests Skip Every Required CI Check Until Retargeted to `main`

**Control:** NIST CM-3 (Configuration Change Control), SA-11 (Developer Testing and Evaluation), CA-7 (Continuous Monitoring)
**Risk Level:** Moderate
**Status:** Closed (2026-11-01) — local `make lint` gap fixed (`#586`, `c8b0a504`), all nine `pull_request` branch filters dropped and guarded (`#589`, `8d167084`, `WF-M1`–`WF-M4`), and live stacked-PR execution verified on `#623` (`2c22152dbe663fe3c61f3a1ae28f96494ce93101` targeting `fix/linkerd-cni-repair`, `40` check-runs triggered including all 8 required status checks; merged on `main` at `7e2ddaec`)
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-29
**Target Closure:** 2026-11-30
**Date Closed:** 2026-11-01

**Description:**
Nine pull-request workflows filtered `on.pull_request.branches` to `[main]` (or `[main, 'release/**']`): [`ci.yml`](../.github/workflows/ci.yml), [`security-scan.yml`](../.github/workflows/security-scan.yml), [`license_guard.yml`](../.github/workflows/license_guard.yml), [`test-hermetic.yml`](../.github/workflows/test-hermetic.yml), [`dependency-review.yml`](../.github/workflows/dependency-review.yml), [`sbom.yml`](../.github/workflows/sbom.yml), [`lula2-pr-compliance.yml`](../.github/workflows/lula2-pr-compliance.yml), [`eu-ecb-compliance.yml`](../.github/workflows/eu-ecb-compliance.yml), and [`apac-mas-compliance.yml`](../.github/workflows/apac-mas-compliance.yml). That set includes every workflow that produces the eight required status checks on `main`. Only [`lula-validate.yml`](../.github/workflows/lula-validate.yml) (`continue-on-error`, POAM-2026-150), [`poam-drift.yml`](../.github/workflows/poam-drift.yml), [`policy_compile.yml`](../.github/workflows/policy_compile.yml) (path-filtered), and the external `cla/google` status ran regardless of a pull request's base branch.

- **Measured on the #578–#582 stack (verified via REST `check-runs` and `status` APIs, 2026-10-29).** Across all 20 pre-`main` head SHAs pushed to #578–#582 while each pull request targeted the branch below it, every SHA ran 1–3 checks (`cla/google`, `POAM vs Lula divergence check`, and where matched `Lula Compliance Assertions`) and **0 of the required status checks**. Saying a stacked pull request was "gated and green" meant the author's local gates passed; CI had not run a single required check on it.
- **Demonstrated consequence.** [`Makefile`](../Makefile)'s `lint` target ran `ruff check` and `uv lock --check` without `mypy src/`. When #578 was retargeted to `main` (`6f61cd02`), the `Lint` job ran for the first time (GitHub Actions run `40899234850`, job `123536661099`) and failed `mypy src/` on [`oscal_ssp_exporter.py`](../src/gateway/governance/oscal_ssp_exporter.py) (`2 errors in 1 file`), forcing a fix at the bottom of the stack (`d99dbeb3`) and a four-branch rebase cascade through #579–#582. Ruleset `25966171` still prevented an untested merge into `main` once each pull request was retargeted, so `main` itself was not exposed; the defect is that stacked pull requests appear green on GitHub before retargeting and defer every CI failure to landing time.

**Remediation:**
1. **Done (local gate).** [`Makefile`](../Makefile) `lint` runs `uv run mypy src/` alongside `ruff check` and `uv lock --check` (`#586`, `c8b0a504`).
2. **Done (workflow triggers + guard test).** All nine pull-request workflows remove `on.pull_request.branches` / `branches-ignore` (`#589`, `8d167084`) so stacked pull requests targeting feature base branches run the full pull-request suite (including all eight required status checks) on every push, while `on.push.branches` stays scoped to `main` across all seven branch-scoped push workflows. [`test_workflow_pr_triggers.py`](../tests/test_workflow_pr_triggers.py) enforces those invariants across every `.github/workflows/*.yml` file and `Makefile`. Sensitivity checks (`2026-10-29`, `rc=1`): `WF-M1` re-adding `branches: [main]` to `ci.yml` `pull_request` failed `test_pull_request_workflows_do_not_filter_target_branches` and `test_required_check_workflows_trigger_on_stacked_pr_branches`; `WF-M2` adding `branches-ignore: ['feat/**']` to `ci.yml` `pull_request` failed `test_pull_request_workflows_do_not_filter_target_branches` and `test_required_check_workflows_trigger_on_stacked_pr_branches`; `WF-M3` dropping `push.branches` in `ci.yml` failed `test_push_triggers_remain_scoped_on_unfiltered_pr_workflows`; `WF-M4` removing `mypy src/` from `Makefile` `lint` failed `test_makefile_lint_runs_mypy`.
3. **Done (live stacked-PR verification, 2026-11-01).** While PR `#623` (commit `2c22152dbe663fe3c61f3a1ae28f96494ce93101`) targeted the non-`main` base branch `fix/linkerd-cni-repair` (`#622`), GitHub Actions triggered **40 check-runs** (`39 success`, `1 skipped`), including all 8 required status checks (`Lint`, `Pytest Logic Tests`, `Quickstart Bootstrap & First-DENY Smoke Gate`, `Secret Scanning (Gitleaks)`, `Dependency Audit (pip-audit)`, `Container & IaC Scanning (Trivy)`, `License Compatibility Guard`, and `Hermetic Test (No ML Dependencies)`), verified via `gh api repos/google/cybernetic-agent-governance-engine/commits/2c22152dbe663fe3c61f3a1ae28f96494ce93101/check-runs` on `2026-11-01`. `#622` (`ac6eb511`, branch head `b98d5276`) and `#623` (`7e2ddaec`, branch head `8230fefb`) subsequently merged on `main` on `2026-11-01`.

### POAM-2026-155: No Magnitude Range Check at Consume, and a Name-Dependent One at Mint (EU_ECB)

**Control:** NIST SI-10 (Information Input Validation), AC-3 (Access Enforcement)
**Risk Level:** Moderate
**Status:** Closed (2026-11-01) — merged in `#620` as `8b462eb7` (branch head `73584932`), verified by `CM-M1`–`CM-M8` mutations and over-the-wire `provider_01` sandbox run `41261195943` (`11 passed, 0 not run`, `2026-11-01`)
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-30
**Target Closure:** 2026-11-30
**Date Closed:** 2026-11-01

**Description:**
A ConsequenceToken authorises one consequential action, and nothing at the consequence boundary checks that the action's magnitude is a positive, finite number at consume. Read at `23f48374` (2026-10-30):
1. **Consume.** [`ConsequenceGateway.evaluate()`](../src/gateway/governance/consequence_gateway.py) verifies the token, rejects payloads that are not I-JSON (non-finite numbers and integers beyond ±(2^53−1)), recomputes the action digest against the token's `act` claim and consumes the single-use marker. It has no range check, so a zero or negative amount passes whenever the digest matches.
2. **Before minting.** The only range checks are in provider_01 ([`provider.py`](../src/integrations/provider_01/provider.py)): `validate_fria()` checks before the partner is called, and the ALLOW path checks again before minting. Each refuses with `CONSEQUENCE_TOKEN_MINT_FAILED` when the first `amount` or `magnitude` value it finds is a boolean, non-finite or not strictly positive. Both look only at the payload's top level and directly under `params`, and both compare `float()` values rather than integer minor units. A magnitude under any other key (for example `value` or `notional`) or nested deeper (for example `params.payment.amount`) is never range-checked, at mint or at consume.
3. Because `act` binds the exact payload, consume cannot see a value that mint did not see. Consume-side protection is therefore exactly the mint-side check, which depends on the field's name and depth.

A token is minted only on the partner's ALLOW, which narrows the exposure; whatever validation the partner applies is not evidence for a CAGE control.

**Remediation:**
1. **Done (kernel contract & three-boundary enforcement).** [`MagnitudePath`](../src/gateway/governance/contracts.py) (`keys: tuple[str, ...]`, `minor_unit_digits: int` in `0..18`) and [`ConsequenceMagnitude`](../src/gateway/governance/contracts.py) (`action`, `paths`), plus [`ConsequenceMagnitudes`](../src/gateway/governance/consequence_magnitude.py) (`MAX_SAFE_MINOR_UNITS = 2**53 - 1`, `MagnitudeReason`, `magnitude_violations()`), enforce that every action at a consequence boundary is declared by its domain plugin (`paths=()` when explicitly magnitude-free) and that every declared path under `params` resolves to a finite `int` or `float` (never `bool`) whose exact decimal representation (`Decimal(int(v))` or `Decimal(float.__repr__(v))`) scales by `10**minor_unit_digits` to a whole integer in `1 .. 2^53 - 1`. Enforced at all three boundaries:
   - **Pre-flight (`EU_ECB` FRIA tier):** [`FriaTier.evaluate()`](../src/gateway/governance/jurisdiction/eu_ai_act/fria_tier.py) checks `magnitude_violations(self._magnitudes, payload)` after FRIA artefact freshness and before calling `provider.validate_fria()`, returning `Violation(code="FRIA_MAGNITUDE_INVALID", kind=ViolationKind.HARD)`.
   - **Mint:** [`mint_consequence_token_finding()`](../src/gateway/governance/consequence_token_service.py) requires keyword-only `magnitudes: ConsequenceMagnitudes | None` and refuses with `CONSEQUENCE_TOKEN_MINT_FAILED` (`severity="blocked"`, `reason_code=refused[0].reason.value`) before KMS signing. [`provider_01`](../src/integrations/provider_01/provider.py) removes its ad-hoc top-level/`params` `"amount"`/`"magnitude"` float checks (`_extract_magnitude`, `_validate_positive_magnitude`) and threads `consequence_magnitudes` alongside [`provider_08`](../src/integrations/provider_08/adapter.py) and [`normative_provider.py`](../src/gateway/governance/normative_provider.py).
   - **Consume:** [`ConsequenceGateway`](../src/gateway/governance/consequence_gateway.py) requires `magnitudes: ConsequenceMagnitudes` at construction and checks `self._magnitudes.violations(action_payload)` at Step 5 (after Step 4 `act` digest verification and before Step 6 `store.consume_once()`), returning `ConsequenceDecision.BLOCK` (`reason_code=refused[0].reason.value`), emitting `CONSEQUENCE_GATEWAY_REFUSAL`, and leaving the single-use marker unconsumed.
   [`assemble_governor()`](../src/gateway/governance/governor/assembly.py) validates that every `ConsequenceMagnitude` names an action in its plugin's `registered_actions`, rejects duplicate declarations across plugins, threads `GovernorComponents.consequence_magnitudes` into `resolve_jurisdiction()`, and rejects any `JurisdictionContribution` or jurisdiction tier carrying a foreign policy (`_reject_foreign_magnitude_policy()`).
2. **Done (per-plugin blast-radius inventory).**
   - **`finance`** ([`src/cage_finance/__init__.py`](../src/cage_finance/__init__.py), [`src/cage_finance/plugin.py`](../src/cage_finance/plugin.py)): all 6 `REGISTERED_ACTIONS` declared — `execute_trade` and `execute_trade_bounded` carry `TRADE_AMOUNT = MagnitudePath(("amount",), minor_unit_digits=2)` (whole cents); `check_balance`, `market_analysis`, `check_market_status`, and `prompt_injection_check` carry `paths=()` (explicitly magnitude-free).
   - **`healthcare`** ([`src/cage_healthcare/constants.py`](../src/cage_healthcare/constants.py), [`src/cage_healthcare/plugin.py`](../src/cage_healthcare/plugin.py)): 5 dosing actions (`administer_dose`, `dose_order`, `administer_medication`, `prescribe_medication`, `order_controlled_substance`) declare `DOSE_MG = MagnitudePath(("dose_mg",), minor_unit_digits=6)` (whole nanograms, admitting sub-microgram doses such as calcitriol `0.25 µg = 0.00025 mg`). Two registered actions are deliberately left undeclared and fail closed with `MAGNITUDE_UNDECLARED` at the `EU_ECB` FRIA pre-flight, token minting, and gateway consumption:
     - `adjust_dosage`: reachable only on `POST /governance/validate-action` outside `EU_ECB` (neither [`ClinicalToolProvider`](../src/cage_healthcare/tools/tool_provider.py) nor [`ClinicalDoseActuator`](../src/cage_healthcare/tools/tool_provider.py) exposes it). No parameter schema exists in the codebase, and a legitimate dosage adjustment may be `0` or negative (dose reduction or discontinuation), which the kernel's strictly-positive magnitude rule would reject. To admit `adjust_dosage` under `EU_ECB`, an adopter must define its parameter model and add a `ConsequenceMagnitude("adjust_dosage", ...)` entry in [`src/cage_healthcare/constants.py`](../src/cage_healthcare/constants.py) (`paths=()` if modeled as a non-transfer directive, or a strictly-positive `MagnitudePath` if modeled as a positive target dose).
     - `override_contraindication`: reachable only on `POST /governance/validate-action` outside `EU_ECB` (no tool or actuator exposes it). No parameter schema defines whether its payload carries a dose or only an override justification. To admit `override_contraindication` under `EU_ECB`, an adopter must define its parameter model and add `ConsequenceMagnitude("override_contraindication", ...)` in [`src/cage_healthcare/constants.py`](../src/cage_healthcare/constants.py) (`paths=()` if magnitude-free, or `(DOSE_MG,)` if it carries a dose).
   - **`physical_ai`** ([`src/cage_physical_ai/plugin.py`](../src/cage_physical_ai/plugin.py)): `domain_config = None` (`POAM-2026-077`), so [`domain_config_of()`](../src/gateway/governance/plugin_loader.py) refuses `CAGE_DOMAIN=physical_ai` before `assemble_governor()` runs; its contribution registers `registered_actions=()` and `consequence_magnitudes=()`, so all 9 actions claimed by its tiers (`move_effector`, `actuate_joint`, `set_velocity`, `apply_force`, `engage_manipulator`, `autonomous_navigation`, `trajectory_update`, `emergency_override`, `disable_collision_avoidance`) fail closed with `MAGNITUDE_UNDECLARED` at pre-flight and mint. When `POAM-2026-077` is remediated to make `physical_ai` runnable, its FTRA terminal registry, `registered_actions`, and `consequence_magnitudes` must be populated together (pinned by `test_physical_ai_plugin_refused_before_assembly_and_undeclared_at_consequence_boundary` in [`test_consequence_magnitude_enforcement.py`](../tests/test_consequence_magnitude_enforcement.py)).
   - **`cloudops`**: governed through the [`EstateProvider`](../src/gateway/governance/seams/estate.py) integration seam (`provider_09`), not a `cage.plugins` entry point in [`pyproject.toml`](../pyproject.toml), and contributes no `PluginContribution`.
3. **Done (hermetic & mutation verification).** [`test_consequence_magnitude.py`](../tests/test_consequence_magnitude.py) (119 unit tests) and [`test_consequence_magnitude_enforcement.py`](../tests/test_consequence_magnitude_enforcement.py) (59 end-to-end enforcement tests across flat and nested paths, top-level-only rejection, non-consumption of authority records on refusal, assembly validation, and per-plugin blast-radius pins). Sensitivity checks (`rc=1` on all 8 mutations): `CM-M1` (drop `FriaTier.evaluate` pre-flight check), `CM-M2` (drop `mint_consequence_token_finding` check), `CM-M3` (drop `ConsequenceGateway.evaluate` Step 5 check), `CM-M4` (`minor <= 0` → `minor < 0`), `CM-M5` (skip whole-minor-unit check), `CM-M6` (skip `MAX_SAFE_MINOR_UNITS` bound), `CM-M7` (allow undeclared action), and `CM-M8` (drop `FinanceCagePlugin` `consequence_magnitudes`).
4. **Done (over-the-wire partner verification & merge SHA).** [`test_provider_01_live.py`](../tests/integrations/provider_01/test_provider_01_live.py) adds `test_live_invalid_magnitude_fails_closed_whatever_partner_decides` (3 parametrized live cases: `zero`, `negative`, `fractional_minor_unit`), verified over the physical wire against the live `provider_01` staging endpoint in `ci-partner-integration.yml` run `41261195943` (2026-11-01, `11 passed, 0 not run`), and merged on `main` in `#620` (`8b462eb7`, branch head `73584932`, `2026-11-01`).

**Closure Criteria:**
1. Tests mint and consume payloads with zero, negative, non-finite and boolean magnitudes at the top level, under `params` and nested deeper, and each is refused at mint and at consume; a mutation that removes either check fails a named test. (**Done:** `tests/test_consequence_magnitude.py`, `tests/test_consequence_magnitude_enforcement.py`, mutations `CM-M1`–`CM-M8`.)
2. If the fix changes provider_01, an over-the-wire run against the partner sandbox covers a refused magnitude (AGENTS.md, Tier 1). (**Done:** `tests/integrations/provider_01/test_provider_01_live.py::test_live_invalid_magnitude_fails_closed_whatever_partner_decides` passed over the wire in `ci-partner-integration.yml` run `41261195943` on 2026-11-01, `11 passed, 0 not run`.)
3. Record the merge commit SHA and the actual verification date here. (**Done:** merged in `#620` as `8b462eb7`, branch head `73584932`, verified `2026-11-01`.)

### POAM-2026-156: Single-Use Consumption Is Not Durable at the Consequence Boundary (EU_ECB)

**Control:** NIST AC-3 (Access Enforcement), SC-24 (Fail in Known State)
**Risk Level:** Moderate
**Status:** Open — items 1 & 4 (plus the two `cbf_engine.py` `WAIT` realization defects `M8`–`M9`) remediated and merged on `main` in `#621` (`460eaeca`, branch head `55661fd5`, `2026-11-01`); item 2 open on POAM-2026-113; item 3 decided 2026-10-30 (AOF persistence with `append_fsync = ALWAYS`); its IaC is merged on `main` in `#619` (`f5aca364`, branch head `8196aad0`, `2026-11-01`), and the staging apply and latency delta are open
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-30
**Target Closure:** 2026-11-30

**Description:**
[`ConsequenceAuthorityStore.consume_once()`](../src/gateway/governance/consequence_authority_store.py) enforces single use of a partner authority record by writing `flowsignal:token:<authority_record_id>` with `SET NX EX` on the governance Valkey instance. [`ConsequenceGateway.evaluate()`](../src/gateway/governance/consequence_gateway.py) executes only if that write wins. Read at `23f48374` and checked against the live staging instance (2026-10-30), single use fails in two ways:
1. **The marker's lifetime is sized to the token but keyed on the record.** The marker expires after 90 s (`FLOWSIGNAL_CONSUMPTION_TTL_SECONDS`; no manifest overrides it).
