That is 1.5 times the ConsequenceToken's 60 s lifetime, and [`ConsequenceToken.verify()`](../src/gateway/governance/consequence_token.py) rejects an `iat` more than 5 s in the future, so a token always expires before its marker. But the marker is keyed on the token's `rec` claim, which provider_01 ([`provider.py`](../src/integrations/provider_01/provider.py)) sets to the partner's `authority_record_id` unless the payload sets `prefer_receipt_id_for_rec`; only a hermetic test sets it. The partner reports that this field equals the mandate id on every call (not verified by CAGE); if so, the marker allows one consumption per mandate per 90 s rather than one per determination. provider_01 also reads no validity or expiry from the partner's authority receipt. If a record stays valid for longer than 90 s and is issued to CAGE again, a newly minted token for it is consumed successfully after the first marker expires.
2. **The marker is not replica-acknowledged.** Valkey replication is asynchronous. `gcloud memorystore instances describe cage-valkey-gov-staging-v2` (read-only, 2026-10-30) reports one replica across two zones, `noeviction`, and persistence `DISABLED`. A failover inside a token's 60 s lifetime can promote a replica that never received the marker, allowing one replay. CBF commits on the same instance don't have this gap: they `WAIT` for a replica acknowledgement and roll back when none arrives under `CAGE_STRICT_REPLICATION` ([`cbf_engine.py`](../src/gateway/governance/safety/cbf_engine.py)).

**Remediation Plan:**
Each item is labelled by what it changes. Items 2 and 4 fail with no failover at all: a record re-issued after 90 s is consumed twice, and two payments under one mandate collide (POAM-2026-157's mutation run demonstrates the collision).
1. **Code.** Replica-acknowledge the marker as CBF commits do: after `SET NX`, `WAIT` for the configured replicas (`CAGE_REDIS_WAIT_REPLICAS`). Refuse the consumption with no `EXECUTE` when the acknowledgement doesn't arrive; the record stays consumed and nothing executes. Valkey replicates asynchronously, so "synchronous replication" is not an available control. A replica-acknowledged write (`WAIT`) is the attainable one, and CBF commits already use it ([`cbf_engine.py`](../src/gateway/governance/safety/cbf_engine.py)). The repository calls `WAIT` "synchronous replication" in several places (`CAGE_REDIS_SYNCHRONOUS_REPLICATION`, [`GKE_TEST_RUNBOOK.md`](operations/GKE_TEST_RUNBOOK.md), [`FORMAL_VERIFICATION.md`](architecture/FORMAL_VERIFICATION.md), POAM-2026-090), but `WAIT` acknowledges a replica write and does not make replication synchronous; recorded, not swept.
2. **Code, plus the partner wire contract (POAM-2026-113).** Retain the marker for the record's full validity. Carry the partner's receipt validity into the token or the store, set the marker TTL to cover both the token's expiry and the record's validity, and fail closed when the partner states no validity. This needs the partner's receipt-validity rules, so agree it within the wire-contract revision tracked in POAM-2026-113.
3. **Configuration.** Persistence for governance state. Decided 2026-10-30: AOF persistence with `append_fsync = ALWAYS` on the governance instance in every enforcing posture, staging included. Staging must match prod or it stops being a rehearsal, which is the divergence that made #591 look green. Availability confirmed 2026-10-30: the Terraform google provider's `google_memorystore_instance` accepts `NEVER`, `EVERY_SEC` and `ALWAYS` for `persistence_config.aof_config.append_fsync` (with `persistence_config.mode = "AOF"`), and Google's Memorystore for Valkey persistence overview states that AOF can persist every second or on every write. Before closure, measure the latency delta on staging for single-use marker consumption and for CBF commits, at p50 and p99, before and after, rather than asserting that write volume is low. Persistence is a backstop: three of the four fixes are code, and two of the failures need no failover.
   - **IaC, merged on `main` in `#619` (`f5aca364`, branch head `8196aad0`, `2026-11-01`).** [`memorystore_valkey`](../infra/modules/memorystore_valkey/main.tf) had no persistence setting. It now always renders `persistence_config`, as `DISABLED` or as `AOF` with an explicit `aof_config.append_fsync`, from two validated inputs (`persistence_mode`: `DISABLED` or `AOF`; `aof_append_fsync`: `NEVER`, `EVERY_SEC` or `ALWAYS`), and a precondition refuses AOF without a policy and a policy without AOF. The gcp-gke `main.tf` (49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf) names the predicate once, `local.enforcing_posture` (the prod and staging tiers, or any compliance flag), which `local.retention_locked` now reads too, and plans the governance instance with `AOF` + `ALWAYS` under it and an explicit `DISABLED` otherwise. The app instance plans an explicit `DISABLED`. [`variables.tf`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/variables.tf) described `enable_apac_mas_compliance` as activating Redis persistence, which no module set (POAM-2026-130 class); the description now says what the flag does.
   - **Replacement risk: none. The change updates the instance in place.** In the provider source (`google/services/memorystore/resource_memorystore_instance.go` at `v6.43.0`, the floor of `~> 6.43`, and at `v6.50.0`, which CI resolves with no lock file), no field under `persistence_config` is `ForceNew`, the resource's `CustomizeDiff` sets only label and project defaults, and the update sends `persistenceConfig` in its PATCH `updateMask`. An offline plan with the real provider at both versions, against a fabricated state holding `DISABLED` (fake token, network refused, no real state), planned `update` for `DISABLED` to `AOF` + `ALWAYS` and `no-op` for `DISABLED`, while a control change to the `ForceNew` cluster `mode` planned `delete, create`. Google's [manage persistence](https://cloud.google.com/memorystore/docs/valkey/manage-persistence) page documents enabling AOF on an existing instance and notes that the instance might need maintenance.
   - **Scope.** Google's [persistence overview](https://cloud.google.com/memorystore/docs/valkey/persistence-overview) calls HA the first line of defense against node failures and zonal outages, and persistence the recovery for rare events in which every node in a shard fails. AOF therefore does not close the failover replay in the description; item 1's replica acknowledgement does. The same page says AOF adds some latency in any configuration, which is why the delta is measured before closure.
   - **Evidence.** [`governance_persistence.tftest.hcl`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/tests/governance_persistence.tftest.hcl) runs in CI's `terraform-test` job with mock providers, no credentials and no state. It plans staging, staging with the NIST flag `deploy_all.sh` injects, and every committed prod profile with `AOF` + `ALWAYS`; the dev profiles that set no compliance flag with `DISABLED`; the dev tier under each compliance flag with `AOF` + `ALWAYS`; and the app instance with `DISABLED` in every one of those runs. It then plans the module alone: its default, what it renders, and its four refusals. No Lula validation covers Memorystore configuration, which Lula's Kubernetes domain cannot see; the tftest is the check. Each mutation below was applied alone and failed the named run or test (`rc=1`); `terraform test` (61 runs) and the three static IaC test files (23 tests) passed before and after.

   | ID | Mutation | Failing run or test (`rc=1`) |
   |---|---|---|
   | `AOF-M1` | Drop staging from `local.enforcing_posture` | `staging_profile_persists_governance_state_on_every_write` |
   | `AOF-M2` | `EVERY_SEC` instead of `ALWAYS` on the governance instance | the nine runs that expect `AOF` + `ALWAYS`, from `staging_profile_persists_governance_state_on_every_write` to `dev_tier_with_nist_flag_persists_governance_state_on_every_write` |
   | `AOF-M3` | Remove the module's `persistence_config` block | every target run, and `module_renders_an_explicit_disabled_by_default` |
   | `AOF-M4` | Drop prod from `local.enforcing_posture` | `prod_tier_without_compliance_flags_keeps_aof_always` |
   | `AOF-M5` | Drop the compliance flags from `local.enforcing_posture` | `eu_dev_profile_persists_governance_state_on_every_write`, `apac_dev_profile_persists_governance_state_on_every_write`, `dev_tier_with_nist_flag_persists_governance_state_on_every_write` |
   | `AOF-M6` | Governance instance `AOF` + `ALWAYS` in every posture, dev included | `dev_profile_keeps_persistence_disabled`, `us_dev_profile_keeps_persistence_disabled` |
   | `AOF-M7` | App instance `AOF` + `ALWAYS` | the ten target runs that assert the app instance's `DISABLED` |
   | `AOF-M8` | The module hard-codes `ALWAYS` instead of its input | `module_renders_aof_with_the_fsync_policy_it_is_given` |
   | `AOF-M9` | The precondition accepts AOF without a policy | `module_refuses_aof_without_a_fsync_policy` |
   | `AOF-M10` | The precondition accepts a policy without AOF | `module_refuses_a_fsync_policy_without_aof` |
   | `AOF-M11` | Remove the `persistence_mode` validation | `module_refuses_rdb` |
   | `AOF-M12` | Remove the `aof_append_fsync` validation | `module_refuses_the_gcloud_fsync_spelling` |
   | `AOF-M13` | Unanchor the noeviction gate's resource scan, so a test's `mock_resource` counts as an instance | `test_instance_scan_finds_resources_and_skips_test_mocks`, `test_memorystore_instances_set_noeviction` |
   | `AOF-M14` | Drop `noeviction` from the module (the anchored scan still sees the instance) | `test_memorystore_instances_set_noeviction` |
   | `AOF-M15` | `local.retention_locked` copies the expression instead of reading `local.enforcing_posture` | `test_worm_bucket_configured_as_system_of_record` |

   - **Open (closure criterion 3):** the staging apply and the measured p50/p99 latency delta for marker consumption and CBF commits, before and after. This branch applies nothing; both need the owner's authorization.
4. **Code.** Key the marker on the partner's per-determination receipt id by default, not on a field that may identify the mandate. Correct the store's module docstring, which calls it a Redis-Lua store although consumption is a plain `SET NX EX`.

**Closure Criteria:**
1. Tests show that a replay is refused after the marker is lost between consumption and replay (a simulated failover). They also show that a token re-issued for a still-valid record is refused after the token-sized TTL has elapsed, and that the production path, without `prefer_receipt_id_for_rec`, keys the marker per determination. A mutation that removes any of these fixes fails a named test. (**Done for items 1 & 4:** `#621`, `460eaeca`, mutations `M1`–`M10`; item 2 open on `POAM-2026-113`.)
2. An over-the-wire run against the partner sandbox exercises the revised receipt validity (AGENTS.md, Tier 1). (**Open** on `POAM-2026-113`.)
3. AOF persistence with `append_fsync = ALWAYS` is applied to the governance instance in every enforcing posture, staging included, and the measured staging latency delta (item 3) is recorded here. (**IaC merged** in `#619`, `f5aca364`; staging apply and latency delta **open**.)
4. Record the merge commit SHA and the actual verification date here. (**Recorded for `#619` (`f5aca364`) and `#621` (`460eaeca`) on `2026-11-01`; final closure SHA and staging verification date pending items 2 & 3.**)

**Remediation Progress & Mutation Verification (`fix/consume-once-durability`, `#621`, merged on `main` as `460eaeca`, branch head `55661fd5`, `2026-11-01`):**
- **Item 1 (Replica-acknowledged single-use consumption on a pinned socket):** [`ConsequenceAuthorityStore.consume_once()`](../src/gateway/governance/consequence_authority_store.py) now pins a single connection via [`redis_replication.py`](../src/gateway/infrastructure/redis_replication.py) (`pinned_connection`, `write_socket`, `acknowledged_replicas`, `ReplicationPolicy.from_env()`), captures the underlying socket after `SET NX PX` (or after `_REASSERT_IF_OURS_LUA` when a lost-reply retry in [`set_nx_outcome()`](../src/gateway/infrastructure/redis_single_use.py) confirms our own marker via `NxOutcome.APPLIED_UNCERTAIN`), and issues `WAIT` directly on that `write_socket` without passing through `redis-py`'s retrying `execute_command`. Under strict replication (`CAGE_STRICT_REPLICATION`, on by default in enforcing postures), a replica shortfall (`acked < wait_replicas`) raises `ConsumptionNotReplicatedError` **without deleting the marker** on the primary, and [`ConsequenceGateway.evaluate()`](../src/gateway/governance/consequence_gateway.py) returns `ConsequenceDecision.BLOCK` with `reason_code="CONSUMPTION_NOT_REPLICATED"`.
- **Item 4 (Per-determination receipt key & docstring correction):** [`provider_01`](../src/integrations/provider_01/provider.py) removes `prefer_receipt_id_for_rec`, unconditionally binds `ConsequenceToken.rec` to `authority_receipt.id` (or `authority_receipt_id`), and fails closed (`ValidationResult(admitted=False)`, `code="cage.authority_denial"`) when an `ALLOW` response carries no receipt id (`authority_record_id` is retained only on the audit finding, never as a fallback for `rec`). [`consequence_authority_store.py`](../src/gateway/governance/consequence_authority_store.py)'s module docstring is corrected to describe `SET NX PX` + `WAIT` (plus `_REASSERT_IF_OURS_LUA` on uncertain retry).
- **CBF `WAIT` realization defects (`POAM-2026-070`, `POAM-2026-090`):** Extracting [`redis_replication.py`](../src/gateway/infrastructure/redis_replication.py) exposed two distinct defects in [`cbf_engine.py`](../src/gateway/governance/safety/cbf_engine.py) that made its prior `WAIT` vacuous:
  1. `ControlBarrierFunction.rollback_state()` was **unconditionally broken** (not racy, needing no disconnect or adversary): it passed the pooled `target_client` (`self.redis_client`) into `_sync_to_replicas(client=target_client)` without `pinned_connection`, so `LUA_ROLLBACK_CBF` and `WAIT` could execute on different pooled connections on every call.
  2. `ControlBarrierFunction.atomic_verify_and_commit()` / `_sync_to_replicas()` had a **failover retry race**: `_sync_to_replicas()` called `target_client.execute_command("WAIT", replicas, timeout)` through the retrying client, so on a socket drop between `LUA_ATOMIC_CBF` and `WAIT` during primary failover, `redis-py`'s `Retry` policy silently reconnected on a fresh socket with zero writes and returned `>= 1` vacuously.
  Both now use `pinned_connection`, `write_socket`, and `acknowledged_replicas`.

| Mutation ID | Target & Fault Injected | Failing Test(s) | Mutated `rc` | Restored `rc` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `M1` | Skip `WAIT` in `ConsequenceAuthorityStore._consume_acknowledged()` (return `True` immediately after `SET NX PX`) | `tests/test_consequence_consumption_durability.py::TestConsumeOnceWaitReplication::test_strict_replication_blocks_when_zero_replicas_acknowledge` | `1` | `0` |
| `M2` | Pass unpinned pool client (`self._redis`) instead of `pinned_connection` in `ConsequenceAuthorityStore._consume_acknowledged()` | `tests/test_consequence_consumption_durability.py::TestUnpinnedWaitIsVacuous::test_consume_once_pins_connection_so_wait_sees_the_set_nx_write` | `1` | `0` |
| `M3` | Swallow `WAIT` shortfall under strict replication in `ConsequenceAuthorityStore._unacknowledged()` (`return False`) | `tests/test_consequence_consumption_durability.py::TestConsumeOnceWaitReplication::test_strict_replication_blocks_when_zero_replicas_acknowledge` | `1` | `0` |
| `M4` | Delete `consequence:token:<rec>` marker on `WAIT` shortfall before raising `ConsumptionNotReplicatedError` | `tests/test_consequence_consumption_durability.py::TestConsumeOnceWaitReplication::test_unreplicated_marker_remains_set_on_primary_blocking_second_attempt` | `1` | `0` |
| `M5` | Restore `or record_id` fallback when `receipt_id` is empty in `provider_01._map_provider_01_decision()` | `tests/integrations/provider_01/test_provider_01_step2_qualification.py::test_allow_without_authority_receipt_id_fails_closed_even_when_mandate_present` | `1` | `0` |
| `M6` | Re-introduce `payload.get("prefer_receipt_id_for_rec")` read in `provider_01._map_provider_01_decision()` | `tests/integrations/provider_01/test_provider_01_payload_contract.py::test_provider_01_reads_only_fria_tier_payload_keys` | `1` | `0` |
| `M7` | Replace `write_socket` `send_command`/`read_response` in `acknowledged_replicas()` with `client.execute_command("WAIT", ...)` (auto-reconnects on dropped socket) | `tests/test_consequence_consumption_durability.py::TestWaitDoesNotReconnectAfterWrite::test_consume_once_blocks_when_connection_drops_between_set_and_wait` | `1` | `0` |
| `M8` | Pass unpinned `target_client` (`self.redis_client`) instead of `pinned_connection` in `ControlBarrierFunction.rollback_state()` | `tests/test_consequence_consumption_durability.py::TestCbfEngineUsesNonRetryingPinnedWait::test_cbf_rollback_state_runs_lua_and_wait_on_one_pinned_connection` | `1` | `0` |
| `M9` | Replace `acknowledged_replicas()` in `ControlBarrierFunction._sync_to_replicas()` with `target_client.execute_command("WAIT", ...)` | `tests/test_consequence_consumption_durability.py::TestCbfEngineUsesNonRetryingPinnedWait::test_cbf_commit_does_not_resend_wait_after_connection_drop` | `1` | `0` |
| `M10` | Remove `if not correlation_id: raise ValueError(...)` from `FriaTier._payload()` in `fria_tier.py` | `tests/governor/test_jurisdiction_wiring.py::test_missing_trace_span_refuses_locally_without_calling_provider` | `1` | `0` |

### POAM-2026-157: provider_01's Step 2 Qualification Test Passes Only Under a Flag Production Never Sets (EU_ECB)

**Control:** NIST SA-11 (Developer Testing and Evaluation), CA-7 (Continuous Monitoring)
**Risk Level:** Moderate
**Status:** Open — remediated and merged on `main` in `#621` (`460eaeca`, branch head `55661fd5`, `2026-11-01`); closes on live partner wire run
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-30
**Target Closure:** 2026-11-30

**Description:**
`test_tc01_to_tc06_step2_consequence_qualification_vectors` in [`test_provider_01_step2_qualification.py`](../tests/integrations/provider_01/test_provider_01_step2_qualification.py) builds every case on a payload that sets `prefer_receipt_id_for_rec: True`. provider_01 ([`provider.py`](../src/integrations/provider_01/provider.py)) reads that flag from the request payload; when it is set, single use is keyed on the partner's `authority_receipt.id` instead of its `authority_record_id`. No production caller sets it. [`FriaTier`](../src/gateway/governance/jurisdiction/eu_ai_act/fria_tier.py) builds the payload from a fixed key set that leaves it out, and nothing else in `src/` or `scripts/` sets it (verified, grep, 2026-10-30). The test's own mock returns an `authority_record_id` equal to the mandate id, which is the behaviour the partner reports from its side. On the production path, therefore, two payments under one mandate share a marker (POAM-2026-156).

**Mutation check (2026-10-30).** The flag and the two assertions that read its effect were removed. TC-06 ("second distinct transaction under the SAME mandate_id with a fresh authority_receipt.id succeeds without false collision") then returned `BLOCK` / `AUTHORITY_RECORD_BINDING_MISMATCH` instead of `EXECUTE`. The test reported green on a path production does not take. That is the masking mechanism POAM-2026-120 records: a fixture configured around a defect rather than detecting it. The flag is also a caller-selectable branch in production code that only a test uses.

**Remediation Plan:**
1. Run the qualification cases on the payload shape production sends, as built by `FriaTier`, with no adapter flags.
2. Remove `prefer_receipt_id_for_rec` and make the per-determination key the only behaviour (POAM-2026-156, item 4).
3. Add a guard test that fails when provider_01 reads a top-level payload key that `FriaTier` never sends. Today it would flag `prefer_receipt_id_for_rec`, `approval_id` and `target`.

**Closure Criteria:**
1. TC-01 to TC-06 pass on the production payload shape with no flag, after POAM-2026-156 item 4 lands. (**Done:** `#621`, `460eaeca`.)
2. The guard test exists, and a recorded mutation that reintroduces a test-only payload key makes it fail. (**Done:** `test_provider_01_payload_contract.py`, mutation `M6`.)
3. Record the merge commit SHA (`#621`, `460eaeca`, branch head `55661fd5`, `2026-11-01`) and the actual live partner wire verification date here.

**Remediation Progress & Mutation Verification (`fix/consume-once-durability`, `#621`, merged on `main` as `460eaeca`, branch head `55661fd5`, `2026-11-01`):**
1. [`FriaTier._payload()`](../src/gateway/governance/jurisdiction/eu_ai_act/fria_tier.py) now raises `ValueError` when `_trace_id()` is empty (no active OpenTelemetry recording span), so `FriaTier.evaluate()` fails closed locally with `FRIA_PROVIDER_UNAVAILABLE` (`ViolationKind.HARD`) without calling the normative provider (`test_missing_trace_span_refuses_locally_without_calling_provider`, mutation `M10`). Both [`test_provider_01_step2_qualification.py`](../tests/integrations/provider_01/test_provider_01_step2_qualification.py) (`TC-01`–`TC-06`) and [`test_provider_01_live.py`](../tests/integrations/provider_01/test_provider_01_live.py) (`_payment()`, plus `_base_allow_payload()` in [`verify_provider_01_wire_and_hostile.py`](../scripts/verify_provider_01_wire_and_hostile.py)) construct their envelopes through [`FriaTier._payload()`](../src/gateway/governance/jurisdiction/eu_ai_act/fria_tier.py) under an active OTel span with no hand-written top-level dictionary literals or test-only adapter flags (enforced by `test_live_and_qualification_suites_build_payloads_via_fria_tier` in [`test_provider_01_payload_contract.py`](../tests/integrations/provider_01/test_provider_01_payload_contract.py)).
2. [`provider.py`](../src/integrations/provider_01/provider.py) deletes `prefer_receipt_id_for_rec`, reads `approval_id` (`params.get("approval_id") or envelope.get("correlation_id", "")`) and `target` (`params.get("target", "")`) from `envelope["params"]` rather than top-level `envelope`, and unconditionally requires `authority_receipt.id` / `authority_receipt_id` for `ConsequenceToken.rec` (tested by `test_allow_without_authority_receipt_id_fails_closed_even_when_mandate_present`, mutation `M5`).
3. [`test_provider_01_payload_contract.py`](../tests/integrations/provider_01/test_provider_01_payload_contract.py) walks the AST of `src/integrations/provider_01/` alongside `FriaTier._payload()` and fails whenever `provider_01` reads a top-level payload key that `FriaTier._payload()` never emits (verified by mutation `M6` in the `POAM-2026-156` table above: `mutated rc=1`, `restored rc=0`, `2026-10-31`).

### POAM-2026-158: The Staging Advisor Deployment Drifted Out of Terraform Ownership

**Control:** NIST CM-2 (Baseline Configuration), CM-3 (Configuration Change Control)
**Risk Level:** Moderate
**Status:** Closed (2026-10-31) — read-only `terraform plan` and ReplicaSet forensics completed `2026-10-31`; unmanaged `deployment.apps/governed-financial-advisor` and `service/governed-financial-advisor` deleted on `cage-staging` and verified with zero post-apply workload drift
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-30
**Target Closure:** Before the next staging apply
**Date Closed:** 2026-10-31

**Description:**
The `governed-financial-advisor` Deployment on the staging cluster drifted out of Terraform ownership on 2026-10-25 (reported by the maintainer).

**Forensic Timeline & Mechanism (verified on `cage-staging`, `2026-10-31`):**
1. **Terraform state inspection (`gs://<project>-tfstate`, `prefix=cage/gcp-gke/cage-staging`, and `backups/cage-gcp-gke/default.tfstate.pre-migration-backup`):** Neither the active `cage-staging` state nor the pre-migration backup ever tracked `module.governed_advisor`. Prior to `#581` (`0a65f2e2`), Terraform state tracked only `module.gcp_geap_advisor[0].terraform_data.geap_reasoning_engine`.
2. **Out-of-band creation (`2026-10-13T12:32:46Z`) and mutation (`2026-10-25`):** Inspecting `deployment.apps/governed-financial-advisor` (`uid: c173be50-a7d5-4ce4-b785-f578516188ab`) and its ReplicaSets in `governance-stack` on `cage-staging` showed that the Deployment and `service/governed-financial-advisor` were created out-of-band on `2026-10-13T12:32:46Z` (Revision 1, `governed-financial-advisor-df6678886`) via `kubectl apply` of rendered `de5534ac:deployment/k8s/backend-deployment.yaml.tpl` (`image: gcr.io/<project>/governed-financial-advisor@sha256:03934c2b89a9b97727652eb00aa3a01dc679be1c7ab4f041825343f70a67bd4d`), and were mutated out-of-band on `2026-10-25` via `kubectl rollout restart` (`2026-10-25T02:18:43Z`, Revision 7, `6ffbd5569`), `kubectl set image` (`2026-10-25T02:44:44Z`, Revision 8, `565bd8fdb7`, `image: gcr.io/<project>/governed-financial-advisor@sha256:57743807de9a10bc86274238d0d08a49c9646d574c90377d5630a86dd5d581cf`), and `kubectl rollout restart` (`2026-10-25T13:16:14Z`, Revision 9, `5fd5b688f5`).

`main.tf` (49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf) sets `advisor_enabled = local.vllm_enabled`, and the staging profile ([`staging.tfvars`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/staging.tfvars)) sets `inference_backend = "none"`, so neither advisor module, `governed_advisor` or `gcp_geap_advisor`, is in the staging configuration (read at `34709d55`, 2026-10-30). Because Terraform state did not track `deployment.apps/governed-financial-advisor`, an apply would have left the unmanaged Deployment and Service running outside Terraform.

**Resolution & Verification (`2026-10-31`):**
1. Ran a read-only `terraform plan -lock=false -no-color -var=enable_nist_compliance=true -var-file=staging.tfvars` directly in `infra/targets/gcp-gke/` against `gs://<project>-tfstate` (`prefix=cage/gcp-gke/cage-staging`) with `image_digests` pinned to the live cluster images (`Plan: 7 to add, 12 to change, 4 to destroy`), confirming zero `governed_advisor` resources in state or plan.
2. Deleted the unmanaged `deployment.apps/governed-financial-advisor` and `service/governed-financial-advisor` in `governance-stack` on `cage-staging` (`kubectl delete deployment governed-financial-advisor -n governance-stack` and `kubectl delete svc governed-financial-advisor -n governance-stack`).
3. Applied `3afda5b7` via `./deploy_all.sh --target gcp-gke --env staging --skip-build --auto-approve` (`Apply complete! Resources: 7 added, 15 changed, 4 destroyed`) and verified via a post-apply read-only `terraform plan` that zero workload drift remains.

**Closure Criteria:**
1. ✅ **Met (`2026-10-31`).** Read-only pre-apply and post-apply `terraform plan` runs show zero advisor or workload drift, and the unmanaged out-of-band Deployment and Service are removed from `cage-staging`.
2. ✅ **Met (`2026-10-31`).** Plan outcome, ReplicaSet forensic timeline, and closure date (`2026-10-31`) recorded here.

### POAM-2026-159: Staging Cannot Be Torn Down: the Langfuse Event Bucket Has No Staging Retention Carve-Out

**Control:** NIST AU-11 (Audit Record Retention), CM-6 (Configuration Settings)
**Risk Level:** Moderate
**Status:** Open — option (a) decided 2026-10-30; implemented and merged on `main` in #600 (`faa6aec4`, branch head `441c64e0`, `2026-10-31`), applied live on `cage-staging` (`2026-10-31`), and wired into `--fresh-project` teardown verification in #605 (`b66c7344`) and #606 (`d599fc04`); closing on walk-project teardown verification
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-30
**Target Closure:** 2026-11-30

**Description:**
`google_storage_bucket.langfuse_events` in `main.tf` (49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf) sets a 220752000 s (2555-day) retention period with `is_locked = local.any_compliance_active ? true : false`. `any_compliance_active` (since removed under `POAM-2026-130`) was true for staging under US_FED, because [`deploy_all.sh`](../deploy_all.sh) injected `enable_nist_compliance=true`. The WORM evidence bucket in the same file has a staging carve-out (`retention_period_seconds = var.environment == "staging" ? 86400 : 220752000`); the Langfuse event bucket does not (read at `34709d55`, 2026-10-30). The code disagrees with itself about the bucket: its IAM comment in `main.tf` calls it a "transient Langfuse S3 event queue bucket", yet it carries a 2555-day lock.

**Live staging project (verified read-only, 2026-10-30).** The bucket `langfuse-events-<project>-<suffix>` has a LOCKED retention policy of 220752000 s, effective 2026-09-29, and holds 35,639 objects. The project carries a `storage.googleapis.com` deletion lien ("A lien is put on project deletion because one or more Cloud Storage resources were put under retention"). Per Google's [bucket-lock documentation](https://cloud.google.com/storage/docs/bucket-lock), a locked bucket cannot be deleted until every object in it has met its retention period, and locking a retention policy places a lien that blocks project deletion until a project owner or organization administrator removes it. Retention runs per object from its creation time, so the bucket cannot be deleted before 2033-09-27 at the earliest (2026-09-29 plus 2555 days), and later for every object written after the lock took effect.

**The documented lifecycle says the opposite.** [`DEPLOYMENT_RULES.md`](operations/DEPLOYMENT_RULES.md)'s staging section describes an ephemeral tier: Provision → Validate → Destroy, ~$2-4 per validation cycle, deletion protection disabled to allow teardown. The [`staging.tfvars`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/staging.tfvars) header reads "Lifecycle: Ephemeral — provision, validate with Lula gates, destroy." This entry records the contradiction and changes neither document.

Every run of the staging posture in a new project repeats this. The apply locks the policy, which places the lien, and from the first Langfuse event the bucket cannot be deleted for 2555 days. That decides whether anyone can afford a clean-project walk twice.

**Decision (2026-10-30):** option (a), a posture-gated staging carve-out for the Langfuse event bucket, rather than option (b), which kept the seven-year lock and corrected the staging lifecycle claims. The maintainer's rationale: "A seven-year retention lock on a throwaway walk project is a category error — retention exists for production evidence, not test scaffolding — and (b) makes the walk unrepeatable, which defeats it, since the whole point is walk, fix, walk again." Constraints: the carve-out is posture-gated and cannot reach prod, and the implementation copies the mechanism of the WORM evidence bucket's staging carve-out, not its value.

**Remediation Plan (implemented and merged on `main` in #600, `faa6aec4`, branch head `441c64e0`, `2026-10-31`, and applied live on `cage-staging` on `2026-10-31`):**
1. **Done (#600, `faa6aec4`).** Both `module.worm_bucket` and `google_storage_bucket.langfuse_event_upload` share `local.retention_period_seconds = var.environment == "staging" ? 86400 : 220752000` and `local.retention_locked = var.environment == "prod" || var.environment == "staging" || local.any_compliance_active` in `main.tf` (49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf), locking staging for 86,400 s (1 day) and failing closed to 2555 days on every non-staging tier (`dev`, `prod`).
2. **Done (#600, `faa6aec4`).** Added Terraform tests in [`retention_posture.tftest.hcl`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/tests/retention_posture.tftest.hcl) (`staging_profile_locks_both_buckets_for_one_day`, `prod_profile_keeps_2555_days_locked`, `eu_prod_profile_keeps_2555_days_locked`, `apac_prod_profile_keeps_2555_days_locked`, `unknown_tier_is_refused`, `near_miss_staging_spelling_is_refused`) proving that every committed prod profile gets 2555 days locked and that any non-`staging` posture fails closed.
3. **Done (#600, `faa6aec4`; applied live on `cage-staging`, `2026-10-31`).** Replaced `google_storage_bucket.langfuse_events` (`langfuse-events-*`) with `google_storage_bucket.langfuse_event_upload` (`langfuse-event-upload-*`) and a `removed { from = google_storage_bucket.langfuse_events lifecycle { destroy = false } }` block so existing staging state detaches cleanly from the 2033-locked bucket, and documented the orphan bucket in [`DEPLOYMENT_RULES.md`](operations/DEPLOYMENT_RULES.md). On `2026-10-31`, the `3afda5b7` staging apply detached `gs://langfuse-events-<project>-fe82cebc` without destroying it and provisioned `gs://langfuse-event-upload-<project>-fe82cebc` with `retentionPeriod: "86400"` and `isLocked: true` (`effectiveTime: "2026-10-31T13:54:46.223000+00:00"`).
4. **Done (#600, `faa6aec4`).** Updated the IAM comment in `main.tf` (49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf) to reflect the posture-gated retention policy.
5. **Done (#605, `b66c7344`; #606, `d599fc04`).** [`staging_lifecycle.sh`](../scripts/staging_lifecycle.sh) `--fresh-project` mode detaches persistent KMS keys prior to `terraform destroy` and verifies post-destroy survivor cleanliness (zero surviving GKE clusters, Cloud SQL instances, workload GCS buckets, or project deletion liens).

**Closure Criteria:**
1. ✅ Merge commit SHAs `faa6aec4` (#600, branch head `441c64e0`), `b66c7344` (#605, branch head `53137452`), and `d599fc04` (#606, branch head `171acc87`) recorded here, and live `cage-staging` rollout verified on `2026-10-31` (`retentionPeriod: "86400"`, `isLocked: true`).
2. A walk project's teardown completes after the short lock expires: the destroy succeeds and no storage lien remains. Merging proves the gate exists, not that a teardown works.
3. Record the actual verification date here.

### POAM-2026-160: The Required Secret Scan Has No Detection Rules

**Control:** NIST IA-5 (Authenticator Management), SA-11 (Developer Testing and Evaluation), CA-7 (Continuous Monitoring)
**Risk Level:** Moderate (lowered from High on 2026-10-30 after the full-history scan below came back with 0 high-entropy secrets)
**Status:** Open — full-history scan completed 2026-10-30; remediated and merged on `main` in #602 (`319c702d`, branch head `867fdb8b`, `2026-10-30`)
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-30
**Target Closure:** 2026-11-15

**Description:**
[`.gitleaks.toml`](../.gitleaks.toml), added on 2026-08-28 in `9a7fabe7` and never changed since, holds only an `[allowlist]`: it has no `[[rules]]` and no `[extend] useDefault = true`. The required check "Secret Scanning (Gitleaks)" in [`security-scan.yml`](../.github/workflows/security-scan.yml) runs `gitleaks detect --source . --log-opts "HEAD~1..HEAD" --redact --verbose --exit-code 1` with gitleaks 8.27.2 and no `--config` (verified 2026-10-30). The job's own comment says it "Scans full commit history", but `--log-opts "HEAD~1..HEAD"` limits each run to the commits between `HEAD` and its first parent: a pull request's commits, or the latest commit on `main` for pushes and the nightly schedule. So the job never scans the full history, with rules or without.

gitleaks' v8.27.2 README gives the configuration precedence as `--config`, then `GITLEAKS_CONFIG`, then `GITLEAKS_CONFIG_TOML`, then `(target path)/.gitleaks.toml`, and says: "If none of the four options are used, then gitleaks will use the default config." The built-in rules are inherited only through `[extend] useDefault = true`. The job sets none of the first three, so gitleaks loads the repository's `.gitleaks.toml`: since 2026-08-28 the scan has loaded a configuration with zero rules, and it cannot report a finding.

Since 2026-10-27 it has been one of the eight required status checks in ruleset 25966171, which POAM-2026-115 cites as the automated change control that remains while human review is uncompensated. This is the class POAM-2026-150 records, CI jobs that cannot fail, but in a required check.

**Full-history scan (2026-10-30).** gitleaks 8.27.2, the CI version, ran in private Cloud Build `728dd9a5-5dc8-4045-bdbc-3a1de39975ed`. Its `linux_x64` tarball's SHA-256 matched both the release checksums file and the GitHub release-asset digest. The configuration held only `[extend] useDefault = true`, passed with `--config`; `GITLEAKS_CONFIG` and `GITLEAKS_CONFIG_TOML` were unset and output was redacted. It scanned every ref a mirror clone fetches: **591 refs** (11 branches, 11 tags, 562 pull-request heads, 7 pull-request merge refs) and **2,172 commits**. Cloud Build `2449773f-7f4d-4e7d-9062-4c71787c2fcb` then scanned the **241 commits** reachable only through **176 SHAs** that force-pushes removed from refs (GitHub still serves those SHAs; 0 unfetchable). In each build, the scan command first detected two runtime-generated fake tokens that had been committed and then deleted in a throwaway repository; the same canary produced zero findings with the repository's `.gitleaks.toml` loaded as CI loaded it, and zero with CI's `HEAD~1..HEAD` range, reproducing both defects above.
- **Ref pass (`728dd9a5-5dc8-4045-bdbc-3a1de39975ed`):** **89 findings** — **30 test fixtures or fakes**, **57 false positives** (UUIDs, SHA-256 digests, KMS resource paths), and **2 potentially real**. The 2 are a single value, the hardcoded default Langfuse init-user password (rule `hashicorp-tf-password`) in commits `1902c92c` and pre-rewrite `e608f3ac` at `main.tf` (49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf) line 411 (`langfuse_init_user_email = "dev-admin@local.com"`), removed from `main.tf` in `e5376b58` (#477, `2026-10-13`, PR commit `b3fd2353`), allowlisted by those two historical commit SHAs in [`.gitleaks.toml`](../.gitleaks.toml) on #602 (`319c702d`) so active Terraform files under `infra/` remain fully scanned, and tracked in POAM-2026-162 (live credential rotated 2026-10-30; historical exposure on `main` reclassified as an accepted risk 2026-11-03).
- **Orphan pass (`2449773f-7f4d-4e7d-9062-4c71787c2fcb`):** **17 findings** — **3 fakes**, **14 false positives**, and **none potentially real** (no full `pk-lf-`/`sk-lf-` key in any orphan commit; 3 orphan merges adding 9 lines produced 0 findings).
- **Additional passes:** Passes with decoding and archive extraction enabled, and over merge-commit conflict resolutions, found nothing further. **0 active secrets at HEAD.**
- **Residual coverage limits:** 4 force-push events (PRs #14, #18, #19, #20) have no retrievable before-commit, and the GitHub Events API window reaches back to `2026-10-25`.

**Remediation Plan (implemented and merged on `main` in #602, `319c702d`, branch head `867fdb8b`):**
1. **Done.** Added `[extend] useDefault = true` and CAGE-specific rules (`cage-langfuse-key`, `cage-huggingface-token`, `cage-google-credential`, `cage-aws-access-key-id`, `cage-private-key`) to [`.gitleaks.toml`](../.gitleaks.toml).
2. **Done.** Updated [`security-scan.yml`](../.github/workflows/security-scan.yml) to scan the full commit history (`--no-banner --redact --verbose --exit-code 1`) and added `test_gate_gitleaks_scans_full_history_and_catches_planted_secret_patterns` in [`test_ci_gate_effectiveness.py`](../tests/test_ci_gate_effectiveness.py) (`#602`, `319c702d`), which verifies the workflow command and all 5 CAGE regex rules against planted credentials (`pk-lf-*`, `sk-lf-*`, `hf_*`, `AIza*`, `AKIA*`, PEM private key).
3. **Done (2026-10-30).** Full-history and orphan scans recorded above; live staging Langfuse password rotated and verified under POAM-2026-162.
4. **Done (2026-10-30).** POAM-2026-115's detail section notes that its eight required checks included this one until `319c702d` (#602) merged on `2026-10-30`.

**Closure Criteria:**
1. The meta-check exists, and a recorded run shows it failing when a secret is planted (`test_gate_gitleaks_scans_full_history_and_catches_planted_secret_patterns` in #602, `319c702d`).
2. The full-history scan result is recorded here (done above).
3. Merge commit SHA `319c702d` (#602) verified on `2026-10-30`.

### POAM-2026-161: The pip-audit Gate Carries Four Dead Suppressions, One Unexplained

**Control:** NIST RA-5 (Vulnerability Monitoring and Scanning), SI-2 (Flaw Remediation), CA-7 (Continuous Monitoring)
**Risk Level:** Low
**Status:** Open — `security-scan.yml` flags removed and guard test landed in #599 (`4970e187`, branch head `7faee848`, `2026-10-30`); `dependency-review.yml` flags removed and `pyasn1` bumped to `0.6.5` in #604 (`260c4189`, branch head `26a7231e`, `2026-10-31`)
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-30
**Target Closure:** 2026-11-15

**Description:**
Tracked at the maintainer's request (2026-10-30), who calls it "the POAM-120 pattern in a security gate: a control configured around a finding rather than detecting it. An unexplained suppression and an explained one are different artifacts, and only one of them survives review." POAM-2026-120 records fixtures configured around a defect rather than detecting it; here the configuration is a security gate's suppression list (see also POAM-2026-163 for the Bandit `exclude_dirs` instance of the same pattern).

The pip-audit step in [`security-scan.yml`](../.github/workflows/security-scan.yml) passed four `--ignore-vuln` flags, and all four were dead at `34709d55` (verified 2026-10-30):
1. **GHSA-rg7c-g689-fr3x (`google-adk`, not `diskcache`).** It is an alias of `google-adk`'s `CVE-2026-4810` (affects `1.7.0` to before `1.28.1`, and `2.0.0a1`). `82dc4847` (2026-06-06) relabelled its comment as `diskcache`'s `CVE-2025-69872`, whose advisory is `GHSA-w8v5-vhqr-4h9v`, and `d72034ab` (2026-06-08) removed `--ignore-vuln CVE-2026-4810` as resolved ("upgraded google-adk to 2.2.0") but kept this GHSA alias of it. `uv.lock` pins `google-adk` `2.3.0`, which is unaffected, so the flag outlived its finding under a comment that names the wrong package.
2. **PYSEC-2026-139 and GHSA-rrmf-rvhw-rf47 (both torch).** The second is CVE-2025-3000, memory corruption in `torch.jit.script`, which OSV lists as fixed in 2.13.0. torch left `uv.lock` in `7ab1acd5` (#224, 2026-09-19). The PYSEC-2026-139 comment still calls torch 2.10.0 "a dev/inference-only dependency", which has been false since then.
3. **PYSEC-2026-3721 (pip).** OSV lists it as fixed in 26.2. CI's audit venv installs pip 26.2 (run 41087195977), the version `uv.lock` pins, but the comment names "pip 26.1.2".

**GHSA-rrmf-rvhw-rf47 is the unexplained one.** `3256100a` (2026-06-13) added it with a five-line comment ending "Suppressed under the same POAM-016 tracking item". `e429b230` (#90, 2026-08-22) deleted that comment and kept the flag. POAM-2026-016's row names only PYSEC-2026-139, never this alias. POAM-2026-016 itself (torch PYSEC-2026-139, Low, open, target 2026-12-31) has rested on a false premise since `7ab1acd5`.

The next step, "Scan compliance extras", ran `uv run pip-audit … || true`. It could not fail, the class POAM-2026-150 records for CI jobs whose test outcomes cannot fail the build, and it scanned the same venv as the first step.

**Risk.** Low: no suppression hid a live finding at `34709d55`. Each flag would silently mask its CVE if the package returned, and torch's GHSA-rrmf-rvhw-rf47 is a critical memory-corruption CVE.

**Remediation Plan & Post-Merge Findings (2026-10-30 / 2026-10-31):**
1. **Done in `security-scan.yml` (#599, `4970e187`, branch head `7faee848`).** Deleted all four flags and their comments from [`security-scan.yml`](../.github/workflows/security-scan.yml) and deleted the duplicate `|| true` step.
2. **Done (#599, `4970e187`).** Added [`test_workflow_vuln_suppressions.py`](../tests/test_workflow_vuln_suppressions.py) (`test_every_workflow_suppression_names_its_reason`), which fails if any `--ignore-vuln` across `.github/workflows/*.yml` has no adjacent comment naming that exact ID.
3. **Cross-PR semantic merge skew caught by `test_workflow_vuln_suppressions.py` (`2026-10-30`, run `41143961049`) and fixed in #604 (`260c4189`, `2026-10-31`).** `#602` (`319c702d`, merged 53 seconds after `#599` `4970e187` without rebasing onto `#599`) replaced the GitHub-Enterprise-only `dependency-review-action@v4` in [`dependency-review.yml`](../.github/workflows/dependency-review.yml) (`260c4189^:.github/workflows/dependency-review.yml` lines 66–70; line 66 at HEAD) with a `pip-audit` command copied from the pre-`#599` `security-scan.yml` carrying the four dead `--ignore-vuln` flags without comments. Because `#599` and `#602` touched disjoint lines, both reported `MERGEABLE` and `8/8 PASS` on their pre-merge branch heads, and `#599`'s new `test_every_workflow_suppression_names_its_reason` immediately caught the four uncommented flags on `main` at `43528275`. `#604` (`260c4189`, branch head `26a7231e`, `2026-10-31`) removed all four `--ignore-vuln` flags from [`dependency-review.yml`](../.github/workflows/dependency-review.yml).
4. **Live catch by the un-suppressed `pip-audit` gate (`2026-10-31 02:12Z`, run `41158877040`) and fixed in #604 (`260c4189`, `2026-10-31`).** Nightly Security Scanning failed on step `Run pip-audit` with `"Found 4 known vulnerabilities in 1 package"` for `pyasn1 0.6.4` (`CVE-2026-119194`–`CVE-2026-119197`, fixed in `0.6.5`). `#604` (`260c4189`, `2026-10-31`) bumped `pyasn1>=0.6.5` in [`pyproject.toml`](../pyproject.toml) and `uv.lock` (`0.6.5`).
5. **Done.** Closed POAM-2026-016, citing `7ab1acd5`, and closed `POAM-017` in [`POAM_US_FED.md`](compliance/us_fed/POAM_US_FED.md) (`4970e187`).

**Closure Criteria:**
1. Merge commit SHA `4970e187` (#599, `2026-10-30`) and `260c4189` (#604, `2026-10-31`) recorded here, removing the four flags from both [`security-scan.yml`](../.github/workflows/security-scan.yml) and [`dependency-review.yml`](../.github/workflows/dependency-review.yml) and bumping `pyasn1` to `>=0.6.5`.
2. A recorded mutation (and live `main` run `41143961049`) shows that an uncommented `--ignore-vuln` fails [`test_workflow_vuln_suppressions.py`](../tests/test_workflow_vuln_suppressions.py).
3. Green `pip-audit` run with zero suppressions across both [`security-scan.yml`](../.github/workflows/security-scan.yml) and [`dependency-review.yml`](../.github/workflows/dependency-review.yml) verified on #604 (`260c4189`, `2026-10-31`).

### POAM-2026-162: Pre-Release Internal Documentation and Rotated Langfuse Initial Password in `main` History

**Control:** NIST CM-3 (Configuration Change Control), SI-12 (Information Management and Retention); IA-5 (Authenticator Management), IA-5(7) (No Embedded Unencrypted Static Authenticators) and CM-6 (Configuration Settings). Live `cage-staging` Langfuse password rotation scope under IA-5, IA-5(7), and CM-6 was resolved on 2026-10-30; see *Resolved scope* below.
**Risk Level:** Low (lowered from High after live `cage-staging` rotation on 2026-10-30 and credential triage of the 14 removed files on 2026-11-03)
**Status:** Accepted risk (2026-11-03). Credential triage on 2026-11-03 confirmed no live credentials across the 14 removed files, and the live `cage-staging` Langfuse password was rotated on 2026-10-30; the historical commits remain reachable in `main`'s own ancestry
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-30 (live credential scope closed 2026-10-30; pre-release history exposure corrected and reclassified as an accepted risk 2026-11-03)
**Closes when:** the public repository is archived or replaced by a fresh repository rooted at a clean tree, and the closure criteria below are met. There is no target date: the item closes when the pre-`fc5592b0` ancestry is no longer served by the canonical public repository, not when any PR-ref cleanup work is done

**Description:**
1. **Reachability in `main`'s own ancestry, not only PR refs (verified 2026-11-03).**
   Earlier statements and triage attributed pre-release history exposure to the pre-rewrite line (`e608f3ac`) referenced by pull-request heads `#1`–`#15`. That was wrong. Verified on `2026-11-03`:
   ```bash
   git merge-base --is-ancestor fc5592b0 origin/main                        # exits 0
   git diff-tree --no-commit-id --name-status -r --diff-filter=D fc5592b0   # 14 files
   ```
   (`git show --name-status --diff-filter=D fc5592b0 | grep '^D'` matches those 14 `D\t<path>` lines plus the commit header's `Date:` line.) Commit `fc5592b0` (`2026-07-08`, `"chore(docs): remove internal files and scrub sensitive references for OSS release"`) is an ancestor of `origin/main` (the first of the four `2026-07-08` public-release commits ending at `a7c13cca` on the `1902c92c` line; see `POAM-2026-170`), so the **14** files it removed are in `main`'s own ancestry (`1902c92c..4134c860`, where `4134c860` is `fc5592b0^`) and present in every clone of the public repository. The pull-request refs (`refs/pull/1/head`–`refs/pull/15/head`) are not the exposure path and never were.
2. **Count and class of the removed content (`14` files).**
   The **14** files removed by `fc5592b0` are **internal planning, readiness and security-audit documentation** from the pre-release development phase (`2026-06-01` to `2026-07-08`). Their paths are intentionally not enumerated here (they are discoverable with the single `git` command above by anyone who needs them; this POA&M is not an index).
3. **Credential triage result (verified 2026-11-03): no live credentials.**
   Triage of all 14 files on `2026-11-03` (together with the full-history gitleaks 8.27.2 scan recorded in `POAM-2026-160`, Cloud Build `728dd9a5-5dc8-4045-bdbc-3a1de39975ed`, `2026-10-30`, across all 591 refs / 2,172 reachable commits) confirmed **no live credentials**. The only literal credential-shaped values anywhere in the 14 files are two 8-hex Langfuse key prefixes in one file (`ed9d4c02` on `main`, `42f3594a` on `#1`–`#15`, matched by gitleaks rule `curl-auth-user`), which were already elided in the source (`-...`) when committed and rotated under that same document's `D-01` (`2fdc67d9`, `2026-06-05`).
4. **Historical Langfuse init-user password in `1902c92c:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf` (line 411; lines 886–887 at `HEAD`).**
   The one-time full-history scan in `POAM-2026-160` (rule `hashicorp-tf-password`) found the hardcoded default Langfuse init-user password in `infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf` (49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf) (`1902c92c:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf` line 411, below `langfuse_init_user_email = "dev-admin@local.com"` at line 410; lines 886–887 at `HEAD`), passed directly as an argument of `module.langfuse`. It was added in `1902c92c` (`"release: v2.0.0-dev.1"`, `2026-06-02`), and also in `e608f3ac` on `#1`–`#15`. `e5376b58` (`#477`, `2026-10-13`; PR commit `b3fd2353`, `fix(infra): remediate secret hygiene, manifest, and doc findings`) replaced the literal with `var.langfuse_init_user_password` (`default = ""`, [`infra/targets/gcp-gke/variables.tf`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/variables.tf) line 751) and moved Langfuse init credentials into `secret_key_ref` on `kubernetes_secret.langfuse_secrets` (`init-user-password`, [`infra/modules/langfuse_stack/main.tf`](../infra/modules/langfuse_stack/main.tf) lines 60–73). `main` `HEAD` no longer contains it, while the historical literal remains in `1902c92c..e5376b58^`, **7 release tags** (`client-v0.1.0`, `client-v0.2.0`, `v2.0.0`, `v2.1.0`, `v2.1.1`, `v3.0.0`, `v3.0.1`), and **451 closed or merged pull-request heads** (`#1`–`#476`). Its live counterpart in `cage-staging` was rotated on `2026-10-30` (*Resolved scope* below).

**Why an accepted risk (2026-11-03):**
Deleting the pull-request refs (`refs/pull/1/head`–`refs/pull/15/head`) — previously the planned remediation when pre-release history exposure was misattributed to `#1`–`#15` — achieves nothing because `fc5592b0` and its ancestor commits (`1902c92c..4134c860`) are ancestors of `origin/main` itself and are present in every clone of the public repository regardless of whether those pull-request refs exist. Meanwhile, `main` is protected against force pushes by ruleset `26377312` (`POAM-2026-170`), release tags `v*` and `client-v*` are protected by ruleset `25965917` (`POAM-2026-116`), and Gate G9 ([`check_doc_references.py`](../scripts/check_doc_references.py)), `CHANGELOG.md`, OSCAL evidence statements, and this POA&M pin commit SHAs across `main`'s ancestry. No history rewrite is planned. Because no remediation work short of archiving or replacing the public repository removes `1902c92c..4134c860` from `main`'s ancestry, and because `2026-11-03` triage confirmed zero live credentials in the 14 removed files while the live `cage-staging` Langfuse password was rotated on `2026-10-30`, this item is an accepted risk rather than an open finding with a target date or a closed finding.

**Compensating controls (verified 2026-11-03):**
1. **Full-history credential triage and automated secret scanning (`POAM-2026-160`).** Triage on `2026-11-03` of all 14 files removed by `fc5592b0` and the full-history gitleaks 8.27.2 scan on `2026-10-30` (Cloud Build `728dd9a5-5dc8-4045-bdbc-3a1de39975ed` across 591 refs / 2,172 commits and `2449773f-7f4d-4e7d-9062-4c71787c2fcb` across 241 force-push orphan commits, run with `[extend] useDefault = true` and no path exclusions) confirmed no live credentials in the 14 files (only the two source-elided `curl-auth-user` Langfuse key prefixes rotated under `D-01` in `2fdc67d9` on `2026-06-05`). Since `#602` (`319c702d`, `2026-10-30`), [`security-scan.yml`](../.github/workflows/security-scan.yml) scans the full commit history on every pull request and push to `main` with `[extend] useDefault = true` and the five CAGE custom rules in [`.gitleaks.toml`](../.gitleaks.toml) (note: `.gitleaks.toml` lines 56–63 retain path exclusions `^docs/`, `^plans/`, `^tests/`, `^contrib/.*/tests/`, and `\.env\.example$` from `9a7fabe7`, so `docs/` and `plans/` were covered by the un-allowlisted Cloud Build scan rather than the per-PR gitleaks config).
2. **Live credential rotation (`2026-10-30`).** The hardcoded default Langfuse init-user password in `infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf` (49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf) (`1902c92c:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf` line 411; lines 886–887 at `HEAD`) was removed at `HEAD` in `e5376b58` (`#477`, `2026-10-13`) and rotated in both Cloud SQL Postgres (`users.password` for `dev-admin@local.com`) and Kubernetes (`langfuse-secrets`) in `cage-staging` on `2026-10-30` (*Resolved scope* below).
3. **Pre-commit and CI hygiene gates.** [`check_project_id_hygiene.py`](../scripts/check_project_id_hygiene.py) and the full-history gitleaks required check in [`security-scan.yml`](../.github/workflows/security-scan.yml) prevent new internal identifiers or credentials from entering `HEAD`.

**Residual risk accepted:**
- **Public reachability of the 14 pre-release documents in `main` (`1902c92c..4134c860`).** Anyone who clones the public repository or reads `fc5592b0^` (`4134c860`) on GitHub can inspect the 14 internal planning, readiness and security-audit documents and the two source-elided 8-hex Langfuse key prefixes (`ed9d4c02` on `main`, `42f3594a` on `#1`–`#15`). Neither prefix is a complete or live credential. GitHub publishes no fetch or clone logs for public repositories, so nothing can be asserted about whether the historical commits were ever fetched.
- **Public reachability of the rotated `1902c92c:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf` initial password string.** The revoked initial password literal remains visible in `1902c92c..e5376b58^`, the 7 release tags above, and PR heads `#1`–`#476`, allowlisted by commit SHA (`1902c92c`, `e608f3ac`) in [`.gitleaks.toml`](../.gitleaks.toml) (lines 51–54).

**Resolved scope (live `cage-staging` Langfuse init-user password rotation under IA-5, IA-5(7), CM-6 — 2026-10-30):**
- **Control mapping:**
  - **IA-5(7), primary:** No unencrypted static authenticators may be embedded in applications or static storage.
  - **IA-5, primary:** Default authenticators must be changed before first use (IA-5e), protected from disclosure (IA-5g), and rotated upon publication (IA-5f).
  - **CM-6, secondary:** The deploy target hard-wired a security-relevant setting with no override prior to `e5376b58`. (`SC-28` is not cited because encryption at rest does not address authenticator disclosure in source history.)
1. **Repository & local-dev scope check (verified 2026-10-30).** [`local-dev.env.example`](../config/environments/local-dev.env.example), [`docker-compose.yml`](../docker-compose.yml), and [`QUICK_START.md`](../infra/QUICK_START.md) were checked and never contained the hardcoded password literal.
2. **The rotation that didn't rotate anything — three false-assurance records (verified 2026-10-30):**
   Because `LANGFUSE_INIT_USER_PASSWORD` only seeds the admin user on initial creation (`users` table insert) and never overwrites an existing `users.password` bcrypt hash on pod restart, updating `langfuse-secrets` in Kubernetes left the live credential in Cloud SQL Postgres untouched for two months behind a secret that looked updated:
   - **`POAM-2026-071` (`2026-09-05`, commit `75fe0ac4` / PR #148):** Closed `POAM-2026-071` on `2026-09-05` claiming *"Sensitive credentials in local Terraform state backups (`infra/targets/gcp-gke/*.tfstate*`) identified during vendor decoupling audit. Remediated by rotating exposed credentials..."* while `langfuse_init_user_password` remained hardcoded in `main.tf` (49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf) until `2026-10-13` and the live `dev-admin@local.com` password in Cloud SQL Postgres remained the initial password. That makes `POAM-2026-071` the second false closure uncovered on `2026-10-30`, after `POAM-2026-149`.
   - **Commit `e5376b58` (`2026-10-13`, PR #477, sub-commit `b3fd2353`):** Removed the literal from `main.tf` (49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf) and moved `init-user-password` into `kubernetes_secret.langfuse_secrets`. Updating the Kubernetes secret gave the appearance that the credential was cleared/rotated, while `users.password` in Cloud SQL Postgres sat unchanged until `2026-10-30`.
   - **[`deployment/update_langfuse_secret.py`](../deployment/update_langfuse_secret.py) (lines 229–296):** When `langfuse-secrets` already exists, the script patches `obj["data"]["init-user-password"]` in Kubernetes and runs `kubectl rollout restart deployment/langfuse-web -n governance-stack` with the comment `# Force a rolling restart so langfuse-web picks up the new secret.` — codifying the misconception that restarting `langfuse-web` after patching `langfuse-secrets` rotates the admin user's password (updated in #602, merged on `main` at `319c702d`, to print an explicit warning when `langfuse-secrets` already exists).
3. **Live `cage-staging` inspection & explicit non-exploitation boundary (`governance-stack`, verified 2026-10-30):**
   - `users`: 1 row (`email = dev-admin@local.com`, `created_at = 2026-08-29 19:20:32.337`, `updated_at = 2026-09-07 20:59:59.092`) whose bcrypt hash **still matched the initial password** (`bcrypt.compareSync(<old_password>, hash) === true`).
   - `sessions`: `0` rows (`SELECT count(*) FROM sessions`); `api_keys`: 1 row (`created_at = 2026-08-29 19:20:32.352`, `last_used = NULL`).
   - **Explicit boundary of non-exploitation evidence:** Zero `sessions` rows and `api_keys.last_used = NULL` rule out access **through Langfuse** (both interactive web login and API-key queries), **not direct Cloud SQL Postgres access**. While `langfuse-web` is a `ClusterIP` service with no public `Ingress` or `LoadBalancer`, local `kubectl port-forward` tunnels on `dev-thin-client` to ports `3000`/`3001` were active from **10 October** (`2026-10-10`) and **22 October** (`2026-10-22`), and **1,111 traces** (`2026-09-29` to `2026-10-03`) and **2,043 observations** holding governance decision payloads and prompts sat in Cloud SQL Postgres throughout that window.
4. **Stakeholder & Disclosure Taxonomy (recorded 2026-10-30):**
   Because CAGE is a reference architecture with no external installed base (`gcr.io/cage-reference/` is a non-existent placeholder registry), POAM findings divide into three distinct categories:
   - **Reference-pattern defects** — fix the pattern in the repository so adopters do not copy a flawed design; no external disclosure or GHSA is applicable because there are no external deployments to notify.
   - **Staging operational defects** (such as this item and POAM-2026-158 / POAM-2026-159) — remediate directly in the maintainer's `cage-staging` environment; affects no external party.
   - **Partner-facing contract defects** (FlowSignal wire contract in POAM-2026-113 / POAM-2026-156, Archytan receipt-verification surface in POAM-2026-098) — the only category with external counterparties where coordination, timing, and disclosure matter.
5. **Rotation verification (2026-10-30):**
   - Generated a fresh 32-byte CSPRNG password (`64` hex chars), computed its cost-12 bcrypt hash, updated `users.password` and `updated_at` for `dev-admin@local.com` in Cloud SQL `langfuse` Postgres (`UPDATE 1`, `DELETE 0` from `sessions`), and updated `langfuse-secrets` (`init-user-password`) in Kubernetes (`governance-stack`).
   - Verified inside `langfuse-web` against the live Cloud SQL `users` row that `bcrypt.compareSync(<old_password>, password) === false` and `bcrypt.compareSync(<new_secret>, password) === true`.
   - Lula: `lula-validation-ia5.yaml` unaffected (Secret resource name and keys unchanged).

**Milestones:**
1. **2026-06-05 (`2fdc67d9`):** Langfuse keys whose 8-hex prefixes were elided (`-...`) in one of the 14 pre-release documents (`ed9d4c02` on `main`, `42f3594a` on `#1`–`#15`) rotated under that document's `D-01`.
2. **2026-07-08 (`fc5592b0`):** 14 internal planning, readiness and security-audit files removed from the working tree on `main`'s line (`4134c860` → `fc5592b0`), leaving their contents in `main`'s commit ancestry (`1902c92c..4134c860`).
3. **2026-10-13 (`e5376b58`, `#477`):** Hardcoded default Langfuse init-user password removed from `infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf` (49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf):411 at `HEAD`.
4. **2026-10-30 (`#602`, `319c702d`):** Full-history gitleaks scan completed (`POAM-2026-160`), live `cage-staging` Cloud SQL Postgres `users.password` hash and Kubernetes `langfuse-secrets` rotated and verified, and [`deployment/update_langfuse_secret.py`](../deployment/update_langfuse_secret.py) updated to warn that patching `langfuse-secrets` does not rotate an existing Postgres user row.
5. **2026-11-03:** Verified that `fc5592b0` is an ancestor of `origin/main` (`14` deleted files reachable in `main`'s own history, not only via PR refs `#1`–`#15`), completed credential triage of all 14 files (no live credentials), recorded that deleting PR refs `#1`–`#15` achieves nothing, and reclassified `POAM-2026-162` as an accepted risk.

**Closure criteria:** The canonical public repository no longer serves `fc5592b0^` (`4134c860`) or `1902c92c` in `origin/main`'s ancestry (for example, if the repository is archived or replaced by a clean-history repository), verified by `git merge-base --is-ancestor fc5592b0 origin/main` exiting non-zero on a fresh clone. This criterion decides when the item closes, not any planned history-rewrite or PR-ref deletion work.

### POAM-2026-163: Bandit SAST Gate Excluded `scripts/`, Hiding `B310` and `B108` Findings

**Control:** NIST SA-11 (Developer Testing and Evaluation), RA-5 (Vulnerability Monitoring and Scanning), CA-7 (Continuous Monitoring)
**Risk Level:** Moderate
**Status:** Open — implemented and merged on `main` in #601 (`7f0c1c4b`, branch head `a19a97cb`, `2026-10-30`)
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-30
**Target Closure:** 2026-11-15

**Description:**
[`Makefile`](../Makefile), [`.github/workflows/ci.yml`](../.github/workflows/ci.yml), [`.github/workflows/security-scan.yml`](../.github/workflows/security-scan.yml), and [`AGENTS.md`](../AGENTS.md) all invoke `bandit -r src/ scripts/ -c pyproject.toml -ll -ii`, claiming SAST coverage over both `src/` and `scripts/`. However, [`pyproject.toml`](../pyproject.toml) set `[tool.bandit] exclude_dirs = ["tests", "scripts", "examples"]`. Because Bandit applies `exclude_dirs` after expanding positional directory arguments, every file under `scripts/` was silently skipped (`76,348` LOC scanned instead of `115,025` LOC). Removing `"scripts"` from `exclude_dirs` exposed **8 medium-severity findings** across 5 scripts:
1. **`B310` (`audit_url_open`) in [`scripts/automated_auditor.py`](../scripts/automated_auditor.py) (`7f0c1c4b^:scripts/automated_auditor.py` line 118; line 133 at HEAD):** `urllib.request.urlopen(url, timeout=10)` was called without `urllib.parse.urlparse` scheme validation (`http://` or `https://`), violating the `AGENTS.md` Strict Scheme Validation rule.
2. **7× `B108` (`hardcoded_tmp_directory`):** hardcoded `/tmp` default paths in [`scripts/check_doc_references.py`](../scripts/check_doc_references.py), [`scripts/check_locust_baseline.py`](../scripts/check_locust_baseline.py), [`scripts/measure_paper_metrics.py`](../scripts/measure_paper_metrics.py), and [`scripts/measure_reconciliation_metrics.py`](../scripts/measure_reconciliation_metrics.py).

**Remediation Plan (implemented and merged on `main` in #601, `7f0c1c4b`, branch head `a19a97cb`):**
1. Remove `"scripts"` from `[tool.bandit] exclude_dirs` in [`pyproject.toml`](../pyproject.toml) so `exclude_dirs = ["tests", "examples"]`.
2. Validate `urllib.parse.urlparse(url).scheme in ("http", "https")` before `urllib.request.urlopen` in [`scripts/automated_auditor.py`](../scripts/automated_auditor.py) (`ValueError` on disallowed schemes) and replace the 7 hardcoded `/tmp` defaults (plus `tempfile.gettempdir()` in [`src/governed_financial_advisor/infrastructure/storage.py`](../src/governed_financial_advisor/infrastructure/storage.py) line 152, replacing `7f0c1c4b^:src/governed_financial_advisor/infrastructure/storage.py` line 148 and removing its `# nosec`) with `tempfile.gettempdir()`.
3. Standardize every `# nosec` comment across `src/` and `scripts/` to `# nosec B<NNN>  # <justification>`.
4. Guard with [`test_bandit_gate_scope.py`](../tests/test_bandit_gate_scope.py) in #601 (`7f0c1c4b`; asserting `scripts` is not in `exclude_dirs`, all `scripts/*.py` files are included in Bandit's file list, every `# nosec` carries a rule ID and justification, and `automated_auditor.py` rejects `file://` URLs) and the `test_bandit_gate_catches_b310_in_src_and_scripts` meta-check in [`test_ci_gate_effectiveness.py`](../tests/test_ci_gate_effectiveness.py) (#602, `319c702d`).

**Closure Criteria:**
1. Merge commit SHA `7f0c1c4b` (#601) verified on `2026-10-30`.

### POAM-2026-164: Clean-Project GKE Deployment Walk and Empirical Release-Gate Verification Have Not Been Run

**Control:** NIST CM-2 (Baseline Configuration), SA-11 (Developer Testing and Evaluation), CA-2 (Control Assessments)
**Risk Level:** High
**Status:** Open — `Step A2` manual clean-project walk completed (`2026-11-01`–`2026-11-02`, through Stage 1 `terraform apply`, post-Stage-1 state import, Stage 2 `terraform plan` `159 to add, 2 to change, 0 to destroy`, and post-retention project teardown to `DELETE_REQUESTED`); PF list retired in favor of `EF1`–`EF13`; `Step A3` outstanding
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-31
**Target Closure:** 2026-11-15

**Description:**
`POAM-2026-131` closed on the first live `cage-staging` apply consuming rendered image digests (`3afda5b7`, `2026-10-31`). However, that apply ran against the long-lived `<project>` GCP project, where foundational resources — enabled APIs, the `<project>-tfstate` GCS bucket, the `cage-cloudbuild-staging` service account, the `cage-signing-staging` Cloud KMS keyring and `binauthz-attestor` key, the Container Analysis note, and the Binary Authorization attestor — already existed from prior deployments.

Prior to `2026-11-01`, no human or script had walked a **clean GCP project from zero** following [`infra/QUICK_START.md`](../infra/QUICK_START.md). While [`scripts/bootstrap_gcp_project.sh`](../scripts/bootstrap_gcp_project.sh) (`#605`, `b66c7344`) and [`scripts/staging_lifecycle.sh`](../scripts/staging_lifecycle.sh) (`#606`, `d599fc04`, plus `#626`, `fed4da94`, which removed the `grep -q` SIGPIPE race under `set -o pipefail`) were merged to automate clean-project bootstrap (`PF1`–`PF4`, `PF8`, `PF10`) and `--fresh-project` lifecycle validation (`PF5`, `PF8`, `PF9`), both had only been executed against hermetic `gcloud`/`terraform` stubs ([`test_gcp_project_bootstrap.py`](../tests/infrastructure/test_gcp_project_bootstrap.py), [`test_staging_lifecycle.py`](../tests/infrastructure/test_staging_lifecycle.py); `git log -p scripts/bootstrap_gcp_project.sh scripts/staging_lifecycle.sh`) and had never created or torn down a real cloud resource.

**Step A2 Empirical Walk and `PF` / `EF` Reconciliation (`2026-11-01`–`2026-11-02`):**
On `2026-11-01`–`2026-11-02`, a fresh throwaway GCP project (`<WALK_PROJECT_ID>`, with `constraints/compute.skipDefaultNetworkCreation` enforced and a `$25` USD Cloud Billing budget alert with `0.5`/`0.9`/`1.0` actual-spend and `1.0` forecasted-spend thresholds) was walked by hand against [`infra/QUICK_START.md`](../infra/QUICK_START.md), [`scripts/bootstrap_gcp_project.sh`](../scripts/bootstrap_gcp_project.sh), [`infra/load_env.sh`](../infra/load_env.sh), and [`deploy_all.sh`](../deploy_all.sh) (`--target gcp-gke --env staging`) through Stage 1 targeted `terraform apply` (`module.kms`, `module.gke`, `module.memorystore_governance` — `6 added, 0 changed, 0 destroyed` on the final pass), post-Stage-1 state import of the foundational `PF3`/`PF8` resources, Stage 2 `terraform plan` (`159 to add, 2 to change, 0 to destroy`), the empirical locked-retention bucket and project deletion lien probe (`EF12`), and hard-stop project deletion (`gcloud projects delete <WALK_PROJECT_ID> --quiet` reaching `lifecycleState: DELETE_REQUESTED` on `2026-11-02T01:36:54Z` after deleting the `storage.googleapis.com` project lien; `terraform destroy` was not run). The walk surfaced **13 empirical findings (`EF1`–`EF13`)**:

1. **Label integrity (`PF1`–`PF10` vs. the eight labels that exist):** Earlier revisions of this entry (introduced in `#609`, `b8fe13d5`, and mechanically updated in `#624`, `d694d29c`) referred to the predicted failure list as `PF1`–`PF10`, but **`PF6` and `PF7` exist nowhere in the repository**. Inspection of `#605` (`b66c7344`), `#606` (`d599fc04`), and `#624` (`d694d29c`) establishes that **the range `W1`–`W10` / `PF1`–`PF10` was always loose**: `#605` introduced `W1` (`line 335`), `W2` (`line 339`), `W4` (`line 353`), `W3` (`lines 374, 485`), `W10` (`line 487`), and `W8` (`lines 42, 188–303`) in [`scripts/bootstrap_gcp_project.sh`](../scripts/bootstrap_gcp_project.sh), and `#606` introduced `W5` (`line 191`), `W8` (`lines 400–405`), and `W9` (`line 440`) in [`scripts/staging_lifecycle.sh`](../scripts/staging_lifecycle.sh). `W6` and `W7` never existed in `#605` or `#606`, and `#624` (`d694d29c`) renamed the eight existing labels `W1`–`W5`, `W8`–`W10` to `PF1`–`PF5`, `PF8`–`PF10` without dropping any labels.
2. **Scorecard for the eight real labels (`PF1`–`PF5`, `PF8`–`PF10`):**
   - **`PF9` ([`scripts/staging_lifecycle.sh`](../scripts/staging_lifecycle.sh) line 1153 — post-destroy bucket/lien survivor check): confirmed and deepened (`EF12`).** Locking a bucket retention policy (`gcloud storage buckets update --lock-retention-policy` or Terraform `retention_policy { is_locked = true }`) automatically creates a project deletion lien with `origin: storage.googleapis.com` and `restrictions: ["resourcemanager.projects.delete"]` (not `cloudstorage.googleapis.com` as earlier text in this entry assumed). Deleting an empty locked bucket (`gcloud storage buckets delete`) succeeds (`200 OK`) **without** removing the `storage.googleapis.com` project lien, so the `PF9` survivor check at [`scripts/staging_lifecycle.sh`](../scripts/staging_lifecycle.sh) lines 1188–1197 (`remaining_liens`) always fails after `terraform destroy` unless the lien is explicitly removed via `gcloud alpha resource-manager liens delete`. Conversely, `gcloud alpha resource-manager liens delete` succeeds (`Deleted [liens/...].`) even while a locked bucket still holds unexpired objects; GCS still enforces `403 RetentionPolicyNotMet` against object/bucket deletion until `retentionExpirationTime`, while `gcloud projects delete` is no longer blocked once the lien is removed.
   - **`PF1` ([`scripts/bootstrap_gcp_project.sh`](../scripts/bootstrap_gcp_project.sh) lines 310–345 — enable 21 required APIs) and `PF3` (`lines 383–494` — pre-create Cloud Build SA, KMS signing keyring/key, Container Analysis note, and Binary Authorization attestor): fired at the predicted step via mechanisms nobody predicted.** `PF1` failed via `EF1`: passing all 21 APIs in one `gcloud services enable "${REQUIRED_APIS[@]}"` call exceeds Service Usage's hard 20-service batch limit (`SU_MAX_BATCH_SIZE_EXCEEDED: Number of services must not exceed the maximum batch size (20)`), even when all 21 APIs are already enabled (since remediated; see the `EF1` row). `PF3`'s pre-creation commands succeeded (`cage-cloudbuild-staging`, `cage-signing-staging`, `binauthz-attestor`, `cage-build-attestor-note-staging`, `cage-build-attestor-staging`), but `--import-state` (`lines 188–303`) failed via `EF4` because [`infra/targets/gcp-gke/providers.tf`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/providers.tf) (`lines 23–36`) binds the root `kubernetes` and `helm` providers to `module.gke.cluster_endpoint`, which fails provider configuration whenever `module.gke` is absent from state. `PF3`'s scope also missed `EF7` (networking prerequisites) and `EF8` (unmaterialized P4SA service identities and missing `gcp-sa-memorystore` CMEK binding).
   - **`PF5` ([`scripts/staging_lifecycle.sh`](../scripts/staging_lifecycle.sh) line 526 — `authorized_networks` pre-flight check): fired three ways and was understated (`EF3`, `EF11`, `EF13b`).** (1) [`infra/QUICK_START.md`](../infra/QUICK_START.md) Step 4 (`lines 138–155`) omits `authorized_networks` (only mentioned under Troubleshooting at `line 397`) (`EF3`). (2) [`scripts/gen_tfvars.py`](../scripts/gen_tfvars.py), invoked unconditionally by [`deploy_all.sh`](../deploy_all.sh) (`line 480`), overwrote `terraform.auto.tfvars` in [`infra/targets/gcp-gke/`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/) with only `otel_exporter_otlp_headers`, wiping out any operator-set `authorized_networks` there (`EF11`, since remediated; see the `EF11` row). (3) [`staging.tfvars`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/staging.tfvars) (`line 92`) leaves `# enable_nist_compliance = true` commented out, so a direct `terraform plan`/`apply`/`destroy -var-file=staging.tfvars` bypasses `resource "terraform_data" "control_plane_access_guard"` (`main.tf` (49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf) `lines 1388–1406`) and sets `master_authorized_networks_config` to `0.0.0.0/0` ([`gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf`](../infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf) `lines 168–176`), which `lifecycle { ignore_changes = [..., master_authorized_networks_config, ...] }` in [`infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf`](../infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf) (`line 315`) then latches (`EF13b`, tracked in `POAM-2026-174`).
   - **`PF2` ([`scripts/bootstrap_gcp_project.sh`](../scripts/bootstrap_gcp_project.sh) line 347 — `<project>-tfstate` GCS bucket), `PF4` (`lines 362–381` — Artifact Registry `gcr.io` Docker repository and `gs://<project>_cloudbuild` staging bucket), and `PF10` (`lines 496–511` — deployer `roles/cloudkms.publicKeyViewer`): did not fire.** `PF2` and `PF4` succeeded on the first pass; `PF10` did not fire during the walk (which ran under operator credentials, with `data.google_kms_crypto_key_version.binauthz_attestor` resolving cleanly in Stage 2 `terraform plan`).
   - **`PF8` ([`scripts/staging_lifecycle.sh`](../scripts/staging_lifecycle.sh) lines 1070–1091 — `terraform state rm` before `terraform destroy` vs. [`scripts/bootstrap_gcp_project.sh`](../scripts/bootstrap_gcp_project.sh) lines 188–303 `--import-state`): untested on the destroy side because Stage 2 `terraform apply` and `terraform destroy` never ran.** On the import side, pre-Stage-1 `--import-state` failed via `EF4`, whereas post-Stage-1 `--import-state` (once `module.gke` existed in state) imported all remaining foundational `PF3`/`PF8` resources (`google_service_account.cloudbuild`, `google_kms_key_ring.signing`, `google_kms_crypto_key.binauthz_attestor`, `google_container_analysis_note.build_attestor_note[0]`, `google_binary_authorization_attestor.build_attestor[0]`, while recognizing `module.kms[0].google_kms_key_ring.keyring` and `module.kms[0].google_kms_crypto_key.cmek_key` as already in state) with zero drift in Stage 2 `terraform plan`.
3. **Empirical findings (`EF1`–`EF13`) and POA&M mapping (11 of 13 had no prediction):**
