Nothing else read the flag. The earlier `staging.tfvars` comment saying it turns on Redis replication, replica scale-out and PDBs was wrong, and step 1 removed it, along with the same claim in `us-dev.tfvars` and that file's two references to a precondition in [`app_secrets/main.tf`](../infra/modules/app_secrets/main.tf), which has none. The table records what step 1 changed. Since `POAM-2026-181` and `POAM-2026-130`, every site in it follows `local.enforcing_posture` (`!local.enforcing_posture` for `force_destroy`), and the compliance flags have been removed.

**Teardown (verified `2026-11-03`).** The guards in the table stop a direct `plan` or `apply`, not a direct teardown. These runs used Terraform 1.15.8 and 1.17.1 under `env -i`, so no `TF_VAR_*`, no `*.auto.tfvars` and no credentials, with the same result on both versions:
1. `terraform plan -destroy -var-file=staging.tfvars` alone stops at `No value for required variable` (`project_id`, `image_digests`) before any guard is evaluated. That is RD-1 and RD-2 in [`BREAKING_CHANGES.md`](BREAKING_CHANGES.md), and the documented teardown at `staging.tfvars` lines 54–55 passes both.
2. The documented teardown against an empty state reports `No objects need to be destroyed`. Terraform skips its pre-destroy refresh when the state is empty, so this run says nothing about the guards.
3. Against a state holding the three guards, the destroy plan's refresh reports `control_plane_access_guard` and `poam_019_empty_key_guard` as warnings (`-json` output), where a normal plan reports them as errors. The full-target destroy plan still exited 1, on errors that only the probe has: an invalid access token and no cluster in state. With the three guards and their variables extracted verbatim and the real `staging.tfvars`, the destroy plan exits 0 and `terraform destroy` completes.
4. The same check written as a variable validation fails the destroy plan with `Invalid value for variable`. The guards therefore stay resource preconditions; a comment below `control_plane_access_guard` in `main.tf` (49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf) says so.

**Closure Criteria:**
1. Merge commit SHA uncommenting `enable_nist_compliance = true` in [`staging.tfvars`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/staging.tfvars), removing `master_authorized_networks_config` and `monitoring_config` from `ignore_changes` in [`gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf`](../infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf), and adding the guarding test is recorded here.
2. `terraform plan -var-file=staging.tfvars` refuses an empty `authorized_networks` via `resource "terraform_data" "control_plane_access_guard"` and shows zero drift on `master_authorized_networks_config` and `monitoring_config` against `<PROJECT_ID>` (after `POAM-2026-183` and `EF10a` in `POAM-2026-164` are reconciled).

### POAM-2026-178: `terraform.auto.tfvars` Silently Overrides the Exported `authorized_networks`

**Control:** NIST AC-3 (Access Enforcement), CM-6 (Configuration Settings)
**Risk Level:** Low
**Status:** Open. Found by code reading on `2026-11-03` while remediating `EF11` (`POAM-2026-164`, `#650`); not observed on a live cluster
**Date Opened:** 2026-11-03
**Target Closure:** 2026-11-30

**Description:**
1. **`#650` made the file's value durable.** Before `#650`, [`scripts/gen_tfvars.py`](../scripts/gen_tfvars.py) rewrote `terraform.auto.tfvars` on every `deploy_all.sh --target gcp-gke` run, so the file never held `authorized_networks` when Terraform ran. Now every operator-written assignment survives the run.
2. **Nothing on the primary path outranks the file.** Terraform reads `TF_VAR_*` variables first, then `*.auto.tfvars`, then `-var` / `-var-file` flags; the last value wins. [`deploy_all.sh`](../deploy_all.sh) adds the profile as `-var-file` (`line 310`), adds operator `--var-file` flags (`line 342`), and prepends `-var=enable_nist_compliance` (`line 442`). It never passes `authorized_networks` on the command line. [`staging_lifecycle.sh`](../scripts/staging_lifecycle.sh) (`line 534`) exports `TF_VAR_authorized_networks` for the deployer's egress; its `deploy_all.sh` call ([`staging_lifecycle.sh`](../scripts/staging_lifecycle.sh) `line 547`) passes none. So a file value beats the value exported for the current run, on both scripts' normal paths and on a bare `terraform apply`, with no message. It loses only when the operator passes `authorized_networks` through `--var` or `--var-file`.
3. **Effect:** the operator's exported list is ignored without warning, and the file's list is deployed instead. That can keep an older CIDR authorized, or lock the current deployer out of `kubectl` and `helm`, whose providers use the public endpoint.
4. **Scope:** this entry covers precedence only. Whether a CIDR that *is* deployed still belongs on the list, including when nothing is exported at all, is `POAM-2026-179`. Fixing this entry does not close that one.

**Remediation Plan:**
1. **Preferred:** when `TF_VAR_authorized_networks` is set, have [`deploy_all.sh`](../deploy_all.sh) also pass it as `-var=authorized_networks=...`, so it outranks `*.auto.tfvars` where Terraform resolves precedence. Pass it **only when the variable is set**; an unconditional `-var` with nothing exported would replace a good file value with an empty one. An operator can still keep a stable CIDR in the file and add a transient one by exporting the combined list.
2. **Rejected:** having `gen_tfvars.py` refuse a conflicting value. That puts a precedence decision in a script that writes one file and cannot see how Terraform will resolve variables. It also breaks the legitimate case of a stable CIDR in the file and a transient one in the environment.
3. Document the precedence in [`staging.tfvars`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/staging.tfvars) next to the `authorized_networks` guidance, and add a regression test.

**Closure Criteria:**
1. Merge commit SHA of the remediation and its test are recorded here.
2. With `authorized_networks` in `terraform.auto.tfvars` and a different `TF_VAR_authorized_networks` exported, a `deploy_all.sh --target gcp-gke` plan uses the exported value.
3. With `authorized_networks` in `terraform.auto.tfvars` and `TF_VAR_authorized_networks` unset, the same plan uses the file value, and no `authorized_networks` `-var` is passed.

### POAM-2026-179: Control-Plane `authorized_networks` Owner Attribution and Adopter Review Guidance

**Control:** NIST SC-7(4) (Boundary Protection — review exceptions to the traffic flow policy and remove those without a current need), AC-3 (Access Enforcement)
**Risk Level:** Low
**Status:** Open. Identified on `2026-11-03` during review of `#650`. Remediation design amended on `2026-11-03` in `#651` (plan-time checks are resource preconditions placed on the cluster's path, not variable validations) and re-scoped on `2026-11-04` in `#655` under the reference-architecture framing (retiring the committed HMAC-SHA256 review ledger and PR-blocking 30-day recency CI gate).
**Date Opened:** 2026-11-03
**Target Closure:** 2026-12-15

**Description:**
1. **No owner or purpose check on `display_name`.** `authorized_networks` decides which source networks may reach the GKE control plane. `resource "terraform_data" "control_plane_access_guard"` (`main.tf` (49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf) `lines 1388–1406`) checks only that the list is non-empty and has no `/0`. Nothing requires `display_name` to identify an owner or purpose (`POAM-2026-183` found all three live `<PROJECT_ID>` CIDRs carrying `displayName = ""`).
2. **It survives the `POAM-2026-178` fix.** An operator who exports no `TF_VAR_authorized_networks` deploys whatever list the profile, a `--var-file`, or `terraform.auto.tfvars` holds.
3. **Reference-architecture scope (`2026-11-04`).** Whether a CIDR still belongs to someone who should have access is an operational judgment for adopters running a persistent cluster, not a property a reference repository's pull-request CI can verify. What the reference architecture *should* ship is (a) a teardown-safe plan-time resource precondition on the cluster's path requiring a non-empty owner/purpose `display_name` format, (b) clean example `.tfvars` files and scripts that satisfy that contract, and (c) explicit adopter operational guidance in [`docs/operations/DEPLOYMENT_RULES.md`](operations/DEPLOYMENT_RULES.md). The earlier proposal for a committed HMAC-SHA256 review ledger and a PR-blocking 30-day recency CI gate was designed for a production allowlist and is retired (`2026-11-04`).
4. **Why Low:** the control-plane API still requires authenticated, authorized credentials.

**Remediation Plan:**
1. ✅ **Done (`2026-11-04`):** Documented periodic `authorized_networks` allowlist review and ephemeral deployer `/32` removal as an adopter operational responsibility in [`docs/operations/DEPLOYMENT_RULES.md`](operations/DEPLOYMENT_RULES.md) (§ *GKE Control-Plane Access*).
2. Require each entry's `display_name` to name an owner/purpose, and enforce the format with a resource precondition, not a `validation` block on `variable "authorized_networks"` in [`variables.tf`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/variables.tf). A sweep of `scripts/`, `.github/`, `infra/`, `tests/` and the docs on `2026-11-03` found no producer that follows an `owner:purpose` convention. All of the following must be migrated in the same change, or they fail at plan:
   - **Committed profiles:** [`prod.tfvars`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/prod.tfvars) (`line 72`), [`apac-prod.tfvars`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/apac-prod.tfvars) (`line 95`), [`eu-prod.tfvars`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/eu-prod.tfvars) (`line 96`), [`dev.tfvars`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/dev.tfvars) (`line 42`), [`us-dev.tfvars`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/us-dev.tfvars) (`line 106`), [`apac-dev.tfvars`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/apac-dev.tfvars) (`line 99`), and [`eu-dev.tfvars`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/eu-dev.tfvars) (`line 99`).
   - **Generated at run time:** the `"deployer-egress"` entry in [`staging_lifecycle.sh`](../scripts/staging_lifecycle.sh) (`line 534`), and the assertion on it in [`test_staging_lifecycle.py`](../tests/infrastructure/test_staging_lifecycle.py) (`line 453`).
   - **Terraform test fixtures:** every `display_name = "test"` in `infra/targets/gcp-gke/tests/`: [`gpu_pool.tftest.hcl`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/tests/gpu_pool.tftest.hcl) (`lines 43, 80, 122, 142, 167, 195`), [`governance_persistence.tftest.hcl`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/tests/governance_persistence.tftest.hcl) (`line 90`), [`image_digests.tftest.hcl`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/tests/image_digests.tftest.hcl) (`line 68`), [`poam_019.tftest.hcl`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/tests/poam_019.tftest.hcl) (`line 71`), [`retention_posture.tftest.hcl`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/tests/retention_posture.tftest.hcl) (`line 78`), and [`w6.tftest.hcl`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/tests/w6.tftest.hcl) (`line 68`).
   - **Examples operators copy:** [`QUICK_START.md`](../infra/QUICK_START.md) (`lines 282, 336`), [`DEPLOYMENT_RULES.md`](operations/DEPLOYMENT_RULES.md) (`line 460`), the target [`README.md`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/README.md) (`line 141`), the module [`README.md`](../infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/README.md) (`line 93`), [`staging.tfvars`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/staging.tfvars) (`line 122`), the commented examples in [`apac-dev.tfvars`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/apac-dev.tfvars) and [`eu-dev.tfvars`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/eu-dev.tfvars) (`line 94` in each), and the commented [`display_name` example in `terraform.auto.tfvars.example`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/terraform.auto.tfvars.example) (`line 86`).
   - **Outside the repository:** the precondition rejects unmigrated local files at plan and apply without stopping a teardown; record the change in [`BREAKING_CHANGES.md`](BREAKING_CHANGES.md).
   - **Not affected:** the `0.0.0.0/0` fallback block in [`gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf`](../infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf) (`line 173`) is built inside the module and never passes through the target variable. It belongs to `POAM-2026-174`.
   - **Why a precondition and not a validation.** Terraform evaluates variable validations in every plan, destroy plans included, and one that fails stops the destroy. A resource precondition is reported only as a warning while a destroy plan refreshes state (verified on Terraform 1.15.8 and 1.17.1 in `POAM-2026-174`, Teardown).
   - **Where the precondition goes.** It must sit on a resource that every plan creating or changing the cluster includes (`google_container_cluster.primary` itself, or a guard the cluster depends on) so `terraform apply -target=module.gke` cannot skip it (`POAM-2026-180`).

**Closure Criteria:**
1. ✅ **Done (`2026-11-04`):** Adopter operational guidance for periodic `authorized_networks` allowlist review and ephemeral deployer CIDR removal is documented in [`docs/operations/DEPLOYMENT_RULES.md`](operations/DEPLOYMENT_RULES.md).
2. `terraform plan` and `apply` reject any `authorized_networks` entry whose `display_name` does not satisfy the owner/purpose convention, through a resource precondition placed on the cluster's path as in item 2. `terraform test` cases show an accepted entry, a rejected entry, and the same rejection in a targeted plan (`plan_options { target = [module.gke] }` or `plan_options { target = [google_container_cluster.primary] }`). A destroy plan that supplies a rejected entry still succeeds. Every producer in item 2 is migrated, and the `terraform test` suite passes.

### POAM-2026-180: Standalone `terraform_data` Guards Are Skipped by Targeted Plans

**Control:** NIST AC-3 (Access Enforcement), SC-7 (Boundary Protection), AU-9 (Protection of Audit Information)
**Risk Level:** Low
**Status:** Open. Identified on `2026-11-03` in `#651`, while working out where `POAM-2026-179`'s checks must sit. Widened the same day from the control-plane guards to every standalone guard in `infra/`.
**Date Opened:** 2026-11-03
**Target Closure:** 2026-12-15

**Description:**
1. **What happens.** A targeted plan includes only the targets and what they depend on. Five `terraform_data` resources in `main.tf` (49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf) exist only to carry preconditions, and nothing refers to any of them, so a plan targeted at what one protects never evaluates it. They are the only such guards in `infra/`. The one other `terraform_data` with a precondition, `geap_reasoning_engine` in `385e502e:infra/modules/gcp_geap_advisor/main.tf` (retired in `AR-1`), deployed the agent runtime itself, so its preconditions sit on the resource they protect.
2. **Verified for each guard (`2026-11-03`, Terraform 1.15.8 and 1.17.1, the real target with `staging.tfvars`, mock providers, no state, no credentials).** An untargeted plan was refused by every guard. The table gives each targeted run and what it returned; `Missing expected failure` means the run had `expect_failures` on the guard and the guard did not fail, although the same inputs fail it in an untargeted plan.

| Guard | Protects | Targeted runs and results | Effect when skipped |
|---|---|---|---|
| `control_plane_access_guard` (`main.tf` (49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf) `lines 1388–1406`) | the cluster's control-plane allowlist | `target = [module.gke]`, empty `authorized_networks`: `Missing expected failure`. Without `expect_failures`, the plan completes with the compliance posture active | The cluster module emits no authorized-networks block, so GKE leaves the allowlist off ([`gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf`](../infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf) `lines 154–176`). `master_authorized_networks_config` is in the cluster's `ignore_changes` (`POAM-2026-174` item 2), so a later untargeted apply does not correct it. No backstop |
| `poam_019_shared_key_guard` (`main.tf` (49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf) `lines 1357–1380`) | the `langfuse-compliance-secrets` secret that `module.app_secrets` writes | `target = [module.app_secrets]`, each compliance key equal to its application-project key: `Missing expected failure`. Without `expect_failures`, the plan completes | The plan writes the application project's keys into the compliance secret, which collapses AU-9 dual-project isolation without an error. No preventive backstop: the compliance bridge's startup check only tests that the keys are set. [`verify_langfuse_posture.py`](../scripts/verify_langfuse_posture.py) would report it when run live, but CI runs it only with `--dry-run` (from the code; not run) |
| `poam_019_empty_key_guard` (`main.tf` (49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf) `lines 1333–1355`) | the same secret | `target = [module.app_secrets]`, both compliance keys empty: `Missing expected failure`. Without `expect_failures`, the plan completes, and so does `target = [module.gateway]`, because `module.gateway` depends on `module.app_secrets` | The plan writes an empty compliance secret. Outside dev, `_validate_langfuse_credentials` in [`audit_workflow.py`](../src/compliance_bridge/audit_workflow.py) raises at import, which [`main.py`](../src/compliance_bridge/main.py) does at startup, so the bridge fails closed at runtime instead of at plan time (from the code; not run) |
| `memorystore_ca_pinning_guard` (`main.tf` (49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf) `lines 1315–1322`) | the Memorystore server CA the gateway and compliance-bridge pods pin | `target = [module.gateway]` and `target = [module.compliance_bridge]`, `managed_server_ca = []`: `Missing expected failure`, and each plan was refused by the deployment's own `redis_ca_pem` precondition ([`gateway/main.tf`](../infra/modules/gateway/main.tf) `lines 63–66`, [`compliance_bridge/main.tf`](../infra/modules/compliance_bridge/main.tf) `lines 42–45`) | None: only the guard's own message is lost |
| `inference_backend_guard` (`main.tf` (49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf) `lines 1435–1447`) | the vLLM and NeMo workloads | `target = [module.vllm]`, `inference_backend = "vllm"`, no GPU pool: `Missing expected failure`. Without `expect_failures`, the plan completes, and so does `target = [the NeMo Guardrails module]` with the NeMo server on and no vLLM | Availability only: the vLLM pods cannot be scheduled, and the NeMo server would crash-loop. No security control depends on it |

3. **One cause.** The placement, not any single check. The comment below `control_plane_access_guard` in `main.tf` (49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf) holds the matrix: a standalone guard is skipped by a targeted plan, a variable validation fails a destroy plan run without its inputs, and a precondition on the protected resource, or a guard every protected resource depends on, passes both. All eight cells were run on both versions, with the guard extracted verbatim.
4. **Exposure.** [`run_staging_validation_once.sh`](../scripts/run_staging_validation_once.sh) (`line 203`) runs `terraform apply -target=module.gke` with `staging.tfvars`. It passes no image digests, so at HEAD it stops first at `No value for required variable` (RD-1), unless its environment supplies `TF_VAR_image_digests`. No committed script targets any other module. Otherwise a targeted apply is an operator's choice.
5. **Why Low.** Each gap takes a deliberate targeted apply. The control-plane API still requires authenticated, authorized credentials (as in `POAM-2026-179`). The compliance secret is in no `ignore_changes`, so the next untargeted plan evaluates both key guards and refuses until the keys are fixed.
6. **Effect on closed items.** `POAM-2026-126`'s closure says the plan refuses an empty allowlist or a `/0` whenever a compliance posture is active, and `POAM-019`'s says it rejects an empty or shared compliance key. Both hold for untargeted plans only. Each closed entry now says so and points here. Both stay closed, and this item tracks the gap.

**Remediation Plan:**
1. Move each security check to where a plan targeted at the resource it protects evaluates it, never to a variable validation, so teardown is never blocked (`POAM-2026-174`, Teardown). Two placements qualify: a `lifecycle` precondition on that resource, or a root guard that the resource's module depends on. A precondition inside a module must be tested from that module's own tests, because a root-level `terraform test` cannot name a child module's resource in `expect_failures` (both versions reject the reference). A root guard keeps the existing root-level tests.
   - The allowlist check: on `google_container_cluster.primary` in [`gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf`](../infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf), or in a guard `module.gke` depends on. Give `POAM-2026-179`'s format and TTL checks the same placement; if both items are still open, do it in one change.
   - Both key checks: on `kubernetes_secret.compliance_secrets` in [`app_secrets/main.tf`](../infra/modules/app_secrets/main.tf), which then needs to know whether a compliance posture is active, or in guards `module.app_secrets` depends on.
2. Delete `memorystore_ca_pinning_guard`. The two deployments already refuse an empty CA.
3. Move `inference_backend_guard`'s two conditions onto the workloads they protect in `module.vllm` and `the NeMo Guardrails module`](49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf), or make both modules depend on it.
4. Remove each guard from `KNOWN_STANDALONE_GUARDS` in [`test_guard_placement.py`](../tests/infrastructure/test_guard_placement.py) as it moves. That test already fails on any new standalone guard in `infra/`.
5. Fix or remove [`run_staging_validation_once.sh`](../scripts/run_staging_validation_once.sh). It predates RD-1, and `POAM-2026-176` records another defect in it.

**Closure Criteria:**
1. For the allowlist, empty-key and shared-key checks, a `terraform test` run whose plan is targeted at the protected resource, with `expect_failures` on the check, passes, and undoing that check's move makes the same run report `Missing expected failure`. For a check inside a module, the run is in that module's tests; for a root guard, it is a root-level run with `plan_options { target = [...] }`.
2. A destroy plan run without the checks' inputs still succeeds, recorded as in `POAM-2026-174`'s teardown section.
3. `KNOWN_STANDALONE_GUARDS` in [`test_guard_placement.py`](../tests/infrastructure/test_guard_placement.py) is empty.
4. [`run_staging_validation_once.sh`](../scripts/run_staging_validation_once.sh) is fixed or removed, and the merge commit SHA is recorded here.

### POAM-2026-181: Fail-Closed Deployment Controls Followed the Jurisdiction Flags, Not the Tier

**Control:** NIST AC-3 (Access Enforcement), SC-7 (Boundary Protection), AU-9 (Protection of Audit Information), CP-9 (System Backup)
**Risk Level:** Moderate
**Status:** Remediated `2026-11-03` in `#651`, pending merge. Identified the same day in the review of `#651`. Moves to Closed Findings with the merge commit.
**Date Opened:** 2026-11-03
**Target Closure:** 2026-12-31

**Description (before the fix):**
1. **Two posture gates, and the controls rode on the wrong one.** `main.tf` (49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf) defines two:
   - `enforcing_posture` (`main.tf` (49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf) `line 213`) is true for every staging and prod plan, and for a dev plan with a compliance flag. It gated only the retention lock and the governance AOF.
   - `any_compliance_active` (`main.tf` (49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf) `lines 384–388`) is true when any of `enable_nist_compliance`, `enable_eu_ecb_compliance` or `enable_apac_mas_compliance` is set, and the three do exactly the same thing (`POAM-2026-130`). It gated controls that are deployment hygiene under any framework:
     - `control_plane_access_guard` and both `POAM-019` key guards;
     - `module.gke`'s `enable_nist_compliance` input, which turns on the cluster's `master_authorized_networks_config` and otherwise emits a `0.0.0.0/0` allowlist ([`gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf`](../infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf) `lines 154–176`);
     - `enable_point_in_time_recovery` outside prod (`main.tf` (49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf) `line 410`) and the NeMo server's `enable_security_context` (`main.tf` (49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf) `line 730`).
2. **Two ways in.** A staging or prod plan that selected no jurisdiction flag lost every control in item 1:
   - the base platform, run with no regulatory overlay;
   - a profile for EU_ECB or APAC_MAS that followed the documentation and omitted its own flag. The NIST flag's description said "US_FED only — do not set for EU_ECB or APAC_MAS deployments" and credited it with deletion protection, which `POAM-024` had moved to its own variable. The cluster module's [`README.md`](../infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/README.md) said EU_ECB and APAC_MAS production get no "NIST-specific hardening". `DEP-01` in [`deploy_all.sh`](../deploy_all.sh) passes the flag as `false` outside US_FED, and for staging printed "STAGING MODE: Full security posture" all the same.

   None of the hardening was NIST-specific. Every committed profile set its own jurisdiction's flag.
3. **Verified (`2026-11-03`, Terraform 1.15.8 and 1.17.1, the real target, mock providers, no state).** With `staging.tfvars` pointed at EU_ECB (`region = "europe-west1"`, EU GPU zones), `enable_nist_compliance = false`, no EU flag, an empty `authorized_networks` and empty compliance keys, the plan completed. `enforcing_posture` was true, `any_compliance_active` false, `module.gke.master_authorized_cidrs == ["0.0.0.0/0"]`, and PITR was off. The same plan with `enable_eu_ecb_compliance = true` was refused by `control_plane_access_guard` and `poam_019_empty_key_guard`. The US form of the gap was this PR's earlier control run with the flag forced off.
4. **What caught it.** `test_enforcing_profiles_are_the_ones_terraform_hardens` classified profiles by flag. A committed edit that dropped a listed profile out of the set failed CI. A new staging or prod profile with no flag did not, nor did a fork's profile or a `-var` override.
5. **Not affected: OSCAL.** The exporter picks its framework from `CAGE_DEPLOYMENT_REGION` ([`oscal_ssp_exporter.py`](../src/gateway/governance/oscal_ssp_exporter.py) `line 505`), which every workload reads from the ConfigMap built from `var.cage_deployment_region` ([`deployment_config/main.tf`](../infra/modules/deployment_config/main.tf) `line 45`). Nothing in `src/` reads a compliance flag, so no flag value changes which framework's artifacts are emitted. `staging.tfvars` has declared `cage_deployment_region = "US_FED"` throughout.
6. **Why separate from `POAM-2026-174`.** That item is about the flag being off in a committed profile, and about the `ignore_changes` latch. This one was about the flag being the gate: the same loss of controls, reachable by a change that looks like a jurisdiction choice rather than a security one.
7. **Why Moderate.** The effect on a staging or prod tier was `POAM-2026-174` item 1's: an open control plane (the API still requires credentials), no key guards, no PITR. Reaching it took a staging or prod plan with no flag, which the NIST flag's own description pointed toward for other jurisdictions.

**Remediation (`2026-11-03`, `#651`, pending merge):**
1. **The controls bind on the tier.** The three guards' conditions, `module.gke`'s allowlist input, PITR and the NeMo securityContext read `local.enforcing_posture` (`main.tf` (49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf) `lines 107, 410, 730, 1338, 1362, 1393`). Only `enforcing_posture` and the dev-only `force_destroy` read `any_compliance_active`, so a jurisdiction flag now decides only whether a dev plan enforces. The flags themselves are unchanged.
2. **Tests.** [`posture_guards.tftest.hcl`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/tests/posture_guards.tftest.hcl) has 20 runs:
   - each of the six staging and prod plans with no flag, in US_FED, EU_ECB and APAC_MAS, plans exactly the supplied allowlist, and is refused with an empty allowlist and with empty compliance keys;
   - the three staging plans also keep PITR;
   - US staging is refused with shared keys;
   - a dev plan with a flag is refused with an empty allowlist.

   The dev-without-a-flag fallback is the control run in [`staging_compliance.tftest.hcl`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/tests/staging_compliance.tftest.hcl). [`test_control_plane_access.py`](../tests/infrastructure/test_control_plane_access.py) classifies profiles by the enforcing posture, so a new staging or prod profile must be classified on purpose. It also pins the guard's predicate, and fails if `any_compliance_active` gates anything else. `poam_019.tftest.hcl`'s two runs without a posture now plan the dev tier.
3. **Mutations** (Terraform 1.15.8). Reverting one binding at a time to `any_compliance_active`:
   - the control-plane, empty-key or shared-key guard: `posture_guards.tftest.hcl` fails;
   - `module.gke`'s input: it fails 6 runs;
   - PITR: it fails 3 runs;
   - the NeMo securityContext: no plan-level assertion, because `the NeMo Guardrails module`](49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf) is not planned without the NeMo server.

   All six reversions fail `test_control_plane_access.py`.
4. **Teardown** (both versions). The three guards, both posture locals and their variables were extracted verbatim, with the real `staging.tfvars`, and state was seeded with a list and keys. Then `plan -destroy -var-file=staging.tfvars` with no list and no keys exits 0 (`3 to destroy`), as it does with `enable_nist_compliance=false` too, and `destroy` completes. Control: a normal plan with no flag, no list and no keys exits 1 on two resource preconditions.
5. **Documentation.** These now describe the gate the code uses:
   - the three flags' descriptions (the EU description's other claims stay with `POAM-2026-130`);
   - the cluster module README;
   - `deploy_all.sh`'s DEP-01 comment and messages;
   - the DEP-01 decision record in [`DEPLOYMENT_DECISION_RECORD.md`](operations/DEPLOYMENT_DECISION_RECORD.md);
   - `DEPLOYMENT_RULES.md`, `JURISDICTION_INVENTORY.md`, `GKE_TEST_RUNBOOK.md` and `LANGFUSE_PROJECT_ISOLATION.md`;
   - `BREAKING_CHANGES.md` CP-3.
6. **Resolved in `POAM-2026-130` (same PR):**
   - the three flags and `any_compliance_active` are deleted and replaced by `variable "enforce_in_dev"`;
   - `module.gke`'s `enable_nist_compliance` input is renamed to `restrict_control_plane`, and `deployment_summary.nist_compliance_enabled` is replaced by `enforcing_posture`;
   - the descriptions' unchecked own-jurisdiction claims are gone.

**Closure Criteria:**
1. Met. For each of US_FED, EU_ECB and APAC_MAS, a staging-tier and a prod-tier `terraform test` run with `enforce_in_dev = false` is refused with an empty `authorized_networks` and with empty compliance keys. With a valid list, it plans exactly that list on the control plane, never `0.0.0.0/0`.
2. Met. A dev-tier run with `enforce_in_dev = false` keeps today's behaviour, the `0.0.0.0/0` fallback and no PITR, and a dev run with `enforce_in_dev = true` still enforces.
3. Met. A destroy plan with no allowlist and no keys still succeeds (item 4).
4. Met. `deploy_all.sh`, the variable descriptions and the cluster module README describe the gate the code uses.
5. Open. The merge commit SHA is recorded here, and the item moves to Closed Findings.

### POAM-2026-130: The Three Jurisdiction Compliance Flags Do the Same Thing and Make Unchecked Jurisdiction Claims

**Control:** NIST CM-6 (Configuration Settings)
**Risk Level:** Low
**Status:** Remediated `2026-11-03` in `#651`, pending merge. Identified on `2026-10-28` during the jurisdiction inventory ([`JURISDICTION_INVENTORY.md`](operations/JURISDICTION_INVENTORY.md)). Moves to Closed Findings with the merge commit.
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-28
**Target Closure:** 2026-12-31

**Description (before the fix):**
1. **Three flags, one boolean.** [`variables.tf`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/variables.tf) declared `enable_nist_compliance`, `enable_eu_ecb_compliance` and `enable_apac_mas_compliance`, and `main.tf` (49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf) (`lines 382–389`) ORed all three into `any_compliance_active`. Nothing in Terraform read the EU or APAC flag individually, and after `POAM-2026-181` moved the deployment guards, the cluster allowlist, PITR and the NeMo securityContext onto `local.enforcing_posture` (`line 210`), the only remaining readers of `any_compliance_active` were `enforcing_posture` itself (for the `dev` tier) and `module.worm_bucket`'s `force_destroy` (`line 326`), while `enable_nist_compliance` was read alone only by the `deployment_summary.nist_compliance_enabled` output in [`outputs.tf`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/outputs.tf).
2. **False and unchecked descriptions.** The EU flag's description claimed GDPR Art. 32 CMEK enforcement, DORA Art. 10 audit logging, and EU AI Act Art. 9 risk management controls — all controlled by their own variables (`enable_cmek`, `enable_audit_logging`, `cage_deployment_region`). Each flag's description also stated that it applied to its own jurisdiction only ("US_FED only", "EU_ECB only", "APAC_MAS only"), and nothing checked that: `enable_eu_ecb_compliance = true` planned cleanly in a `US_FED` `us-central1` prod deployment, and `enable_nist_compliance = true` planned cleanly in an `EU_ECB` `europe-west1` one (verified `2026-11-03`, mock providers). By contrast, `var.cage_deployment_region` itself is checked against geography by the Langfuse bucket's data-residency precondition (`main.tf` (49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf) `lines 279–285`), the WORM bucket's precondition ([`worm_bucket/main.tf`](../infra/modules/worm_bucket/main.tf)), and the KMS key ring's location precondition ([`kms/main.tf`](../infra/modules/kms/main.tf) `lines 47–50`).
3. **Why the flags were deleted rather than derived from `cage_deployment_region`.** The shipped profiles split `dev` across jurisdictions: [`us-dev.tfvars`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/us-dev.tfvars) sets `cage_deployment_region = "US_FED"` and is intentionally non-enforcing (`enforcing_posture = false`), while [`eu-dev.tfvars`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/eu-dev.tfvars) (`EU_ECB`) and [`apac-dev.tfvars`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/apac-dev.tfvars) (`APAC_MAS`) enforce in `dev`. Deriving a boolean from `cage_deployment_region` (`contains(["US_FED", "EU_ECB", "APAC_MAS"], var.cage_deployment_region)`) would force `us-dev.tfvars` into `enforcing_posture = true` (requiring `authorized_networks`, distinct compliance keys, a 2555-day locked WORM bucket, and Valkey AOF in US dev), while gating the derivation on `var.environment != "dev"` would drop `eu-dev.tfvars` and `apac-dev.tfvars` out of the enforcing set. The only question the flags still answered was whether a `dev` plan enforces.

**Remediation (`2026-11-03`, `#651`, pending merge):**
1. **Replaced the three flags and `any_compliance_active` with `variable "enforce_in_dev"`.**
   - [`infra/targets/gcp-gke/variables.tf`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/variables.tf) (`lines 65–71`) declares `variable "enforce_in_dev"` (`type = bool`, `default = false`, `nullable = false`), and removes `enable_nist_compliance`, `enable_eu_ecb_compliance` and `enable_apac_mas_compliance`.
   - `infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf` (49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf) defines `enforcing_posture = var.environment == "prod" || var.environment == "staging" || var.enforce_in_dev` (`line 210`) and `force_destroy = !local.enforcing_posture` (`line 326`), and removes `any_compliance_active` (`lines 382–389`).
   - [`infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/variables.tf`](../infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/variables.tf) (`line 47`) and [`infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf`](../infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf) (`line 158`) rename the module input from `enable_nist_compliance` to `restrict_control_plane`, wired as `restrict_control_plane = local.enforcing_posture` at `infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf` (49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf) (`line 93`).
   - [`infra/targets/gcp-gke/outputs.tf`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/outputs.tf) (`line 100`) replaces `deployment_summary.nist_compliance_enabled` with `enforcing_posture = local.enforcing_posture`.
2. **Profiles, scripts, and CI gates.**
   - Every `dev` profile sets `enforce_in_dev` explicitly (`false` in [`dev.tfvars`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/dev.tfvars) `line 26` and [`us-dev.tfvars`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/us-dev.tfvars) `line 86`; `true` in [`eu-dev.tfvars`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/eu-dev.tfvars) `line 86` and [`apac-dev.tfvars`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/apac-dev.tfvars) `line 86`). Every `staging` and `prod` profile ([`staging.tfvars`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/staging.tfvars), [`prod.tfvars`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/prod.tfvars), [`eu-prod.tfvars`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/eu-prod.tfvars), [`apac-prod.tfvars`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/apac-prod.tfvars)) sets `environment` and neither `enforce_in_dev` nor any removed flag.
   - [`deploy_all.sh`](../deploy_all.sh) (`lines 378–443`), [`scripts/staging_lifecycle.sh`](../scripts/staging_lifecycle.sh) (`lines 1097–1103`), and [`scripts/bootstrap_gcp_project.sh`](../scripts/bootstrap_gcp_project.sh) (`lines 201–203`) no longer pass `-var=enable_nist_compliance=...`.
   - [`.github/workflows/eu-ecb-compliance.yml`](../.github/workflows/eu-ecb-compliance.yml), [`.github/workflows/apac-mas-compliance.yml`](../.github/workflows/apac-mas-compliance.yml), [`scripts/check_eu_ecb_posture.py`](../scripts/check_eu_ecb_posture.py), and [`scripts/check_apac_mas_posture.py`](../scripts/check_apac_mas_posture.py) verify `environment = "prod"` in `eu-prod.tfvars` / `apac-prod.tfvars` and `enforce_in_dev = true` in `eu-dev.tfvars` / `apac-dev.tfvars`.
3. **Tests.**
   - [`test_control_plane_access.py`](../tests/infrastructure/test_control_plane_access.py) asserts that none of `enable_nist_compliance`, `enable_eu_ecb_compliance`, `enable_apac_mas_compliance` or `any_compliance_active` appears in non-comment HCL lines across `infra/targets/gcp-gke/*.tf` and `infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/*.tf`; that only `enforcing_posture` reads `var.enforce_in_dev`; that `restrict_control_plane = local.enforcing_posture` and `force_destroy = !local.enforcing_posture`; and that every committed `.tfvars` profile sets `environment`, sets no removed flag, and sets `enforce_in_dev` iff `environment == "dev"`.
   - All five `.tftest.hcl` suites ([`posture_guards.tftest.hcl`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/tests/posture_guards.tftest.hcl), [`staging_compliance.tftest.hcl`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/tests/staging_compliance.tftest.hcl), [`poam_019.tftest.hcl`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/tests/poam_019.tftest.hcl), [`retention_posture.tftest.hcl`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/tests/retention_posture.tftest.hcl), [`governance_persistence.tftest.hcl`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/tests/governance_persistence.tftest.hcl)) run against `enforce_in_dev` and `enforcing_posture`.

**Closure Criteria:**
1. Met. `enable_nist_compliance`, `enable_eu_ecb_compliance`, `enable_apac_mas_compliance` and `any_compliance_active` do not appear in any active `.tf` or `.tfvars` line in `infra/`.
2. Met. `terraform test` (86 tests across 8 files) passes on Terraform 1.15.8 and 1.17.1, and `test_control_plane_access.py`, `test_gke_state_isolation.py`, `test_staging_lifecycle.py` and `test_ci_gate_effectiveness.py` pass.
3. Open. The merge commit SHA is recorded here, and the item moves to Closed Findings.

### POAM-2026-182: `database_encryption` in `ignore_changes` Is a Required `hashicorp/google` Provider-Enum Workaround, and Dead `database_encryption_state` Variable

**Control:** NIST SC-28 (Protection of Information at Rest), CM-6 (Configuration Settings), SA-11 (Developer Testing and Evaluation)
**Risk Level:** Low
**Status:** Open (`database_encryption_state` removal and self-firing CI detector [`test_gke_database_encryption_workaround.py`](../tests/infrastructure/test_gke_database_encryption_workaround.py) implemented and live-mutation verified `2026-11-04` on `fix/gke-encryption-latch-split`, pending merge; `database_encryption` in `ignore_changes` remains required until `hashicorp/google` supports `"ALL_OBJECTS_ENCRYPTION_ENABLED"`). Split from `POAM-2026-174` item 2 on `2026-11-04`
**Date Opened:** 2026-11-04
**Target Closure:** 2026-12-31

**Description:**
1. **Why this row is split from `POAM-2026-174`:** `POAM-2026-174` item 2 originally grouped `database_encryption` and `master_authorized_networks_config` in `lifecycle.ignore_changes` on `resource "google_container_cluster" "primary"` ([`infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf`](../infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf) `lines 312–319`) under a single verdict ("remove both from `ignore_changes`"). Comparing the live `<PROJECT_ID>` cluster, the `gs://<PROJECT_ID>-tfstate/cage/gcp-gke/cage-staging/default.tfstate` (`serial: 695`) state file, `hashicorp/google` `v6.50.0`, and `<PROJECT_ID>` Cloud Audit Logs on `2026-11-04` established that the two attributes have **opposite verdicts**: `master_authorized_networks_config` is a latch that hides control-plane allowlist drift (`POAM-2026-174`, `POAM-2026-183`), whereas `database_encryption` in `ignore_changes` is a **required workaround** for a `hashicorp/google` `v6.50.0` schema enum that trails the GKE 1.35+ API.
2. **GKE 1.35+ API state vs. `hashicorp/google` `v6.50.0` schema enum:**
   - [`infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf`](../infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf) (`lines 186–194`) configures `dynamic "database_encryption"` with `state = "ENCRYPTED"` and `key_name = var.kms_key_id` whenever `var.kms_key_id != ""`.
   - On GKE 1.35+ (`1.35.8-gke.1796000` on `<PROJECT_ID>`), enabling CMEK causes the GKE API to return `databaseEncryption.state = "ALL_OBJECTS_ENCRYPTION_ENABLED"` (with `lastOperationErrors = []`) rather than `"ENCRYPTED"`.
   - In `hashicorp/google` `v6.50.0` (`terraform-provider-google_v6.50.0_x5`), the schema description for `google_container_cluster.primary.database_encryption.state` is `"ENCRYPTED or DECRYPTED."`, `strings` on the provider binary returns zero matches for `ALL_OBJECTS_ENCRYPTION_ENABLED`, and `terraform validate -json` rejects `state = "ALL_OBJECTS_ENCRYPTION_ENABLED"` with `expected database_encryption.0.state to be one of ["ENCRYPTED" "DECRYPTED"], got ALL_OBJECTS_ENCRYPTION_ENABLED`.
3. **Cloud Audit Log proof from `<PROJECT_ID>` (`2026-09-29`) that removing `database_encryption` from `ignore_changes` causes a perpetual ~9-minute `UpdateCluster` loop:**
   - Before commit `36b6bd60` (`#323`, `2026-09-29T17:31:28Z`) added `database_encryption` to `ignore_changes`, `Terraform/1.15.8` (`terraform-provider-google/6.50.0`) ran four consecutive `google.container.v1beta1.ClusterManager.UpdateCluster` operations sending `update.desiredDatabaseEncryption = {keyName: "projects/<PROJECT_ID>/locations/us-central1/keyRings/cage-keyring-staging/cryptoKeys/cage-cmek-staging", state: "ENCRYPTED"}`:

| # | Start (UTC) | End (UTC) | Duration | GKE Operation ID | Result on subsequent `GET` |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 0 | `2026-09-29T02:43:29Z` | `2026-09-29T02:43:29Z` | `0s` | — (admission error `code: 9`) | Failed precondition (`service-<num>@container-engine-robot.iam.gserviceaccount.com` lacked `cloudkms.cryptoKeyVersions.useToDecrypt`/`useToEncrypt`) |
| 1 | `2026-09-29T02:47:06Z` | `2026-09-29T02:56:15Z` | `9m 09s` | `operation-1790650026367-cff27ed3-efc9-4d43-ad85-daa95867919f` | Enabled CMEK on `cage-staging`; GKE returned `state = "ALL_OBJECTS_ENCRYPTION_ENABLED"` |
| 2 | `2026-09-29T02:57:56Z` | `2026-09-29T03:07:04Z` | `9m 08s` | `operation-1790650676165-2124c543-b067-42f3-b77a-769e7a405bc3` | Next `terraform apply` (1m 41s later) saw `"ALL_OBJECTS_ENCRYPTION_ENABLED" -> "ENCRYPTED"`, re-sent `desiredDatabaseEncryption`, ran 9m 08s, and GKE still returned `"ALL_OBJECTS_ENCRYPTION_ENABLED"` |
| 3 | `2026-09-29T03:20:16Z` | `2026-09-29T03:29:22Z` | `9m 07s` | `operation-1790652016136-f7630c4a-01b8-4e41-860b-40394c5891c9` | 3rd `terraform apply` re-sent `desiredDatabaseEncryption`, ran 9m 07s, and GKE still returned `"ALL_OBJECTS_ENCRYPTION_ENABLED"` |
| 4 | `2026-09-29T03:30:34Z` | `2026-09-29T03:39:33Z` | `8m 58s` | `operation-1790652634725-0b172b62-a92c-4efc-9318-34b82127b768` | 4th `terraform apply` re-sent `desiredDatabaseEncryption`, ran 8m 58s, and GKE still returned `"ALL_OBJECTS_ENCRYPTION_ENABLED"` |

4. **Why narrowing `ignore_changes` to `database_encryption[0].state` fails:**
   - Tested on `2026-11-04` against `hashicorp/google` `v6.50.0` (Terraform 1.15.8): when a cluster in state has `database_encryption = [{"state": "DECRYPTED", "key_name": ""}]` and `var.kms_key_id` is then set, `ignore_changes = [database_encryption[0].state]` causes Terraform to preserve `state = "DECRYPTED"` from state while planning `+ key_name = "projects/.../cryptoKeys/cage-cmek-staging"`. Sending `state = "DECRYPTED"` with a non-empty `key_name` is an invalid GKE API request, so `[0].state` narrowing neither remediates an unencrypted cluster nor fails cleanly.
5. **Dead `database_encryption_state` variable (`030b18d2:infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/variables.tf` `lines 176–180`, `030b18d2:infra/targets/gcp-gke/variables.tf` `lines 132–136`, `030b18d2:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf` `line 109`):**
   - Both `030b18d2:infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/variables.tf` (`lines 176–180`) and `030b18d2:infra/targets/gcp-gke/variables.tf` (`lines 132–136`) declared `variable "database_encryption_state"` (`default = "ENCRYPTED"`, description `"State of etcd encryption (ENCRYPTED or DECRYPTED)"`), and `030b18d2:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf` (`line 109`) passed `database_encryption_state = var.database_encryption_state` into `module "gke"`, while [`infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf`](../infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf) (`line 191`) hardcoded `state = "ENCRYPTED"` and referenced `var.database_encryption_state` nowhere. The interface advertised a knob the implementation ignored.

**Remediation Plan:**
1. ✅ **Done (`2026-11-04`, `fix/gke-encryption-latch-split`, pending merge):**
   - Removed the dead `database_encryption_state` variable declarations from [`infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/variables.tf`](../infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/variables.tf) and [`infra/targets/gcp-gke/variables.tf`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/variables.tf) and the dead argument from `module "gke"` in `infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf` (49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf).
   - Documented `POAM-2026-182` directly on `dynamic "database_encryption"` (`lines 186–194`) and `lifecycle.ignore_changes` (`line 294`) in [`infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf`](../infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf).
   - Added [`tests/infrastructure/test_gke_database_encryption_workaround.py`](../tests/infrastructure/test_gke_database_encryption_workaround.py) and wired it into the required `terraform-test` job in [`.github/workflows/ci.yml`](../.github/workflows/ci.yml) (`python3 tests/infrastructure/test_gke_database_encryption_workaround.py --require-provider`).
   - **Live mutation verification (`PW-M1`–`PW-M6`, `2026-11-04`):** Both `assert_schema_lacks_all_objects_encryption()` and `run_live_provider_check()` were mutated directly against the live test runner (`uv run pytest tests/infrastructure/test_gke_database_encryption_workaround.py -k test_live_provider_schema_and_validator_when_initialized` and `python3 tests/infrastructure/test_gke_database_encryption_workaround.py --require-provider`) and pinned permanently in `test_run_live_provider_check_end_to_end_mutations`:

| Mutation ID | Fault injected into live `run_live_provider_check()` | `pytest` exit | `--require-provider` CLI exit | Observed failure message |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `PW-M1` | Stub `terraform providers schema -json` output to include `ALL_OBJECTS_ENCRYPTION_ENABLED` in `database_encryption.state.description` | `1` (FAILED) | `1` | `AssertionError: hashicorp/google provider schema now includes ALL_OBJECTS_ENCRYPTION_ENABLED in google_container_cluster.database_encryption.state.description!` |
| `PW-M2` | Stub `database_encryption.state.description` to `""` while `database_encryption.description` still lists `"ENCRYPTED"; "DECRYPTED"` (tests that one description cannot mask an empty lookup in the other) | `1` (FAILED) | `1` | `AssertionError: Provider schema database_encryption.state.description did not list ENCRYPTED and DECRYPTED (got ''); check cannot pass vacuously` |
| `PW-M3` | Stub `terraform providers schema -json` output to `{}` (empty lookup) | `1` (FAILED) | `1` | `AssertionError: Provider schema is missing google_container_cluster.database_encryption.state` |
| `PW-M4` | Stub `terraform validate -json` on `state = "ALL_OBJECTS_ENCRYPTION_ENABLED"` to `{"valid": true, "diagnostics": []}` | `1` (FAILED) | `1` | `AssertionError: hashicorp/google now accepts state = "ALL_OBJECTS_ENCRYPTION_ENABLED" on google_container_cluster.database_encryption!` |
| `PW-M5` | Stub `terraform validate -json` on `state = "ALL_OBJECTS_ENCRYPTION_ENABLED"` to fail for an unrelated reason | `1` (FAILED) | `1` | `AssertionError: terraform validate failed for an unexpected reason (did not reject ALL_OBJECTS_ENCRYPTION_ENABLED against ENCRYPTED/DECRYPTED)` |
| `PW-M6` | Stub control probe `terraform validate -json` on `state = "ENCRYPTED"` to `{"valid": false}` | `1` (FAILED) | `1` | `AssertionError: Control probe with state = "ENCRYPTED" failed validation: {'valid': False}` |

2. **When `hashicorp/google` is upgraded to a release that accepts `"ALL_OBJECTS_ENCRYPTION_ENABLED"`:** [`test_gke_database_encryption_workaround.py`](../tests/infrastructure/test_gke_database_encryption_workaround.py) will fail in CI (`terraform-test`), prompting removal of `database_encryption` from `lifecycle.ignore_changes` in [`infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf`](../infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf) (`line 313`) and alignment of `dynamic "database_encryption"` with the upgraded provider.

**Closure Criteria:**
1. Merge commit SHA for `database_encryption_state` removal and [`test_gke_database_encryption_workaround.py`](../tests/infrastructure/test_gke_database_encryption_workaround.py) is recorded here.
2. `hashicorp/google` is upgraded to a version whose `google_container_cluster.primary.database_encryption.state` enum accepts `"ALL_OBJECTS_ENCRYPTION_ENABLED"`, `database_encryption` is removed from `lifecycle.ignore_changes` in [`infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf`](../infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf), `terraform plan` against `<PROJECT_ID>` shows zero drift on `database_encryption`, and the merge commit SHA is recorded here.

### POAM-2026-183: Live `cage-staging` Control-Plane Allowlist Holds Three Unattributed `/32` CIDRs Applied Out-of-Band

**Control:** NIST AC-3 (Access Enforcement), SC-7(4) (Boundary Protection — Traffic Flow Policy Exceptions), CM-6 (Configuration Settings)
**Risk Level:** Moderate
**Status:** Open — verified live on `<PROJECT_ID>` (`cage-staging`), across all three GCS state files, and in Cloud Audit Logs on `2026-11-04`. **Blocks `POAM-2026-174` item 2 (removing `master_authorized_networks_config` from `ignore_changes`) and `POAM-2026-179`**
**Date Opened:** 2026-11-04
**Target Closure:** 2026-11-30

**Description:**
1. **Live cluster vs. GCS Terraform state files vs. committed `.tfvars` (`2026-11-04`, read-only inspection on `<PROJECT_ID>`):**
   - `gcloud container clusters describe cage-staging --location=us-central1-a --project=<PROJECT_ID> --format='json(masterAuthorizedNetworksConfig)'` returns:
     ```json
     {
       "masterAuthorizedNetworksConfig": {
         "cidrBlocks": [
           {"cidrBlock": "136.115.30.143/32"},
           {"cidrBlock": "10.128.0.70/32"},
           {"cidrBlock": "108.31.95.35/32"}
         ],
         "enabled": true,
         "gcpPublicCidrsAccessEnabled": false
       }
     }
     ```
   - Every one of the three live entries has an empty/omitted `displayName` (`""`).
   - Inspection of the three Terraform state objects in `gs://<PROJECT_ID>-tfstate` shows how the state evolved across the `POAM-2026-127` backend prefix migration:
     - **`gs://<PROJECT_ID>-tfstate/cage/gcp-gke/cage-staging/default.tfstate` (`serial: 695`, active state):** refreshed after the `2026-10-29` `gcloud` update; holds the same three `/32` CIDRs with `display_name = ""`:
       `[{"cidr_block": "10.128.0.70/32", "display_name": ""}, {"cidr_block": "108.31.95.35/32", "display_name": ""}, {"cidr_block": "136.115.30.143/32", "display_name": ""}]`.
     - **`gs://<PROJECT_ID>-tfstate/backups/cage-gcp-gke/default.tfstate.pre-migration-backup` (`serial: 689`, pre-migration backup from `2026-10-28` before the out-of-band `gcloud` updates):** holds `[{"cidr_block": "0.0.0.0/0", "display_name": "All (Initial Staging Deployment)"}]`, proving that `cage-staging` was latched at `0.0.0.0/0` in Terraform state until the manual `gcloud` updates on `2026-10-28`/`29`.
     - **`gs://<PROJECT_ID>-tfstate/cage/staging/default.tfstate` (`serial: 249`, orphaned legacy prefix from `2026-09-29T02:27:32Z`):** holds `[{"cidr_block": "136.115.30.143/32", "display_name": "thin-client"}]`.
   - None of the three CIDRs appears in any committed `.tfvars` profile ([`staging.tfvars`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/staging.tfvars) leaves `authorized_networks` unset so it defaults to `[]` in [`infra/targets/gcp-gke/variables.tf`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/variables.tf) `lines 219–226`) or in any local `.tfvars` file.
2. **Complete Cloud Audit Log provenance (`<PROJECT_ID>`, `2026-09-06`–`2026-10-29`):**
   - Querying `protoPayload.serviceName="container.googleapis.com" AND protoPayload.methodName=~"Cluster"` on `<PROJECT_ID>` shows every control-plane allowlist mutation across `cage-staging`'s history:

| Timestamp (UTC) | GKE Operation ID | Caller User-Agent | Method | Allowlist written to cluster |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `2026-09-06T19:11:08Z` | `operation-1788721868190-6fb2b4b5-b371-40d4-be22-ab3542d414cf` | `Terraform/1.15.8 (...; terraform-provider-google/6.50.0)` | `CreateCluster` | `masterAuthorizedNetworksConfig` omitted (control plane unrestricted at initial creation) |
| `2026-09-29T02:27:32Z` | `operation-1790648852414-a8136537-bd43-4e28-8fa1-2c1343343d33` | `Terraform/1.15.8 (...; terraform-provider-google/6.50.0)` | `CreateCluster` (separate state prefix `cage/staging`, `serial: 249`) | `[{"cidrBlock": "136.115.30.143/32", "displayName": "thin-client"}]` |
| `2026-09-29T02:34:02Z` | `operation-1790649242764-8cd6d470-0b5b-4bd4-8150-031772011a4f` | `Terraform/1.15.8 (...; terraform-provider-google/6.50.0)` | `UpdateCluster` | `[{"cidrBlock": "0.0.0.0/0", "displayName": "All (Dev Only)"}]` |
| `2026-09-29T03:30:24Z` | `operation-1790652623881-42e9c46c-acd0-4d14-9f91-89a74b201fb4` | `Terraform/1.15.8 (...; terraform-provider-google/6.50.0)` | `UpdateCluster` | `[{"cidrBlock": "0.0.0.0/0", "displayName": "All (Initial Staging Deployment)"}]` |
| `2026-09-29T03:47:08Z` | `operation-1790653628011-59096419-1623-48e6-b9de-99a095951003` | `Terraform/1.15.8 (...; terraform-provider-google/6.50.0)` | `UpdateCluster` | `[{"cidrBlock": "0.0.0.0/0", "displayName": "All (Initial Staging Deployment)"}]` (latched for 29 days after commit `36b6bd60` at `17:31:28Z` added `master_authorized_networks_config` to `ignore_changes`) |
| `2026-10-28T19:18:17Z` | `operation-1793215097467-21819817-6ef6-47a4-bcee-ff0df8d58ed9` | `google-cloud-sdk gcloud/561.0.0 command/gcloud.container.clusters.update` | `UpdateCluster` (`desiredMasterAuthorizedNetworksConfig`) | `[{"cidrBlock": "136.115.30.143/32"}, {"cidrBlock": "10.128.0.70/32"}]` (`displayName` stripped to `""` because `gcloud container clusters update --master-authorized-networks=...` accepts only comma-separated CIDRs) |
| `2026-10-29T22:19:40Z` | `operation-1793312380712-a4d64f2f-ac28-4dbc-86cc-9dc6694e2985` | `google-cloud-sdk gcloud/561.0.0 command/gcloud.container.clusters.update` | `UpdateCluster` (`desiredMasterAuthorizedNetworksConfig`) | `[{"cidrBlock": "136.115.30.143/32"}, {"cidrBlock": "10.128.0.70/32"}, {"cidrBlock": "108.31.95.35/32"}]` (`displayName` empty on all three) |

3. **Why this item blocks `POAM-2026-174` item 2 and `POAM-2026-179`:**
   - **Silent lockout / CIDR stripping on the next staging apply (`POAM-2026-174` item 2):** Because `master_authorized_networks_config` is currently in `lifecycle.ignore_changes` ([`infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf`](../infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf) `line 314`), Terraform ignores the two out-of-band `gcloud` updates. The day `POAM-2026-174` step 2 removes `master_authorized_networks_config` from `ignore_changes`, running [`scripts/staging_lifecycle.sh`](../scripts/staging_lifecycle.sh) (`line 534`, which detects only the caller's single public egress IPv4 and exports `TF_VAR_authorized_networks=[{"cidr":"<egress>/32","display_name":"deployer-egress"}]`) will plan and apply a replacement of `cidr_blocks` that **silently deletes `10.128.0.70/32` (an internal `10.128.0.0/20` `us-central1` default-VPC VM IP that `ifconfig.me` never returns) and `108.31.95.35/32`**, cutting off whichever runner or operator depends on them.
   - **Unattributed live state vs. `POAM-2026-179`:** All three live entries have `displayName = ""` because `gcloud container clusters update --master-authorized-networks` cannot set `display_name`. Even after `POAM-2026-179` enforces an owner format on `var.authorized_networks` in Terraform, any entry applied out-of-band via `gcloud` while `master_authorized_networks_config` is ignored (or between Terraform runs) carries an empty `displayName` on the live cluster.

**Remediation Plan:**
1. Identify the owner and purpose of each of the three live `<PROJECT_ID>` CIDRs (`136.115.30.143/32` = `thin-client` external egress; `10.128.0.70/32` = internal `us-central1` VPC VM address; `108.31.95.35/32` = external operator/workstation egress added `2026-10-29T22:19:40Z`), revoke any CIDR that no longer has an active need, and record the remaining active CIDRs with explicit `display_name` values in the staging deployment inputs (`terraform.auto.tfvars` or the reviewed allowlist source under `POAM-2026-178` / `POAM-2026-179`) before removing `master_authorized_networks_config` from `ignore_changes` (`POAM-2026-174` step 2).
2. Update [`scripts/staging_lifecycle.sh`](../scripts/staging_lifecycle.sh) (`line 534`, `TF_VAR_authorized_networks`) so that when `cage-staging` is updated from `thin-client` or when persistent staging runner CIDRs (such as `136.115.30.143/32` / `10.128.0.70/32`) are configured in `terraform.auto.tfvars`, the script merges or preserves those persistent CIDRs rather than overwriting the entire allowlist with a single `curl ifconfig.me` `/32`.

**Closure Criteria (Closure Path: `W` / `§W` Clean-Walk Cluster Replacement, `2026-11-05`):**
1. Clean-walk cluster replacement (`W`, `§W`) provisions `cage-staging` from Terraform with `master_authorized_networks_config` removed from `lifecycle.ignore_changes` (`PR-2` / `POAM-2026-174` step 2), and every CIDR in `gcloud container clusters describe cage-staging --location=us-central1-a --project=<PROJECT_ID> --format='json(masterAuthorizedNetworksConfig.cidrBlocks)'` has a non-empty `displayName` (`owner:purpose`) matching the Terraform-managed `authorized_networks` state with zero drift.
2. `POAM-2026-174` step 2 records that its `POAM-2026-183` prerequisite is satisfied via `W`, and `gcloud` output and the merge commit SHA are recorded here with the verification date.

### POAM-2026-184: Reference Terraform Interface Solicits `oscal_api_key` and Wires It Nowhere

**Control:** NIST CM-6 (Configuration Settings), SA-11 (Developer Testing and Evaluation)
**Risk Level:** Low
**Status:** Open (`oscal_api_key` removal and [`infra/load_env.sh`](../infra/load_env.sh) comment correction implemented and verified `2026-11-04` on `fix/poam-184-185-bootstrap`, pending merge)
**Date Opened:** 2026-11-04
**Target Closure:** 2026-11-30

**Description:**
1. **Reference Terraform interface asked operators for `oscal_api_key` and wired it nowhere:**
   - `2bacc478:infra/targets/gcp-gke/variables.tf` (`lines 758–763`) solicited `variable "oscal_api_key"` (`type = string`, `sensitive = true`, `default = ""`, `description = "OSCAL API key (set via gitignored terraform.auto.tfvars)"`).
   - `2bacc478:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf` (`line 1195`) passed `oscal_api_key = var.oscal_api_key` into `module "app_secrets"`.
   - `2bacc478:infra/modules/app_secrets/variables.tf` (`lines 128–132`) declared `variable "oscal_api_key"` (`sensitive = true`), and [`infra/modules/app_secrets/main.tf`](../infra/modules/app_secrets/main.tf) (`lines 78–104`, `kubernetes_secret.compliance_bridge_secrets` and `kubernetes_secret.compliance_secrets`) **never referenced `var.oscal_api_key`**.
   - In Python runtime code, `src/compliance_bridge/` never reads `OSCAL_API_KEY`: [`src/compliance_bridge/auth.py`](../src/compliance_bridge/auth.py) (`lines 114–175`, `require_internal_token`) authenticates internal endpoints solely against `CAGE_INTERNAL_TOKEN` or `COMPLIANCE_BRIDGE_INTERNAL_TOKEN` (populated from `var.cage_internal_token` in `kubernetes_secret.compliance_bridge_secrets` at [`infra/modules/app_secrets/main.tf`](../infra/modules/app_secrets/main.tf) `lines 78–89`).
   - Because CAGE is a reference architecture, someone reading or copying `variables.tf` and `main.tf` was taught that the OSCAL compliance bridge is authenticated by a separate `oscal_api_key` credential when the module silently discarded the input.
2. **Misleading `TF_VAR_` export comment in `2bacc478:infra/load_env.sh` (`lines 52–53`):**
   - `2bacc478:infra/load_env.sh` (`lines 52–53`) stated `# Export as both the original name and TF_VAR_ prefixed version` immediately above `export "$var_name=$var_value"`, but only exported `TF_VAR_*` for keys explicitly listed in its `case "$var_name"` block (`lines 56–157`). Any `.env` variable not in that `case` block was exported only under its original uppercase name, never as `TF_VAR_<lowercase>`.

**Remediation Plan:**
1. ✅ **Done (`2026-11-04`, `fix/poam-184-185-bootstrap`, pending merge):**
   - Removed `variable "oscal_api_key"` from `2bacc478:infra/targets/gcp-gke/variables.tf` (`lines 758–763`, [`infra/targets/gcp-gke/variables.tf`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/variables.tf)) and `2bacc478:infra/modules/app_secrets/variables.tf` (`lines 128–132`, [`infra/modules/app_secrets/variables.tf`](../infra/modules/app_secrets/variables.tf)), and removed `oscal_api_key = var.oscal_api_key` from `module "app_secrets"` in `2bacc478:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf` (`line 1195`, `infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf` (49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf)).
   - Fixed the comment in [`infra/load_env.sh`](../infra/load_env.sh) (`lines 52–56`) so it accurately states that variables are exported under their original name and only keys mapped in the `case` statement below are also exported with a `TF_VAR_` prefix.
   - Added `TestDeadOscalApiKeyRemoval` (`test_no_terraform_file_declares_or_references_oscal_api_key` and `test_load_env_sh_accurately_documents_selective_tf_var_mapping`) in [`tests/infrastructure/test_gke_sole_target_6f.py`](../tests/infrastructure/test_gke_sole_target_6f.py).

**Closure Criteria:**
1. `oscal_api_key` is absent from `infra/targets/` and `infra/modules/`, enforced by `TestDeadOscalApiKeyRemoval` (49c0d09a:tests/infrastructure/test_gke_sole_target_6f.py).
2. Merge commit SHA is recorded here.

### POAM-2026-186: 43 Remaining Unreferenced `variable` Declarations and 17 Dead Module-Call Arguments Across `infra/`

**Control:** NIST CM-6 (Configuration Settings), SA-11 (Developer Testing and Evaluation)
**Risk Level:** Moderate
**Status:** Open — full per-module inventory verified across all 22 remaining Terraform directories in `infra/` on `2026-11-04`
**Date Opened:** 2026-11-04
**Target Closure:** 2026-12-31

**Description:**
1. **Why dead Terraform variables matter in a reference architecture:** In a deployed stack, 39 unreferenced `variable` declarations and 13 dead `module` call arguments are minor operational debt; in a reference architecture whose `variables.tf` and `main.tf` exist to be read and copied as an example, they are a defect in the deliverable. Anyone copying `variables.tf` or `main.tf` inherits a dead interface advertising 39 knobs (`database_url`, `redis_password`, `governance_salt`, `binary_authorization_enforcement_mode`, `cage_kms_provider`, `gpu_node_pool_spot`, etc.) that the implementation silently ignores (the same defect class as `database_encryption_state` in `POAM-2026-182` and `oscal_api_key` in `POAM-2026-184`).

   - **Child modules — 23 unreferenced `variable` declarations across 7 modules, and 13 dead module-call arguments in `infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf` (49c0d09a:infra/targets/gcp-gke/main.tf):**

**Remediation Plan:**
1. Remove all unreferenced `variable` declarations and dead module-call arguments across `infra/modules/` and `infra/targets/gcp-gke/` (alongside any stale assignments in `.tfvars` / `.tftest.hcl` files and [`infra/load_env.sh`](../infra/load_env.sh)), or wire any variable whose underlying feature was unintentionally disconnected (such as `gpu_node_pool_spot` in [`infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf`](../infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf) or resource requests/limits in [`infra/modules/compliance_bridge/main.tf`](../infra/modules/compliance_bridge/main.tf)).
