**Closure Criteria:**
1. Zero unreferenced `variable` declarations exist across `infra/**/variables.tf`, enforced by a regression test in `tests/infrastructure/`.
2. `terraform test` in `infra/targets/gcp-gke` passes, and the merge commit SHA is recorded here.

### POAM-2026-187: GKE Node Pools Omit `node_config.service_account` and Default to the Over-Privileged Compute Engine Default Service Account

**Control:** NIST AC-6 (Least Privilege), AU-9 (Protection of Audit Information), AU-10 (Non-Repudiation), CM-6 (Configuration Settings)
**Risk Level:** High
**Status:** Open (Step 1 IaC node-pool rebind implemented `2026-11-04`; Step 2 live cluster walk + Step 3 revocation of `<PROJECT_NUMBER>-compute@developer.gserviceaccount.com`'s `storage.admin` and `serviceAccountTokenCreator` bindings outstanding)
**Date Opened:** 2026-11-04
**Target Closure:** 2026-11-30

**Description:**
Every `node_config` block in `2bacc478:infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf` omitted `service_account` while setting `oauth_scopes = ["https://www.googleapis.com/auth/cloud-platform"]`:
- `resource "google_container_cluster" "primary"` (`2bacc478:infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf` `lines 279–284`, temporary default node pool with `remove_default_node_pool = true`);
- `resource "google_container_node_pool" "primary_nodes"` (`2bacc478:infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf` `lines 333–374`);
- `resource "google_container_node_pool" "general_spot_nodes"` (`2bacc478:infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf` `lines 408–443`);
- `resource "google_container_node_pool" "gpu_nodes"` (`2bacc478:infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf` `lines 486–544`);
- `resource "google_container_node_pool" "clickhouse_nodes"` (`2bacc478:infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf` `lines 604–647`);
- `resource "google_container_node_pool" "extra_pools"` (`2bacc478:infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf` `lines 681–727`).

1. **Why omitting `node_config.service_account` violates `AC-6`, `AU-9`, and `AU-10` on live `<PROJECT_ID>` (`2026-11-04`):**
   - When `node_config.service_account` is omitted, GKE attaches the project's Compute Engine default service account (`<PROJECT_NUMBER>-compute@developer.gserviceaccount.com`) to every node VM. With `oauth_scopes = ["https://www.googleapis.com/auth/cloud-platform"]`, the node's effective permissions equal the IAM roles granted to that service account.
   - Read-only `gcloud` inspection on live `<PROJECT_ID>` (`2026-11-04`: `gcloud container clusters describe cage-staging --zone=us-central1-a`, `gcloud projects get-iam-policy <PROJECT_ID>`, and `gcloud iam service-accounts get-iam-policy` on `cage-gateway@<PROJECT_ID>.iam.gserviceaccount.com` and `sa-cage-deployer@<PROJECT_ID>.iam.gserviceaccount.com`) verified that all four live node pools (`primary-pool`, `general-spot`, `gpu-l4`, `clickhouse`) run as `<PROJECT_NUMBER>-compute@developer.gserviceaccount.com` (`serviceAccount: "default"`), which holds:
     - Project-level `roles/editor`, `roles/storage.admin`, `roles/container.admin`, `roles/compute.admin`, `roles/cloudkms.admin`, `roles/artifactregistry.admin`, `roles/secretmanager.admin`, and `roles/logging.admin`;
     - Direct `roles/iam.serviceAccountTokenCreator` on `cage-gateway@<PROJECT_ID>.iam.gserviceaccount.com` (`google_service_account.gateway.email`, the sole KMS signer on `google_kms_crypto_key.gateway_seal` in [`infra/targets/gcp-gke/kms_signing.tf`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/kms_signing.tf) `lines 57–74, 152–164`);
     - Direct `roles/iam.serviceAccountTokenCreator` on `sa-cage-deployer@<PROJECT_ID>.iam.gserviceaccount.com` (the owner-equivalent deployer tracked in `POAM-2026-165`).
   - Although workloads use GKE Workload Identity (`workload_metadata_config { mode = "GKE_METADATA" }`), any compromise of a node VM, kubelet, or privileged `hostNetwork` DaemonSet reaches the underlying GCE metadata server as `<PROJECT_NUMBER>-compute@developer.gserviceaccount.com`. Because project-level `roles/storage.admin` is additive with and overrides bucket-level IAM exclusivity on `gs://<PROJECT_ID>-evidence-worm-staging`, and `roles/iam.serviceAccountTokenCreator` on `cage-gateway@<PROJECT_ID>.iam.gserviceaccount.com` mints tokens for the `gateway-seal` KMS key directly, **a single node compromise suffices to both forge `gateway-seal` signatures (`AU-10`) and control the WORM evidence bucket (`AU-9`) without even traversing `sa-cage-deployer`**.

2. **Coupled architectural decisions (`CP-5` in [`docs/BREAKING_CHANGES.md`](BREAKING_CHANGES.md), Decisions A3 and A4):**
   - **Why this is a breaking change (`CP-5`):** In `hashicorp/google`, `node_config.service_account` is `ForceNew`. Changing it from omitted (`"default"`) to a dedicated GSA forces `google_container_cluster.primary` and every `google_container_node_pool` in any existing deployment to be **destroyed and recreated (`-/+`)**.
   - **Decision A3 (Minimal IAM roles on the dedicated node GSA):** [`infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf`](../infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf) (`lines 784–833`, with `locals` and resources at `lines 811–833`) provisions `resource "google_service_account" "gke_nodes"` (`account_id = "cage-gke-nodes-${var.environment}"`) and grants it **only** two project-level roles:
     1. `roles/container.defaultNodeServiceAccount` (`google_project_iam_member.gke_nodes_default_node_sa`, covering Cloud Logging, Cloud Monitoring, and node-metadata reporting per Google's GKE hardening guidance); and
     2. `roles/artifactregistry.reader` (`google_project_iam_member.gke_nodes_artifact_registry_reader`, required for kubelet / containerd to pull container images from Artifact Registry).
     Both `google_container_cluster.primary` (`lines 301–304`) and `google_container_node_pool.primary_nodes` (`lines 393–397`) declare `depends_on` on both IAM bindings so node creation cannot race ahead of node-SA role propagation, and [`infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/outputs.tf`](../infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/outputs.tf) (`lines 88–91`) exposes `output "node_service_account_email"`.
   - **Decision A4 (Why `oauth_scopes = ["https://www.googleapis.com/auth/cloud-platform"]` stayed — recorded in [`infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf`](../infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf) `lines 378`, `549`, and `799–809`):** Every `node_config` block retains `oauth_scopes = ["https://www.googleapis.com/auth/cloud-platform"]`. Legacy GCE OAuth scopes are a coarse, pre-IAM capability ceiling on the VM metadata token (`effective_permissions = oauth_scope ∩ iam_roles`). When `service_account` was omitted (`"default"`), `cloud-platform` exposed every IAM role granted to `<PROJECT_NUMBER>-compute@developer.gserviceaccount.com`. Once `service_account = local.node_service_account_email` binds a dedicated, minimal GSA, Google's GKE node-hardening guidance explicitly recommends setting `oauth_scopes = ["https://www.googleapis.com/auth/cloud-platform"]` and enforcing least privilege strictly through the dedicated GSA's IAM roles (`Decision A3`), rather than maintaining legacy per-API OAuth scope lists (`logging.write`, `monitoring`, `devstorage.read_only`) that neither cover Artifact Registry v2 endpoints cleanly nor add an authorization boundary beyond IAM. Recording this rationale directly in [`infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf`](../infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf) (`lines 378`, `549`, `799–809`), [`infra/targets/gcp-gke/tests/w6.tftest.hcl`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/tests/w6.tftest.hcl) (`lines 327–344`), and [`tests/infrastructure/test_gke_node_pool_service_account.py`](../tests/infrastructure/test_gke_node_pool_service_account.py) ensures the retained `cloud-platform` scope on the diff is documented as a deliberate architectural invariant rather than an unfinished half of `A4`.
   - **Why rebinding node pools does NOT de-privilege `<PROJECT_NUMBER>-compute@developer.gserviceaccount.com` itself:** Rebinding `node_config.service_account` to `cage-gke-nodes-${var.environment}` changes which identity GKE nodes run as after `terraform apply`, **not** what `<PROJECT_NUMBER>-compute@developer.gserviceaccount.com` holds on `<PROJECT_ID>`. Furthermore, [`infra/targets/gcp-gke/iam.tf`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/iam.tf) (`lines 384–455`) manages `google_service_account_iam_binding` only for `role = "roles/iam.workloadIdentityUser"` and uses additive `google_project_iam_member` for project-level roles; `roles/iam.serviceAccountTokenCreator` is not managed in `infra/targets/gcp-gke/` at all. Consequently, `terraform apply` neither detects nor revokes `<PROJECT_NUMBER>-compute@developer.gserviceaccount.com`'s existing `roles/iam.serviceAccountTokenCreator` bindings on `cage-gateway@<PROJECT_ID>.iam.gserviceaccount.com` and `sa-cage-deployer@<PROJECT_ID>.iam.gserviceaccount.com` or its project-level `roles/storage.admin` (plus `roles/editor`, `roles/container.admin`, `roles/compute.admin`, `roles/cloudkms.admin`). Any future workload in `<PROJECT_ID>` that defaults to `<PROJECT_NUMBER>-compute@developer.gserviceaccount.com` — a Cloud Build step that omits `serviceAccount`, a GCE VM, or a Cloud Run service deployed without an explicit runtime SA — inherits the exact same `AU-9` / `AU-10` collapse until those bindings are explicitly revoked (Closure Criterion 4 below and `POAM-2026-165` Closure Criterion 3).
   - **Explicit warrant distinction (weaker warrant than `EF8b`):** Unlike `EF8b` in `POAM-2026-164` — where the missing `service-<num>@gcp-sa-memorystore.iam.gserviceaccount.com` binding on `google_kms_crypto_key.cmek_key` was established from an observed `400 Permission 'cloudkms.cryptoKeyVersions.useToEncrypt' denied` during Stage 1 `terraform apply` on `<WALK_PROJECT_ID>` (`2026-11-01`) and verified when granting `roles/cloudkms.cryptoKeyEncrypterDecrypter` resolved the failure (`6 added, 0 changed, 0 destroyed`) — **the two-role set on `google_service_account.gke_nodes` cannot be established from a fired `PERMISSION_DENIED` walk here**. It is derived from Google's GKE node-hardening documentation and has **not** yet been exercised by a live cluster creation + Artifact Registry image pull + DaemonSet/workload scheduling walk (`Step A2` on `<WALK_PROJECT_ID>` stopped at `EF10` before `CreateCluster` created nodes). `terraform test` (`command = plan`) and unit tests verify only that the HCL plans the expected resources and attributes; they cannot verify that no node-level agent, CSI driver, or GPU driver installer on GKE 1.35 requires an additional role at runtime. That is a weaker warrant, and `POAM-2026-187` therefore remains **Open** until both the live cluster/node-pool walk and the revocation of `<PROJECT_NUMBER>-compute@developer.gserviceaccount.com`'s privileges complete.

3. **Static & plan-time verification (`2026-11-04`, mutation table `NP-M1`–`NP-M4`):**
   - Guarded by [`infra/targets/gcp-gke/tests/w6.tftest.hcl`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/tests/w6.tftest.hcl) (`run "gke_module_binds_dedicated_node_service_account_to_every_node_pool"`) and [`tests/infrastructure/test_gke_node_pool_service_account.py`](../tests/infrastructure/test_gke_node_pool_service_account.py) (`12 passed`).

| Mutant ID | Injected Mutation in [`infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf`](../infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf) | Killing Test(s) | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| `NP-M1` | Omit `service_account = local.node_service_account_email` from `google_container_node_pool.gpu_nodes.node_config` (`line 521`) | `test_every_cluster_and_node_pool_sets_dedicated_service_account_and_cloud_platform_scope[google_container_node_pool-gpu_nodes]`; `terraform test`: `gke_module_binds_dedicated_node_service_account_to_every_node_pool` | **KILLED** |
| `NP-M2` | Add a third `google_project_iam_member` granting `roles/editor` to `local.node_service_account_email` | `test_node_service_account_grants_only_minimal_hardening_roles` | **KILLED** |
| `NP-M3` | Delete `resource "google_project_iam_member" "gke_nodes_artifact_registry_reader"` (`lines 800–804`) | `test_node_service_account_grants_only_minimal_hardening_roles`, `test_cluster_and_primary_pool_depend_on_node_sa_iam_bindings` | **KILLED** |
| `NP-M4` | Narrow `oauth_scopes` on `primary_nodes.node_config` from `https://www.googleapis.com/auth/cloud-platform` to `https://www.googleapis.com/auth/logging.write` | `test_every_cluster_and_node_pool_sets_dedicated_service_account_and_cloud_platform_scope[google_container_node_pool-primary_nodes]`; `terraform test`: `gke_module_binds_dedicated_node_service_account_to_every_node_pool` | **KILLED** |

**Remediation Plan:**
1. ✅ **Step 1 (IaC & Breaking-Change Documentation — completed `2026-11-04`, pending merge):** Provision `google_service_account.gke_nodes` with `roles/container.defaultNodeServiceAccount` and `roles/artifactregistry.reader` in [`infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf`](../infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf) (`lines 755–804`), bind `service_account = local.node_service_account_email` and `oauth_scopes = ["https://www.googleapis.com/auth/cloud-platform"]` (with inline `Decision A4` rationale) across all six `node_config` blocks, expose `output "node_service_account_email"` in [`infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/outputs.tf`](../infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/outputs.tf), document breaking change `CP-5` in [`docs/BREAKING_CHANGES.md`](BREAKING_CHANGES.md), and guard with [`w6.tftest.hcl`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/tests/w6.tftest.hcl) and [`test_gke_node_pool_service_account.py`](../tests/infrastructure/test_gke_node_pool_service_account.py).
2. **Step 2 (Live Cluster / Node-Pool Walk — outstanding):** Exercise live node-pool creation, Artifact Registry image pull, and pod scheduling under `cage-gke-nodes-${var.environment}@<project>.iam.gserviceaccount.com` during the `POAM-2026-164` `Step A3` clean-project walk (and/or `cage-staging` node-pool replacement).
3. **Step 3 (Revoke `<PROJECT_NUMBER>-compute@developer.gserviceaccount.com` Privileges — outstanding, shared with `POAM-2026-165` step 3):** Revoke `<PROJECT_NUMBER>-compute@developer.gserviceaccount.com`'s `roles/iam.serviceAccountTokenCreator` bindings on `cage-gateway@<PROJECT_ID>.iam.gserviceaccount.com` and `sa-cage-deployer@<PROJECT_ID>.iam.gserviceaccount.com` and remove its project-level `roles/storage.admin`, `roles/editor`, `roles/container.admin`, `roles/compute.admin`, `roles/cloudkms.admin`, `roles/artifactregistry.admin`, and `roles/secretmanager.admin` bindings on `<PROJECT_ID>`, so no future workload defaulting to `<PROJECT_NUMBER>-compute@developer.gserviceaccount.com` inherits the `AU-9`/`AU-10` collapse.

**Closure Criteria:**
1. Merge commit SHA for Step 1 is recorded here.
2. Live GKE node-pool creation, Artifact Registry container image pull, and pod scheduling succeed with `node_config.service_account = "cage-gke-nodes-${var.environment}@<project>.iam.gserviceaccount.com"` and only `roles/container.defaultNodeServiceAccount` + `roles/artifactregistry.reader` (or any additional role proven necessary by a live `PERMISSION_DENIED` is added to [`infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf`](../infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf) and recorded here with the exact error output and date).
3. `gcloud container node-pools describe` output confirming no node pool runs as `default` / `<PROJECT_NUMBER>-compute@developer.gserviceaccount.com` is recorded here with the verification date.
4. `gcloud iam service-accounts get-iam-policy` output on `cage-gateway@<PROJECT_ID>.iam.gserviceaccount.com` and `sa-cage-deployer@<PROJECT_ID>.iam.gserviceaccount.com` and `gcloud projects get-iam-policy <PROJECT_ID>` output, recorded here with the verification date, confirm that `<PROJECT_NUMBER>-compute@developer.gserviceaccount.com` no longer holds `roles/iam.serviceAccountTokenCreator` on either service account and no longer holds project-level `roles/storage.admin`, `roles/editor`, or `roles/*Admin` (shared with `POAM-2026-165` Closure Criterion 3; neither row may close on the node-pool rebind alone).

### POAM-2026-188: `EU_AI_ACT.json` and `MAS_FEAT.json` Omit `UCA-10` and `UCA-12` From `uca_mappings`, Emitting Empty Regional Crosswalk Lines (`POAM-2026-150` Family)

**Control:** NIST SA-11 (Developer Testing and Evaluation), CA-2 (Control Assessments); EU AI Act Art. 9; DORA Art. 10; MAS FEAT; MAS TRM §6.3
**Risk Level:** Low
**Status:** Open — remediated `2026-11-08` on `fix/ssp-deployed-narratives` (mappings added, both regional SSPs regenerated, Remediation Plan items 1–2 done); closes when the merge commit SHA is recorded here (Closure Criterion 2). Discovered during `POAM-2026-177` regional SSP regeneration on `2026-11-04`
**Date Opened:** 2026-11-04
**Target Closure:** 2026-11-30

**Description:**
1. **Incomplete regional UCA crosswalk tables (`EU_AI_ACT.json` and `MAS_FEAT.json`):**
   - Merging [`config/stpa/core_system.yaml`](../config/stpa/core_system.yaml) and [`src/cage_finance/config/stpa/trade_hazards.yaml`](../src/cage_finance/config/stpa/trade_hazards.yaml) yields 12 active STPA Unsafe Control Actions (`UCA-1` through `UCA-10`, `UCA-12`, and `UCA-13`).
   - While [`config/oscal/framework_mappings/NIST_SP800_53.json`](../config/oscal/framework_mappings/NIST_SP800_53.json) (`lines 8–70`) and [`config/oscal/framework_mappings/ISO_42001.json`](../config/oscal/framework_mappings/ISO_42001.json) (`lines 8–64`) map all 12 UCAs, [`config/oscal/framework_mappings/EU_AI_ACT.json`](../config/oscal/framework_mappings/EU_AI_ACT.json) (`lines 8–49`) and [`config/oscal/framework_mappings/MAS_FEAT.json`](../config/oscal/framework_mappings/MAS_FEAT.json) (`lines 8–49`) map only 10 UCAs (`UCA-1`–`UCA-9` and `UCA-13`), omitting:
     - `UCA-10` (`wrong_timing`: agent reads account balance with stale connection data; mapped in `NIST_SP800_53.json` to `SI-10`, `CA-7`).
     - `UCA-12` (`unsafe_action`: agent executes bounded trade when bounding contracts `B1`–`B10` fail validation; mapped in `NIST_SP800_53.json` to `AC-3`, `SC-7`).
   - When `oscal_ssp_exporter.py` generates the `sa-11` (`g7000099-stpa-4000-8000-compiler00001`) `implemented-requirement` block for `EU_ECB` and `APAC_MAS`, both regional SSPs omit control citations for those two UCAs; [`compliance/oscal/system-security-plan-eu-ecb.yaml`](../compliance/oscal/system-security-plan-eu-ecb.yaml) (`lines 831–838`) and [`compliance/oscal/system-security-plan-apac-mas.yaml`](../compliance/oscal/system-security-plan-apac-mas.yaml) (`lines 466–473`) emit empty `→ EU_AI_ACT:` and `→ MAS_FEAT:` crosswalk lines for `UCA-10` and `UCA-12`.
2. **Why existing tests missed the gap (`POAM-2026-150` family):**
   - `dd272b23^:tests/test_framework_router.py` (`line 62`) hardcoded `_EXPECTED_UCAS = [f"UCA-{i}" for i in range(1, 10)]` (only `UCA-1`–`UCA-9`), never loading the active control structures.
   - `TestUcaMappings` in [`tests/test_oscal_ssp_exporter.py`](../tests/test_oscal_ssp_exporter.py) (`lines 994–1014`) loads the full merged control structure (`_load_full_cs()`), but only asserts `uca.id in mappings` for `NIST` and `ISO42001`, omitting `EU_AI_ACT` and `MAS_FEAT`.

**Remediation Plan:**
1. Add statutory/principle mappings for `UCA-10` and `UCA-12` to `uca_mappings` in [`config/oscal/framework_mappings/EU_AI_ACT.json`](../config/oscal/framework_mappings/EU_AI_ACT.json) and [`config/oscal/framework_mappings/MAS_FEAT.json`](../config/oscal/framework_mappings/MAS_FEAT.json), and regenerate [`compliance/oscal/system-security-plan-eu-ecb.yaml`](../compliance/oscal/system-security-plan-eu-ecb.yaml) and [`compliance/oscal/system-security-plan-apac-mas.yaml`](../compliance/oscal/system-security-plan-apac-mas.yaml).
2. Expand `TestUcaMappings` in [`tests/test_oscal_ssp_exporter.py`](../tests/test_oscal_ssp_exporter.py) (`lines 994–1014`) and [`tests/test_framework_router.py`](../tests/test_framework_router.py) (`line 75`) so every active UCA in the merged control structure is required to have a non-empty mapping in all four frameworks (`NIST`, `ISO42001`, `EU_AI_ACT`, `MAS_FEAT`).

**Closure Criteria:**
1. Every active UCA in `load_control_structures()` maps to at least one control in all four frameworks (`NIST`, `ISO42001`, `EU_AI_ACT`, `MAS_FEAT`), with zero empty `→ <FRAMEWORK>:` lines in any committed SSP.
2. Removing any UCA key from `EU_AI_ACT.json` or `MAS_FEAT.json` fails both `test_framework_router.py` and `test_oscal_ssp_exporter.py`, and the merge commit SHA is recorded here.

### POAM-2026-165: Any Branch Can Impersonate the Owner-Equivalent Deployer Through Workload Identity Federation

**Control:** NIST AC-6 (Least Privilege), CM-5 (Access Restrictions for Change), SI-7 (Software, Firmware, and Information Integrity)
**Risk Level:** Moderate (WIF deployer/partner-harness partition, `sa-cage-deployer` role reduction, and revocation of `<PROJECT_NUMBER>-compute@developer.gserviceaccount.com` Token Creator on `sa-cage-deployer` and `cage-gateway` applied live on `<PROJECT_ID>` and codified in [`infra/targets/gcp-gke/wif.tf`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/wif.tf) on `2026-11-05`; GKE node-pool GSA rebind merged in `38e12586` under `POAM-2026-187`; open pending live `cage-staging` node-pool rollout, project-level `roles/storage.admin`/`roles/editor`/`roles/*Admin` revocation on `<PROJECT_NUMBER>-compute@developer.gserviceaccount.com`, and merge commit SHA)
**Status:** Open — live GCP WIF/IAM/KMS partition and SA Token Creator revocation applied, verified over the wire in GitHub Actions (`run 41944192524` `PERMISSION_DENIED` on `sa-cage-deployer`; `run 41943975961` `success` under `cage-partner-harness`), and imported into staging Terraform state with zero drift (`2026-11-05`); remaining closure items are live `cage-staging` node-pool rollout to `cage-gke-nodes-staging` and project-level role revocation on `<PROJECT_NUMBER>-compute@developer.gserviceaccount.com` (`POAM-2026-187` Steps 1 & 3) plus recording the merge commit SHA
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-31
**Target Closure:** 2026-11-07

**Description:**
GitHub Actions authenticates to the staging project (`<PROJECT_ID>`) through the `github` provider of the `github-actions` Workload Identity pool. As configured on 2026-10-31:

1. **The provider's condition names only the repository.** Its attribute condition is `assertion.repository == 'google/cybernetic-agent-governance-engine'`. Its mapping carries `google.subject`, `attribute.actor`, `attribute.repository` and `attribute.repository_owner`, but no ref and no environment, so no binding can tell `main` from any other branch.
2. **The deployer trusts the whole repository.** `sa-cage-deployer` grants `roles/iam.workloadIdentityUser` and `roles/iam.serviceAccountTokenCreator` to the principal set `attribute.repository/google/cybernetic-agent-governance-engine`, which is every workflow run in the repository from any ref. Anyone with write access can push a branch whose workflow sets `permissions: id-token: write` and runs on `push`, and GitHub issues that run a token the provider accepts. So can anyone holding a leaked token or session for such a person. Fork pull requests receive no ID token, and no workflow uses `pull_request_target`, so the exposure is the repository's writers and their credentials, not the public.
3. **The deployer is owner-equivalent.** It holds these project-level roles:
   - `roles/editor`
   - `roles/resourcemanager.projectIamAdmin`
   - `roles/iam.serviceAccountAdmin`, `roles/iam.serviceAccountUser` and `roles/iam.serviceAccountCreator`
   - `roles/cloudkms.admin` and `roles/cloudkms.publicKeyViewer`
   - `roles/cloudbuild.builds.editor`
   - `roles/artifactregistry.admin`
   - `roles/secretmanager.admin`
   - `roles/container.admin`, `roles/compute.admin` and `roles/run.admin`
   - `roles/storage.admin` and `roles/storage.hmacKeyAdmin`
   - `roles/servicenetworking.networksAdmin`

   `roles/resourcemanager.projectIamAdmin` alone lets it grant itself any role.
4. **The deployer reaches the image-signing chain** (`POAM-2026-083`). [`kms_signing.tf`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/kms_signing.tf) holds the `binauthz-attestor` key's policy authoritatively, and only `cage-cloudbuild-staging` is a signer on it, which matches the live key policy. The deployer can still sign in two ways:
   - it can submit a Cloud Build that runs as that identity (`roles/cloudbuild.builds.editor` with `roles/iam.serviceAccountUser`);
   - it can grant itself the signer role (`roles/cloudkms.admin`, `roles/resourcemanager.projectIamAdmin`).

   It can then push the images those attestations admit (`roles/artifactregistry.admin`). A branch push can therefore defeat the deploy-time supply-chain control.
5. **The partner harness runs as the deployer.** Two repository variables name `sa-cage-deployer`:
   - `ACTUATOR_01_WIF_SERVICE_ACCOUNT`, which actuator_01 impersonates directly;
   - `PROVIDER_01_WIF_DELEGATE`, which provider_01 delegates through to mint its Cloud Run ID token.

   Every partner run, including the branch-dispatched runs that verified `#610` before merge, therefore holds deployer authority. Restricting the single binding to `main` would close the hole but break those runs.
6. **A second, even more direct path exists on `<PROJECT_NUMBER>-compute@developer.gserviceaccount.com` (`POAM-2026-187`) — and rebinding GKE nodes alone does not revoke it.** Because `2bacc478:infra/modules/gcp_gke_cluster/main.tf` omitted `node_config.service_account` across all six `node_config` blocks (`POAM-2026-187`), every `cage-staging` node pool runs as the project's Compute Engine default service account (`<PROJECT_NUMBER>-compute@developer.gserviceaccount.com`). On `<PROJECT_ID>` (`verified 2026-10-31` and `2026-11-04`), `<PROJECT_NUMBER>-compute@developer.gserviceaccount.com` holds not only `roles/iam.serviceAccountTokenCreator` on `sa-cage-deployer`, but also:
   - `roles/iam.serviceAccountTokenCreator` **directly on `cage-gateway@<PROJECT_ID>.iam.gserviceaccount.com`** (`google_service_account.gateway.email`, the `gateway-seal` KMS signer in [`kms_signing.tf`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/kms_signing.tf) `lines 57–74, 152–164`); and
   - project-level **`roles/storage.admin`** (alongside `roles/editor`, `roles/container.admin`, `roles/compute.admin`, and `roles/cloudkms.admin`), which is additive with and overrides bucket-level IAM exclusivity on `gs://<PROJECT_ID>-evidence-worm-staging`.

   Pods use `GKE_METADATA` workload identity and cannot read the node credential directly, so on GKE this path requires a node VM or privileged DaemonSet compromise — but once that identity is reached, **the attacker does not even need to traverse `sa-cage-deployer`: `<PROJECT_NUMBER>-compute@developer.gserviceaccount.com` alone suffices to both sign evidence with `gateway-seal` (`AU-10`) and control the WORM bucket where that evidence lands (`AU-9`)**. Crucially, rebinding GKE node pools to `cage-gke-nodes-${var.environment}` (`POAM-2026-187` Step 1) only changes which identity GKE nodes use; it does **not** de-privilege `<PROJECT_NUMBER>-compute@developer.gserviceaccount.com`, and [`infra/targets/gcp-gke/iam.tf`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/iam.tf) (`lines 384–455`) binds `google_service_account_iam_binding` only for `roles/iam.workloadIdentityUser` while using additive `google_project_iam_member` for project-level roles (so `terraform apply` neither detects nor revokes those bindings). After the node-pool rebind applies, any future workload defaulting to `<PROJECT_NUMBER>-compute@developer.gserviceaccount.com` — a Cloud Build step that omits `serviceAccount`, a GCE instance, or a Cloud Run service — inherits the exact same `AU-9`/`AU-10` collapse until those roles are explicitly revoked on `<PROJECT_NUMBER>-compute@developer.gserviceaccount.com`.
7. **None of this has a reviewed source of truth.** Neither the pool and provider nor `sa-cage-deployer` is defined in `infra/` ([`DEPLOYMENT_RULES.md`](operations/DEPLOYMENT_RULES.md) § Unverified Deployer Service Account).

**Remediation Applied (`2026-11-05`) & Remaining Step:**
1. **OIDC Provider Ref & Environment Projection (`github-actions/providers/github`):** Updated `attributeMapping` to map `attribute.ref = assertion.ref` and `attribute.environment = assertion.environment` alongside `google.subject`, `attribute.actor`, `attribute.repository`, and `attribute.repository_owner`, preserving `attributeCondition: assertion.repository == 'google/cybernetic-agent-governance-engine'`.
2. **Dedicated Low-Privilege Partner Test Identity (`cage-partner-harness@<PROJECT_ID>.iam.gserviceaccount.com`):**
   - Bound `roles/iam.workloadIdentityUser` and `roles/iam.serviceAccountTokenCreator` to `principalSet://iam.googleapis.com/projects/<PROJECT_NUMBER>/locations/global/workloadIdentityPools/github-actions/attribute.repository/google/cybernetic-agent-governance-engine` (plus self on `roles/iam.serviceAccountTokenCreator`).
   - Replaced the `roles/iam.serviceAccountTokenCreator` binding on `cage-gateway@<PROJECT_ID>.iam.gserviceaccount.com` so **only** `serviceAccount:cage-partner-harness@<PROJECT_ID>.iam.gserviceaccount.com` is bound (revoking both `sa-cage-deployer` and `<PROJECT_NUMBER>-compute@developer.gserviceaccount.com`).
   - Replaced the key-level IAM policy on `projects/<PROJECT_ID>/locations/us-central1/keyRings/cybernetics-keyring/cryptoKeys/actuator-01-signing` so **only** `serviceAccount:cage-partner-harness@<PROJECT_ID>.iam.gserviceaccount.com` holds `roles/cloudkms.signer`, `roles/cloudkms.publicKeyViewer`, and `roles/cloudkms.viewer` (revoking `sa-cage-deployer`).
   - Repointed GitHub Actions repository variables `ACTUATOR_01_WIF_SERVICE_ACCOUNT` and `PROVIDER_01_WIF_DELEGATE` to `cage-partner-harness@<PROJECT_ID>.iam.gserviceaccount.com`.
3. **Deployer Restricted to `refs/heads/main` and Compute Default SA Impersonation Revoked (`sa-cage-deployer@<PROJECT_ID>.iam.gserviceaccount.com`):**

### POAM-2026-166: Over-the-Wire Partner Sandbox Verification for `actuator_03` (`QuarantineDirective` `refusal_proof_hash` / `governance_decision_digest` Signed Wire Body)

**Control:** NIST AU-10 (Non-Repudiation), SC-7 (Boundary Protection), SA-11 (Developer Testing and Evaluation)
**Risk Level:** Moderate
**Status:** Open — blocked on external partner infrastructure (`actuator_03` / NVIDIA Sentry on BlueField-4 DPU is at specification and conformance stage with no live partner sandbox endpoint)
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-31
**Target Closure:** 2026-12-31

**Description:**
`POAM-2026-149` (`#617`, `81a70602`) split the decision reference carried by [`QuarantineDirective`](../src/gateway/governance/seams/quarantine.py) into two mutually exclusive lowercase SHA-256 hex digest fields so each field has a single meaning:
- `refusal_proof_hash`: the `proof_hash` of the `RefusalReceipt` whose `DENY` triggered a kernel workload quarantine ([`handle_deny()`](../src/gateway/governance/governor/verdicts.py)).
- `governance_decision_digest`: `seal_digest(seal)` of the routing seal on an `ExecutionClearance` bridged into a quarantine directive ([`Actuator03Adapter.actuate()`](../src/integrations/actuator_03/adapter.py)).

Because [`Actuator03Adapter.quarantine_workload()`](../src/integrations/actuator_03/adapter.py) canonicalizes `directive.to_dict()` per RFC 8785 (JCS), signs it with `CAGE_QUARANTINE_ASSERTION_V1:`, hashes it for `X-CAGE-Envelope-Digest`, and sends it to `POST /v1/dpu/quarantine`, this change alters `actuator_03`'s signed wire body for kernel-issued quarantines ([wire change notice](partners/actuator_03/README.md#wire-change-notice-decision-digest-fields)).

While CAGE-side emission, signature coverage, and stream-oracle conformance (`cage-conformance` `0.3.0` `digest_format` and `refusal_evidence`) are verified hermetically (`DD-M9`, `DD-M14`, `DD-M18`–`DD-M25` in [`test_decision_digest_format.py`](../tests/test_decision_digest_format.py) and [`test_actuator_03.py`](../src/integrations/actuator_03/tests/test_actuator_03.py)), no over-the-wire run against a live partner endpoint has been executed:
1. **External partner infrastructure dependency:** `actuator_03` (NVIDIA Sentry / DOCA) is at the specification and conformance stage and has no live partner sandbox endpoint or mTLS/key-manifest credentials provisioned for CI ([`docs/partners/README.md`](partners/README.md#live-verification-status)).
2. **No partner harness target:** [`scripts/partner_harness.py`](../scripts/partner_harness.py) and [`.github/workflows/ci-partner-integration.yml`](../.github/workflows/ci-partner-integration.yml) have no `actuator_03` target or live test module under [`tests/integrations/`](../tests/integrations/).

Split from `POAM-2026-149` on `2026-10-31` so the external partner sandbox dependency is tracked explicitly here without blocking closure of the internal CAGE evidence-writer and conformance-suite remediation in `POAM-2026-149`.

**Remediation Plan:**
1. Coordinate partner adoption of the [`actuator_03` wire change notice](partners/actuator_03/README.md#wire-change-notice-decision-digest-fields) (accepting `refusal_proof_hash` on kernel-triggered directives and requiring exactly one of `refusal_proof_hash` or `governance_decision_digest`) once the partner provisions a reachable DPU quarantine sandbox endpoint and detached-receipt key manifest (`ACTUATOR_03_ENDPOINT`, `ACTUATOR_03_CERT_PATH`, `ACTUATOR_03_KEY_PATH`, `ACTUATOR_03_CA_PATH`, `ACTUATOR_03_RECEIPT_KEY_MANIFEST_URL`).
2. Add an over-the-wire conformance test under [`tests/integrations/`](../tests/integrations/) (marked `partner_integration` and `live_external`) and register `actuator_03` in [`scripts/partner_harness.py`](../scripts/partner_harness.py) and [`.github/workflows/ci-partner-integration.yml`](../.github/workflows/ci-partner-integration.yml) exercising both a `refusal_proof_hash` directive and a `governance_decision_digest` clearance-bridged directive, plus refusal of a directive carrying neither or both fields.

**Closure Criteria:**
1. `actuator_03` is registered in [`scripts/partner_harness.py`](../scripts/partner_harness.py) and [`.github/workflows/ci-partner-integration.yml`](../.github/workflows/ci-partner-integration.yml), and a live partner harness run against the partner sandbox passes (`0 skipped`, `0 failed`) covering both `refusal_proof_hash` and `governance_decision_digest` directives and Ed25519 receipt verification.
2. The passing GitHub Actions workflow run ID, merge commit SHA, and verification date are recorded here and in [`docs/partners/README.md`](partners/README.md#live-verification-status).

### POAM-2026-167: Mesh Identity Header Trusted From Any Peer, and Governance Pods Admitted Without the Proxy

**Control:** NIST IA-9 (Service Identification and Authentication), AC-3 (Access Enforcement), SC-8 (Transmission Confidentiality and Integrity)
**Risk Level:** Low (code and IaC remediated on `main` in `#625` / `#622` / `#623`; open only pending live staging apply)
**Status:** Open. Remediation merged on `main` in `#625` (`923c9d1a`, branch head `61ccbc5b`), `#622` (`ac6eb511`, branch head `b98d5276`), and `#623` (`7e2ddaec`, branch head `8230fefb`) (`2026-11-01`) and verified in hermetic tests and on staging probes (below); not yet applied to the running staging deployment
**Date Opened:** 2026-11-01
**Target Closure:** 2026-11-08

**Description:**
1. **The header was trusted from any peer.** [`workload_identity.py`](../src/gateway/server/workload_identity.py) (`WorkloadIdentityMiddleware`, `extract_client_identity`) took the caller's identity from `l5d-client-id`. In the compliance bridge, [`auth.py`](../src/compliance_bridge/auth.py) (`require_operator_identity`) turned the same header into an operator SVID. The header is sound only when the pod's own Linkerd inbound proxy wrote it. The middleware's docstring promised more: "no proxy means no header, and no header means 403". That holds only for callers that don't send the header themselves.
2. **What the header decides.** On the gateway:
   - the proposer and approver identities on the refinement approval routes;
   - the caller of `/tools/execute` and of MCP tool calls;
   - rate-limit principals;
   - the domain role bound to the caller (`current_client_identity`).

   On the bridge, it decides the operator identity on the defer-escalation (dual-control) path.
3. **The proxy's behaviour, tested on staging on 2026-11-01.** A header-echo server ran in a meshed pod, built from the attested gateway image.
   - A meshed caller that sent a forged `l5d-client-id` was seen with its own certificate identity only.
   - An unmeshed caller's forged header was removed.
   - Every request reached the app from the pod's own IP address.

   So a pod with its proxy cannot be fed a forged identity, and a pod without one can.
4. **Pods without the proxy get created.** The proxy injector's webhook has `failurePolicy: Ignore` (the chart default), and the injector runs one replica, so a pod created while it is not ready is admitted without the proxy. In each of the three October `linkerd-cni` crash loops, the injector was one of the stuck pods ([runbook](operations/DEPLOYMENT_RULES.md#linkerd-network-validator-crashloopbackoff-mitigation-vs-root-cause)).

   On 2026-10-29 at 22:25:01 UTC, during the manual recovery, the ReplicaSet controller recreated a gateway pod 1 second after the stuck one was deleted. That was 13 seconds before the stuck injector was deleted. The Kubernetes audit log records the gateway pod admitted with no init containers and only the `gateway` container. It was deleted two minutes later, before it passed its startup probe.
5. **Who could reach such a pod.** On staging, the governance-stack NetworkPolicies restrict egress only, so any pod in the cluster could reach an unmeshed gateway on port 8080. Neither of the repository's gateway ingresses is deployed on staging. Neither of them removes the header, and nothing in front of the gateway would remove it either:
   - `de5534ac:deployment/k8s/ingress.yaml` (ingress-nginx);
   - `de5534ac:deployment/k8s/gcp/ingress-gke.yaml` (GKE load balancer).

   Where one of them is deployed, an unmeshed gateway would take the header from the internet.
6. **Automatic repair would make it routine.** The `linkerd2-cni` repair controller in `#622` (`ac6eb511`) deletes stuck pods as their validators fail. In the October incidents, the application pods' validators failed 3 to 17 seconds before the injector's, so their replacements would have been created while it was still stuck.

**Remediation:**
1. **Refuse unmeshed governance pods at admission.** [`infra/modules/service_mesh`](../infra/modules/service_mesh/main.tf) installs the `cage-mesh-proxy-required` ValidatingAdmissionPolicy ([chart](../infra/modules/service_mesh/charts/mesh-proxy-required/templates/proxy-required-policy.yaml)) for `proxy_required_namespaces`. The gcp-gke target lists the namespace it meshes. A pod created there without the `linkerd-proxy` container is refused (`failurePolicy: Fail`, `Deny`), with no exemption. Its controller retries, and the pod is created with the proxy once the injector is back. The rendered policy was tested on staging on 2026-11-01 against a probe namespace:
   - A pod without the proxy was refused.
   - So was a pod with `linkerd.io/inject: disabled`.
   - The same pod in an unbound namespace was admitted.
   - A Deployment's ReplicaSet recorded `FailedCreate` with the policy's message. After injection was turned on, it created a meshed pod 21 seconds later.
2. **Accept the header only from the pod's own IP (`POD_IP`), never loopback (`Update 2026-11-05`, `F-38`, `F-39`, `PR-4`, folding `POAM-2026-194`).** `#625` (`923c9d1a`) initially allowed both loopback (`127.0.0.1`, `::1`, `localhost`) and `POD_IP` in `is_pod_local_connection` ([`src/gateway/server/workload_identity.py`](../src/gateway/server/workload_identity.py) and [`src/compliance_bridge/auth.py`](../src/compliance_bridge/auth.py)), and neither [`infra/modules/gateway/main.tf`](../infra/modules/gateway/main.tf) nor [`infra/modules/compliance_bridge/main.tf`](../infra/modules/compliance_bridge/main.tf) injected downward-API `POD_IP` (`status.podIP`), so `pod_ips` resolved solely from `socket.getaddrinfo(socket.gethostname(), None)`. In Linkerd, inbound connections decrypted by `linkerd-proxy` arrive at the application socket with `peer = POD_IP` (the pod's eth0 IP), whereas `127.0.0.1` / `::1` is reachable by any unprivileged sidecar/container in the pod network namespace and by `kubectl port-forward`. Furthermore, [`tests/integration/test_linkerd_mesh_conformance.py`](../tests/integration/test_linkerd_mesh_conformance.py) (`F-106`) deployed a 20-line inline `python:3.11-slim` `BaseHTTPRequestHandler` (`gateway-mesh-probe`) instead of the real gateway image and `WorkloadIdentityMiddleware`. `PR-4` removes loopback from `_LOOPBACK_HOSTS` / `is_pod_local_connection` (requiring `POD_IP` in enforcing postures and accepting only `peer == POD_IP`), injects downward-API `POD_IP` in both Terraform modules, and `PR-11` (`R-12`) replaces `gateway-mesh-probe` with the real gateway image in `mesh-conformance`. For a header from any peer other than `POD_IP`:
   - the middleware refuses the request and logs `INGRESS_IDENTITY_NOT_FROM_PROXY`;
   - `extract_client_identity` raises;
   - the bridge returns 401.

   The gateway, the standalone MCP server and the bridge run uvicorn with proxy headers off, so `X-Forwarded-For` cannot set the peer. Tests: [`test_workload_identity_middleware.py`](../tests/test_workload_identity_middleware.py), [`test_defer_dual_control_auth.py`](../tests/test_defer_dual_control_auth.py), [`test_servers_keep_proxy_headers_off.py`](../tests/test_servers_keep_proxy_headers_off.py), [`test_mesh_proxy_required_policy.py`](../tests/infrastructure/test_mesh_proxy_required_policy.py) and [`mesh_proxy_required.tftest.hcl`](../infra/modules/service_mesh/tests/mesh_proxy_required.tftest.hcl).
3. **The middleware's docstring states the guarantee it gives.**

**Closure Criteria:**
1. The service_mesh module is applied to staging (`W`). The policy is bound to governance-stack, and a pod created there with `linkerd.io/inject: disabled` is refused. Recorded here with the date.
2. Gateway and compliance-bridge images built from the remediation run on staging (`W`). An unmeshed copy of the gateway, in a namespace outside the policy, refuses a trusted `l5d-client-id` sent from another pod and logs `INGRESS_IDENTITY_NOT_FROM_PROXY`. A meshed caller is admitted through the proxy (`peer == POD_IP`). Recorded here with the date.
3. The initial merge commit SHA is recorded here. (**Done:** merged on `main` in `#625` as `923c9d1a`, branch head `61ccbc5b`, alongside `#622` as `ac6eb511`, branch head `b98d5276`, and `#623` as `7e2ddaec`, branch head `8230fefb`, on `2026-11-01`.)
4. **(Added `2026-11-05`, folding `POAM-2026-194`, `F-38`, `F-39`, `F-106`):** `PR-4` removes loopback (`127.0.0.1`, `::1`, `localhost`) from `is_pod_local_connection` in [`src/gateway/server/workload_identity.py`](../src/gateway/server/workload_identity.py) and [`src/compliance_bridge/auth.py`](../src/compliance_bridge/auth.py) and injects `POD_IP` (`status.podIP`) in [`infra/modules/gateway/main.tf`](../infra/modules/gateway/main.tf) and [`infra/modules/compliance_bridge/main.tf`](../infra/modules/compliance_bridge/main.tf) so a forged `l5d-client-id` over loopback / `kubectl port-forward` is rejected with `403`/`401` (`INGRESS_IDENTITY_NOT_FROM_PROXY`), and `PR-11` (`R-12`) runs the real gateway image in [`tests/integration/test_linkerd_mesh_conformance.py`](../tests/integration/test_linkerd_mesh_conformance.py) confirming that Linkerd inbound connections arrive with `peer == POD_IP` and pass `is_pod_local_connection`.

### POAM-2026-168: The Base Install and the Kernel Carried LLM Clients the Deterministic Core Claims Not to Need

**Control:** NIST CM-7 (Least Functionality), SA-8 (Security and Privacy Engineering Principles), CA-7 (Continuous Monitoring)
**Risk Level:** High. This is a reference-correctness defect: the artefact asserts a property it does not have, rated High for the same reason as POAM-2026-165. The property is the project's central claim, and anyone who lists the installed packages sees it fail before reading any code.
**Status:** Closed (2026-11-01) — merged in `#628` as `a3ab1b52` (branch head `e92e2d12`, tree `efd181c166e0`), verified by `Lint` check-run `124977642517`, `Quickstart Bootstrap & First-DENY Smoke Gate` check-run `124977642484`, and VM gateway image build `4ba28740724c` (`168` installed distributions, `0` of 23 forbidden model packages, 7/7 quickstart smoke steps `PASS`, `2026-11-01`)
**Date Opened:** 2026-11-01
**Target Closure:** 2026-11-08
**Date Closed:** 2026-11-01

**Description:**
CAGE's claim is seven deterministic tiers, no model in the decision path, and no GPU required. Two subsystems are optional and off by default: LLM critic consensus (`CAGE_CONSENSUS_CRITICS_ENABLED`) and the NeMo rails (`CAGE_NEMO_ENABLED`).

- **The dependency set** (measured from `uv.lock` at `5b2d4593`, ignoring environment markers):
  - The base install resolved 102 packages, 5 of them model packages: `litellm` and `openai`, plus `tiktoken`, `tokenizers` and `huggingface-hub` via litellm.
  - The gateway image (extras `gateway`, `advisor`, `compliance`, `langfuse`) resolved 253 packages, 14 of them model packages. These included `nemoguardrails`, with `fastembed` and `onnxruntime`, and through the `advisor` extra `langchain`, `langchain-core`, `langchain-openai`, `langgraph`, `google-adk` and `google-genai`.
- **The import level.**
  - The kernel's `GatewayClient` imported `openai` at module level, and `src/gateway/governance/consensus/engine.py` imported that client.
  - `assemble_governor()` imports the consensus engine for any plugin that contributes `ConsensusContribution`, and finance does.
  - So a finance gateway loaded the openai SDK during assembly with `CAGE_INFERENCE_BACKEND=none`, where consensus can only refuse (`CONSENSUS_NO_INFERENCE_BACKEND`). No client was ever built on that path, but the module was imported anyway.
- **The gateway image's stated reason was false.** `src/gateway/Dockerfile` installed the `advisor` extra and copied `src/governed_financial_advisor`, on the stated ground that the "gateway imports from governed_financial_advisor". Nothing in the image imports it.
- **litellm configured the process from `.env`.**
  - litellm 1.104.0 calls `load_dotenv()` on import whenever `LITELLM_MODE` is unset or `DEV`, which is the default.
  - With `LITELLM_DEV_ENV_HOT_RELOAD=True` it also overrides variables that are already set.
  - Any process that imported it from inside the tree took `.env` into its environment, a configuration source no operator chose. The test-session case is POAM-2026-120's, closed by `#627` (`6ebffb8c`).

**Why nothing caught it.** Two checks were configured not to look. This is the shape of POAM-2026-160 (gitleaks with no rules), POAM-2026-161 (dead pip-audit suppressions) and POAM-2026-163 (Bandit excluding `scripts/`).
1. G3's `FORBIDDEN_VENDOR_SDKS` listed eight vendor SDKs, `nemoguardrails` among them, and no LLM client. AGENTS.md described only five of the eight.
2. The hermetic job, "Hermetic Test (No ML Dependencies)", asserted that torch, transformers, sentence-transformers, tensorflow and jax were absent, in an environment that held `openai` and `litellm`.

**Remediation (merged on `main` in `#628` as `a3ab1b52`, branch head `e92e2d12`, `2026-11-01`):**
1. **Kernel.**
   - The consensus gate keeps its deterministic half: the trigger, context selection, prompt rendering, vote parsing, aggregation and refusal codes.
   - The model call crosses a new seam, `CriticBackend` ([`seams/critic.py`](../src/gateway/governance/seams/critic.py)).
   - The client and the per-role routing moved to Layer 3, in [`src/integrations/inference_client/`](../src/integrations/inference_client/__init__.py). `InferenceClient` was the kernel's `GatewayClient`, and `RoutedCriticBackend` was the kernel's `ConsensusModelRegistry`.
   - The allowlisted [`critic_backend_factory.py`](../src/gateway/governance/critic_backend_factory.py) binds the backend only when critics are enabled and an inference backend exists.
   - If the `inference` extra is missing, assembly fails closed with `CONSENSUS_CRITIC_BACKEND_UNAVAILABLE`.
2. **Packaging.**
   - `litellm` and `openai` left the base install, as did a test tool, the coverage plugin.
   - Two new extras: `nemo` (`nemoguardrails`, `litellm`) and `inference` (`openai`).
   - The `advisor` extra declares the `litellm` it imports.
   - The gateway image installs only `gateway`, `compliance` and `langfuse`.
   - The NeMo, advisor, local-dev and compliance-bridge images add the extras they use.
3. **Gates.**
   - G3 forbids LLM clients and model runtimes in `src/gateway/`.
   - [`check_model_free_install.py`](../scripts/check_model_free_install.py), in the required Lint job, fails in any of these cases:
     - the base install or the gateway image resolves one of 23 model packages;
     - the gateway Dockerfile installs one with `pip install`;
     - `FORBIDDEN_VENDOR_SDKS` stops listing an LLM client or is reassigned.
   - The hermetic job now checks for LLM clients too.
4. **Tests.**
   - [`test_kernel_assembly_model_free.py`](../tests/test_kernel_assembly_model_free.py) assembles a finance governor in a subprocess. It asserts that no LLM client or model runtime is in `sys.modules`, both by default and under `none`.
   - A positive control (critics enabled, model present) shows that the probe sees `openai` when it is loaded.
   - [`test_check_model_free_install.py`](../tests/test_check_model_free_install.py) plants each regression in a copy of the real inputs.

**Residual risk:**
- Presidio's spaCy model (`en_core_web_lg`) stays in the gateway image. It redacts evidence and is not in the decision path, which is why the claim says "decision path".
- Turning on critics or the NeMo rails needs an image built with the `inference` or `nemo` extra.

**Closure Criteria:**
1. The merge commit SHA is recorded here, with the green Lint run that includes the model-free install gate. (**Done:** merged on `main` in `#628` as `a3ab1b52`, branch head `e92e2d125485d790f7663b3f992ce26d349aa9b3`, tree `efd181c166e0`, `Lint` check-run `124977642517` `success`, `2026-11-01`.)
2. A gateway image built from the merge commit lists none of the gate's model packages (`uv pip list` in the image), and the quickstart smoke job passes against it. Both are recorded with the date. (**Done:** `docker build -f src/gateway/Dockerfile` image `4ba28740724c` built from tree `efd181c166e0` on `2026-11-01` listed `168` installed distributions and `0` of the 23 forbidden model packages via `uv pip list`; all 7 quickstart smoke steps exited `0` against it on `2026-11-01`, and `Quickstart Bootstrap & First-DENY Smoke Gate` check-run `124977642484` passed on `#628`.)

### POAM-2026-169: Evidence Documents Assert Controls That Are Not in Force

**Control:** NIST CA-2 (Control Assessments), CA-7 (Continuous Monitoring), PL-2 (System Security Plan)
**Risk Level:** High
**Status:** Open — 17 instances cataloged (`2026-11-02`–`2026-11-05`; prose instances corrected in `PR-0`, Instance 6 resolved in `#636`/`#664`, Instance 12 schema migrated in `#664`, OSCAL claim-census gate merged in `#679` `c75947e1`, OSCAL/exporter restatements open for `PR-7c`/`PR-10`, and four-check POA&M/Lula gate open for `PR-9`). Note (`F-64`, `P-3`): Gate G9 ([`check_doc_references.py`](../scripts/check_doc_references.py)) enforces only the **historical** half of the detection rule (`HISTORICAL_CLAIM_PATTERN`); present-tense OSCAL claims are enforced by [`check_oscal_claim_census.py`](../scripts/check_oscal_claim_census.py) (`#679`) and POA&M closure/Lula checks open for `PR-9`
**Date Opened:** 2026-11-02
**Target Closure:** 2026-11-30

**Description:**
A statement in an evidence document that a control is in force is evidence only if someone saw the control in force. Each statement below was written from a configuration file, a module, an intent, an earlier state, a point-in-time snapshot misread as history, or a measurement nobody repeated, and none was true when checked on 2026-11-01, 2026-11-02, 2026-11-04, or 2026-11-05. This is not POAM-2026-120's failure, where a check passes that should fail: nothing here agreed with itself, because nothing was checked. Nor is it POAM-2026-150's, where a gate is configured not to look: no gate looks at these documents at all. Earlier instances were filed under their own entries: POAM-2026-121 (a validation report's composition claim), POAM-2026-133 (required-check lists that disagree with the ruleset) and POAM-2026-148 (an OSCAL component that does not exist). Per `P-2`, all instances below take `POAM-2026-169`'s High rating and are not given separate severity ratings.

**Detection rule (historical half enforced in Gate G9, [`check_doc_references.py`](../scripts/check_doc_references.py); OSCAL claim-census enforced in [`check_oscal_claim_census.py`](../scripts/check_oscal_claim_census.py) `#679`; POA&M/Lula four-check gate in `PR-9`, `F-64`, `P-3`).** A statement that a control is in force names the observation that showed it (a command and its output, a run, a test) and its date. A statement that names only the file, module, setting or design that should produce the control has not been checked, and is not evidence until it is. Furthermore, a point-in-time snapshot (`describe`, `get`) proves only the state at the moment of the query, never how the resource reached that state or whether a defect fired earlier (`instance 9`): any historical claim ("verified unfired", "never", "has always", "no evidence of", "was applied via") requires historical evidence (state serial / `.tfstate`, Cloud Audit Logs / `UpdateCluster` / `CreateCluster`, `git log`, or a CI run / check-run ID), enforced mechanically across `docs/` by `_audit_historical_assertions()` in [`scripts/check_doc_references.py`](../scripts/check_doc_references.py). Because `HISTORICAL_CLAIM_PATTERN` matches only those nine historical phrases (`F-64`), present-tense OSCAL `implemented` assertions are guarded by [`scripts/check_oscal_claim_census.py`](../scripts/check_oscal_claim_census.py) (`#679`, `c75947e1`) against [`evidence_claim_census.jsonl`](../compliance/oscal/evidence_claim_census.jsonl), while POA&M closures citing uncalled code or unapplied manifests require `PR-9`'s four-check gate in [`scripts/check_poam_lula_divergence.py`](../scripts/check_poam_lula_divergence.py).

**Instances (verified 2026-11-02, 2026-11-04, and 2026-11-05):**

|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | [`TECH_STACK.md`](architecture/TECH_STACK.md), Perimeter row | VPC Service Controls, Cloud Armor and Cloud DNS in [`perimeter.tf`](../infra/targets/gcp-gke/perimeter.tf) | `enable_vpc_sc` and `enable_cloud_dns` default to `false`, and no shipped profile sets either, so neither is created. The Cloud Armor policy is created, but the BackendConfig that would attach it (`local.gateway_backend_config_manifest`) is used by no resource. The same file's `module.vpc_network` (`create_vpc_network`, default `true`) builds a default-deny VPC that no resource uses: the cluster, Cloud SQL, Memorystore and the GEAP advisor all take `var.network`, which is `default` in every shipped profile (verified live on `<PROJECT_ID>` on `2026-11-01`, `EF7a`) | Row corrected in #634 (`70609b27`). **Remediation:**
1. ✅ **Done (`2026-11-04`, historical half):** Gate G9 ([`scripts/check_doc_references.py`](../scripts/check_doc_references.py), `_audit_historical_assertions`) mechanically checks every markdown document in `docs/` inside `DOC_SCOPE` and rejects historical assertions (`HISTORICAL_CLAIM_PATTERN`) unless the line cites historical evidence (`.tfstate` / `serial: N`, `Cloud Audit Logs` / `UpdateCluster` / `CreateCluster` / `gcloud logging read`, `git log`, or CI `run` / `check-run`, tested in [`tests/test_check_doc_references.py`](../tests/test_check_doc_references.py)).
2. ✅ **Done (`2026-11-02`–`2026-11-07`, repository rules, Lula v0.16.0 schema, OSCAL claim census, and `PR-0` prose reconciliation):** [`check_repo_rules.py`](../scripts/check_repo_rules.py) compares `main` rulesets against `.github/branch-protection-rules.md` in [`repo-rules.yml`](../.github/workflows/repo-rules.yml) (`#635`); `#636` and `#664` (`372daad2`) resolved Instance 6, migrated all 33 `lula-validation-*.yaml` specs to Lula v0.16.0 schema (Instance 12), and added [`verify_lula_assessment_results.py`](../scripts/verify_lula_assessment_results.py); `#679` (`c75947e1`) added [`check_oscal_claim_census.py`](../scripts/check_oscal_claim_census.py) and [`evidence_claim_census.jsonl`](../compliance/oscal/evidence_claim_census.jsonl); and `PR-0` reopened `POAM-021`, `POAM-2026-011`, `POAM-2026-072`, `POAM-2026-080`, and `POAM-2026-096` while correcting all prose claims across `COMPLIANCE.md`, `DEFERRAL_QUEUE.md`, `README.md`, `SECURITY.md`, `infra/QUICK_START.md`, `SECURITY_STATUS.md`, `POAM_US_FED.md`, `JURISDICTIONS.md`, `AI_600_1_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md`, `NIST_AI_600_1_US_FED_ANALYSIS.md`, `ASSURANCE_STATUS_GUIDE.md`, `NIST_RMF_CHUNK1_CURRENT_STATE.md`, `NIST_RMF_CHUNK5_MONITOR_ROADMAP.md`, `ACCOUNT_MANAGEMENT_PROCEDURES.md`, and `compliance/lula/README.md`.
3. **Open (`PR-1`/`PR-2`, `PR-3`, `PR-5`, `PR-7a`–`PR-7c`, `PR-9`, `PR-10`, `§11a`):**
   - `PR-1` & `PR-2`: wire `module.vpc_network` into the deployment (`POAM-2026-173`, `POAM-2026-190`).
   - `PR-3` (`AR-1`): retire `gcp_geap_advisor` and delete `GEAP_INTEGRATION_ARCHITECTURE.md` (Instance 14).
   - `PR-5`: enforce mandatory WebAuthn binding and operator allowlist checks on `escalate` (Instance 13, `POAM-2026-196`).
   - `PR-7a`, `PR-7b`, `PR-7c`, `PR-10`, `§11a`: bring network and Linkerd policies into Terraform, retire unapplied static manifests and AgentSight, enable KMS `DATA_READ` audit logs, and restate OSCAL component-definition and SSP narratives (Instances 2, 10, 11, 15, 16).
   - `PR-9`: bring `lula-cron` into Terraform state and enforce the four-check gate in [`scripts/check_poam_lula_divergence.py`](../scripts/check_poam_lula_divergence.py) (`F-64`, `P-3`).

### POAM-2026-170: `main` Could Be Force-Pushed by a Repository Admin

**Control:** NIST CM-5 (Access Restrictions for Change), SI-7 (Software, Firmware, and Information Integrity)
**Risk Level:** Moderate
**Status:** Closed 2026-11-02 — ruleset 26377312, checked in CI by [`repo-rules.yml`](../.github/workflows/repo-rules.yml) (closure criteria below)
**Date Opened:** 2026-11-02
**Target Closure:** 2026-11-30

**Description:**
On 2026-11-02, `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/rules/branches/main` returned two rules: `pull_request` (ruleset 19769096) and `required_status_checks` (ruleset 25966171). Classic branch protection was off (`GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/branches/main/protection` returned 404, "Branch not protected"), and no organisation ruleset applied. Nothing blocked a non-fast-forward update or a deletion. Ruleset 19769096's only bypass actor is the repository admin role, in mode `always`, and 25966171 has none, so an admin's push was held only by the required checks. On 2026-11-02 a copy of 25966171's current rule was applied to a disposable branch (ruleset 26398326, deleted after the probe). Under it, a force push to `93d4bf67`, which has passing results for seven of the eight checks and none for `Terraform Tests (gcp-gke, mock providers)`, was refused with `422 Repository rule violations found` and `Required status check "Terraform Tests (gcp-gke, mock providers)" is expected.`, and a force push to its child `f43efeba` (#585, 2026-10-29), which has all eight passing, was accepted. Every commit of `main` before `f43efeba` lacks a Terraform result, and `f43efeba` is the first that has one (check runs of all 756 commits reachable from `main`, read 2026-11-02). Inferred from the two probed cases, not observed for each commit: none of the earlier commits could be a target, and `main` could be moved back only to one of its own commits from `f43efeba` on whose eight checks passed, or to the head of a pull request whose eight checks passed. Neither a failed check nor a pull request head was probed. The commit citations in this document, the CHANGELOG and the OSCAL statements, and every `<sha>^:<path>` pin Gate G9 verifies, assumed a history that nothing protected. `.github/branch-protection-rules.md` said force pushes and deletion were disabled (POAM-2026-169, instance 7).

It has happened. The repository's activity log (`GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/activity?ref=refs/heads/main&activity_type=force_push`, read 2026-11-02), which begins on 2026-07-07, records two force pushes to `main`, both by the maintainer's account on 2026-07-09. The first, at 00:24 UTC, moved `main` from `90824861`, the merge of #15, to `a7c13cca`, and replaced its history. `a7c13cca` shares no ancestor with `751ce907`, the commit `main` held before that merge, or with #15's head `694bbf37`: `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/compare/…` returned "No common ancestor" for both on 2026-11-02. `90824861` itself is not in the maintainer's clone, and GitHub no longer serves it (`GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/commits/90824861…` returned 422, "No commit found"). The second force push, at 12:08 UTC, moved `main` from `41dfb938` back to `a7c13cca`, its parent in the maintainer's clone, and was undone two minutes later, when `41dfb938` was pushed again. None since.

The line the first push installed is a rewritten copy of the one it replaced, rooted at `1902c92c` instead of `e608f3ac`. Of the 217 commits reachable from #15's head, 215 have a commit in the new line with the same author date and subject, and the new line then adds four commits of 2026-07-08 for the public release, ending at `a7c13cca` (#15's head and `a7c13cca` fetched and compared on 2026-11-02). Because the first of those four release commits (`fc5592b0`, `"chore(docs): remove internal files and scrub sensitive references for OSS release"`) deleted 14 internal planning, readiness and security-audit documents by a regular commit on the `1902c92c` line rather than filtering them out of `1902c92c..4134c860` (`4134c860` is `fc5592b0^`), those 14 files remain in `main`'s own ancestry (`POAM-2026-162`). None of the 240 known commits of the replaced `e608f3ac` line is in `main`'s history now; POAM-2026-160 and POAM-2026-162 call it the pre-rewrite line. No OSCAL statement or partner document cites one of them. This document cites `e608f3ac` in POAM-2026-150, POAM-2026-160 and POAM-2026-162 and `90824861` here, and `.gitleaks.toml` allowlists `e608f3ac` (every tracked file searched, 2026-11-02).

**Remediation (2026-11-02):**
Ruleset 26377312, "Protect main history" (`enforcement: active`, no bypass actors), applies `non_fast_forward` and `deletion` to `refs/heads/main`. It was created against a disposable branch first and then retargeted to `main`, so the rule seen refusing is the rule now applied. Every step used the REST API (`git/refs`), and the probe commits carried `[skip ci]`, so no workflow ran:

| Step | Rules on the probe branch | Result |
|---|---|---|
| Force-move the probe branch to an older commit | none | accepted |
| The same, after creating 26377312 against the probe branch | `non_fast_forward`, `deletion` | refused: `422 Repository rule violations found`, "Cannot force-push to this branch" |
| Delete the probe branch | the same | refused: "Cannot delete this branch" |
| Fast-forward the probe branch | the same | accepted |
| Retarget 26377312 to `refs/heads/main`, then delete the probe branch | none | accepted |

`GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/rules/branches/main` now returns `pull_request`, `non_fast_forward`, `deletion` and `required_status_checks`, and the ruleset's history holds two versions (55884219, the probe target; 55884291, `main`). Fast-forward merges are unaffected. The rules sit in their own ruleset, not in 25966171, because the private-fork advisory procedure ([`SECURITY.md`](../SECURITY.md)) disables 25966171 for one merge, and history protection must not lapse with it. Rewriting `main` now means editing or disabling 26377312 first, which its history records.

**Residual risk:** a repository admin can still edit or disable the ruleset. Bypass actors are returned only to admins, so a check without admin credentials cannot see one added.

**Closure criteria:** a CI job reads `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/rules/branches/main`, which needs no credentials on a public repository, and fails unless `non_fast_forward` and `deletion` apply; a recorded run shows it failing when either is absent. **Met on 2026-11-02:** [`check_repo_rules.py`](../scripts/check_repo_rules.py) runs in [`repo-rules.yml`](../.github/workflows/repo-rules.yml) on every pull request, every push to `main` and daily. Run against `main` it exits 0; run against a branch no ruleset covers (`--branch spike/no-rules-here`) it reports `non_fast_forward`, `deletion`, `pull_request` and `required_status_checks` missing and exits 1. [`test_check_repo_rules.py`](../tests/test_check_repo_rules.py) plants the removal of each rule, and eight mutants of the checker, including one that stops reporting missing rules, each fail it.

### POAM-2026-171: The Required Checks Do Not Test the Commit That Lands on `main`

**Control:** NIST CM-3 (Configuration Change Control), CM-3(2) (Testing, Validation, and Documentation of Changes)
**Risk Level:** Moderate
**Status:** Open
**Date Opened:** 2026-11-02
**Target Closure:** 2026-11-30

**Description:**
Ruleset 25966171 requires eight status checks on `main` and has no bypass actor, but it does not require branches to be up to date (`strict_required_status_checks_policy: false`, `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/rulesets/25966171`, 2026-11-02). A pull request's checks therefore never run on the commit that will land. Each run tests GitHub's merge of the pull request's head into `main` as `main` stood when the run started, and reports the result on the head. #598's `Pytest Logic Tests` job (check run `124349162293`, 2026-10-30) logged `HEAD is now at a676021 Merge 1ef835ee… into 34709d55…` and passed. #598 merged at 23:21 UTC, after four other merges had moved `main` to `319c702d`, so the tree that landed as `43528275` had never been tested as a whole. Its own push run then failed `Pytest Logic Tests` (check run `124364683189`). #623 shows the same pattern: its head `8230fefb` passed `Pytest Logic Tests` (`124965009484`), and its squash commit `7e2ddaec` failed it on `main` (`124967332730`).

A commit's own runs start only after it lands, and they do not always finish. [`ci.yml`](../.github/workflows/ci.yml), [`security-scan.yml`](../.github/workflows/security-scan.yml) and [`license_guard.yml`](../.github/workflows/license_guard.yml), which report the eight checks, set `cancel-in-progress: true` on a concurrency group keyed by workflow, event and ref, so each push to `main` cancels the run still in progress for the commit before it. Of the 47 commits on `main` from `f43efeba` (#585, 2026-10-29) to `2431ec35` (2026-11-02), 36 have all eight checks passing on their own SHA, 9 have one cancelled or missing, and 2 failed one (`43528275`, `7e2ddaec`) (check runs read 2026-11-02).

POAM-2026-115 counts these checks as a compensating control for admin merges because `--admin` cannot skip them. That holds, but the checks verify the pull request merged into an earlier `main`, not the commit that lands. Skew is caught only after the merge, and only by a later run on `main` that completes. This is the POAM-2026-169 class: the control was stated from its configuration. [`.github/branch-protection-rules.md`](../.github/branch-protection-rules.md) said no commit reaches `main` unless the eight checks passed on it; the change that opened this entry corrected that sentence and added this caveat to POAM-2026-115.

**Remediation:**
1. Make the tree that lands the tree that was tested. One way is to require branches to be up to date (`strict_required_status_checks_policy: true`), so that a pull request merges only after its checks ran against the current `main`. That costs a CI cycle for every pull request that falls behind, which is the cost POAM-2026-115's skew policy declined to pay. The other way is a merge queue, which runs the checks on the merge result before it lands (`merge_group` triggers in the three workflows). Either way, pin the new setting in [`check_repo_rules.py`](../scripts/check_repo_rules.py) and `.github/branch-protection-rules.md` in the same change.
2. Optional: let runs on `main` finish rather than cancelling them, by keeping `cancel-in-progress` for pull requests only, so that every commit on `main` gets its own result.

**Closure criteria:** a recorded probe shows GitHub refusing to merge a pull request whose eight checks ran against an earlier `main`, or shows the merge queue running them on the merge result. In addition, `repo-rules.yml` fails if that setting is removed.

### POAM-2026-172: Two Unrelated Jobs Report the Required Check `license-check`

**Control:** NIST SA-11 (Developer Testing and Evaluation), CA-7 (Continuous Monitoring), CM-3 (Configuration Change Control)
**Risk Level:** Moderate
**Status:** Open
**Date Opened:** 2026-11-02
**Target Closure:** 2026-11-30

**Description:**
Ruleset 25966171 requires eight status checks on `main`, each pinned to `integration_id: 15368` (GitHub Actions, `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/rulesets/25966171`, verified 2026-11-02). One of the eight required contexts is `license-check`. Two unrelated jobs in two workflow files report that exact context name because neither sets `name:` and both use the job key `license-check` (read 2026-11-02):
1. [`ci.yml`](../.github/workflows/ci.yml) line 108 (`jobs.license-check`, step `"Check Apache 2.0 License Headers"`), which checks that `src/**/*.{py,js,ts,tsx}` files contain an Apache 2.0 or `Copyright 2026` header.
2. [`license_guard.yml`](../.github/workflows/license_guard.yml) line 39 (`jobs.license-check`, step `"Run License Audit"`), which runs `pip-licenses` and fails on `GPL`, `AGPL` or `LGPL` packages without a permissive dual-licence alternative (POAM-2026-150).

On `f43efeba` (#585, 2026-10-29), both jobs posted check runs named `license-check`: check run `123570375167` (workflow run `40908683552`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `success`) and check run `123570378192` (workflow run `40908684322`, `.github/workflows/license_guard.yml`, `success`), read 2026-11-02.

Sharing one required context name across two unrelated checks is an instance of the POAM-2026-150 class (a gate that cannot fail in a specific and reachable scenario): if either job is removed, skipped by a condition or path filter, or fails to trigger, the other job still posts a passing `license-check` run from `integration_id: 15368` and leaves ruleset 25966171's requirement silently satisfied while half of the check stops running. How GitHub's ruleset evaluator resolves the requirement when both jobs run and one succeeds while the other fails has not been observed. ([`check_pr_mergeable.sh`](../scripts/check_pr_mergeable.sh) fails a context when any check run of that name is not `success`, though nothing requires that script to run before a merge, POAM-2026-115.)

[`.github/branch-protection-rules.md`](../.github/branch-protection-rules.md) §3 and §9 previously attributed `license-check` only to `license_guard.yml` (POAM-2026-169 class). The change that opened this entry updated §3 and §9 to list both `ci.yml` and `license_guard.yml`, and added `test_document_names_every_workflow_that_reports_each_check` in [`test_check_repo_rules.py`](../tests/test_check_repo_rules.py), which parses `.github/workflows/*.yml` and fails if any workflow adds or removes a job reporting a required check without §3's `Workflow files` column changing. That test prevents silent drift in the repository's workflow files, but does not separate the two checks in ruleset 25966171.

**Remediation:**
1. Rename `jobs.license-check` in [`ci.yml`](../.github/workflows/ci.yml) (for example by setting `name: "Apache 2.0 License Headers"`, or by renaming the job key and its `needs:` references at lines 532, 604 and 640) so that `license-check` is reported only by [`license_guard.yml`](../.github/workflows/license_guard.yml).
2. Decide whether the Apache 2.0 header check is itself a required status check on `main`. If it is, add the new context name (pinned to `integration_id: 15368`) to ruleset 25966171, `EXPECTED_REQUIRED_CHECKS` in [`check_repo_rules.py`](../scripts/check_repo_rules.py) and §3 / §9 of [`.github/branch-protection-rules.md`](../.github/branch-protection-rules.md) in the same change; either way, update §3's `Workflow files` column so `test_document_names_every_workflow_that_reports_each_check` passes.

**Closure criteria:** each required status-check context in ruleset 25966171 is reported by exactly one job across `.github/workflows/*.yml`, verified by `test_document_names_every_workflow_that_reports_each_check` and a passing [`repo-rules.yml`](../.github/workflows/repo-rules.yml) run.

### POAM-2026-175: Partner Identity, Slot, and Seam-Role Misattribution Across Partner and Architecture Documents

**Control:** NIST SA-9 (External System Services), CM-8 (System Component Inventory), CA-7 (Continuous Monitoring)
**Risk Level:** Moderate
**Status:** Closed (2026-11-02)
**Date Opened:** 2026-11-02
**Target Closure:** 2026-11-02

**Description:**
Three partner-identity misattributions surfaced in two days (`2026-11-01`–`2026-11-02`):
1. `POAM-2026-098` (`#391`, `e1f46eca`) attributed CAGE's own [`verify_partner_receipt()`](../src/integrations/actuator_01/receipt_verifier.py) preimage and `envelope_digest` mismatch to the `actuator_01` partner (**Archytan**) not yet signing receipts.
2. The initial draft correction for `POAM-2026-098` named **OpsCanvas** (`provider_09`, [`EstateProvider`](../src/gateway/governance/seams/estate.py)) as the `actuator_01` partner before `#640` (`667ca6a6`) corrected it to **Archytan**.
3. [`docs/partners/README.md`](partners/README.md) (`## Directory Index`, line 26) listed `provider_02`'s Partner / Vendor as `**InferTheta / NexArt / Vector3**` while listing `provider_07` (**InferTheta**) at line 33 with Integration Role `Graph Topology & Governance Schema Provider` (which is **NexArt**'s native CAGE DAG schema in [`NATIVE_SCHEMA_SPEC.md`](partners/provider_02/nexart/NATIVE_SCHEMA_SPEC.md), not InferTheta's `NormativeProvider` Bayesian Causal Suitability Oracle). `"Vector3"` is not a company at all: `#218` (`fcb98bef`, `2026-09-17`, *"chore(governance): centralize partner docs and update gitignore"*) moved **Archytan**'s two `ArbiterKernel` reference test-vector PDFs (`667ca6a6^:docs/partners/provider_02/vector3_direct_json.pdf` and `667ca6a6^:docs/partners/provider_02/vector3_escalate_json.pdf`, generated by `arbiter-kernel/cmd/gen-cage-vectors`, see [`ARCHYTAN_VECTOR_ALIGNMENT.md`](operations/ARCHYTAN_VECTOR_ALIGNMENT.md)) into `docs/partners/provider_02/` and read the `vector3_` filename prefix as a third vendor name alongside `InferTheta` and `NexArt`. That row survived seven weeks on `main`, including `#608` (`2f9c0f38`, `2026-10-31`), which added the `## Live Verification Status` table with the right one-vendor-per-slot names directly below `## Directory Index` without reconciling the two tables.

This is a distinct error class from `POAM-2026-169` (controls asserted as in force without observation): all three errors are in documents partners read and concern **who is who** — which partner owns which anonymized slot (`provider_01`–`provider_09`, `actuator_01`–`actuator_03`), which kernel seam protocol (`NormativeProvider`, `AttestationProvider`, `WarrantSource`, `EstateProvider`, `ExecutionActuator`, `PolicyAdvisorBridge`, `QuarantineActuator`) that adapter implements, and where its artifacts live.

A repository-wide audit across `docs/`, `src/integrations/`, `src/gateway/governance/`, and `config/` checked every partner name, slot, and seam-role pairing against the actual adapter classes and kernel seam factories, uncovering 12 instances across 14 files:

| # | File | Defect | Fix |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | [`docs/partners/README.md`](partners/README.md) (`line 26`) | `provider_02` Partner / Vendor listed as `**InferTheta / NexArt / Vector3**` | Corrected to `**NexArt**` with role `Attestation Provider & Cryptographic Evidence Resolver (\`AttestationProvider\`, CER)` |
| 2 | [`docs/partners/README.md`](partners/README.md) (`line 33`) | `provider_07` (**InferTheta**) role listed as `Graph Topology & Governance Schema Provider` (`provider_02`'s role) | Corrected to `Synchronous Normative Provider (\`NormativeProvider\`) — Bayesian Causal Suitability Oracle`; explicit seam protocol tokens added to all 11 rows |
| 3 | [`docs/partners/actuator_01/`](partners/actuator_01/README.md) & [`docs/partners/README.md`](partners/README.md) (`line 28`) | Archytan (`actuator_01`) test-vector PDFs (`vector3_direct_json.pdf`, `vector3_escalate_json.pdf`) misfiled under `docs/partners/provider_02/` since `#218` (`fcb98bef`), while `actuator_01` had no directory (`*(Reference)*`) | Relocated both PDFs to `docs/partners/actuator_01/`, added [`docs/partners/actuator_01/README.md`](partners/actuator_01/README.md) with `CAGE-PARTNER-CONTENT-HEADER: v1`, and linked `[`actuator_01/`](actuator_01/)` in `## Directory Index` |
| 4 | [`docs/operations/ARCHYTAN_VECTOR_ALIGNMENT.md`](operations/ARCHYTAN_VECTOR_ALIGNMENT.md) (`lines 122, 126, 172, 173`) | Cited pre-`#218` root paths `fcb98bef^:docs/partners/vector3_direct_json.pdf` and `fcb98bef^:docs/partners/vector3_escalate_json.pdf` | Updated all four references to `docs/partners/actuator_01/vector3_direct_json.pdf` and `docs/partners/actuator_01/vector3_escalate_json.pdf` |
| 5 | [`docs/POAM.md`](POAM.md) (`line 1347`, `POAM-2026-155` detail item 2) | Attributed `cloudops` / [`EstateProvider`](../src/gateway/governance/seams/estate.py) to `provider_02` instead of `provider_09` (**OpsCanvas**) | Corrected `(provider_02)` to `(provider_09)` |
| 6 | [`src/gateway/governance/normative_provider.py`](../src/gateway/governance/normative_provider.py) (`line 50`) | Module docstring listed `"provider_02"` as a valid `CAGE_NORMATIVE_PROVIDER` value, while `get_normative_provider("provider_02")` at line 552 raises `ValueError("Provider 02 implements the AttestationProvider protocol, not NormativeProvider...")` | Replaced with `"provider_01", "provider_03", "provider_06", "provider_07", or "provider_08"` |
| 7 | [`src/integrations/provider_02/README.md`](../src/integrations/provider_02/README.md) (`lines 14–18`) | Stated that `Provider02AttestationProvider` is not a subclass of `AttestationProvider` and is resolvable via `get_normative_provider()` (both inverted since `#312` / `0b8d7e12`) | Updated to state that `Provider02AttestationProvider` subclasses `AttestationProvider` ([`src/integrations/provider_02/provider.py:201`](../src/integrations/provider_02/provider.py#L201)), is resolved via `AttestationAggregator`, and raises `ValueError` in `get_normative_provider("provider_02")` |
| 8 | [`docs/architecture/VENDOR_NEUTRALITY_CONTRACT.md`](architecture/VENDOR_NEUTRALITY_CONTRACT.md) (`§2.3`) | Listed `src/integrations/provider_02/` under `NormativeProvider`, omitted `provider_03`/`06`/`07`/`08` and `actuator_02`/`03`, and omitted `WarrantSource`, `EstateProvider`, and `QuarantineActuator` | Removed `provider_02` from `NormativeProvider` and aligned §2.3 with all kernel seams and Layer 3 packages |
