| 9 | [`docs/architecture/EXTENSIBILITY_ARCHITECTURE.md`](architecture/EXTENSIBILITY_ARCHITECTURE.md) (`§2.5.5, §2.6, §4.4`) | Listed `provider_02/provider.py` as `NormativeProvider interface` and omitted `provider_06`–`provider_09`, `actuator_02`–`actuator_03`, `warrant.py`, `estate.py`, and `quarantine.py` | Updated `provider_02/provider.py` to `Provider02AttestationProvider (AttestationProvider interface)` and aligned all seam and provider listings |
| 10 | [`docs/partners/provider_05/veip_three_axioms_architecture.md`](partners/provider_05/veip_three_axioms_architecture.md) (`lines 108, 696, 712, 818`) | Listed `Provider 02` alongside `Provider 01` and `Provider 03` as a `NormativeProvider` implementation | Removed `Provider 02` from the `NormativeProvider` lists and noted its `AttestationProvider` role |
| 11 | [`docs/architecture/TECH_STACK.md`](architecture/TECH_STACK.md) (`§5`) & [`docs/architecture/GOVERNANCE_SUBSTRATE_DUALITY.md`](architecture/GOVERNANCE_SUBSTRATE_DUALITY.md) (`§10`) | `TECH_STACK.md` §5 cited stale class names (`Provider01NormativeProvider`, `Provider02Adapter`, `Provider08Client`), marked `Provider 01`, `Provider 05`, and `Actuator 01` as `Stub (HTTP client pending)`, and omitted `Provider 09`, `Actuator 02`, and `Actuator 03`; `GOVERNANCE_SUBSTRATE_DUALITY.md` §10 stated `provider_05` had no HTTP transport or cryptographic signature verification | Updated `TECH_STACK.md` §5 and `GOVERNANCE_SUBSTRATE_DUALITY.md` §10 to match the implemented classes and live HTTP `Provider05WarrantSource` |
| 12 | [`docs/API_MAP_EXTERNAL.md`](API_MAP_EXTERNAL.md) (`§7`), [`docs/CAGE_OPEN_INTEROP_SPEC.md`](CAGE_OPEN_INTEROP_SPEC.md) (`§13`), [`src/integrations/__init__.py`](../src/integrations/__init__.py), [`config/environments/partner-sandbox.env.example`](../config/environments/partner-sandbox.env.example) | Listed outdated slot ranges (`provider_01`–`07` or `08`, `actuator_01` only) and omitted `WarrantSource` on `provider_05` | Updated all four inventories to cover `provider_01`–`provider_09` and `actuator_01`–`actuator_03` |

**Remediation & CI Enforcement:**
1. [`scripts/check_partner_headers.py`](../scripts/check_partner_headers.py) (executed in the required `Lint` job in [`.github/workflows/ci.yml`](../.github/workflows/ci.yml)) now defines `PARTNER_SLOTS` mapping all 11 partner slots (`provider_01`–`provider_03`, `provider_05`–`provider_09`, `actuator_01`–`actuator_03`) to their canonical `vendor`, `status_label`, and `seams`, and validates `docs/partners/README.md` (`## Directory Index` and `## Live Verification Status`) and `docs/partners/` subdirectories on every run:
   - Every `docs/partners/<slot>/` subdirectory must be in `PARTNER_SLOTS` and have a unique row in both `## Directory Index` and `## Live Verification Status`.
   - Every row in `## Directory Index` must carry the exact bold vendor name `**{spec.vendor}**`, include every required seam token `` `{seam}` `` in `spec.seams`, contain no seam token from `ALL_SEAM_PROTOCOLS - set(spec.seams)`, and link to `` [`{slot}/`]({slot}/) ``.
   - Every row in `## Live Verification Status` must carry the exact adapter label `` `{slot}` {spec.status_label} ``.
2. [`tests/test_check_partner_headers.py`](../tests/test_check_partner_headers.py) (executed in the required `Pytest Logic Tests` job) adds:
   - `test_partner_readme_attribution_and_role_drift_is_reported` (10 parametrized planted defects in `README_PLANTS`, including the `#218` `**InferTheta / NexArt / Vector3**` defect, the `OpsCanvas` actuator misattribution, the `provider_07` missing `NormativeProvider` role defect, `provider_02` claiming `NormativeProvider`, `*(Reference)*` doc links, status-table vendor swaps, duplicate rows, and unknown partner directories).
   - `test_partner_slots_match_adapter_classes_and_seam_factories`, which asserts `set(_SEAM_METHODS) == gate.ALL_SEAM_PROTOCOLS` (all 7 seam protocols, including `EstateProvider`), dynamically imports `src.integrations.<slot>` for every slot in `integration_slots` (no hardcoded `provider_09` skip, so `provider_09` is checked automatically the moment its package directory lands under `src/integrations/`), resolves [`get_estate_provider("provider_09")`](../src/gateway/governance/estate_provider.py) while `provider_09` is not yet in `src/integrations/`, and verifies that `get_normative_provider("provider_02")` and `_load_attestation_provider("provider_01")` fail closed with `ValueError`.
   - `test_seam_check_enforces_provider_09_when_package_is_present`, which injects a synthetic `_PROVIDER_09_PACKAGE` module and proves that a class implementing the wrong seam (`NormativeProvider`) fails `_module_implemented_seams` while `Provider09EstateProvider` implementing `EstateProvider` passes both `_module_implemented_seams` and `get_estate_provider("provider_09")`.

**Mutation Verification (`2026-11-02`, `tests/test_check_partner_headers.py`):**

| Mutation ID | Target in `scripts/check_partner_headers.py` | Planted Mutation | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| `PH-M1` | `_status_table_violations` | Skip unknown partner subdirectory check (`subdir not in PARTNER_SLOTS`) | **KILLED** (`rc=1`, `1 failed`) |
| `PH-M2` | `_status_table_violations` | Skip `_index_table_violations()` | **KILLED** (`rc=1`, `7 failed`) |
| `PH-M3` | `_index_table_violations` | Skip `vendor_cell != expected_vendor` check | **KILLED** (`rc=1`, `2 failed`) |
| `PH-M4` | `_index_table_violations` | Skip required seam check (`f"\`{seam}\`" not in role_cell`) | **KILLED** (`rc=1`, `1 failed`) |
| `PH-M5` | `_index_table_violations` | Skip forbidden seam check (`f"\`{other_seam}\`" in role_cell`) | **KILLED** (`rc=1`, `1 failed`) |
| `PH-M6` | `_index_table_violations` | Skip documentation directory link check (`doc_cell != expected_doc`) | **KILLED** (`rc=1`, `1 failed`) |
| `PH-M7` | `_status_table_violations` | Skip `## Live Verification Status` adapter label check (`label != spec.status_label`) | **KILLED** (`rc=1`, `1 failed`) |
| `PH-M8` | `PARTNER_SLOTS["provider_02"]` | Change `seams` from `("AttestationProvider",)` to `("NormativeProvider",)` | **KILLED** (`rc=1`, `18 failed`) |

### POAM-2026-176: OSCAL SSP Exporter Is Non-Idempotent on Unchanged Inputs and Omitted by `check_stpa_freshness.py` (`POAM-2026-150` Family)

**Control:** NIST SA-11 (Developer Testing and Evaluation), CM-3 (Configuration Change Control), CA-7 (Continuous Monitoring)
**Risk Level:** Moderate
**Status:** Closed (`2026-11-04`)
**Date Opened:** 2026-11-02
**Target Closure:** 2026-11-30

**Description:**
During the `POAM-2026-175` verification run (`2026-11-02`), executing `python -m src.gateway.governance.oscal_ssp_exporter export` on a clean working tree modified three committed OSCAL files under [`compliance/oscal/`](../compliance/oscal/) across 10 single-line hunks:
- [`compliance/oscal/component-definition.yaml`](../compliance/oscal/component-definition.yaml) (`lines 7, 643, 703`): `component-definition.metadata.last-modified`, the `STPA-to-Policy Compiler` component `props[last-compiled]`, and the `FTRA Irreversibility Classifier` component `props[last-compiled]` changed from `'2026-11-01T14:30:40Z'` to the current wall-clock second.
- [`compliance/oscal/stpa_compiler_ssp_patch.yaml`](../compliance/oscal/stpa_compiler_ssp_patch.yaml) (`lines 2, 23, 67, 238`): `stpa-compiler-ssp-patch.generated`, `Last compiled: ...` inside `implemented-requirement.description`, `implemented-requirement.props[last-compiled]`, and `new-component.props[last-compiled]` changed from `'2026-11-01T14:30:40Z'` to the current wall-clock second.
- `2bacc478:compliance/oscal/system-security-plan.yaml` (`lines 6, 1468, 1512`; now [`compliance/oscal/system-security-plan.yaml`](../compliance/oscal/system-security-plan.yaml) `lines 6, 1467, 1511`): `system-security-plan.metadata.last-modified`, `Last compiled: ...` inside `implemented-requirement[sa-11].description`, and `implemented-requirement[sa-11].props[last-compiled]` changed from `'2026-11-01T14:30:40Z'` to the current wall-clock second.

Inspecting [`src/gateway/governance/oscal_ssp_exporter.py`](../src/gateway/governance/oscal_ssp_exporter.py), [`scripts/check_stpa_freshness.py`](../scripts/check_stpa_freshness.py), and [`COMPLIANCE.md`](../COMPLIANCE.md) identified two distinct defects in the STPA-to-OSCAL export and freshness pipeline (the regional SSP corruption and incompleteness defect found during the same inspection is tracked separately under `POAM-2026-177`):
1. **Freshness-gate omission (`POAM-2026-150` family, `2bacc478:scripts/check_stpa_freshness.py` `lines 82–91`) and false pipeline claim (`2bacc478:COMPLIANCE.md` `line 259`):** `_GENERATED_ARTIFACTS` in [`scripts/check_stpa_freshness.py`](../scripts/check_stpa_freshness.py) regenerated and hashed the 8 outputs of [`src/gateway/governance/stpa_compiler.py`](../src/gateway/governance/stpa_compiler.py) (masking volatile timestamps via `_VOLATILE_STAMP_RE`), **but omitted the 3 OSCAL files generated by `oscal_ssp_exporter.py`** from the exact same STPA sources (`385e502e:config/stpa_control_structure.yaml` + [`src/cage_finance/config/stpa/trade_hazards.yaml`](../src/cage_finance/config/stpa/trade_hazards.yaml)) and [`config/oscal/framework_mappings/NIST_SP800_53.json`](../config/oscal/framework_mappings/NIST_SP800_53.json). A change to STPA sources or framework mappings that ran `stpa_compiler.py compile` without `oscal_ssp_exporter export` passed `check_stpa_freshness.py` while leaving `compliance/oscal/` stale. Meanwhile, `2bacc478:COMPLIANCE.md` (`line 259`) stated that `src/gateway/governance/oscal_ssp_exporter.py` *"automatically compiles these exact control configurations and implementation narratives into the authoritative 1,449-line Open Security Controls Assessment Language (OSCAL) document on every build pipeline run"* — an instance of `POAM-2026-169` (zero CI workflows in `.github/workflows/` and zero `Makefile` targets invoked `oscal_ssp_exporter.py`, and the sole script in the repository that attempted to call it, `2bacc478:scripts/run_staging_validation_once.sh` at `lines 397, 403`, invoked `src/gateway/governance/oscal_ssp_exporter.py` without the required `export` subcommand and failed with `argparse` exit code `2`).
2. **Non-idempotent wall-clock stamping (`2bacc478:src/gateway/governance/oscal_ssp_exporter.py` `line 701`):** Despite claiming idempotency in module and function docstrings (`2bacc478:src/gateway/governance/oscal_ssp_exporter.py` `lines 44, 1054, 1091, 1175, 1249`), `_now_iso()` returned `datetime.datetime.now(datetime.timezone.utc).strftime("%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ")` and was called **7 separate times** inside a single `cmd_export()` invocation (`generate_ssp_patch:788`, `generate_component_entry:906`, `generate_ftra_component_entry:1000`, `_apply_ssp_patch:1110`, `_apply_component_patch:1180`, `_apply_ftra_component_patch:1254`, `_write_standalone_patch:1280`). Every run unconditionally rewrote all 10 timestamp fields even when the exported requirements, components, and narratives were byte-for-byte identical to the committed files, and if the wall clock crossed a second boundary during the ~150 ms export, a single run stamped disagreeing timestamps across the three files.

**Remediation (`fix/oscal-ssp-freshness`, `2026-11-04`):**
1. **Single timestamp propagation and content-aware idempotency ([`src/gateway/governance/oscal_ssp_exporter.py`](../src/gateway/governance/oscal_ssp_exporter.py) `lines 115–128, 1381–1472, 1665–1750`):**
   - `cmd_export()` computes `now = _now_iso()` once (`line 1665`) and threads `now` through `generate_ssp_patch()`, `generate_component_entry()`, `generate_ftra_component_entry()`, `_apply_ssp_patch()`, `_apply_component_patch()`, `_apply_ftra_component_patch()`, and `_write_standalone_patch()`.
   - `_render_targets_in_memory()` (`lines 1381–1437`) renders the target OSCAL YAML artifacts into a temporary directory first, and `cmd_export()` (`lines 1739–1750`) skips writing any target file whose on-disk content matches `fresh_text` after `mask_volatile_stamps()` (`lines 115–128`, which masks `last-modified`, `generated`, `props[last-compiled]`, and `Last compiled:` across PyYAML 80-column double-quoted line wraps). Re-running `oscal_ssp_exporter export` on a clean tree is now a zero-diff no-op.
   - Added `oscal_ssp_exporter export --check` (`lines 1546–1553, 1710–1737`), which compares on-disk OSCAL files against a fresh in-memory render via `mask_volatile_stamps()` and exits `1` on drift without modifying files on disk.
2. **STPA freshness gate coverage and documentation/script fixes:**
   - [`scripts/check_stpa_freshness.py`](../scripts/check_stpa_freshness.py) (`lines 82–98, 125–134, 247–263`) adds `_OSCAL_SSP` ([`compliance/oscal/system-security-plan.yaml`](../compliance/oscal/system-security-plan.yaml)), `_OSCAL_COMP_DEF` ([`compliance/oscal/component-definition.yaml`](../compliance/oscal/component-definition.yaml)), and `_OSCAL_PATCH` ([`compliance/oscal/stpa_compiler_ssp_patch.yaml`](../compliance/oscal/stpa_compiler_ssp_patch.yaml)) to `_GENERATED_ARTIFACTS`, masks OSCAL volatile timestamps in `_VOLATILE_STAMP_RE`, and regenerates all three in memory via `render_default_oscal_artifacts()` inside `_regenerate_all()`.
   - [`COMPLIANCE.md`](../COMPLIANCE.md) (`line 259`) now accurately states what `oscal_ssp_exporter.py` compiles and that CI verifies freshness via `check_stpa_freshness.py` (`US_FED`) and `oscal_ssp_exporter export --check` (`EU_ECB` / `APAC_MAS`).
   - [`scripts/run_staging_validation_once.sh`](../scripts/run_staging_validation_once.sh) (`lines 397, 403`) passes the required `export` subcommand.
   - [`scripts/check_poam_lula_divergence.py`](../scripts/check_poam_lula_divergence.py) (`lines 183–211, 341–348`) registers `POAM-2026-176` and `POAM-2026-177` in `CODE_EVIDENCE_FINDINGS` and adds `find_duplicate_poam_headers()` to fail on any duplicate `### POAM-YYYY-NNN` header in `docs/POAM.md`.

**Closure Verification (`2026-11-04`):**
- `uv run pytest tests/test_check_poam_lula_divergence.py tests/test_oscal_ssp_exporter.py tests/test_stpa_freshness_gate.py tests/test_check_doc_references.py -q` → `115 passed in 12.13s`.
- Mutation verification across [`src/gateway/governance/oscal_ssp_exporter.py`](../src/gateway/governance/oscal_ssp_exporter.py), [`scripts/check_stpa_freshness.py`](../scripts/check_stpa_freshness.py), and [`scripts/check_poam_lula_divergence.py`](../scripts/check_poam_lula_divergence.py) (`8/8 KILLED`):

| Mutation ID | Target | Planted Mutation | Result |
|---|---|---|---|
| `OS-M1` | `oscal_ssp_exporter.py` (`cmd_export` `--check`) | Return `0` instead of `1` when `drifted` is non-empty | **KILLED** (`rc=1`, `2 failed, 2 passed` in `TestRegionalSspExportAndCheck`) |
| `OS-M2` | `oscal_ssp_exporter.py` (`mask_volatile_stamps`) | Return unmasked `text` without substituting `_VOLATILE_STAMP_RE` | **KILLED** (`rc=1`, `4 failed` in `TestRegionalSspExportAndCheck`) |
| `OS-M3` | `oscal_ssp_exporter.py` (`_infer_region_from_ssp`) | Always return `None` when `--ssp` is passed | **KILLED** (`rc=1`, `4 failed` in `TestRegionalSspExportAndCheck`) |
| `OS-M4` | `scripts/check_stpa_freshness.py` (`_GENERATED_ARTIFACTS`) | Omit `_OSCAL_SSP`, `_OSCAL_COMP_DEF`, `_OSCAL_PATCH` from `_GENERATED_ARTIFACTS` | **KILLED** (`rc=1`, `1 failed, 12 passed` in `test_stpa_freshness_gate.py`) |
| `OS-M5` | `scripts/check_stpa_freshness.py` (`_regenerate_all`) | Skip `render_default_oscal_artifacts()` call | **KILLED** (`rc=1`, `2 failed, 11 passed` in `test_stpa_freshness_gate.py`) |
| `OS-M6` | `scripts/check_stpa_freshness.py` (`_VOLATILE_STAMP_RE`) | Remove OSCAL timestamp patterns (`last-modified`, `last-compiled`, `Last compiled:`) | **KILLED** (`rc=1`, `1 failed, 12 passed` in `test_stpa_freshness_gate.py`) |
| `OS-M7` | `scripts/check_poam_lula_divergence.py` (`find_duplicate_poam_headers`) | Return `[]` instead of `sorted(duplicates)` | **KILLED** (`rc=1`, `1 failed, 10 passed` in `test_check_poam_lula_divergence.py`) |
| `OS-M8` | `scripts/check_poam_lula_divergence.py` (`main` `duplicate_headers`) | Return `0` instead of `1` when `duplicate_headers` is non-empty | **KILLED** (`rc=1`, `1 failed, 10 passed` in `test_check_poam_lula_divergence.py`) |

### POAM-2026-177: Regional OSCAL SSP Export Overwrites `US_FED` SSP by Default While Leaving `EU_ECB` and `APAC_MAS` SSPs Without Generated STPA/FTRA Implementation Blocks

**Control:** NIST CM-3 (Configuration Change Control), CA-2 (Control Assessments), SA-11 (Developer Testing and Evaluation); EU AI Act Art. 9; DORA Art. 10; GDPR Art. 22; MAS FEAT; MAS Notice 655; MAS TRM §6.3
**Risk Level:** High
**Status:** Closed (`2026-11-04`)
**Date Opened:** 2026-11-02
**Target Closure:** 2026-11-30

**Description:**
In `2bacc478:src/gateway/governance/oscal_ssp_exporter.py` (`lines 98, 155–182, 1411–1470`), `cmd_export()` selected the compliance framework from `CAGE_DEPLOYMENT_REGION` via `REGIONAL_PROFILES` (`US_FED` → `NIST_SP800_53`, `EU_ECB` → `EU_AI_ACT`, `APAC_MAS` → `MAS_FEAT`) and logged `REGIONAL_PROFILES[region]["profile_path"]`, **while `args.ssp` remained hardcoded to `_DEFAULT_SSP` (`compliance/oscal/system-security-plan.yaml`)** unless `--ssp` was explicitly passed on the command line (which [`AGENTS.md`](../AGENTS.md) § *OSCAL & POAM Synchronization Guardrails* forbids for standard invocations: *"Do not pass `--ssp` unless explicitly targeting a non-canonical schema path"*). This produced a two-sided defect across all three jurisdictional postures:
1. **Silent cross-region corruption of the `US_FED` SSP (`system-security-plan.yaml`):** Running `python -m src.gateway.governance.oscal_ssp_exporter export` in an environment where `CAGE_DEPLOYMENT_REGION=EU_ECB` or `CAGE_DEPLOYMENT_REGION=APAC_MAS` silently overwrote the `US_FED` SSP ([`compliance/oscal/system-security-plan.yaml`](../compliance/oscal/system-security-plan.yaml), where the generated `sa-11` `implemented-requirement` block `_STPA_IMPL_UUID` `g7000099-stpa-4000-8000-compiler00001` spans `lines 1630–1853`) and standalone patch ([`compliance/oscal/stpa_compiler_ssp_patch.yaml`](../compliance/oscal/stpa_compiler_ssp_patch.yaml)), replacing the `NIST_SP800_53` UCA-to-control crosswalk narratives (`AC-3`, `AC-6`, `AU-2`, `AU-12`, `CA-7`, `IA-2`, `IA-5`, `SC-7`, `SI-10`) with `EU_AI_ACT` (`EU AI Act Art. 9 / Art. 14 / DORA Art. 6, Art. 9, Art. 10 / GDPR Art. 22, Art. 25, Art. 30, Art. 32`) or `MAS_FEAT` (`MAS FEAT / MAS Notice 655 / MAS TRM §4.2, §6.3, §9.1.1, §11.1, §12.1`) narratives and exiting `0`.
2. **Never-generated `EU_ECB` and `APAC_MAS` SSPs (`system-security-plan-eu-ecb.yaml` and `system-security-plan-apac-mas.yaml`):** Because default exports had always written only to `compliance/oscal/system-security-plan.yaml` (`git log -S _STPA_IMPL_UUID compliance/oscal/`), neither `2bacc478:compliance/oscal/system-security-plan-eu-ecb.yaml` (332 lines, 10 hand-authored `implemented-requirements`) nor `2bacc478:compliance/oscal/system-security-plan-apac-mas.yaml` (323 lines, 10 hand-authored `implemented-requirements`) had ever received the exporter's generated `_STPA_IMPL_UUID` (`g7000099-stpa-4000-8000-compiler00001`) block, the `EU_AI_ACT` or `MAS_FEAT` UCA-to-control crosswalk narratives from [`config/oscal/framework_mappings/EU_AI_ACT.json`](../config/oscal/framework_mappings/EU_AI_ACT.json) and [`config/oscal/framework_mappings/MAS_FEAT.json`](../config/oscal/framework_mappings/MAS_FEAT.json), the `assurance-posture` property, or the `customer_responsibility_tier3_ledger_apis` statement. Any EU or APAC compliance claim resting on those two SSP documents rested on an artifact that was never populated by the exporter.

**Empirical verification on `main` (`2026-11-02`):**
- **Has the `US_FED` overwrite fired on `main`?** **No.** Searching [`compliance/oscal/system-security-plan.yaml`](../compliance/oscal/system-security-plan.yaml) at `HEAD` and across full git history (`git log -S "EU AI Act" -S "EU_AI_ACT" -S "MAS FEAT" -S "MAS_FEAT" -S "DORA" -p -- compliance/oscal/system-security-plan.yaml`) shows that the `_STPA_IMPL_UUID` block (`lines 1630–1853`) contains only `NIST_SP800_53` (`AC-3`, `AC-6`, `AU-2`, `AU-12`, `CA-7`, `IA-2`, `IA-5`, `SC-7`, `SI-10`) narratives; the only occurrences of `EU AI Act` or `MAS FEAT` in the file are the hand-written `metadata.remarks` scope notes (`bb48cc39`).
- **Did `eu-ecb-compliance.yml` or `apac-mas-compliance.yml` invoke `oscal_ssp_exporter` prior to remediation?** **No.** Neither `2bacc478:.github/workflows/eu-ecb-compliance.yml`, `2bacc478:.github/workflows/apac-mas-compliance.yml`, `385e502e^:.github/workflows/compliance-matrix.yml`, nor `385e502e^:scripts/run_matrix_tests.sh` invoked `oscal_ssp_exporter.py` (they ran `scripts/verify_langfuse_posture.py` and `pytest` only). The corruption path was triggered whenever an engineer or script ran `python -m src.gateway.governance.oscal_ssp_exporter export` with `CAGE_DEPLOYMENT_REGION=EU_ECB` or `APAC_MAS` set in the shell environment.

**Remediation (`fix/oscal-ssp-freshness`, `2026-11-04`):**
1. **Region-aware SSP routing and `--ssp` region inference ([`src/gateway/governance/oscal_ssp_exporter.py`](../src/gateway/governance/oscal_ssp_exporter.py) `lines 181–203, 1350–1379, 1590–1628`):**
   - `REGIONAL_PROFILES` (`lines 181–203`) maps each deployment region to its canonical `ssp_path` (`US_FED` → `compliance/oscal/system-security-plan.yaml`, `EU_ECB` → `compliance/oscal/system-security-plan-eu-ecb.yaml`, `APAC_MAS` → `compliance/oscal/system-security-plan-apac-mas.yaml`).
   - When `--ssp` is omitted (`args.ssp is None`, `lines 1594–1597`), `cmd_export()` resolves `ssp_path` from `REGIONAL_PROFILES[region]["ssp_path"]`. When `--ssp` is explicitly passed (`lines 1598–1603`), `_infer_region_from_ssp()` (`lines 1350–1379`) infers the deployment region from the canonical path or `metadata.props[name=="cage-deployment-region"]` so `--check --ssp <file>` uses the matching regional framework without requiring `CAGE_DEPLOYMENT_REGION` in the environment.
   - `comp_def_path` and `patch_out_path` default to `_DEFAULT_COMP_DEF` and `_DEFAULT_PATCH_OUT` only when exporting the default `US_FED` SSP (`is_default_us_ssp`, `lines 1616–1628`), preventing regional exports from overwriting the `US_FED` standalone patch or component definition.
2. **Exported `EU_ECB` and `APAC_MAS` SSPs and wired CI drift gates:**
   - Exported [`compliance/oscal/system-security-plan-eu-ecb.yaml`](../compliance/oscal/system-security-plan-eu-ecb.yaml) (`1,078` lines; `_STPA_IMPL_UUID` `g7000099-stpa-4000-8000-compiler00001` at `lines 809–1029`) and [`compliance/oscal/system-security-plan-apac-mas.yaml`](../compliance/oscal/system-security-plan-apac-mas.yaml) (`686` lines; `_STPA_IMPL_UUID` `g7000099-stpa-4000-8000-compiler00001` at `lines 444–643`).
   - Added `uv run --no-sync python -m src.gateway.governance.oscal_ssp_exporter export --check --ssp ...` to [`.github/workflows/eu-ecb-compliance.yml`](../.github/workflows/eu-ecb-compliance.yml) (`lines 68–73`) and [`.github/workflows/apac-mas-compliance.yml`](../.github/workflows/apac-mas-compliance.yml) (`lines 68–73`); added `TestRegionalSspExportAndCheck` in [`tests/test_oscal_ssp_exporter.py`](../tests/test_oscal_ssp_exporter.py) (`lines 1233–1319`).
   - Recorded the missing `UCA-10` and `UCA-12` mappings in `EU_AI_ACT.json` and `MAS_FEAT.json` discovered during regeneration as `POAM-2026-188`.

### POAM-2026-121: Validation Report Makes a False Composition Claim

**Control:** NIST CA-2 (Control Assessments), CA-7 (Continuous Monitoring)
**Risk Level:** High
**Status:** Open — decided 2026-10-28: the claim is withdrawn, not proven. §3 and the report's head carry do-not-rely notes until the withdrawal is applied
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-28
**Target Closure:** 2026-11-15

**Description:**
The [Regional Posture Validation Report](compliance/REGIONAL_POSTURE_VALIDATION_REPORT.md) is a published compliance validation report.
- Its §3 is the evidence that domain plugins and jurisdictional postures compose independently.
- It states an "Independence Guarantee": installing `cage_finance` and `cage_healthcare` "adds domain-specific controls without colliding on common controls".
- It pins a control count and an RFC 8785 JCS SHA-256 hash for each of 12 cells: no plugin, `cage_finance`, `cage_healthcare` and both, in each of US_FED, EU_ECB and APAC_MAS.
- It concludes that "Domain plugins and regional postures compose orthogonally".

**The claim is false, and was false when written.**
- `cage_finance`'s US_FED overlay redefines the kernel's `CTRL_FTRA_001`. At `main`, its internal ID, description, primary framework, legacy citation and co-frameworks all differ from the kernel's. A plugin overlay replacing a kernel control is the negation of independent composition.
- The redefinition is present at every commit checked, from `3b69207c` (the tree the hashes were computed on) through both report commits to `main`.
- The registry logs it as a "Control mapping collision" whenever it loads the cell. Rerunning the documented method at `3b69207c` logs it for both US_FED cells that include `cage_finance`, so the run that produced the hashes logged it too (inferred).

**The hashes were computed by the documented method, one commit early.** The documented method registers each plugin's overlay directory with `register_overlay_dir()`, calls `ControlRegistry.reconfigure()` for the region and reads `active_hash`. Rerun with each commit's own registry code:
- **`3b69207c`**, the parent of the commit that published the table: all 12 hashes and all 12 counts reproduce exactly.
- **`2889b955`** (the publishing commit) and **`f2363d07`** (the report's last commit): no hash reproduces, though every count does.
  - `2889b955` also added a `pii_retention_authority` key to each kernel baseline. The key is not a control, so it changed every cell's hash and no cell's count.
- **`main`** (`2ef4f013`): neither the hashes nor 6 of the 12 counts reproduce. #378 moved `CTRL_MRM_004` out of `cage_finance`'s overlays into the kernel baselines, and since then `cage_finance` adds no control in any region.

The table has therefore never described a tree that contains it. That part is a recoverable error, not a fabrication.

**What the `CTRL_FTRA_001` override changes at runtime.** The registry serves the overlay's definition (verified). But no runtime path reads that mapping's content (read): FTRA violations carry no control-ID prefix for `_control_payload()` to resolve, and deferrals record only the ID. FTRA's allow, deny and defer behaviour is therefore unaffected today. That is by circumstance, not design: the moment anything reads that mapping, the same overlay changes FTRA too. The mechanism that permits the override is a kernel defect in its own right, recorded as POAM-2026-123.

**What this settles.**
- Independence is not merely unproven: it is violated today in a path that changes a tier's verdict.
- A plugin overlay that defines `CTRL_FRIA_006` moves the EU_ECB FRIA tier past its Art. 27(2) staleness gate. A hard denial becomes a provider consultation (POAM-2026-123, verified).
- Independence is therefore an aspiration the report stated, not a property CAGE has. The claim is withdrawn rather than proven.

**Nothing would have caught this.**
- No test pins a cell or fails on a collision.
- The two workflows that exist to check the postures cannot fail the build. A workflow that cannot fail is telemetry, not a test.
  - [`regional-posture.yml`](../.github/workflows/regional-posture.yml) is not a required check. It runs only after merge or on dispatch, its path filter leaves out the plugins' overlay directories, and it soft-gates EU_ECB and APAC_MAS.
  - The test step of `385e502e^:.github/workflows/compliance-matrix.yml` was `continue-on-error` until #602 (`319c702d`), and the step it masked stopped at `uv: command not found` (POAM-2026-150).

**Remediation:**
1. Withdraw the independence claim in the report.
   - Withdraw §3's "Independence Guarantee" and "Key Finding".
   - Withdraw the executive summary's "confirming independent composability of domain plugins and jurisdictional overlays".
   - Withdraw the conclusion's "true composability".
   - Keep the cell table only as a description of the matrix, not as evidence of independence. If it stays, regenerate it from a committed script at a stated commit, listing each cell's control set and any kernel control a plugin redefines.
2. Restate independence as a property only after both of these hold:
   - POAM-2026-123's fix makes a plugin overlay that redefines a kernel control fail closed;
   - a test in the required job fails on a collision.
3. Make `regional-posture.yml` able to fail, or describe it as telemetry wherever it is cited as a check.

Until the withdrawal is applied, §3 and the head of the report tell readers not to rely on the claim.

### POAM-2026-120: Closed Controls Without a Guarding Test

**Control:** NIST SA-11 (Developer Testing and Evaluation), CM-4 (Impact Analyses), CA-7 (Continuous Monitoring)
**Risk Level:** Moderate
**Status:** Open — forward requirement recorded; High backfill not started
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-27
**Target Closure:** 2026-12-31

**Description:**
POAM-2026-023's remediation (#102) moved the CBF balance read out of `LUA_ATOMIC_CBF`. That removed the serialization POAM-2026-070's control depended on: from #102 until #256, two concurrent trades that were each admissible alone, and not together, both committed. Every test still passed. The POA&M/Lula drift checker ([`check_poam_lula_divergence.py`](../scripts/check_poam_lula_divergence.py)) still counted POAM-2026-070 as covered, because its coverage is a file-name match: a closed entry counts as covered when the name of a Lula validation file contains one of its control IDs. The two files that matched POAM-2026-070, `lula-validation-sc4.yaml` and `lula-validation-si2.yaml`, do not mention the CBF. The defect was found by following a stale path, not by any check, and one undetected instance is weak evidence that it is the only one.

A closure in this document records that a control was implemented and verified once. Nothing requires a test that fails when the control stops operating, so a later change can disable a closed control silently. On 2026-10-27, 34 of the 80 closed entries cited no test in their row or detail section: POAM-2026-001, POAM-2026-007, POAM-2026-012, POAM-2026-013, POAM-2026-024, POAM-2026-027, POAM-2026-028, POAM-2026-030-B, POAM-2026-031, POAM-2026-037, POAM-2026-038, POAM-2026-039, POAM-2026-040, POAM-2026-041, POAM-2026-042, POAM-2026-044, POAM-2026-050, POAM-2026-051, POAM-2026-052, POAM-2026-053, POAM-2026-054, POAM-2026-055, POAM-2026-056, POAM-2026-057, POAM-2026-058, POAM-2026-059, POAM-2026-060, POAM-2026-063, POAM-2026-064, POAM-2026-067, POAM-2026-068, POAM-2026-071, POAM-2026-072 and POAM-2026-074. Citing a test is not the same as being guarded by one: POAM-2026-070 was among the other 46, citing a test module that has never existed (`git log --all -- "**/test_reconciliation_daemon*"`). Only the entries that record a mutation or sensitivity check (POAM-2026-109, POAM-2026-110, POAM-2026-111 and now POAM-2026-070, for example) show that their tests fail without the control. POAM-2026-023, POAM-2026-069 and POAM-2026-070 are now listed under `CODE_EVIDENCE_FINDINGS`, so the drift checker counts them by their tests rather than by a Lula file name.

Recorded, not swept: the drift checker's other 52 "covered" entries rest on the same file-name match and are not checked against what the files assert (for 26 of them the matched files share no identifier the entry cites). ADR-010 and the docstring of `tests/test_cbf_atomic_cas.py` credit the fence-epoch compare-and-set with stopping concurrent commits; since #506 (2026-10-22) it retries instead, and the in-script debit total is what stops them (POAM-2026-070's sensitivity check).

**The same shape in the consensus path (2026-10-28).** Finance's only high-stakes action is `HIGH_VALUE_TRADE`. [`test_consensus_config_failclosed.py`](../tests/cage_finance/test_consensus_config_failclosed.py) asserts it is present, but no tool or classifier has ever emitted that action name (`git log -S HIGH_VALUE_TRADE src/`). The test proves the override is configured, not that it operates: the override has never fired, and consensus is triggered by amount alone, with any inference backend. Recorded as an instance, not swept.

**Fixtures configured around a defect rather than detecting it (2026-10-29).** Four defects have shared the same masking mechanism in tests: `MockTelemetryProvider` ([`telemetry_provider.py`](../src/gateway/governance/telemetry_provider.py)) and synthetic causal telemetry generating outcomes from the predictor's own formula (POAM-2026-094, POAM-2026-118); output-rail tests mocking a response channel the production call path never populated (POAM-2026-125, POAM-2026-140); `MagicMock` truthiness tripping an adjacent guard and the test stubbing out the function under test; and 20 test modules setting `CAGE_SEAL_STRICT_MODE=false`, which hid that [`.env.example`](../.env.example) and [`docker-compose.yml`](../docker-compose.yml) minted HMAC seals rejected by [`verify_seal()`](../src/gateway/governance/routing_seal.py)'s default strict mode until `#588` (`99d0affd`) ran the stream oracle against a live Compose stack. A test that disables an enforcement mode is a test that cannot observe it. Unlike declared-but-unconsumed configuration variables, no static gate detects fixtures that turn off runtime enforcement flags; all four instances were found by reading or live-stack execution. Recorded as an instance, not swept.

**The same switch, set by a file no test names (2026-11-01).** [`tests/conftest.py`](../tests/conftest.py) loaded the repository's `.env` into every session, once through a bare `load_dotenv()` that also searched every parent directory. `git clean` keeps ignored files, so a `.env` outlives the run that wrote it in a reused worktree. On 2026-11-01 one left by a quickstart run turned 87 tests red. The quiet direction is the same mechanism: a `.env` that sets `CAGE_SEAL_STRICT_MODE=false` would do what the 20 modules above did, for a whole session, without appearing in any test file or command. Removing conftest's loaders is not enough. litellm, which `src` imports, calls `load_dotenv()` when it is imported, and its search passes the repository root because the virtualenv lives inside it. Closed for test sessions on `fix/hermetic-dotenv-guard` (`#627`, merged on `main` as `6ebffb8c`, branch head `66021c07`, `2026-11-01`): conftest reads no `.env`, and a hermetic session refuses to start while one can reach it ([`dotenv_guard.py`](../tests/fixtures/dotenv_guard.py), [`test_dotenv_guard.py`](../tests/test_dotenv_guard.py)). Live runs are exempt and take their values from the environment.

**Tests whose inputs cannot produce what they claim (2026-11-01).** A test observes only what its inputs can produce, and three instances now pass or fail on inputs the production path never presents. (1) provider_01's step 2 qualification vectors set `prefer_receipt_id_for_rec`, a flag no production caller sets (POAM-2026-157). (2) The same vectors run on payloads built by hand, with keys [`FriaTier`](../src/gateway/governance/jurisdiction/eu_ai_act/fria_tier.py) never sends (`approval_id`, `target`), so TC-06 passes only on a path production does not take (POAM-2026-157). (3) [`test_gke_trade_flow.py`](../tests/e2e/test_gke_trade_flow.py) records its two-operator quorum by sending each operator's identity as `l5d-client-id` from one meshed Job. The bridge's Linkerd proxy replaces that header with the Job's own identity (observed on staging on 2026-11-01; POAM-2026-167, #625), so the bridge sees the same caller twice and cannot record a quorum: a repeat approval gets 409. Through the mesh the test cannot present two operators. It could pass only against a bridge that takes the header from the caller, which is the defect POAM-2026-167 closes. Recorded as instances, not swept.

**Detection rule (2026-11-02).** A result that agrees with itself is suspect on its face: a before/after with no difference, a mutant that passes, a negative control that behaves like the positive, a planted defect the gate does not notice, a fixture derived from the code under test. Before citing a check as evidence, ask what it would have reported with the control removed; if the answer is the same, it is not evidence.

**Remediation:**
1. Forward: **a closure cites the verification, not the change** (verified across every consumer and store that holds the state or control — e.g. `POAM-2026-149` and `POAM-2026-071` / `POAM-2026-162`), and no entry closes without a test that a recorded sensitivity check shows fails when the control is disabled, or a stated reason the control cannot be tested that way.
2. Backfill High entries only: the eight closed entries recorded High (POAM-2026-079, POAM-2026-080, POAM-2026-081, POAM-2026-087, POAM-2026-088, POAM-2026-092, POAM-2026-093, POAM-2026-102), and any of the 58 closed entries with no recorded severity that a reading of their text rates High. POAM-2026-070, one of the 58, is done.
3. Nothing else is swept. The other closed entries, the drift checker's file-name matches and the claims above stay recorded here.

### POAM-2026-119: Cited Commits That Do Not Resolve

**Control:** NIST CA-5 (Plan of Action and Milestones), CA-7 (Continuous Monitoring)
**Risk Level:** Moderate
**Status:** Open — recorded; the repair follows the commit-citation check
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-27
**Target Closure:** 2026-11-15

**Description:**
Found on 2026-10-27 while extending Gate G9 to this document. Before this entry was added, the document cited 49 distinct hexadecimal identifiers in code spans. 31 named commits on `main`, one named an annotated tag, and four are Cloud Build IDs (POAM-2026-101's verification table), which are not git objects. The other 13 are cited as commits and name no object in the repository. Each was looked up in a full clone of every ref (including the maintainer's private evidence refs), the offsite bundle, the maintainer's workstation clone, and GitHub's commit API, which also resolves pull-request commits that no branch holds:

| Cited | Entries | Found in | Commit on `main` that carries it |
|---|---|---|---|
| `88fa9d7` | POAM-2026-043, POAM-2026-046 | GitHub; workstation | `c1d45f53` (100%) |
| `9c68a6e8`, `72574529` | POAM-2026-098 | GitHub; workstation | `e1f46eca`, #391 (72%, 100%) |
| `f566e9e` | POAM-2026-079, POAM-2026-080 | GitHub; workstation | `8dbd5506`, #300 (100%) |
| `420854f` | POAM-2026-080 | GitHub; workstation | `5aa1523d`, #303 (100%) |
| `8bdd5b2` | POAM-2026-081 | workstation only | `55f97922`, #314 (93%) |
| `c8d66189a94cb782f4e0f61e14e05e341d27a8f6` | POAM-2026-011 | nowhere | — |
| `e0f7700`, `b2369c6`, `9d5d61a` | POAM-2026-079 | nowhere | — |
| `8df072e` | POAM-2026-080 | nowhere | — |
| `ad9ba9c7`, `8d2ccf03` | POAM-2026-083 | nowhere | — |

The percentage is the share of files the cited commit touched whose content is byte-identical in the mainline commit; below 100%, the pull request changed some of those files again before it merged. The workstation copies are unreferenced objects, which `git gc` prunes.

None of the 13 is reachable from the private refs, so none is an advisory-fork commit preserved there. The six that resolve are pre-squash branch commits: recoverable, but cited at an object an assessor checking `main` cannot find. The seven that resolve nowhere leave POAM-2026-011's closure with no checkable commit evidence, POAM-2026-079 and POAM-2026-080 with part of theirs, and POAM-2026-083 (open) with two attested commits that nobody can inspect.

**Three additional citation-integrity failures observed on `2026-10-30`:** (1) `#582`'s hash citation before merge, (2) the `archive/causal-outcome-detector` tag (`5735a3d7`), and (3) `d103ff9d` (a non-existent SHA initially written into [`.gitleaks.toml`](../.gitleaks.toml) and `POAM-2026-160` / `POAM-2026-162` before being caught by inspection and corrected to `e5376b58` / sub-commit `b3fd2353`). Together these argue for extending Gate G9 beyond `<sha>^:<path>` ancestors and `archive/*` tags to **any SHA-shaped string in tracked docs**.

**Remediation:**
1. Extend Gate G9 to verify commit citations across **any SHA-shaped string in tracked docs** alongside the pinned-path machinery (the commit exists and is an ancestor of HEAD), telling apart identifiers that are not commits and this entry's own inventory. A commit kept off `main` on purpose is cited together with an `archive/*` tag (POAM-2026-094 cites `5735a3d7` as `archive/causal-outcome-detector`); for those the check verifies that the tag exists and peels to the cited commit, instead of ancestry.
2. Repoint the six pre-squash citations to their mainline commits.
3. For each of the seven, find the mainline commit by content; where there is none, re-verify the closure against HEAD with dated evidence, or state in the entry that its commit evidence cannot be checked.

### POAM-2026-118: Enforcing Postures Can Select the Mock Telemetry Provider

**Control:** NIST SI-10 (Information Input Validation), CA-7 (Continuous Monitoring); `CTRL_MRM_004`
**Risk Level:** Moderate
**Status:** Open — latent; adding a timestamp column to `MockTelemetryProvider` would arm it
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-27
**Target Closure:** 2026-11-15

**Description:**
POAM-2026-088 closed on the rule that enforcing postures never substitute synthetic data for live telemetry. For the spec's synthetic factory that rule is checked by posture: `CausalGatekeeper._synthetic_telemetry_permitted()` ([`gatekeeper.py`](../src/gateway/governance/causal/gatekeeper.py)) refuses whenever `is_enforcing()` is true. Provider selection is checked by environment name instead. [`get_telemetry_provider()`](../src/gateway/governance/telemetry_provider.py) refuses `CAGE_TELEMETRY_PROVIDER=mock` only when `CAGE_ENV` is `prod` or `production`. [`is_enforcing()`](../src/gateway/governance/env_posture.py) also treats `staging` and `local` as enforcing, since only `dev`, `test` and `ci` relax it, and no other code path rejects `mock`. A staging or local deployment set to `mock` would therefore feed the causal tier `MockTelemetryProvider` rows as if they were live. Those rows are generated from the formula the model is calibrated on (POAM-2026-094).

The path is refused today only by accident. `MockTelemetryProvider` returns no timestamp column, and the freshness check in `validate_world_model()` fails closed when no timestamp column is present (verdict `stale_telemetry`). Adding a timestamp column is the obvious change for anyone trying to use the mock. Its rows would then pass the minimum-sample, freshness and placebo checks, as the finance synthetic rows already do in `dev`; this is inferred from that path, not exercised. The tier would then decide `execute_trade` in an enforcing posture from synthetic data, using POAM-2026-117's baseline. The shipped manifests (`de5534ac:deployment/k8s/gateway.yaml`, [`main.tf`](../infra/modules/gateway/main.tf)) set `remote`, so reaching this path needs explicit configuration.

This is tracked apart from POAM-2026-117 so that a change elsewhere cannot close it as a side effect.

**Remediation:**
1. Select on `is_enforcing()` instead of the environment name.
2. Add a test that `mock` is refused in `staging` and `local`, whatever the mock emits.

### POAM-2026-117: Causal Risk Boundary Can Fail Open as Volatility Rises

**Control:** NIST SI-10 (Information Input Validation), CA-7 (Continuous Monitoring); `CTRL_MRM_004` (marginal risk boundary)
**Risk Level:** High
**Status:** Open — latent while POAM-2026-094 is open. **POAM-2026-094 MUST NOT close before this item.**
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-27
**Target Closure:** 2026-12-31, and before POAM-2026-094

**Description:**
[`CausalGatekeeper._within_risk_boundary`](../src/gateway/governance/causal/gatekeeper.py) refuses a request when [`_marginal_risk`](../src/gateway/governance/causal/gatekeeper.py) exceeds `CAUSAL_LOCK_RISK_BOUNDARY` (0.95). Both branches of `CausalGatekeeper.evaluate()` call it once the world model is trusted, and the finance tier sends every `execute_trade` through it. `_marginal_risk` is `min(1, max(0, 0.5 + β × amount / normalization_scale))`, the formula POAM-2026-057 records as implemented.

Phase 1 estimates β with backdoor adjustment for volatility, but the prediction has no volatility term. Its 0.5 is neither configured nor estimated. It is not the intercept of the formula the tier is calibrated on (0), nor that formula's mean outcome at zero treatment (0.25). The trip amount `0.45 × normalization_scale / β` is therefore the same in calm and volatile markets. Where volatility drives most of the risk, the boundary is too strict in calm markets and too loose in volatile ones: it opens wider as risk rises. The constant is in the kernel (`src/gateway/`), so every domain plugin inherits it. G3 cannot flag it, because its forbidden-literal set holds domain strings and a bare `0.5` is not one.

**Demonstration (reproducible frame):**
1. **Process.** Draw n = 600 rows from `numpy.random.default_rng(20261027)`, in this order:
   - volatility `v ~ U(0.1, 0.9)`;
   - amount `a = clip(N(5000, 1500) − 2000·v, 100, 10000)`;
   - noise `N(0, 0.05)`.

   The outcome is `risk = 0.05 + 0.8·v + 0.2·a / 10,000 + noise`. Add a fresh timestamp column, because the freshness check requires one.
2. **Fit.** Run `validate_world_model()` with the finance causal graph (`market_volatility → trade_amount`, `market_volatility → risk_score`, `trade_amount → risk_score`) and `normalization_scale` = 10,000. It returns a trusted verdict with β = 0.1764373. Ordinary least squares of `risk` on `[1, v, a / 10,000]` gives intercept 0.0626284, volatility coefficient 0.7973928 and amount coefficient 0.1764373, which equals β.
3. **Kernel trip.** `0.45 × 10,000 / β` = **$25,504.81**, at any volatility.
4. **Volatility-aware trip of the same fit.** `(0.95 − 0.0626284 − 0.7973928·v) × 10,000 / β` gives $45,774.46 at v = 0.1, $27,696.82 at v = 0.5 and **$9,619.17 at v = 0.9**.
5. **True trip of the process at v = 0.9:** **$9,000**.

At v = 0.9 the kernel permits 2.65× the volatility-aware limit and 2.83× the true one. The frame's amounts stop at $10,000. Within the data, the over-permission is therefore $9,619.17 to $10,000; above $10,000 both trip points are extrapolations.

The direction depends on the process. When amount drives most of the risk (`0.05 + 0.3·v + 0.6·a / 10,000` in the same frame), the kernel trips at $7,806.57. The volatility-aware trips range from $14,878.15 at v = 0.1 to $10,750.83 at v = 0.9, so the kernel is too strict at every volatility. The values are stable across BLAS builds to better than 1e-12 relative.

**Reachability:**
- **Enforcing postures (`staging`, `production`, `local`): latent.** No live source produces the causal rows (POAM-2026-094), so no verdict is trusted, and `evaluate()` refuses before it reaches the boundary.
- **Permissive postures (`dev`, `test`, `ci`): active**, on the finance synthetic telemetry.
- **Ordering.** Remediating POAM-2026-094 gives enforcing postures trusted verdicts, which turns this boundary on. **POAM-2026-094 MUST NOT close before this item.** The natural order of work is the dangerous one. Closing POAM-2026-094 first moves the tier from refusing every trade to a gate that can permit 2.65× its volatility-aware limit at high volatility.

**Remediation options:**
1. *(Preferred)* **Bound the effect, not the level.** Refuse when the estimated causal contribution `β × amount / normalization_scale` exceeds a configured budget. This removes the baseline instead of moving it somewhere else, needs no per-request volatility value, and stays domain-agnostic. It is a breaking change, because `CAUSAL_LOCK_RISK_BOUNDARY` changes meaning.
2. **Predict the level from the fitted model** at the request's current volatility. This needs volatility on the request path. Finance's live volatility is a constant 0.15 today (POAM-2026-094), so this option inherits that gap.
3. **Supply the baseline from the domain** in `CausalSpec`. The constant would still be invented, only owned elsewhere.

**Closure requires:**
1. a remediation on `main`;
2. a test that reproduces the frame above, failing before the remediation and passing after it;
3. the boundary tests' pinned trip point re-derived. It is $9,143.93 today, from β = 0.4921298 on the finance synthetic telemetry.

### POAM-2026-116: Release Tags Were Not Protected

**Control:** NIST SI-7 (Software, Firmware, and Information Integrity), CM-5 (Access Restrictions for Change)
**Risk Level:** Moderate
**Status:** Closed 2026-11-02 — remediated and verified on 2026-10-27; checked in CI since 2026-11-02 by [`repo-rules.yml`](../.github/workflows/repo-rules.yml) (closed-findings row)
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-27
**Target Closure:** 2026-10-30

**Description:**
No ruleset protected any tag. Ruleset 19769024, named "Restrict tag creation to maintainer", targeted branches: its conditions were `refs/heads/v*-ref` and `refs/heads/v*-cage-*`, so its deletion, non-fast-forward and creation rules applied to no tag. Any account with write access could move or delete a release tag, including `v4.0.0`: the version GHSA-c8pw-f3g3-xg7c names as patched, and the tag the v4.0.0 release notes and the archived evidence bundles point at. `.github/branch-protection-rules.md` §4 described tag protection as configured.

**Remediation (2026-10-27):**
1. Tag ruleset 25965917 ("Protect release tags"): target `tag`; include `refs/tags/v*` and `refs/tags/client-v*`, which covers all 10 existing tags; rules `deletion`, `update` and `non_fast_forward`; no bypass actor. Changing a release tag now means editing or disabling the ruleset, which ruleset history records. Creating tags is not restricted; only admins have write access.
2. Ruleset 19769024 set to `disabled`. It protected nothing, and its name said otherwise.
3. `.github/branch-protection-rules.md` §4 now describes the tag ruleset.

**Verification (2026-10-27, over the wire):**
A probe tag, `v0-ruleset-probe-20261027`, was created, which the ruleset allows. GitHub then refused to delete it (`GH013 … Cannot delete this tag`) and to move it to another commit (`GH013 … Cannot update this protected ref`), and the rule-suite log records `deletion=fail` and `update=fail` against ruleset 25965917. The move was a fast-forward, so `non_fast_forward` alone would not have refused it; the `update` rule did. The probe was removed by excluding it, deleting it and restoring the ruleset, which left three entries in the ruleset's history. `v4.0.0` still resolves to tag object `64d9a2bc` and commit `8caa7eaf`.

### POAM-2026-115: Review Rule on `main` Cannot Be Met With One Maintainer

**Control:** NIST AC-5 (Separation of Duties), CM-3 (Configuration Change Control — human change-review bandwidth). Automated check-bypass scope under CM-3 and CM-5 was resolved on 2026-10-27; see *Resolved scope* below.
**Risk Level:** Moderate
**Status:** Accepted risk (2026-11-02). The compensating controls below bound what an admin merge can skip and record it; none supplies an independent reviewer
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-27 (amended 2026-10-29, 2026-10-30, 2026-10-31; reclassified as an accepted risk 2026-11-02)
**Closes when:** a second maintainer is onboarded and the closure criteria below are met. There is no target date: the item closes when a second maintainer exists, not when any work is done

**Description:**
Ruleset 19769096 ("Protect main branch") requires one approving review on `refs/heads/main` (`require_code_owner_review: false`, `dismiss_stale_reviews_on_push: true`). Its one bypass actor is the repository admin role, in mode `always`. The project has one maintainer, and GitHub does not let an author approve their own pull request, so none of the maintainer's own pull requests can meet the rule, and each is merged as an admin bypass. Pull requests by other authors can meet it: in the period measured below, 5 did, each approved by the maintainer.

Since 2026-10-27 the required status checks (Lint; AI 600-1 Unit Tests; OPA Rego Policy Lint and Test; Secret Scanning (Gitleaks); license-check; Pytest Logic Tests; Terraform Tests (gcp-gke, mock providers); and, since 2026-10-29, Quickstart Bootstrap & First-DENY Smoke Gate — eight checks total, all pinned to the GitHub Actions app since 2026-11-02 and three of them before) are in ruleset 25966171 ("Require status checks on main"), which has no bypass actor, so an admin merge skips only the review. The one exception is an advisory fix landed from a private fork, where Actions does not run: the ruleset is disabled for that single merge and re-enabled, and its history records both edits ([`SECURITY.md`](../SECURITY.md)). [`check_pr_mergeable.sh`](../scripts/check_pr_mergeable.sh) verifies the required checks on the exact head SHA and, with `--comment`, posts the result on the pull request; nothing requires it to run (*Compensating controls* below). Before `POAM-2026-160` closed on 2026-10-30 (`319c702d`, #602), Secret Scanning (Gitleaks) loaded a configuration with no detection rules and so could not report a finding; since `319c702d`, [`.gitleaks.toml`](../.gitleaks.toml) extends the built-in ruleset (`[extend]` `useDefault = true`).

Until 2026-11-02 the review rule could also be met with no one reviewing. The repository let GitHub Actions create and approve pull requests (`can_approve_pull_request_reviews: true`), so a workflow's token could approve a pull request and satisfy the rule, the case GitHub introduced the setting to prevent: a change reaching a protected branch that no other person reviewed. No merge did: none of the 75 merged from #552 to #632 had an approving review, and no workflow approves pull requests. The setting is off since 2026-11-02 (`GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/actions/permissions/workflow`).

`.github/branch-protection-rules.md` stated that code-owner review was enabled. The live rule does not require it, and one maintainer could not satisfy it. The change that opened this entry corrected the document.

**Why an accepted risk (2026-11-02):**
Until 2026-11-02 this entry was an open finding with remediation milestones, and its rolling count of review-bypassed merges grew with each day's merges. An open finding implies that someone can close it by doing work. Nothing closes this one but a second maintainer: with one maintainer, every merge of a maintainer-authored pull request bypasses the review rule, whatever its size or the care taken (the separation-of-duties point in the analysis below). None of the 75 pull requests merged from #552 (`2d65e15c`, 2026-10-27) to #632 (`d8c9f226`, 2026-11-02) had an approving review. #632 shows the path: every required check passed on its head (`b310ed8c`), its pre-merge record is on the pull request, and the merge still needed `--admin`, because no one else can approve. The counts in that analysis stop at 2026-10-31. Every maintainer-authored merge adds one, so from here on they would measure merge volume, not a change in the risk.

**Compensating controls (verified 2026-11-02):**
1. **Required checks that `--admin` cannot skip.** `GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/rulesets/25966171` returns `enforcement: active`, no bypass actors and eight required status checks. Its version history (`GET /repos/{owner}/{repo}/rulesets/25966171/history`) has no version between 2026-10-29 and 2026-11-02, so it was not disabled in that time; disabling it for a private-fork advisory merge adds a version. `--admin` therefore bypasses ruleset 19769096's review rule and nothing else. Until 2026-11-02 only three of the checks (Pytest Logic Tests; Terraform Tests (gcp-gke, mock providers); Quickstart Bootstrap & First-DENY Smoke Gate) were pinned to the GitHub Actions app (`integration_id` 15368). The other five accepted a result of that name from any source that could post one, and [`check_pr_mergeable.sh`](../scripts/check_pr_mergeable.sh), which fails a check when any source reporting its name is not `success`, could not tell such a stand-in from a run that never happened. On three recent heads all five came only from GitHub Actions; since version 55884759 (2026-11-02) all eight are pinned to it. **Caveat (POAM-2026-171):** the checks run on the pull request merged into `main` as it stood when they started, and report on its head. Nothing runs them on the commit that lands before it lands. This control therefore bounds what `--admin` can skip, not what reaches `main`: #598's head passed `Pytest Logic Tests`, and its squash commit `43528275` failed it on `main`.
2. **A pre-merge record on the pull request.** `check_pr_mergeable.sh <pr> --comment` verifies every required check on the exact head SHA through the REST check-runs and commit-status APIs, posts the result table on the pull request, and prints the merge command pinned to that SHA with `--match-head-commit`. Nothing requires it to run, and nothing notices when it has not. Of the 75 merges above, 55 carry a record posted before the merge, one carries a record posted 20 seconds after it (#557), and 19 carry none: #571, #586, #587, #589, #592, #593 and all 13 merged on 2026-11-01 (UTC), #617–#629.

**Residual risk accepted:**
- **Independent review (AC-5).** Neither control supplies a reviewer, as `check_pr_mergeable.sh`'s header says. Required checks catch mechanical, type and pinned-contract regressions, not missing invariants or tests configured around a defect (POAM-2026-120, POAM-2026-150), and those have entered `main` on green pull requests.
- **The merge result.** The ruleset does not require branches to be up to date (`strict_required_status_checks_policy: false`), so nothing re-runs the checks when `main` moves between their run and the merge. Skew between sequential merges can be caught on `main` only after the merge, and only by a later run that completes. Each push to `main` cancels the run in progress for the commit before it, and 9 of the 47 commits from `f43efeba` to `2431ec35` have no complete run of their own (POAM-2026-171; policy below).
- **The acceptance.** With one maintainer, the same person writes a change, merges it and accepts this risk. An adopter decides acceptance for its own deployment.

**What the review-bypassed merges measure (2026-10-30 / 2026-10-31; counts corrected 2026-11-02):**
From `2026-09-30` to `2026-10-31` (UTC), 233 of the 240 pull requests merged into `main` had no approving review, about 7.3 a day. The batch this analysis was written about merged on `2026-10-29` (`#588` `99d0affd`, `#589` `8d167084`, `#590` `009b1973`), `2026-10-30` (`#592` `23f48374`, `#593` `34709d55`, `#597` `0d83eb4c`, `#599` `4970e187`, `#601` `7f0c1c4b`, `#602` `319c702d`, `#598` `43528275`) and `2026-10-31` (`#604` `260c4189`, `#594` `7edbf230`, `#595` `e07102b9`, `#600` `faa6aec4`). **Correction (2026-11-02):** this paragraph said 105 merges in that window, from `gh pr list --state merged --base main`, about 3.5 a day, and the entry's first row said 94 from 2026-09-29 to 2026-10-29. GitHub's search API returns 240 merged and 7 approved for the first window and 226 merged and 7 approved for the second (`gh api search/issues -f q='repo:<repo> is:pr is:merged base:main merged:2026-09-30..2026-10-31'`, with `review:approved` added for the approved count), and `gh pr list` with a limit above the total gives the same 240. The paragraph also put `#588`, `#589` and `#590` on 2026-10-30 and left out `#592`. The rule-suites table below, 215 pushes in the 30 days to 2026-10-27, agreed with the corrected counts all along. The three meanings below are unchanged; the throughput behind the second was about twice what it said:
- **Separation of duties (AC-5):** with `n = 1` maintainer and self-approval disabled on GitHub, every maintainer-authored merge necessarily bypasses the review rule regardless of change size or care taken.
- **Change velocity vs. comprehension bandwidth (CM-3):** 233 merges from 2026-09-30 to 2026-10-31 (~7.3 pull requests/day, many authored with AI coding agents across multiple subsystems) exceeds the rate at which a single human author/reviewer can audit every cross-module interaction before merge. Automated status checks catch mechanical, type, and pinned-contract regressions, not missing invariants or tests configured around a defect: unguarded closed controls (`POAM-2026-120`), CI jobs whose flags or exit codes could not fail (`POAM-2026-150`), and 20 test modules disabling `CAGE_SEAL_STRICT_MODE` while [`.env.example`](../.env.example) minted unverifiable seals (`#588`, `99d0affd`) all entered `main` on green pull requests and were caught afterward by dedicated audit sweeps rather than merge-time review. Adding a second maintainer addresses both halves only if review pace stays bounded by human comprehension rather than rubber-stamping throughput.
- **Sequential fast-merge semantic skew and explicit post-merge gate policy (`2026-10-31`):** On `2026-10-30`, five textually conflict-free pull requests (`#597`, `#599`, `#601`, `#602`, `#598`) were checked with pairwise `git merge-tree` against `main` as it stood before the batch and merged sequentially without rebasing onto each intermediate `main`. `--match-head-commit` protects against the *branch* moving under the merge command; nothing protects against *`main`* moving under the branch. Because `git merge-tree` detects only textual overlap, `#599` (`4970e187`, removing four dead `--ignore-vuln` flags from [`security-scan.yml`](../.github/workflows/security-scan.yml) and adding [`test_workflow_vuln_suppressions.py`](../tests/test_workflow_vuln_suppressions.py)) and `#602` (`319c702d`, copying pre-`#599` `security-scan.yml` content into [`dependency-review.yml`](../.github/workflows/dependency-review.yml)) touched disjoint lines and were both legitimately `8/8 PASS` on their own heads, yet merging `#602` 53 seconds after `#599` produced a semantic regression on `main` (`run 41143961049`). **Policy:** rather than rebasing and burning a ~15-minute CI cycle after every individual conflict-free merge in a sequence, sequential admin merges of textually disjoint pull requests consciously rely on `main`'s post-merge CI gates to catch semantic skew — a policy that is only defensible because `#602` (`319c702d`) made `main`'s gates fail-closed — and require clearing a red `main` (here via `#604`, `260c4189`) before rebasing or merging subsequent pull requests (`#594` `7edbf230`, `#595` `e07102b9`, `#600` `faa6aec4`). **Not in force for Compliance Matrix (2026-11-02):** it has failed on every completed run on `main` since `#598` (`43528275`, 2026-10-30), 32 failures with 33 commits merged since. `385e502e^:scripts/run_matrix_tests.sh`, which its regional jobs ran, has called `uv` since 2026-07-08, and `385e502e^:.github/workflows/compliance-matrix.yml` has never installed it (`git log -S setup-uv -- .github/workflows/compliance-matrix.yml`). Until `#602` (`319c702d`) removed the step's `continue-on-error`, that failure was reported as a pass: the run for `34709d55` logs `uv: command not found` and finishes green. The workflow runs only on pushes to `main`, so no pull request showed the break (POAM-2026-169).

**Resolved scope (automated check bypass under CM-3, CM-5), until 2026-10-27:**
Both rules sat in ruleset 19769096, so the admin bypass also skipped the required checks. The first version of this entry said the bypass "lifts enforcement" of the checks, which implied they had passed and were merely unenforced. GitHub's rule-suite log, which records each rule's own result for every push, shows otherwise:

| Pushes to `main`, 2026-09-27 to 2026-10-27 | Count |
|---|---|
| Passed both rules: other authors' pull requests #455, #469, #484, #485 and #510 | 5 |
| Bypassed the review only; required checks passed | 47 |
| Bypassed with required checks not passing at push time | 163 |

The 163 are 157 merges of the maintainer's pull requests, 2 approved pull requests by other authors (#479, #483), 2 direct pushes and 2 advisory merges. At least 35 went in with a required check reported as failing: Lint on all 35, and on 16 of them, all before 2026-10-06, also the region-matrix Pytest jobs that #407 replaced. The rest had required checks still running or not yet reported.

From 2026-10-05 13:45 EDT the ruleset required a "Pytest Logic Tests" check that CI did not emit until #407 merged on 2026-10-06 at 09:22. Two pushes fell in that window: a direct push, which had no checks at all, and #407 itself, whose Lint check failed. Neither went in failing because of the missing check.

`gh pr checks` was no safe substitute: on #546 it listed 48 of the PR's 50 check-runs and left out the required "OPA Rego Policy Lint and Test" check. The merges of #550 and #551 were still decided from it; a check through the REST check-runs API on 2026-10-27 found all six required checks successful on both merged heads.

To re-run the counts (this needs admin access, and the window moves with the date of the query):

```bash
R=google/cybernetic-agent-governance-engine
gh api --paginate "repos/$R/rulesets/rule-suites?ref=refs/heads/main&time_period=month&per_page=100" \
  --jq '.[] | [.id, .result] | @tsv' | sort -u > suites.tsv
cut -f2 suites.tsv | sort | uniq -c
cut -f1 suites.tsv | while read -r id; do
  gh api "repos/$R/rulesets/rule-suites/$id" \
    --jq '[.result, (.rule_evaluations[] | select(.rule_type == "required_status_checks") | .result)] | @tsv'
done | sort | uniq -c
```

**Milestones:**
1. **2026-10-27:** [`check_pr_mergeable.sh`](../scripts/check_pr_mergeable.sh) landed in `2d65e15c` (#552), with hermetic tests in [`test_check_pr_mergeable.py`](../tests/test_check_pr_mergeable.py).
2. **2026-10-27:** the required checks moved to ruleset 25966171, which has no bypass actor; ruleset 19769096 keeps only the review rule.
3. **2026-10-29:** `Quickstart Bootstrap & First-DENY Smoke Gate` added to ruleset 25966171, pinned to GitHub Actions (`integration_id: 15368`), making eight required status checks, of which three are pinned to that app.
4. **2026-11-02:** reclassified as an accepted risk, with both compensating controls re-verified (above).
5. **2026-11-02:** all eight required checks pinned to GitHub Actions (ruleset 25966171 version 55884759), and GitHub Actions can no longer approve pull requests.
6. **On second maintainer onboarding:** a second maintainer reviews the first maintainer's pull requests.

**Closure criteria:** Five consecutive merges to `main` recorded as `pass` in the rule-suites log, beginning after a second maintainer is onboarded, of which at least three are pull requests authored by the original maintainer and approved by the second. This criterion decides when the item closes, not when merging is allowed.

### POAM-2026-114: Causal Tier Refusal Paths Never Operated (Finance)

**Control:** NIST SI-10 (Information Input Validation), CA-7 (Continuous Monitoring); `CTRL_MRM_004` (marginal risk boundary), `CTRL_TEL_003` (placebo refutation)
**Risk Level:** Moderate (advisory severity pending maintainer decision)
**Status:** Open — remediation implemented, closes with the fix release and its security advisory
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-26
**Target Closure:** With the fix release

**Description:**
The finance causal tier ([`causal_tier.py`](../src/cage_finance/tiers/causal_tier.py), `execute_trade` only) has two refusal paths once its telemetry gates pass, and neither operated in v3.0.0–v3.3.0:
1. **Marginal risk boundary.** DoWhy estimated β on the raw trade amount (risk per dollar), and the boundary then divided `β × amount` by `normalization_scale` (10,000) a second time. On the finance synthetic telemetry the boundary tripped above about $91.4M instead of the documented rule's $9,144 (`0.5 + β × amount > 0.95`, see POAM-2026-057), above OPA's $1M ceiling, so it never decided anything.
2. **Placebo refutation (phase 2).** The refuter used dowhy's default placebo, which for a float treatment is drawn from N(0, 0): every simulated effect is 0.0 and the p-value is NaN (normal test) or a finite 1.0 (bootstrap test, from 100 simulations). The gatekeeper defaulted a missing p-value to 1.0 and skipped the lock on NaN, so phase 2 always returned a trusted verdict and the `GOVERNANCE_TRACE` evidence recorded the causal stage as `PASS`.

Reach: finance `execute_trade` in non-enforcing postures on synthetic telemetry (the quickstart, GKE `--env dev`), and in any posture with at least `causal.min_samples` fresh matching live traces. Other controls (OPA, FTRA HITL above $10,000, fiscal, CBF) still bounded the trades the tier admitted. Releases before 3.0.0 never reached these checks because of a separate defect. **Re-scoped 2026-10-29:** since `db73f40b` (#573, 2026-10-28) the tier is opt-in (`CAGE_CAUSAL_TIER_ENABLED`, default `false`), so this reach applies only to deployments that enable it; this is a defect of the optional tier, not of the core admissibility path.

**Remediation:**
1. [`validate_world_model()`](../src/gateway/governance/causal/gatekeeper.py) fits β on `treatment / normalization_scale` (both call sites pass the spec's scale: `CausalGatekeeper._validate_world_model` and the [`WorldModelRefresher`](../src/gateway/governance/causal/world_model_refresher.py) process-pool call). The boundary formula is unchanged, so the scale cancels and the trip point is `0.45 × normalization_scale / β` ($9,144 on synthetic telemetry). The scale is part of the verdict cache key, `CausalSpec` rejects a non-finite or non-positive scale, and the `CAUSAL_NORMALIZATION_SCALE` environment override was removed.
2. Phase 2 permutes the treatment (`placebo_type="permute"`), keeps every simulated effect, and returns an untrusted verdict when the result is missing or non-finite (`placebo_result_invalid`) or the effects have zero spread (`placebo_degenerate`). The `p < 0.05` lock direction is unchanged.

**Verification (2026-10-26, local branch, hermetic):**
1. [`tests/test_causal_risk_boundary.py`](../tests/test_causal_risk_boundary.py) and [`tests/test_causal_placebo_refutation.py`](../tests/test_causal_placebo_refutation.py): the real estimator trips between $9,143 and $9,145 for scales 1, 200 and 10,000; the tier and a DRY_RUN pipeline deny $9,500 with `("causal", "FAIL")` evidence; dowhy's degenerate placebo is refused at 50 and 100 simulations; every missing or non-finite result is refused; the process-pool and in-thread paths agree.
2. **Pending before closure:** the fix commit on `main` (via the advisory), the OSCAL component update and exporter run (within 2 business days of merge), and Lula (the `lula` CLI is not installed locally; no Kubernetes resource was added, removed or renamed).

### POAM-2026-113: provider_01 Authority Request Facts Are Self-Asserted (EU_ECB)

**Control:** EU AI Act Art. 27 (`CTRL_FRIA_006`), NIST AC-3, SI-10
**Risk Level:** Moderate
**Status:** Open
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-25
**Target Closure:** 2026-12-31
**Reported by:** Dominik Blain (`@dom-omg`), Ironproof, in GHSA-f6xq-23f7-9grm. Thank you.

**Description:**
[`_build_cage_authority_request()`](../src/integrations/provider_01/provider.py) maps the kernel's FRIA payload to the partner's 37-field authority request. Three facts the partner relies on are constants: `mandate_status: "ACTIVE"`, `kya_status: "VERIFIED"` and `actor_authenticated: true`. The mandate (`mandate_id`, `mandate_max_amount`, `mandate_currency`, `mandate_valid_until`, `permitted_source_accounts`, `permitted_counterparty_class`), screening (`screening_status`, `screening_captured_at`, `screening_max_age_seconds`, `screening_source`) and counterparty/account state come from request params, with permissive defaults when absent (`DEFAULT-MANDATE`, 1,000,000, `2099-12-31T23:59:59Z`, `UNRESTRICTED`, `CLEAR`, now). `target` is read from a top-level envelope field that the kernel payload does not carry, so it goes out empty on the governor path. Request params are partly caller-controlled, so the external authority can be asked to decide on facts that nobody verified. The GHSA-f6xq-23f7-9grm fix made the kernel send the verified workload identity (`actor_id` / `operator_urn`) and trace id; it deliberately left this mapping unchanged, because provider_01 is a partner-built Tier 1 adapter on the frozen Phase 3 v0.2 wire contract, every change to it must be validated over the wire against the partner sandbox (AGENTS.md), and the Step 1 harness uses these params to simulate an expired mandate and stale screening.

**Remediation Plan:**
1. Derive `actor_authenticated` and `kya_status` from the verified workload identity in the payload (false / unverified when `operator_urn` is empty), never from constants.
2. Stop defaulting mandate, screening, counterparty and account facts, and populate `target` from a server-bound route. Either resolve these facts server-side from a posture-completing reference source (deterministically seeded, with fault injection for every fail-closed path), or omit them so the partner resolves its own records; a request missing a required fact fails closed in the adapter.
3. Agree the change with the partner as a wire-contract revision, and update [`FLOWSIGNAL_PARTNER_SPECIFICATION.md`](partners/provider_01/FLOWSIGNAL_PARTNER_SPECIFICATION.md) bilaterally.

**Closure Criteria:**
1. The partner has adopted the revised contract, and an over-the-wire conformance run against the partner sandbox covers ALLOW, REFUSE, ESCALATE and every fail-closed path.
2. The Step 1 harness ([`verify_provider_01_wire_and_hostile.py`](../scripts/verify_provider_01_wire_and_hostile.py)) is re-run live without injecting mandate or screening facts through params.
3. Record the merge commit SHA and the actual date of the live run here.

### POAM-2026-112: Deferral Envelope Wrapping and Evidence Format Normalization

**Control:** NIST AU-3 (Content of Audit Records), AU-10 (Non-Repudiation), SC-13 (Cryptographic Protection)
**Risk Level:** Moderate
**Status:** Closed
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-07
**Date Closed:** 2026-10-07
**Related Commit:** `fix/defer-evidence-format` (pending squash merge)

**Description:**
Generating the real-code-path VEIP v0.1 sample evidence bundle against `bootstrap_governor()` and the ASGI gateway uncovered five evidence-format gaps:
1. **Unwrapped `DEFER` and `REQUIRE_APPROVAL` gateway responses and duplicate `external_attestations`:** [`validate_action_endpoint`](../src/gateway/server/governance_middleware.py) wrapped only `ALLOW` and `NARROW` verdicts in a KMS-signed v3.0 [`GovernanceEnvelope`](../src/gateway/governance/governance_envelope.py), returning `DEFER` (non-`EXTERNAL_HOLD`) and `REQUIRE_APPROVAL` as unsigned flat `{"schema_version": "1.0.0", ...}` JSON. On `ALLOW`/`NARROW`, `result["external_attestations"]` was also left inside `envelope.payload`, serializing `ExternalAttestation` dataclasses twice under two different schemas.
2. **`Violation` objects serialized as Python `repr(...)` strings:** `_park_defer_context()` and `post_hitl_deferral_event()` in [`verdicts.py`](../src/gateway/governance/governor/verdicts.py) recorded `[str(v) for v in violations]`. Because [`Violation`](../src/gateway/governance/contracts.py) defines `to_dict()` rather than `__str__`, `str(v)` emitted `"Violation(tier='warrant', ..., kind=<ViolationKind.RELIANCE_INELIGIBLE: 'reliance_ineligible'>, bound=None)"` into `DeferToken.opa_input_snapshot["violations"]` and `GOVERNANCE_DEFERRAL` stream records.
3. **Empty `control_id: ""` on `GOVERNANCE_DEFERRAL` and `GOVERNANCE_REFUSAL` stream entries:** `deferral_event()`, `post_hitl_deferral_event()`, and `publish_refusal()` in [`verdicts.py`](../src/gateway/governance/governor/verdicts.py) omitted `controlId`, so [`EvidenceStreamSink._append`](../src/gateway/governance/evidence/stream.py) wrote `control_id: ""` in the Redis stream header.
4. **Truncated 16-hex (`64`-bit) `params_hash`:** [`generate_seal_with_evidence()`](../src/gateway/governance/routing_seal.py), `deferral_event()`, and `post_hitl_deferral_event()` truncated `params_hash` to `[:16]`, whereas `compute_action_hash()` and `envelope.subject.action_hash` use the full 64-hex SHA-256 digest.
5. **Empty `governance_context.tiers_passed`:** [`SymbolicGovernor.validate_action()`](../src/gateway/governance/governor/governor.py) did not project `PipelineResult.stage_outcomes` into `tiers_passed`, leaving `envelope.governance_context.tiers_passed` empty (`[]`).

**Remediation:**
1. [`governance_middleware.py`](../src/gateway/server/governance_middleware.py): wrap all HTTP 200 `/validate-action` verdicts (`ALLOW`, `NARROW`, `DEFER`, `REQUIRE_APPROVAL`) in `GovernanceEnvelopeBuilder().build(...)`, exclude `external_attestations` from `envelope.payload` (keeping them at the top-level `envelope.external_attestations`), and serialize any `Violation` instances in `payload.violations` via `v.to_dict()`.
2. [`verdicts.py`](../src/gateway/governance/governor/verdicts.py): serialize `violations` via `v.to_dict()` in `_park_defer_context()` and `post_hitl_deferral_event()`; use `_error_message(violations[0])` in `build_refusal_receipt()`; resolve `controlId` via `_resolve_deferral_control_id()` on `GOVERNANCE_DEFERRAL` and `receipt.control_id` on `GOVERNANCE_REFUSAL`; and emit the full 64-hex `params_hash`.
3. [`routing_seal.py`](../src/gateway/governance/routing_seal.py) & [`stream.py`](../src/gateway/governance/evidence/stream.py): remove `[:16]` from `params_hash` in `GOVERNANCE_DECISION` and accept `controlId` or `control_id` in `_append()`.
4. [`governor.py`](../src/gateway/governance/governor/governor.py): add `_context_meta(result)` to include `tiers_passed` (`[name for name, outcome in result.stage_outcomes if outcome == "PASS"]`) on every `validate_action()` return path.

**Verification (2026-10-07):**
1. [`tests/test_envelope_transport.py`](../tests/test_envelope_transport.py), [`tests/test_governance_middleware.py`](../tests/test_governance_middleware.py), [`tests/governor/test_warrant_reliance_e2e.py`](../tests/governor/test_warrant_reliance_e2e.py), and [`tests/governor/test_post_hitl_approval_spend.py`](../tests/governor/test_post_hitl_approval_spend.py) pass (104 passed), asserting v3.0 envelope wrapping on `DEFER`/`REQUIRE_APPROVAL`, no `external_attestations` duplication in `payload`, populated `tiers_passed`, non-empty `control_id`/`controlId`, 64-hex `params_hash`, and structured `Violation` dicts.
2. **Lula: not run.** No Kubernetes resource was added, removed or renamed. The finding is evidenced by the pytest files above, listed under `CODE_EVIDENCE_FINDINGS` in [`check_poam_lula_divergence.py`](../scripts/check_poam_lula_divergence.py).

### POAM-2026-111: Reliance Evidence Missing Warrant Contract Fields

**Control:** NIST AU-3 (Content of Audit Records), AU-10 (Non-Repudiation)
**Risk Level:** Moderate
**Status:** Closed
**Date Opened:** 2026-10-07
**Date Closed:** 2026-10-07
**Related Commit:** `fix/reliance-contract-fields` (pending squash merge; rebased onto #436, which holds POAM-2026-110)

**Description:**
The VEIP Warrant Contract v0.1 evidence contract expects every decision's hash-chained evidence to carry `warrant_id`, `norm_id`, `digest`, `reliance_status`, `governing_version`, `residual_risk_ref` (opaque, partner Q5) and `attested_at`. The kernel [`RelianceRecord`](../src/gateway/governance/warrant/reliance.py), bound into seal, deferral and refusal evidence since `0cdb5aaa` (#427), had no `residual_risk_ref`, `attested_at` (it had `evaluated_at`) or `issuing_authority`.
